# Acute Subclavian Artery Thrombosis

*Hand · 1 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Definition** — acute UE ischaemia from **proximal occlusion at subclavian artery** level

**Incidence** — rare, <1%

**Risk factors** — **aortic arch abnormality**, **aortic arch syndrome**, **trauma**, arterial catheterisation, pre-existing stenosis/atheroma at subclavian ostium

**Patterns** — slow occlusion (atherosclerosis) · **blue finger syndrome** (repeated microemboli) · acute global ischaemia (large emboli)

### Anatomy

**Left subclavian** — **3rd branch of aortic arch**

**Right subclavian** — branch of **brachiocephalic (innominate)** artery

**Vertebral arteries** — arise from subclavian → join as **basilar** → cerebellum → risk of **vertebrobasilar insufficiency / basilar stroke**

![Subclavian & vertebral branching](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12302-arterial-anatomy-demonstrating-branching-of-the-subclavian-a.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest cause of UE thromboembolism** — **atrial fibrillation** (80% of UE thromboembolism)

**Cardiac embolism** — up to **93%** of acute UE ischaemia; AF → loss of atrial contraction → stasis → thrombus

**Other cardiac sources** — cardiomyopathy, valvular disease, ASD, aortic arch abnormality; endocarditis → septic emboli

**Hand arterial occlusion (general)** — usually **post-traumatic ulnar artery**; venous occlusion → hypercoagulable state

## 3 Presentation

**History** — sudden onset, no inciting event

**Symptoms** — **arm pain**, **paraesthesia**, nausea, exertional pain/fatigue

**Exam** — **cyanotic/pale, cold** limb; **pulses absent** (axillary, brachial, radial, ulnar); **neuro exam often normal**

**Non-occlusive** — pulses absent but **Doppler signal present** (monophasic)

**BP** — differs between arms

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Arteriography** | **Recommended diagnostic test**; allows **percutaneous treatment** at same sitting |
| CT angiography | Fast; rules out other causes |
| MRA | cannot treat at diagnosis |
| Doppler US | Signal may persist in non-occlusive disease |
| Echocardiography | Rules out **cardiac embolus** after treatment |

![Arch angiogram, subclavian thrombus, stent with vertebral protection balloon](../../MCQ/attachments/q5818-illa-illus-a.jpg)

### Labs

**Hypercoagulable panel** — rule out underlying disorder; labs usually unremarkable

## 5 Management

```
Acute subclavian thrombosis
├─ Symptomatic limb ischaemia → percutaneous or open revascularisation
│      ├─ percutaneous → thrombolysis / PTA / aspiration / stent
│      └─ open → embolectomy-thrombectomy / bypass
└─ Adjunct (all) → IV + oral anticoagulation
```

### Arterial thromboembolism – treatment & anticoagulation

**Percutaneous** — **intra-arterial thrombolysis**, **PTA**, **manual aspiration**, **stenting** (protect vertebral artery with balloon)

**Open** — embolectomy/thrombectomy, bypass grafting

**Medical** — heparin, aspirin, clopidogrel, IV thrombolysis, GP IIb/IIIa antagonists; limited role alone if limb-threatening

**✗ Wrong option: aortic root replacement** — for **ascending thoracic aortic dissection**, not subclavian disease

**AF prophylaxis** — **anticoagulants**\
✗ Wrong options: antiplatelets = mechanical valves · statin = ischaemic cardiomyopathy · antibiotics = endocarditis · β-blocker = rate only

| AF anticoagulation (AHA/ACC/HRS 2014) | Factors |
|---|---|
| **High risk** (any 1 → anticoagulate) | Prior stroke/TIA, systemic thromboembolism, **age ≥75** |
| **Moderate** (>1 → anticoagulate) | Age 65–74, ♀, **HTN**, **DM**, heart failure, arterial disease |

## 6 Complications

**Limb ischaemia** — gangrene, limb loss; **delayed diagnosis** → amputation

**Basilar stroke** — prolonged ischaemia, or **iatrogenic emboli** if vertebrobasilar system unprotected during endovascular Rx

**Prognosis** — favourable with prompt Rx; untreated → stroke + amputation

## How it's asked

**"Absent pulses + Doppler signal — treatment EXCEPT"** → **aortic root replacement**\
✗ Wrong options: thrombolysis, PTA, aspiration, stent

**"Best diagnostic test"** → **arteriography** (diagnose + treat)

**"Commonest cause of UE thromboembolism"** → **atrial fibrillation**

**"AF patient — reduce hand thromboembolism"** → **anticoagulants**

**"Feared complication of delay / endovascular Rx"** → **basilar stroke**

# AIN Compressive Neuropathy

*Hand · 2 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & aetiology

**Eponym** — **Kiloh-Nevin syndrome** = AIN compression (TO) (✗ Wrong options: PIN compression (TO))

**Incidence** — rare; **<1%** of median neuropathies

**Commonest cause** — **transient neuritis (Parsonage-Turner)**; non-traumatic AIN palsy usually = **neuritis** (viral, autoimmune)

**Parsonage-Turner** — viral **brachial neuritis** → **bilateral AIN signs** (TO); preceded by **intense shoulder pain + viral prodrome**

**Compression sites** — **deep head of pronator teres** (most common anatomical, TO) · **FDS arch** (TO) · **edge of lacertus fibrosus** (TO) · **Gantzer's muscle** = accessory head FPL (TO) · thrombosed radial/ulnar/AI artery · accessory FDS→FDP muscle · FCRB, palmaris profundus

**Surgical finding (Park)** — commonest compressive structure at exploration = **fibrous band of FDS**; 4/11 had **no compression**

![Gantzer's muscle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6019-gantzer-39-s-muscle.jpg)

### AIN motor distribution (FPL, FDP index)

**Origin** — **terminal motor branch of median n.**; ~**4 cm distal to medial epicondyle** (Orthobullets) · **5–8 cm distal to lateral epicondyle** (OB/Orthobullets); dorsoradial aspect of median n.

**Course** — between FDS & FDP → between **FPL & FDP** → on **interosseous membrane** with AI artery → **pronator quadratus** → wrist capsule (radiocarpal, intercarpal, DRUJ)

**Innervates (3)** — **FPL** · **FDP index + middle** · **pronator quadratus**

**Sensory** — **none** cutaneous (articular branches only)

**Not AIN** — **pronator teres** (median, before AIN leaves) · FCR (median) · FCU (ulnar) · FDB (foot, medial plantar)

![AIN lineage](../../MCQ/attachments/q9008-illa-ain-lineage.jpg)

### Martin-Gruber anastomosis

**MGA** — **motor** connection **median/AIN → ulnar** in **proximal 1/3 forearm**; sensory spared; **most common forearm anomalous innervation**

**Prevalence** — **15%** (Orthobullets) · **15–40%** (OB) · pooled **19.5%**, 57.6% from AIN (meta-analysis); more often **right**

**Supplies** — **adductor pollicis + dorsal interossei** (intrinsics) → complete AIN palsy may show **intrinsic weakness**

**High ulnar laceration + intrinsics work** — **Martin-Gruber**

**MGA type II** — **most common**; median fibres → **1st dorsal interosseous** → median laceration = median deficit **+ cannot abduct index**; ulnar sensation preserved

**MGA II on NCS** — usually **normal** (1st DI not routinely tested) → careful exam

| Anastomosis | Site | Direction | Clue / prevalence |
|---|---|---|---|
| ![MGA](../../MCQ/attachments/q219850-illa-mga.jpg) **Martin-Gruber** | Forearm | Median/AIN → ulnar (motor) | High ulnar cut, intrinsics work · median cut, no index abduction |
| ![Marinacci](../../MCQ/attachments/q219850-illb-ma.jpg) **Marinacci** | Forearm | Ulnar → median ("reverse MG") | **Rarest** (0.7% / 4%); proximal median lesion with **APB/thenar preserved** |
| ![Riche-Cannieu](../../MCQ/attachments/q219850-illc-rc.jpg) **Riche-Cannieu** | Hand | Recurrent median ↔ deep ulnar (motor ± sensory) | 55%; **preserved thenar function**; "all-ulnar hand" |
| ![Berrettini](../../MCQ/attachments/q219850-illd-ba.jpg) **Berrettini** | Hand | Common digital nn. (3rd–4th), **sensory** | >80%; injured at CTR → ↓ 3rd web sensation; no motor |

**Not median–ulnar** — **Linburg-Comstock** = FPL–FDP index **tendinous** connection · **anconeus epitrochlearis** = cubital tunnel compression

## 3 Presentation

**Hallmark** — **motor deficit without sensory loss** (TO); **normal median sensory exam**

**OK sign** — **cannot make OK sign** = Kiloh-Nevin sign (TO) → weak **thumb IP flexion (FPL)** + **index DIP flexion (FDP)**

**Pain** — vague deep forearm pain, often **absent**; if present lasts **2–3 wk**

**PQ test** — weak resisted pronation with **elbow maximally flexed** (neutralises pronator teres)

**Tenodesis** — **intact tenodesis** = tendons intact → distinguishes from **FPL attritional rupture** (RA)

**Severe** — forearm atrophy

![Inability to flex thumb IP and index DIP](../../MCQ/attachments/q725-a-ain-palsy.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical + EMG/NCS**

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — if **mass** suspected; T2/STIR ↑ signal FPL, FDP, PQ → **PQ oedema = most reliable**

**EMG** — **fibrillations, positive sharp waves** in **FPL + FDP index/middle**; rules out proximal lesion, grades severity, tracks recovery

**Differentials** — flexor tendon rupture · proximal lesion (C-spine, plexus) · **pronator syndrome** · CTS · conversion disorder (not if objective EMG findings)

## 5 Management

```
AIN palsy
├─ No mass / no trauma → OBSERVATION, rest, PT (12 mo)
│      └─ no recovery at 12 mo → SURGICAL DECOMPRESSION
├─ Space-occupying mass → DECOMPRESSION + excision
└─ Penetrating injury → EXPLORE + primary repair
```

### AIN syndrome – diagnosis & observation (neuritis)

**First line** — **observation, rest, PT** in all without a mass; treatment mostly **non-surgical** (TO); splinting at 90° (Rodner) · massage, forearm stretches

**Recovery** — begins **3–12 mo**; full by **18 mo** (mean ~9 mo) → "**gradual improvement over 1 y**" · "**persistent sx <2 y then spontaneous resolution**"

**Better prognosis** — **age <40** (faster recovery)

**Not indicated** — steroid injection (no data) · CTR · early tendon/nerve transfer

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Clear **space-occupying mass** | Early decompression |
| Failed nonop | **12 mo** (Orthobullets, Chi & Harness) · **6 mo** in complete palsy (Sood & Burke) |
| **Penetrating injury** | Explore + **end-to-end repair** |
| Age <40 | Surgery controversial (uniform nonop response) |

**Surgical success** — ~**75%**

**Decompression** — lazy-S proximal volar forearm; release superficial head PT, **lacertus fibrosus**, FDS arch, **Gantzer's**, ligate crossing vessels; elbow ROM day 2

## 6 Complications

**Persistent AIN deficit** — rare (~5–10 cases) → **tendon transfer: brachioradialis → FPL**

## X vs Y

| | **AIN syndrome** | Pronator syndrome | PIN syndrome | FPL rupture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory | **Normal** | Radial 3½ + thenar | Normal | Normal |
| Motor | FPL, FDP-I/M, PQ | ± | **Thumb extension/retropulsion**, finger ext | FPL only |
| Pain | Absent / 2–3 wk | **Proximal forearm** | Lateral elbow | — |
| Tenodesis | **Intact** | Intact | Intact | **Lost** |
| Release | Gantzer's, FDS arch | **Lacertus fibrosus** | **Arcade of Frohse** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Kiloh-Nevin — all true EXCEPT"** → **compression of PIN** (TO)

**"Parsonage-Turner + weak pronation, no sensory loss"** → **AIN syndrome → observation**\
✗ Wrong options: Gantzer release

**"Fibs in FPL + FDP-I after flu, no numbness — next step?"** → **observation, improves over 1 y**

**"Weak thumb IP flexion, normal 2PD, normal MRI — course?"** → **resolves spontaneously <2 y**

**"Stab proximal forearm, no index DIP flexion — what else fails?"** → **FPL**\
✗ Wrong options: pronator teres, FCR

**"High ulnar laceration, intrinsics still work"** → **Martin-Gruber**

**"Antecubital laceration, median deficit + no index abduction"** → **Martin-Gruber type II**

**"Most reliable MRI sign"** → **PQ oedema**

**"Persistent AIN palsy — transfer?"** → **brachioradialis → FPL**

# Apert Syndrome

*Hand · 3 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### FGFR2 mutation & clinical features

**Synonym** — **acrocephalosyndactyly type 1**: cranium + hands + feet

**Incidence** — rare; **1/80,000** live births

**Features** — **bilateral complex syndactyly** hands & feet (**index, middle, ring** most affected) · **symphalangism** · **craniosynostosis** → flat skull, broad forehead (**acrocephaly**) · **hypertelorism** · glenoid hypoplasia · radioulnar synostosis

**Cognition** — normal → moderately disabled

**Feet** — predictable dysmorphism + **progressive fusions** through skeletal maturity

### Genes & lab markers

| Gene | Condition |
|---|---|
| **FGFR2** | **Apert** (AD, most new cases **sporadic**) |
| ✗ Wrong option: FGFR3 | Achondroplasia |
| ✗ Wrong option: COL2A1 (chr 12) | SED congenita |
| ✗ Wrong option: SEDL | X-linked SED tarda |
| ✗ Wrong option: fibrillin | Marfan |
| ✗ Wrong option: SHH | Radial longitudinal deficiency (TAR, Holt-Oram, VACTERL) |
| ✗ Wrong option: PMP22 | Charcot-Marie-Tooth |
| ✗ Wrong option: COL1A1 | Osteogenesis imperfecta |

## 3 Presentation

**Face** — **dysmorphic**, flattened features, **wide-set eyes**

**Rosebud hand** — complex syndactyly; **index, middle, ring share a common nail**

![Apert facies](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6078-apert-facies.jpg)

![Rosebud hands](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6078-apert-hands.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **complex syndactyly** (bony fusion of central digits)

![Rosebud hand X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6078-xray-rosebud-hand-colorado-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Apert hand
├─ ~1 y → release BORDER digits
└─ ~1.5 y → digit reconstruction: central 3 → 2 digits
```

## How it's asked

**"Gene in Apert?"** → **FGFR2**\
✗ Wrong options: FGFR3, COL2A1, SEDL, fibrillin

**"Flat face + wide-set eyes + fused fingers"** → **Apert**\
✗ Wrong options: MED, cleidocranial, Noonan, achondroplasia

**"Infant rosebud hands — mutation?"** → **FGFR2**\
✗ Wrong options: SHH, PMP22, COL1A1

**"Digits most affected"** → **index, middle, ring**

# Atypical Mycobacterium Infections

*Hand · 4 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & organisms

**Incidence** — rare; **0.05 / 100,000 / y**

**Who** — **marine workers**, fishermen; **immunocompromised** host (most have some immunosuppression)

**Site** — **hand & wrist in 50%**

**Incubation** — avg **2 wk** (up to 6 mo); time to diagnosis often **>1 y** (missed Dx)

**Prior steroid injection** — **poorer outcome**

| Organism | Habitat | Culture |
|---|---|---|
| ***M. marinum*** | **Most common**; stagnant fresh/salt water, **aquariums**, fish nets | **Löwenstein-Jensen at 30–32 °C** |
| *M. kansasii* | Soil | — |
| *M. terrae* | **Soil** | **37 °C** |
| *M. avium intracellulare* | **Terminal AIDS** (also non-HIV) | **Room temperature** |

### Mycobacterium marinum

**Classic** — fisherman/aquarium cut → dorsal hand swelling weeks later → grows on **Löwenstein-Jensen at 30 °C** after ~2 wk → ***M. marinum***

**Other aquatic pathogens** — *Vibrio* spp., *Aeromonas hydrophila*

### Fishmonger's disease (erysipeloid)

**Organisms (TO)** — commonest: ***Streptococcus iniae*, *Vibrio vulnificus*, *M. marinum*** · others: ***Erysipelothrix rhusiopathiae***, *Aeromonas hydrophila*, *Vibrio damsela*, *Proteus vulgaris*, *Morganella morganii*, *Lactococcus garvieae*, *M. abscessus*, *Streptococcus halichoeri*

**Mechanism (TO)** — penetration by sharp instruments or **fish bony spines**; immunosuppressed → **high morbidity & mortality**

**NOT a cause (TO)** — ***Pseudomonas aeruginosa***

### Compound palmar ganglion (TB tenosynovitis)

**Definition (TO)** — **tuberculous tenosynovitis** of wrist/hand flexor tendons; **radial + ulnar bursae**

**Shape (TO)** — **hourglass / dumb-bell** swelling **proximal & distal to flexor retinaculum** (across wrist crease) → **positive cross-fluctuation**

**Bursal anatomy (TO)** — ulnar bursa communicates with radial bursa in **80%** → **horseshoe** tenosynovitis

**Spread (TO)** — **little finger → ulnar bursa → Parona's space** (more commonly associated) · thumb → radial → ulnar bursa

**Causes (TO)** — **TB** (commonest comorbidity) · **RA** · infection · SLE · gout · PVNS of tendon sheath · fungal

**NOT a cause (TO)** — **malignancy**

**Diagnosis (TO)** — clinical, **always confirmed by histopathology**

**Treatment (TO)** — progresses to gross destruction → **excision without delay**

## 3 Presentation

**Early** — **papules, ulcers, nodules** on hand; cutaneous rash; often **single nodule → lymphatic spread**

**Late** — **tenosynovitis, septic arthritis, osteomyelitis**

**Look-alike** — indistinguishable from TB infection

![M. marinum nodules and ulcers](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6107-mycobacterium-infection.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical + **culture** (cultures & sensitivities = key)

**Histology** — **granulomas ± acid-fast bacilli** on AFB stain

**Culture** — **Löwenstein-Jensen**; *M. marinum* **30–32 °C** · MAI room temp · *M. terrae* 37 °C

![M. marinum on Löwenstein-Jensen agar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6107-image-of-lowenstein-jensen-agar-growing-m-marinum.jpg)

**Differentials** — herpetic whitlow, fungal infection, psoriasis

## 5 Management

```
Atypical mycobacterial hand infection
├─ Early (papules / nodules) → ORAL ANTIMYCOBACTERIALS
│      └─ osteomyelitis → ADD RIFAMPIN
└─ Late (tenosynovitis / joint / bone) → DEBRIDEMENT + combination Abx 3–6 mo
```

**Oral agents** — **ethambutol, tetracycline, TMP-SMX, clarithromycin, azithromycin**; **+ rifampin** if osteomyelitis

**Mortality** — **32%** with sepsis / untreated

## X vs Y

| Organism | Classic exposure |
|---|---|
| ***M. marinum*** | Fish nets, aquarium; LJ 30 °C |
| *M. terrae* | Soil; 37 °C |
| *Sporothrix schenckii* | **Thorn** (rose gardener) — fungus |
| *Bartonella henselae* | **Cat scratch**; epitrochlear lymphadenopathy; endocarditis |
| *Eikenella corrodens* | **Human bite**; Gram −ve |
| *Erysipelothrix* / *S. iniae* / *Vibrio* | Fishmonger's disease |

## How it's asked

**"Fisherman cut, LJ medium at 30 °C"** → ***M. marinum***\
✗ Wrong options: *M. terrae*, *Sporothrix*, *Bartonella*, *Eikenella*

**"MAI culture temperature"** → **room temperature**

**"Add for osteomyelitis"** → **rifampin**

**"Fishmonger's disease NOT caused by"** → ***Pseudomonas aeruginosa*** (TO)

**"Compound palmar ganglion — true"** → **more commonly with little finger infection** (TO)

**"Dumb-bell wrist swelling, cross-fluctuation — comorbidity?"** → **tuberculosis** (TO)

**"Compound palmar ganglion NOT seen in"** → **malignancy** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Base of Thumb Fractures

*Hand · 5 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & anatomy

**Incidence** — **80% of thumb fractures involve the MC base**; commonest pattern = **extra-articular epibasal**

**CMC joint** — **saddle-shaped**, trapezium + 1st MC base; flex–ext ~53°, abd–add ~42°, very limited axial rotation

**Volar beak ligament** — = **anterior oblique ligament (AOL)**; trapezial tuberosity → volar edge 1st MC → **keeps volar-ulnar Bennett fragment attached to trapezium**

**Dorsoradial ligament** — dorsoradial trapezial tubercle → dorsal MC base; important for CMC stability but does not hold Bennett fragment

**Do not hold the fragment** — anterior/posterior intermetacarpal ligaments (no trapezial attachment), posterior oblique ligament

![CMC ligaments: AOL (volar beak) holds the Bennett fragment](../../MCQ/attachments/q213993-illa-screen-shot-2020-02-23-at-11-58-16-am.jpg)

### Deforming forces (APL, adductor pollicis, EPL) & reduction manoeuvre

| Muscle | Nerve | Pull on shaft fragment |
|---|---|---|
| **Abductor pollicis longus** (main force, TO) | **PIN** | **Proximal, dorsal, radial** |
| **Adductor pollicis** | **Ulnar** | **Supination + adduction** (ulnar angulation) |
| Extensor pollicis longus | PIN | Proximal, dorsal, radial; adds ulnar angulation (less important) |
| EPB | PIN | Small contribution |

**Primary deforming pair** — **APL + adductor pollicis** (OB, TO) · three-muscle answer = **APL + AdP + EPL** (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Nerves of deforming muscles** — **ulnar + PIN**\
✗ Wrong options: AIN · recurrent median

**Not deforming** — **APB**, FPB, opponens (thenar, recurrent median), FPL, PQ, **brachioradialis** (distal radius)

**Shaft displacement (TO)** — Bennett: shaft **lateral, proximal, dorsal**; Rolando: diaphyseal fragment **usually not displaced**

**Reduction manoeuvre** — **longitudinal traction + palmar abduction + pronation** + pressure on MC base

![Deforming forces on Bennett fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-bennett.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **axial load on partially flexed (adducted) thumb**, e.g. clenched-fist punch (TO)

**Pathoanatomy** — imperfect reduction → ↑ joint contact pressure → **early arthritis**; excessive angulation → **MCP hyperextension deformity** (malunion)

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — base-of-thumb pain, swelling, ecchymosis, CMC tenderness, painful ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **Roberts view** (true AP: full pronation, dorsum of thumb on cassette) + **true lateral (hand pronated 30°, beam 15° distal)** + oblique

**Best view** — **30° pronated view**

**Bennett** — small MC base fragment still articulating with trapezium

**Rolando** — **Y sign** = base split into volar + dorsal fragments

**Traction view** — Rolando / comminuted pattern

**CT** — complex patterns, fragment detail

![Rolando Y sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-y-sign.jpg)

### Classification of 1st MC base fractures

| Type | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| Extra-articular oblique | Oblique line, joint spared | ![Extra-articular oblique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-extraarticular-oblique.jpg) |
| Extra-articular transverse | Transverse line, joint spared | ![Extra-articular transverse](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-extraarticular-transverse.jpg) |
| **Bennett** | **Partial intra-articular**, palmar-ulnar fragment; oblique fracture-dislocation | ![Bennett](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-life-in-fast-lane.jpg) |
| **Rolando** | **Complete intra-articular**, **Y / T / V-shaped**, 3-part (TO) | ![Rolando](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6036-cow70-1-moved.jpg) |
| Comminuted | Severely comminuted complete intra-articular | |

**Rolando (TO)** — **intra-articular** fracture base 1st MC\
✗ Wrong options: extra-articular · "adductor longus" · commonest thumb fracture-dislocation

### Treatment criteria

| Pattern | Accept / nonop | Operate |
|---|---|---|
| Extra-articular | **<30° angulation** after CR | >30° or can't hold in spica → **CRPP** |
| Bennett | **<1 mm** step-off / displacement | **>1 mm** → CRPP or ORIF |
| Rolando | — | <1 mm → CRPP; >1 mm → ORIF / ex-fix; comminution dictates strategy |

**Open reduction threshold** — **>1 mm** displacement (Orthobullets, Soyer) · **>2 mm** step-off despite CR (Carlsen) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
1st MC base fracture
├─ Extra-articular <30° → CR + thumb spica
│      └─ >30° / lost reduction → CRPP
├─ Bennett / Rolando ≤1 mm → spica (Bennett) / CRPP (Rolando)
└─ >1 mm displaced
       ├─ large fragments → ORIF (screws / T-plate)
       ├─ small fragments → CRPP
       └─ soft-tissue injury / too small / impacted → distraction ex-fix
```

**Nonop** — CR (traction, palmar abduction, pronation) + **thumb spica**; >3 wk old with no motion at fracture → accept step-off + cast

**Small avulsion (TO)** — CR + thumb spica **6 wk**; unstable → closed/open reduction + K-wires + spica **4–6 wk** (TO)

**CRPP** — transverse extra-articular → **transarticular** K-wire; oblique → **intermetacarpal** K-wire; risk **loss of reduction**

**ORIF** — **volar Gedda–Moberg approach**; thenar reflected, longitudinal capsulotomy; screws or T-plate; risk **SBRN injury**, wound problems if oedema

**Ex-fix** — two 3 mm pins dorsoradial MC + two in radius ± 2nd MC; risk pin-site infection

**Key outcome factor** — **anatomic articular congruity** > fixation method; reduction predicts **radiographic** arthritis\
✗ Wrong options: symptomatic

## 6 Complications

**Post-traumatic CMC arthritis** — risk ↑ with comminution, major step-off, multiple small fragments

**Malunion** — MCP hyperextension deformity, long-term disability

**Poor prognosis** — Bennett, Rolando, comminuted, delayed treatment · good: acute, extra-articular

## X vs Y

| | **Bennett** | **Rolando** |
|---|---|---|
| Articular | **Partial** intra-articular | **Complete** intra-articular |
| Shape | Oblique, single volar-ulnar fragment | **Y / T / V**, volar + dorsal fragments |
| Held by | AOL (volar beak) | AOL holds volar fragment |
| Shaft | Displaced proximal/dorsal/radial | Usually not displaced (TO) |
| X-ray sign | Fragment stays with trapezium | **Y sign** |

## How it's asked

**"Primary deforming forces on Bennett / Rolando?"** → **APL + adductor pollicis**\
✗ Wrong options: adductor pollicis longus + abductor pollicis

**"Three deforming muscles overcome by reduction?"** → **APL, adductor pollicis, EPL**\
✗ Wrong options: APB · brachioradialis

**"Nerves to the deforming muscles?"** → **ulnar + PIN**\
✗ Wrong options: AIN · recurrent median

**"Structure holding fragment to trapezium?"** → **anterior oblique (volar beak) ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsoradial · intermetacarpal · posterior oblique

**"Reduction manoeuvre?"** → **traction + palmar abduction + pronation**

**"True of Rolando?"** → **intra-articular fracture base 1st MC** (TO)

**"Best radiographic view?"** → **30° pronated true lateral / Roberts view**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Basilar Thumb Arthritis

*Hand · 6 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency in hand** — **DIP > thumb CMC > PIP > MCP** (2nd most common hand OA)

**Who** — **postmenopausal ♀**, Caucasian; 40% ♀ / 25% ♂ >75 y

**Second patient group** — young **manual labourer**, post-traumatic (old intra-articular #) → fusion candidate

**Associated** — **CTS in up to 50%** · **MCP hyperextension** · STT OA (62% at trapeziectomy)

### Anatomy

**Joint** — trapeziometacarpal (TMC) = **biconcave saddle**; CMC joint reactive force = **13× pinch force**

**Anterior oblique (volar beak) lig** — **primary stabiliser vs subluxation**; palmar tubercle trapezium → ulnar MC base; **its attenuation = cause of OA**

**Dorsoradial lig** — **strongest/thickest**; primary restraint to **dorsal dislocation**

**Intermetacarpal lig** — 2nd MC base → 1st MC base; restrains radial translation

**FCR** — runs in palmar groove of trapezium (used in LRTI)

![Ligament anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-ligament-anatomy.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Beak lig attenuation** → **dorsoradial subluxation** of MC base → cartilage wear

**Z (zig-zag) deformity** — painful abduction avoided → **MC adduction + palmar flexion** → 1st web contracture → **compensatory MCP hyperextension** (EPB pull) → ± IP flexion

**NOT a sequela** — **CMC extension deformity** (EXCEPT question)

**Why MCP hyperext matters** — left untreated → deforming force → recurrent MC subluxation after CMC surgery

![Z deformity forces: arrowhead = MC base subluxation, arrow = EPB vector](../../MCQ/attachments/q3669-illa-zigzag-deformity-cmc.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — pain at thumb base, ↓ **pinch/grip**, worse **opening jars**, key pinch

**Exam** — TTP over CMC · **grind test** (axial load + circumduction) · **shear test** (circumduction + translation) · crepitus

**Late signs** — **MC adduction**, 1st web contracture, **fixed MCP hyperextension (Z)**

![Z deformity of thumb](../../MCQ/attachments/q4730-a-fig-a-z-deformity-of-thumb.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Finding / view | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| **Roberts view** | True AP of TMC: thumb flat, **hyperpronated**, beam on trapezium |
| Standard series | AP + lateral hand |
| OA signs | Joint space narrowing, sclerosis, osteophytes (**<2 mm = II, >2 mm = III**), dorsoradial subluxation of MC base |
| **STT involvement** | = **Eaton IV (pantrapezial)** → contraindicates CMC fusion |
| **MCP hyperextension** | Seen on lateral / clinical → measure angle (drives MCP treatment) |
| STT OA + **DISI** | Lateral: extended lunate, volar-tilted capitate → STT fusion, not distal pole excision |
| After trapeziectomy | **MC subsidence** (~25%) → no clinical effect |

![MCP hyperextension on radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-hyperextnsion.jpg)

### Eaton–Littler classification

**Eaton–Littler** — radiographic; **stage IV = pantrapezial (STT)** → decides fusion vs not; historical value, limited clinical utility

| Stage | Radiograph | Key decider | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | **Joint widening** (pre-arthritis, laxity) | Osteotomy / volar lig recon | ![I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-stage1.jpg) |
| II | Narrowing, sclerosis, **osteophytes <2 mm** | Osteotomy if stable; fusion (labourer) | ![II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-early-stage-stage-ii.jpg) |
| III | Marked narrowing, **osteophytes >2 mm** | Trapeziectomy; **fusion if young labourer** | ![III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-stage3.jpg) |
| IV | **Pantrapezial — STT involved** | Trapeziectomy (± prox ⅓ trapezoid); **no fusion** | ![IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-stage4.jpg) |

**Differentials** — **C6 radiculopathy** (neck → thumb/index paraesthesia) · **de Quervain** (Finkelstein +) · **STT OA** (TTP just proximal to TMC) · SNAC / radioscaphoid OA (on X-ray)

## 5 Management

### Nonoperative care – splinting & injections

**1st line** — NSAIDs + **thumb spica / night splint**

**2nd line** — **steroid injection** (moderate evidence)

**RCT trap** — **saline = steroid = hylan** (all ≈ 3 mo relief; Heyworth) → answer "all equally effective"

**Hyaluronic acid** — not indicated (no better than placebo/steroid)

**Thumb MCPJ OA (post-RCL insufficiency, labourer)** — 1st: **spica 4–6 wk + NSAIDs** → fail → **MCP fusion** (5–20° flex, 15° pronation); no arthroplasty in lig-deficient thumb/labourer

### Operative algorithm by stage

```
Failed nonop
├─ Stage I–II, stable joint, MCP hyperext <10° → 1st MC dorsal closing-wedge EXTENSION osteotomy
│      (unstable/hypermobile Stage I → volar lig recon with FCR)
├─ Stage II–III, young male heavy labourer, NO STT OA → CMC ARTHRODESIS
└─ Stage III–IV (most patients) → TRAPEZIECTOMY ± LRTI / suspension
       (Stage IV → + excise proximal ⅓ trapezoid)
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Contraindication |
|---|---|---|
| Arthroscopic debridement | Early stage (I–II) | Stage III–IV |
| Volar lig recon (FCR) | **Stage I, hypermobile/unstable** | Established OA |
| **1st MC extension osteotomy** | **Stage I–II**, stable joint | **Hypermobile/fixed subluxed CMC · MCP hyperext >10°** |
| **CMC arthrodesis** | **Stage II–III, young (<50) male heavy labourer**, post-traumatic | **STT (pantrapezial) OA** |
| **Trapeziectomy ± LRTI** | **Stage I–IV** (most patients, older ♀) | Young heavy labourer (relative) |
| + excise proximal ⅓ trapezoid | **Stage IV** / ST-trapezoid OA (62%) | — |
| Prosthetic arthroplasty | not recommended | Lig-deficient thumb, labourer |
| **MCP fusion** | MCP hyperext **>30–40°**, not correctable, MCP OA | — |

**Osteotomy contraindications** — **hypermobile / fixed subluxed CMC** · **MCP hyperext >10°** (volar joint stays overloaded)

**Osteotomy result** — unloads volar joint; 93% improved at 7 y

**Arthroscopic debridement** — early stage only; portals **1R** (radial to APL) & **1U** (ulnar to EPB)

**Prosthetic arthroplasty** — not recommended; silicone → **silicone synovitis**; pyrocarbon → subluxation (shallow cup)

**Denervation** — resect 4 nerves; pain ≈ trapeziectomy/fusion; ROM > fusion

### Trapeziectomy ± LRTI – equivalence, technique & complications

**Key fact** — **trapezial excision is the essential step**; **no technique superior** to trapeziectomy alone

**LRTI vs trapeziectomy alone** — **no difference** in pain, grip, **key pinch**, ROM, DASH, subsidence → answer "**no benefit of LRTI**"

**Complications** — LRTI/Weilby **> complications** than simple trapeziectomy (OR 4.3)

**Tendon for LRTI** — **FCR** most common; alternatives **ECRL** or **APL**\
✗ Wrong options: FPL

**FCR avulsed intra-op (trapezium already out)** — **LRTI with ECRL**\
✗ Wrong options: fusion (no trapezium left) · implant

**Revision after FCR LRTI** — use **APL**

**Subsidence** — ~**25%** (21% rest, +11% pinch) → **no effect on outcome** → **observe**

**Hematoma arthroplasty** — trapeziectomy alone ± pin; simple, cheap, = LRTI

**Wagner approach — radial artery** — **most at risk**: dorsoradial capsule **proximal to trapezium** · **least at risk**: level of **1st MC base / TMC joint**

**Other nerve at risk** — superficial radial nerve

![Subsidence after trapeziectomy → observe](../../MCQ/attachments/q214671-a-subsidence.jpg)

### MP hyperextension – MP fusion/capsulodesis with CMC reconstruction

**Must address at CMC surgery** — else adduction contracture + recurrent subluxation

| MCP hyperextension | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **<10°** | Nothing |
| **10–20°** | **Pin MCP in 25–35° flexion × 3–4 wk** ± EPB transfer |
| **20–40°** | **Volar capsulodesis** or sesamoidesis |
| **>40°** (or >30° in some stems), not passively correctable, MCP OA | **MCP arthrodesis** |

**Mild (<30°) untreated** — 1-y outcome **same** as no hyperextension (key pinch, grip, abduction) → "no significant difference"

**Classic answer** — end-stage CMC + MCP hyperext 40° → **CMC resection arthroplasty + MCP fusion**

**Failed LRTI + MCP 60°** — **CMC revision stabilisation (APL) + MCP fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: pinning alone

### CMC arthrodesis – young labourers & contraindications (pantrapezial arthritis)

**Indication** — **young (<50) male heavy labourer**, stage II–III, post-traumatic → **preserves grip**, stable

**Contraindication** — **STT / pantrapezial OA (stage IV)**\
✗ Wrong options: radiocarpal OA · chronicity · post-traumatic

**Position** — 35° radial abduction · 30° palmar abduction · 15° pronation

**Main complication** — **nonunion 8–21%** (~12–17%); also STT/MCP OA (mostly asymptomatic)

**Downside** — ↓ ROM, **can't lay hand flat**

**Avoid in labourer** — trapeziectomy/LRTI (↓ grip, no salvage), arthroplasty (bone loss)

![CMC fusion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6054-fusion.jpg)

### STT arthritis – STT arthrodesis

**STT OA** — 16% of population, ♀ >50; pain with pinch; tender proximal to TMC; associated **type II lunate**

**STT OA + DISI (extended lunate, volar-tilted capitate)** — **STT arthrodesis** (lowest revision risk)

**Avoid distal pole excision with DISI** — worsens midcarpal instability → revision

**Avoid CMC arthrodesis for STT OA** — STT not addressed, ↑ shear

![STT OA with DISI on lateral](../../MCQ/attachments/q211336-b-stt-disi-lateral.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**After trapeziectomy** — MC subsidence (asymptomatic → observe); radial artery; superficial radial n.

**If subsidence symptomatic & FCR used** — LRTI with **ECRL or APL**

**After fusion** — nonunion; adjacent STT/MCP OA

**After silicone implant** — **silicone synovitis**, implant fracture/loosening

**Prognosis** — OA in one joint of a row predicts OA in other joints of same row

## X vs Y

| | Trapeziectomy ± LRTI | CMC arthrodesis | 1st MC osteotomy |
|---|---|---|---|
| Patient | Older ♀, low demand | **Young male labourer** | Early, stable joint |
| Stage | I–IV (mainly III–IV) | **II–III** | **I–II** |
| Absolute contraindication | — | **STT OA** | **Hypermobile CMC, MCP hyperext >10°** |
| Signature complication | **Subsidence (ignore)** | **Nonunion** | Osteotomy nonunion |
| Grip | ↑ | **Preserved best** | Preserved |

| | Basilar thumb OA | STT OA | De Quervain | C6 radiculopathy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tender point | TMC joint | Just **proximal** to TMC | 1st dorsal compartment | Neck |
| Test | **Grind** | Injection/X-ray | **Finkelstein** | Spurling |
| Surgery | Trapeziectomy / fusion | **STT fusion** (if DISI) | 1st compartment release | ACDF |

| Thumb deformity | MCP | IP |
|---|---|---|
| **CMC OA (Z deformity)** | **Hyperextension** | ± Flexion |
| **Lupus (hitchhiker)** | Flexion | Hyperextension |

## How it's asked

**"Compared with trapeziectomy alone, which gives superior pain relief / key pinch?"** → **None of the above**

**"Which injection gives greatest improvement?"** → **All equally effective** (saline = steroid = hylan)

**"Radiograph 1 y after trapeziectomy shows subsidence — next?"** → **Observation**

**"FCR avulsed during LRTI — next?"** → **ECRL LRTI**

**"35-y mechanic / rancher, TMC OA, no STT"** → **CMC arthrodesis**; complication asked → **nonunion**

**"Contraindication to CMC arthrodesis?"** → **Pantrapezial (STT) arthritis**

**"Contraindication to osteotomy?"** → **CMC hypermobility / MCP hyperextension >10°**

**"All are sequelae EXCEPT"** → **CMC extension deformity**

**"Patient with STT OA + DISI — surgery that avoids secondary procedure?"** → **STT arthrodesis**

**"MCP hyperextension 9.8°/ <30° untreated — outcome?"** → **No significant difference**

**Stem clue: deformity with web contracture + MCP hyperextension** → **TMC OA**\
✗ Wrong options: hypoplasia · median neuropathy · lupus · tendon rupture

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Blood Supply to Hand

*Hand · 7 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Source arteries

**Radial artery** — between **brachioradialis & FCR** → dorsum via **snuffbox** (between FCR and APL/EPB) → gives **superficial palmar branch** → dives between **2 heads of 1st dorsal interosseous** → **deep palmar arch**

**Ulnar artery** — under FCU; **lateral (radial) to ulnar nerve** at wrist → **Guyon's canal** → lies on TCL

**Supplemental** — anterior interosseous, posterior interosseous, median artery (occasional)

### Vascular anatomy – deep palmar arch & digital neurovascular relationship

![Superficial and deep palmar arches](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6007-arches.jpg)

| | **Superficial arch** | **Deep arch** |
|---|---|---|
| Main supply | **Ulnar artery** | **Deep (terminal) branch of radial artery** |
| Minor supply | Superficial palmar br. of radial a. | Deep palmar br. of ulnar a. |
| Depth | Deep to palmar fascia | **Deep to flexor tendons**, on MC bases & interossei, under oblique adductor pollicis |
| Position | **Distal** | **Proximal** (1 fingerbreadth proximal to superficial) |
| Surface marking | Line across palm from distal edge of fully abducted thumb | 1 fingerbreadth proximal to that line |
| From Kaplan's cardinal line | 15 mm distal | 7 mm distal |
| From distal wrist crease | 50 mm distal | 40 mm distal |
| Complete | **80%** | **97%** |
| Direct digital branch | **Ulnar digital a. of little finger** | **Radial digital a. of index** |
| Other branches | Deep branch (to deep arch), **common palmar digital aa.** | **Princeps pollicis** (between 1st DI & adductor pollicis), common digital aa. to 2nd–4th webs |

**Deep arch main contributor** — **deep dorsal (terminal) branch of radial a.**\
✗ Wrong options: superficial palmar br. radial · princeps pollicis · ulnar a.

**Wrist course of radial a.** — between radial collateral ligament & 1st dorsal compartment → between heads of 1st DI → anastomoses with deep palmar br. of ulnar

### Digital arteries

**Origin** — common digital aa. from **superficial arch** → divide into proper digital at web spaces → dorsal branches distal to PIP

**Palm** — digital artery **volar** to nerve

**Finger** — **artery DORSAL to nerve** (nerve palmar: pads feel, nail refill)\
✗ Wrong options: artery palmar · nerve dorsal

**Ligament relations** — NV bundle **volar to Cleland's** (not in Dupuytren's) · Grayson's volar (involved in Dupuytren's)

![Digital artery dorsal to digital nerve in the finger](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6007-digital-artery-and-nerve.jpg)

| Digit | Dominant digital artery |
|---|---|
| Rule | **Side closer to midline (median side)** |
| Index | **Ulnar** |
| Little | **Radial** |
| Middle / ring | Ulnar / radial (less obvious) |

### Dorsal arteries & veins

**Dorsal carpal arch** — from posterior interosseous + dorsal perforating br. of anterior interosseous → **dorsal metacarpal aa.** (flaps; 1st & 2nd most consistent)

**Veins** — deep = **venae comitantes**; superficial dorsal → basilic & cephalic

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Allen test

**Purpose** — patency of collateral (dual) circulation before **radial artery cannulation/harvest** (thrombosis risk) (TO)

**Positive Allen (TO)** — colour returns quickly = **adequate dual supply** → **indication** for cannulation

**Negative Allen (TO)** — pallor persists = **no patent dual supply** → **contraindication** to radial cannulation

| | **Original Allen (TO)** | **Modified Allen (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Hands | **Both hands**, one artery at a time | **One hand** at a time |
| Method | Clench both fists 1 min, compress **both radial aa.** simultaneously, open → repeat with ulnar | Raise hand, clench 30 s, compress **radial + ulnar together**, open (blanched) → **release ulnar** |
| Normal | Pale → pink quickly | Pink **within 6 s** |

## How it's asked

**"Main contributor to deep palmar arch, between heads of 1st DI?"** → **deep dorsal (terminal) branch of radial artery**

**"Main supply of superficial arch?"** → **ulnar artery**

**"Artery–nerve relation in the finger?"** → **artery dorsal to nerve**

**"True about Allen test?"** → **negative = no patent dual supply** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: positive is a contraindication · original compresses radial + ulnar of one hand · modified compresses them one by one

**"Dominant digital artery of index?"** → **ulnar**

**"Which arch is more complete?"** → **deep (97%)** vs superficial 80%

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Boutonniere Deformity

*Hand · 8 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy – extensor mechanism

**Central slip** — central part of extensor hood → inserts **base of middle phalanx** → **extends PIP**

**Lateral bands** — deep head dorsal interossei + volar interossei → insert **base of distal phalanx** → **extend DIP**

**Lumbricals** — origin **FDP** → insert on **lateral bands**

**Triangular ligament** — spans the 2 lateral bands dorsally → **prevents volar subluxation**

**Transverse retinacular ligament** — **prevents dorsal subluxation** of lateral bands

### Epidemiology

**Rheumatoid** — up to **50% of RA** patients → boutonniere in ≥1 digit

**Definition** — **Zone III** extensor injury → **PIP flexion + DIP hyperextension** ("buttonhole": PP head buttons through extensor)

## 2 Mechanism

### Pathophysiology – central slip injury & lateral band relocation

**Causes of central slip failure** — **laceration** · **traumatic avulsion (jammed finger)** · **PIP synovitis / capsular distension (RA)**

**Primary trigger (TO)** — **attenuated central slip + volar displacement of lateral bands**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal subluxation of lateral bands · ORL shortening · volar plate rupture

**Chronic injury to** — **central slip** of extensor tendon\
✗ Wrong options: terminal tendon → mallet · sagittal band → MCP extensor subluxation · volar plate → swan neck · FDP insertion → jersey

| Step | Event | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Central slip rupture/attenuation** | EDC extension of PIP lost |
| 2 | **Triangular ligament attenuates** | Lateral bands free to slide volarly |
| 3 | **Lateral bands drop volar to PIP axis** | Become **PIP flexors** (TO); all extensor force → **DIP hyperextension** |

![Boutonniere pathoanatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6012-lineage-medical-500-moved.jpg)

**Lumbricals** — unopposed pull through lateral bands → flex PIP + extend DIP

**Pseudo-boutonniere** — PIP flexion contracture **without DIP extension**

## 3 Presentation

**Deformity** — **PIP flexion + DIP hyperextension**; RA patient or jammed/lacerated finger

![Boutonniere clinical](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6012-clinical-photo-surgery-missouri-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic test – Elson

**Elson test** — **most reliable** for central slip injury **before deformity evident**

**Technique** — PIP 90° over table edge → extend middle phalanx against resistance

**Positive** — **weak PIP extension + rigid DIP** (lateral bands fire)

**Negative (intact slip)** — **DIP floppy**

![Elson test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6012-elson.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **not required** for diagnosis; shows **avulsion fracture** of middle phalanx base → ORIF/repair

### Rheumatoid staging (TO)

| Stage | Findings | Surgery |
|---|---|---|
| **I (mild)** | **Passively correctable** (PIP extends, DIP flexes) | **Dolphin-Nalebuff terminal tenotomy** |
| **II (moderate)** | **Fixed PIP flexion contracture**, joint space preserved | Matev lateral band transfer / central slip reattachment |
| **III (severe)** | Fixed PIP + **radiographic destruction** or severe soft-tissue contracture | **PIP arthrodesis** (most reliable) |

## 5 Management

```
Boutonniere
├─ Acute closed (<4 wk) → PIP extension splint 6 wk
│      └─ then part-time splint 4–6 wk more
├─ Displaced avulsion fx / open wound → primary central slip repair
├─ Chronic, supple (after full PROM) → tenotomy / lateral band relocation / reconstruction
└─ RA stiff arthritic PIP → PIP arthrodesis
```

### Nonoperative

**Splint** — **PIP in full extension 6 wk** (continuous) + part-time 4–6 wk; **acute closed <4 wk**

**In splint** — **active DIP flexion/extension** → prevents **ORL contracture**

**Traumatic vs RA (TO)** — traumatic responds well to splinting; **RA tissue "sick" → splinting less successful**, progresses

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Primary central slip repair** | **Acute displaced avulsion fx** · open wound needing I&D (ORIF of attachment fractures successful) |
| **Lateral band dorsal relocation** | ~**40° active flexion contracture with full passive extension** |
| **Terminal tendon tenotomy** (modified Fowler / **Dolphin**) | Chronic, after FROM; PIP flexible but can't flex DIP; **never central slip tenotomy** |
| **Matev** (TO) | One lateral band → central slip; other extends DIP |
| Secondary reconstruction | Tendon graft, Littler, Matev, triangular ligament reconstruction |
| **PIP arthrodesis** | **RA**, painful stiff arthritic PIP; **stage III** (TO) |
| Silastic arthroplasty (TO) | Occasionally ulnar digits; high recurrence |

**PIP fusion angles (TO)** — **ring/small 40–50°**; **index 25–30°**

**RA outcome (TO)** — boutonniere surgery **less predictable than swan-neck**

## X vs Y

| (TO) | **Swan-neck** | **Boutonniere** |
|---|---|---|
| PIP | Hyperextension | **Flexion** |
| DIP | Flexion | **Hyperextension** |
| Primary defect | **Volar plate / FDS** | **Central slip** |
| Lateral bands | Subluxate **dorsally** | Subluxate **volarly** |

| Structure injured | Deformity |
|---|---|
| **Central slip** | **Boutonniere** |
| Terminal extensor tendon | Mallet |
| Sagittal band | Extensor subluxation at MCP |
| Volar plate (chronic) | Swan neck |
| FDP insertion avulsion | Jersey finger |

## How it's asked

**"Primary trigger of boutonniere cascade?"** → **attenuated central slip + volar lateral bands** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal lateral bands · ORL

**"Surgery relocates lateral bands dorsally — which deformity?"** → **boutonniere**\
✗ Wrong options: swan neck · mallet · jersey · lumbrical plus

**"Chronic injury to which structure → boutonniere?"** → **central slip**\
✗ Wrong options: terminal tendon · sagittal band · volar plate · FDP

**"Detect central slip injury before deformity?"** → **Elson test** (rigid DIP + weak PIP extension)

**"Acute closed central slip injury — treatment?"** → **PIP extension splint 6 wk, DIP free**

**"Stage III RA boutonniere?"** → **PIP arthrodesis** (TO)

**"Tenotomy for chronic boutonniere?"** → **terminal tendon (Dolphin/Fowler)**\
✗ Wrong options: central slip

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Camptodactyly

*Hand · 9 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition (TO)** — Greek "**bent finger**"; **nontraumatic flexion deformity of the PIP joint**, classically **small finger**

**Prevalence** — **<1%**

**Laterality** — unilateral 33% · **bilateral 66%** (often **asymmetric** (TO))

**Other digits (TO)** — occasionally; incidence & severity ↑ toward **ulnar side** of hand

**Genetics** — mostly **sporadic**; familial = **AD, incomplete penetrance / variable expressivity**

**Isolated case** — usually idiopathic → **no genetic workup**; Type III / multi-digit → syndromes (e.g. arthrogryposis)

### Etiology

**Commonest causes** — **abnormal lumbrical** insertion/origin · **abnormal FDS** insertion (adherent, hypoplastic, tight)

**Less common** — central slip, extensor hood, volar plate, skin/subcutaneous contracture

**Lumbrical theory (TO)** — lumbrical inserts into lumbrical canal/FDS instead of extensor hood → like an **intrinsic-minus digit** (Jones, Marmor, Lankford)

**Other TO theories** — FDS tightness / "slow retraction" of flexors · short FDP · faulty dorsal extensor aponeurosis

**Unifying concept (TO)** — **final common expression of flexion–extension imbalance at PIP** ("virtually every structure implicated", Smith & Kaplan)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **PIP flexion deformity, small finger**; flexible or fixed; **painless**, no swelling/erythema; normal strength, sensation, perfusion

**Neighbouring joints** — DIP & MCP usually normal; untreated → compensatory **MCP hyperextension** (commonest adjacent)

**Course** — progressive; **rapid worsening in growth spurts**

**Function** — rarely compromised; often unnoticed

![Camptodactyly small finger PIP](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141286-1.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

**Onset (TO)** — congenital (♂ = ♀) or **adolescent** (early teens, ♀, gradual)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — often **normal** early (articular congruity maintained)

**Late X-ray** — ↓ **P1 head convexity** · volar subluxation + flattening of **P2 base**

### Benson classification

| Type | Features | Treatment | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** (infantile) | Isolated small finger, **infancy**, ♂ = ♀; **most common** (Orthobullets) | **Stretching/splinting** | ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6074-campto-2-copy.jpg) |
| **II** (adolescent) | Same features, **adolescence**; **♀ > ♂** | If **full active PIP extension with MCP flexed** → **FDS transfer to radial lateral band** | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6074-camptodactyly-1-copy.jpg) |
| **III** (syndromic) | **Birth**, severe, **multiple digits**, syndrome | Nonop; after maturity if functional deficit → **osteotomy / fusion** | — |
| Kirner's | Small finger **distal phalanx** volar-radial curve (apex dorsal-ulnar); preadolescent ♀; bilateral; no deficit | — | ![Kirner deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6074-kirner-1.jpg) |

**Commonest type — conflict** — **Type I/infantile** (Orthobullets) · **adolescent type more common** (TO) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
Camptodactyly
├─ Most cases / PIP contracture <30° → passive stretching + static (night) extension splinting
│      (best results with EARLY treatment)
├─ Progressive, functional impairment, contracture ~60° despite splinting → surgery
│      ├─ passive (correctable) → FDS tenotomy
│      └─ full active PIP ext with MCP flexed → FDS transfer to radial lateral band
└─ Severe FIXED deformity → osteotomy / arthrodesis
```

### Treatment – stretching & extension splinting

**First line** — **night extension splinting + stretching** (intrinsic-plus splint) → always try first, even mild deformity\
✗ Wrong options: observation alone: shorter duration → better result

**Mild (<30°)** — stretching ± splint → expect resolution (Rhee: **20° → 1°** over 5 mo, ≥20 sessions/day, children <3 y)

**Severe (>60°)** — stretching helps but slower (Rhee: **75° → 28°**, up to 2 y) → may need **FDS tenotomy** if bothersome

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — complete correction in 1–2 mo) · **serial casting 90% for >50°** · progressive contracture of all joints · routine bilateral tenotomy\
✗ Wrong options: not effective for significant deformity

**Serial casting — conflict** — not effective for significant deformity (OB) · listed among nonop options for **mild** cases (TO)

**TO nonop list** — active exercises, dynamic splints, serial casts, passive stretching (mild cases)

### Surgery

**Indication** — **progressive** deformity → functional impairment; failed splinting

**Principle** — address **all** abnormal anatomy; rebalance PIP flexion–extension

| Deforming factor (TO) | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **FDS contracture** | **FDS tenotomy** |
| **Anomalous lumbrical** | Excise or reinsert into **lateral band** + volar Z-plasties + active post-op splinting |
| **Flexor–extensor imbalance** | Volar release + **FDS transfer through lumbrical canal to extensor aponeurosis** |
| Extreme | **FDP lengthening + PIP capsulotomy** |

**Outcomes** — **inconsistent / variable**; complications: persistent PIP contracture & MCP hyperextension, residual stiffness, **P1 head remodelling** blocking extension

## X vs Y

| Differential (TO) | Distinguishing point |
|---|---|
| **Camptodactyly** | **PIP flexion**, painless, slow, no trauma |
| **Clinodactyly** | Deviation in **radioulnar plane** |
| Boutonnière | Trauma, swelling/pain, **DIP hyperextension** |
| **Dupuytren** | **MCP contracture**, palmar dimpling/fascia |
| Trigger digit | Triggering; affects **MCP** |
| Congenital absent extensor mechanism | Affects **MCP**, not PIP |

## How it's asked

**"3-y-old, bilateral asymmetric fixed little-finger PIP flexion, painless, no trauma, normal DIP/MCP"** → **camptodactyly** ("compatodactyly" in TO)\
✗ Wrong options: clinodactyly, Dupuytren, absent extensor mechanism

**"PIP 25° one side, 60° other — counsel"** → **stretching fixes 25°; 60° slower ± FDS tenotomy**

**"5-y-old, isolated small finger, limited passive extension — next step"** → **night extension splint + stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · genetic workup · FDS transfer first

**"Full active PIP extension with MCP flexed"** → **FDS transfer to radial lateral band**

**"Severe fixed deformity"** → **osteotomy / arthrodesis**

**"Commonest causes"** → **abnormal lumbrical or FDS insertion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Carpal Instability Nondissociative (CIND)

*Hand · 10 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**CIND (TO)** — instability **between carpal rows** (midcarpal) or **between radius and carpus** (radiocarpal) **without intrinsic (SLIL/LTIL) tear**; each row intact, **extrinsic ligament** insufficiency

**Not CIND** — ligament disruption within a single row (= CID) · trans-scaphoid perilunate (= CIC) · DR malunion tilt alone

**Types** — **midcarpal** (proximal ↔ distal row; **most common CIND**, TO) · **radiocarpal** (radius ↔ proximal row; "inferior arc injury")

**Incidence** — rare, **<1 per 100,000/y**

**Associations** — SL/LT injury, **acute CTS**, compartment syndrome

### Extrinsic ligament anatomy

| Ligament | Origin → insertion | Role (TO) | Failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Radioscaphocapitate (RSC)** | Radial styloid → scaphoid waist + capitate | Fulcrum / sling for scaphoid | Scaphoid instability |
| **Long radiolunate (LRL)** | Radial styloid → volar lunate + triquetrum | **Primary stabiliser vs ulnar translocation** | **Ulnar drift of carpus** (RA) |
| Short radiolunate (SRL) | Ulnar notch rim → volar-ulnar lunate | Lunate anchor | Lunate dislocation |
| Ulnolunate / ulnotriquetral | TFCC → lunate / triquetrum | Ulnar column | **VISI** |
| Arcuate (triquetrohamatocapitate) | Palmar midcarpal | Midcarpal kinematics | **Midcarpal instability** |

**Space of Poirier (TO)** — weak area between RC and LRL over capitolunate joint → carpus dissociates here in perilunate (Mayfield II); lunate stays on SRL

## 2 Mechanism

**Midcarpal** — lax **palmar arcuate ligament** + dorsal radiocarpal ligaments (TO); usually **no trauma**, **generalised laxity**

**Radiocarpal** — high energy; ulnar translation = **global extrinsic rupture**; ± radial/ulnar styloid fractures

**Commonest cause of radiocarpal instability** — **distal radius malunion** (Orthobullets) · ulnar translocation commonly **RA / RSC disruption** (TO) — read the options

**Dorsal radiocarpal instability (TO)** — DR malunion with **loss of volar tilt**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — wrist **giving way**, painful/painless subluxation, **clunk**

**Midcarpal shift test (TO)** — stabilise forearm, **axial + palmar load**, wrist **neutral → ulnar deviation** (pronation) → painful **"catch-up clunk"** as proximal row snaps **VISI (flexed) → neutral/extended**

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractor exams** — radial pain with ulnar deviation (**Finkelstein**)) · tenderness distal to ulnar styloid (**fovea sign**) · clunk radial deviation + scaphoid pressure (**Watson**) · lunate pain with dorsal pressure (**Kienböck**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Static X-ray** — AP + lateral; often **normal or mild VISI**

**Dynamic fluoroscopy / cineradiography** — **diagnostic** (TO); sudden jump of proximal row **flexion → extension** with ulnar deviation

**Ulnar translation** — **>50% of lunate width** translated ulnarly off the lunate fossa

![Ulnar translation >50% lunate width](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6044-ulnar-translation-with-text-moved.jpg)

### Carpal instability classification

| Type | Lesion | Example |
|---|---|---|
| CID (dissociative) | **Intrinsic** ligament, within a row | SL dissociation |
| **CIND** | **Extrinsic**, between rows / radius–carpus | **Midcarpal instability**, ulnar translocation |
| CIC (complex) | CID + CIND | Perilunate injury |
| CIA (adaptive) | Extracarpal cause | Madelung, DR malunion |

## 5 Management

```
CIND
├─ Midcarpal → splint + FCU/ECU proprioceptive rehab (first line)
│      └─ refractory → capsular plication / reefing → midcarpal fusion
├─ Radiocarpal acute + styloid fractures → immediate open repair + pinning
├─ DR malunion → corrective osteotomy
└─ Failed above → wrist arthrodesis
```

**Nonop** — first line; **midcarpal most amenable to splinting**; rehab FCU + ECU (dynamic stabilisers, TO), activity modification, NSAIDs

**Midcarpal surgery (TO)** — thermal capsulorrhaphy (controversial, capsular necrosis) · open plication of arcuate ligament / dorsal capsule · reefing (Apergis: 13/14 good–excellent) · **limited midcarpal arthrodesis (4-corner / triquetrohamate)** · total wrist fusion if intractable

**Midcarpal fusion** — **preferred over ligament reconstruction** and for late diagnosis failing nonop; **20–35% loss of motion**

**Radiocarpal** — immediate open repair/reduction/pinning if **ulnar translation + styloid fractures**; **late repair poor**; **ligament reconstruction poor long term**

**Ulnar translocation in RA (TO)** — radiocarpal or total wrist arthrodesis

**Radiocarpal arthritis + functional midcarpal joint** — **radioscapholunate (RSL) fusion + distal scaphoid excision** (↑ ROM)

**Wrist arthrodesis** — salvage; radiocarpal fusion → **55–60% loss of motion**

**Not for isolated radiocarpal arthritis** — 4-corner fusion (radiocarpal motion still painful) · capitolunate fusion (midcarpal OK) · TWA (young manual labourer, fails) · total fusion (salvage only)

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — **volar dislocation worse than dorsal**; late repair and ligament reconstruction poor

## X vs Y

| | **Midcarpal CIND** | **Radiocarpal CIND** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Most common** | Less common |
| History | No trauma, lax | High energy / DR malunion / RA |
| Sign | **Catch-up clunk** on ulnar deviation | Ulnar translation >50% lunate |
| Key ligament | Palmar arcuate | **LRL** / RSC |
| Surgery | Plication → **midcarpal fusion** | Open repair, osteotomy, RSL / wrist fusion |

## How it's asked

**"Defining feature of CIND?"** → **instability between rows / radius–carpus without intrinsic tear** (TO)

**"Clunk on ulnar deviation, proximal row jumps flexion → extension, no SL/LT dissociation?"** → **CIND – midcarpal instability** (TO)

**"Most diagnostic exam for midcarpal instability?"** → **clunk with axial + palmar load, neutral → ulnar deviation**\
✗ Wrong options: Watson · Finkelstein · fovea

**"Primary volar restraint to ulnar translocation?"** → **long radiolunate ligament** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: RSC · SRL · ulnolunate

**"Radioscaphoid + radiolunate arthritis, preserve motion?"** → **RSL fusion + distal scaphoid excision**

**"Diagnostic investigation?"** → **dynamic fluoroscopy**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Carpal Tunnel Syndrome

*Hand · 11 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & associations

**Commonest** — **most common compressive neuropathy**; 0.1–10% population; **♀:♂ 3:1**; 40–60 y; bilateral up to 70%

**Commonest causes (TO)** — **idiopathic > hypothyroidism > pregnancy**

**Risk factors** — ♀, **obesity**, **pregnancy**, **hypothyroidism**, **RA**, DM, **smoking**, vibration, acromegaly, amyloidosis, **hyperparathyroidism**, CRF, OCP, DRF malunion; **children → mucopolysaccharidosis**

**NOT associated (TO)** — **Addison's disease**

**Pregnancy CTS (TO)** — usually **resolves after delivery**

**Diabetics (TO)** — **worse surgical outcome** than idiopathic

### Anatomy – tunnel contents, TCL attachments, recurrent motor branch & anastomoses

**Contents** — median nerve + **FPL** + 4 FDS + 4 FDP (9 tendons); **not FCR** (own sheath, trapezial groove) · not ulnar n.

**FDS layering** — FDS 3rd & 4th **volar** to FDS 2nd & 5th

**Most radial structure** — FPL

**TCL (roof) attachments** — radial: **scaphoid tubercle + trapezium**; ulnar: **hook of hamate + pisiform**

**Narrowest** — level of **hook of hamate**

**Floor** — proximal carpal row

**Pressures** — normal 2.5 mmHg (neutral) → 30 (flexion); **20 mmHg → venous congestion**; **60–80 → arteriolar arrest**; CTS 30–110

**Recurrent motor branch** — **APB, OP, superficial FPB** (+ lumbricals 1–2 via digital nn.); arises radial/central

| Recurrent branch variant | % |
|---|---|
| Extraligamentous | 50 |
| Subligamentous | 30 |
| **Transligamentous** (at risk) | **20** |

**Surgical rule** — **cut TCL far ulnar** (radial cut → transects transligamentous branch → **loss of opposition**)

**Palmar cutaneous branch** — arises **~5 cm (4–8)** proximal to crease, **ulnar side of FCR / between PL & FCR**, **superficial to TCL** → sensation **thenar eminence** → **spared in CTS**

**Median nerve laceration vs CTS** — laceration proximal to wrist adds **thenar eminence numbness** (palmar cutaneous br.)

**Spared muscles in CTS** — **FPL, PQ, FDP index** (AIN, proximal); **adductor pollicis** (ulnar)

**Weak muscle in CTS** — **APB** (only purely median-distal-to-TCL); inserts **radial base of thumb proximal phalanx**

![Recurrent motor branch variants](../../MCQ/attachments/q210259-illa-recurrent-motor-branch-anatomy.jpg)

| Median–ulnar connection | Site | Direction | Clinical clue |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Martin-Gruber** | Forearm | Median/AIN → ulnar | Intrinsics spared with high ulnar lesion |
| **Marinacci** | Forearm | Ulnar → median | Reverse MG |
| **Riche-Cannieu** | **Hand** | **Deep ulnar → recurrent median** | **"All-ulnar hand"**: thenar strength **preserved** despite median laceration / severe CTS |
| **Berrettini** | Hand | Sensory, common digital | no motor sparing |

## 2 Mechanism

**Idiopathic** — **inflamed synovium** = commonest cause; ↑ pressure → ↓ intraneural blood flow

### Lab & histology markers

**Histology** — **oedema, fibrosis, vascular sclerosis**; scattered lymphocytes; ± amyloid; no acute inflammation

**Secondary (space-occupying)** — ganglion, tumour, **gout tophus** (nodule worse after alcohol) → **tissue diagnosis + rheumatology**\
✗ Wrong options: splint · excision first

**Acute CTS** — after DRF/carpal dislocation/ORIF → **urgent release**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — numbness/tingling **radial 3½ digits**, **night waking**, clumsiness, ↓ grip

**Late** — **thenar atrophy**, weak APB

**Sensation spared** — thenar eminence (palmar cutaneous br.)

![Thenar atrophy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6018-thenar-atrophy.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – provocative tests, CTS-6 & electrodiagnostics

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; EDS not required (AAOS: **limited** rec)

| Test | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| **Durkan** (carpal compression, 30 s) | **Most sensitive provocative test** |
| **Semmes-Weinstein monofilament** | **Most sensitive sensory test / early CTS** (single fibre threshold) |
| **Hand diagram** (self-administered) | **Most SPECIFIC (76%)** (TO ×3) |
| **Tinel** | High specificity (TO) |
| Phalen (wrist flexion 60 s) | Less sensitive than Durkan |
| **2-point discrimination** | Positive if fails at **5 mm**; innervation density test; best for **nerve regeneration after repair** |
| Scratch collapse | Supportive |

**High sensitivity (TO)** — Durkan, hand diagram, night pain, Semmes-Weinstein

**High specificity (TO)** — **hand diagram, Tinel**

**NOT CTS tests** — **Murphy** (TO) · **Wartenberg** (cubital tunnel) · **Froment** (ulnar) · **OK sign** (AIN) · hypothenar atrophy (ulnar)

### Diagnostic criteria

**Clinical criteria** — median-territory numbness · **nocturnal** numbness · thenar weakness/atrophy · Tinel + · Phalen + · **2PD loss >5 mm** (= CTS-6 items)

**CTS-6** — max 26; **≥12 → 80% probability**; **>5 → 25%**

| CTS-6 item | Points |
|---|---|
| Numbness mainly median territory | 3.5 |
| **Nocturnal numbness** | 4 |
| Thenar atrophy/weakness | 5 |
| Phalen + | 5 |
| **Loss of 2PD (>5 mm)** | 4.5 |
| Tinel + | 4 |

**High/low pretest probability** — EDS **won't change probability** → treat clinically

**EDS role** — confirm (work comp), **prognosis**; AAOS: obtain if surgery planned

| NCS (latency/velocity) | EMG (muscle) |
|---|---|
| **Distal sensory latency >3.5 ms** | ↑ insertional activity |
| **Distal motor latency >4.5 ms** | **Positive sharp waves** |
| **Velocity <52 m/s** (less specific) | **Fibrillations**, fasciculations |
| Side-to-side asymmetry >1 m/s (TO) | ↓ motor recruitment, CRDs |
| Only **large myelinated fibres** tested | = **axonal loss → severe** |

**Cut-off conflict** — **DSL >3.5 / DML >4.5 ms** (CTS note, TO, OB) · **DSL >3.2 / DML >4.3 ms** (Orthobullets NCS note) — read the options

**EMG EXCEPT trap** — **distal sensory latency** is NCS, not EMG

| AAEM severity | Findings |
|---|---|
| Mild | Sensory only |
| Moderate | Sensory + motor latency ↑ |
| **Severe** | **Denervation (fibs, ↓ CMAP)** → early surgery |

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| **Ultrasound** | Median nerve **CSA >10 mm²** at **pisiform / proximal TCL** |
| X-ray | **Not routine** (not needed for diagnosis) |
| MRI / CT | Only if **space-occupying lesion** suspected (ganglion, tumour, **gout tophus**) |
| NCS/EMG | See table above: **DSL >3.5 ms · DML >4.5 ms · CV <52 m/s**; fibs/PSW = severe |

**Differentials** — AIN syndrome (motor only), **pronator syndrome** (thenar numbness + forearm pain), ulnar tunnel, **C6 radiculopathy** (steroid injection helps discriminate)

## 5 Management

```
CTS
├─ Mild–moderate → night splint NEUTRAL ± steroid injection  (reassess at 2–7 wk)
│      └─ fails (no response by ~2 mo) → carpal tunnel release
├─ Severe (thenar atrophy / denervation on EMG) → early CTR
└─ Acute CTS (trauma / post-ORIF) → urgent CTR
```

### Nonoperative care – splint position & steroid injection

**Splint** — **neutral** (night); **30° extension (cock-up) ↑ pressure** (avoid)

**AAOS strong** — splints ✓, steroid injection ✓, **surgery > nonop at 6 & 12 mo**, no post-op immobilisation, no magnets

**AAOS moderate** — NSAIDs no benefit; oral steroids ✓ · **limited** — endoscopic short-term benefit

**not appropriate** — B6, phonophoresis, bumetanide, PT alone

**Steroid injection** — **80% improve, 20% of these symptom-free at 1 y** (Green: 81%, mean 3.3 mo)

**Injection response** — good relief → **good surgical prognosis**; no relief → poorer outcome (but still may benefit)

**Dose/agent** — no difference (methylpred = triamcinolone; dose ≠ duration)

**With neck pain** — injection still indicated (diagnostic + partial relief)

**Time rule** — effective nonop works in **2–7 wk**; failed 8 wk splint → **CTR**\
✗ Wrong options: more splinting

### Surgical release – indications, guidelines, open vs endoscopic & prognosis

### Operative criteria

| Indication for CTR | Note |
|---|---|
| **Failed nonop** (splint ± injection) | No response in **2–7 wk** → operate |
| **Severe CTS**: thenar atrophy / **denervation on EMG** | **Early CTR** (limit further denervation) |
| **Acute CTS** (DRF, carpal dislocation, post-ORIF) | **Urgent** release |
| Classic Hx + exam despite splints | Elective CTR (EDS won't change probability) |
| **Revision** | Failure to improve → **incomplete release** commonest → **open** revision |

**Avoid endoscopic (TO)** — revision · anatomic variant · prior tendon surgery/scar · limited wrist extension

**Open (Kaplan's cardinal line)** — 1st web → hook of hamate = **superficial palmar arch**; incision in line with **ulnar border of long finger**; stay ulnar

**No benefit** — internal neurolysis, **tenosynovectomy** (idiopathic), antebrachial fascia release, Guyon release (decompressed anyway)

**Lengthen TCL** — only if flexor tendon repair done (allows wrist flexion splint)

**Endoscopic vs open** — endoscopic: **↓ early pain/analgesia** (main advantage), earlier RTW, better early key pinch, ↓ scar tenderness; **long term = open**

**Endoscopic cons** — learning curve, **incomplete release** (commonest complication), ↑ revision, ↑ nerve injury risk

**Endoscopic contraindications (TO)** — **revision/recurrent CTS** · anatomic variant (APB wasting without sensory change) · previous tendon surgery/scar · **limited wrist extension**

**Recovery** — **pinch 6 wk**; **grip 100% of pre-op by 12 wk (3 mo)**

**Persistent symptoms at 1 y** — **2% moderate · 20% severe**

**Poor prognosis** — **severe EDS (EMG denervation)** = strongest predictor; DM; poor injection response; male

**Good prognosis** — **night symptoms**, relief with injection

**Worst EDS option** — **DML >4.5 AND DSL >3.5** (e.g. 5.0 / 4.0)

### Perioperative care & complications

**Antibiotics** — **not indicated** for clean elective hand surgery → **standard prep only**

**Analgesia** — **ibuprofen + paracetamol only** for soft-tissue procedures (CTR, trigger finger, cubital tunnel)

**Post-op** — neutral night splint 2–3 wk for comfort; ROM/nerve glides immediately; strengthen after 4 wk

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key numbers / fact |
|---|---|
| **Scar tenderness** | **Most common** (19–61%); massage |
| **Pillar pain** | Thenar/hypothenar ache; 41% → 25% → 6% (1, 3, 12 mo) |
| **Incomplete TCL release** | **Commonest cause of failure/revision**; ↑ with endoscopic |
| Recurrence | 1% 5 y, 1.6% 10 y |
| **Recurrent motor branch** | Open 3% / endo 0.7%; transligamentous; → repair or **opponensplasty (FDS ring → APB, Bunnell)**; EIP/PL alternatives |
| **Palmar cutaneous branch** | **Open 19%** / endo 2.4%; painful neuroma |
| **Proper digital n. index** | → **lumbrical weakness** (index MCP flexion + PIP extension weak) |
| Arterial arch | Endo 0.02% |

**Revision CTR** — 25% complete relief, 25% none

![Nerve diagram: injury to D = proper digital nerve index → lumbrical weakness](../../MCQ/attachments/q212018-a-hand-nerves.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **CTS** | **Pronator syndrome** | **AIN syndrome** | C6 radiculopathy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory | Radial 3½ digits, **night** | Radial 3½ **+ thenar eminence** | **None** (pure motor) | Thumb/index + neck |
| Motor | APB/OP | FPL, FDP-I, PQ ± thenar | **FPL, FDP-I, PQ (OK sign)** | Biceps/wrist ext |
| Provocative | Durkan, Phalen, Tinel at wrist | Resisted pronation; Tinel forearm | — | Spurling |
| Night sx | **Yes** | No | No | ± |

| | Open CTR | Endoscopic CTR |
|---|---|---|
| Early pain, RTW, key pinch | Worse | **Better** |
| Scar tenderness | ↑ | ↓ |
| Incomplete release / revision | ↓ | **↑** |
| Long-term | = | = |
| Revision cases | **✓** | **No (contraindicated)** |

## How it's asked

**"Most specific test?"** → **Hand diagram** (TO)

**"Most sensitive provocative?"** → **Durkan**

**"Most sensitive early sensory?"** → **Semmes-Weinstein**

**"Not a test for CTS"** → **Murphy** / Wartenberg

**"CTS not common in"** → **Addison's** (TO)

**"Not true"** → **endoscopic better in recurrent cases**\
✗ Wrong options: — open is used

**"Contents EXCEPT"** → **FCR**

**"TCL attaches to"** → **trapezium + hook of hamate**

**"Recurrent branch cut — deficit & how?"** → **opposition lost; cut TCL radially**

**"Median laceration but can oppose thumb"** → **Riche-Cannieu**

**"Laceration vs CTS — extra finding"** → **thenar eminence numbness**

**"Which muscle weak in CTS?"** → **APB**\
✗ Wrong options: FPL, PQ, FDP-I, adductor pollicis

**"Cock-up splint at 30°"** → **↑ pressure** (use neutral)

**"Counsel on injection"** → **80% improve / 20% last 1 y**

**"Splinted 8 wk, still symptomatic"** → **CTR**

**"Main advantage of endoscopic"** → **↓ post-op pain**

**"Grip at 3 mo"** → **100%**

**"Persistent sx at 1 y, severe CTS"** → **20%**

**"Predicts poor outcome"** → **EMG/EDS severity**

**"Reduce SSI"** → **standard prep; no antibiotics**

**"Weak index MCP flexion + PIP extension after CTR"** → **proper digital nerve to index (lumbrical)**

**"Volar wrist nodule worse after alcohol + CTS"** → **gout → tissue Dx + rheumatology**
