# Cleft Hand

*Hand · 12 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Synonyms** — **lobster-claw deformity**, ectrodactyly, split hand

**Definition** — **central longitudinal deficiency** → absence of ≥1 central digits (index, middle, ring) of hand or foot

**Incidence** — **rare (1:10,000–1:90,000)**

**Sex** — **♂:♀ 5:1** (Orthobullets + TO)

**Site** — hands, **usually bilateral**; often feet too (**cleft foot**)

**Absent metacarpals** — present in cleft hand → separates from **symbrachydactyly**

**Small finger** — **always present** on ulnar side

### Pathogenesis

**AER defect** — wedge-shaped degeneration of **central part of apical ectodermal ridge** → loss of signalling → progress zone stops proliferating → central ray defect

**Swanson** — **type I failure of formation** (longitudinal arrest) of central ray

### Genes & inheritance

| | Typical cleft hand | Atypical cleft hand (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| Shape | **V-shaped** central cleft | **U-shaped** |
| Laterality | Bilateral | Unilateral |
| Feet | Cleft foot common | — |
| Nature | Genetic | Form of **symbrachydactyly** (absent/syndactylous/hypoplastic digits) |
| Inheritance | See below | **Sporadic, not inherited**; few syndromes |

**Inheritance (typical)** — **autosomal dominant**, reduced penetrance (**70%**) (Orthobullets, OB) · **X-linked dominant**, incomplete penetrance (TO) — read the options; OB pedigree answer = **AD**

**Severity** — inherited forms **worsen each generation**; sporadic cases = de novo mutation

**Gene** — deletions/inversions/translocations of **7q** → **split hand–split foot syndrome**

**Counselling** — affected families → **genetic counselling**

**Associations** — **EEC syndrome** (ectrodactyly–ectodermal dysplasia–cleft lip/palate), **sensorineural hearing loss**, syndactyly, polydactyly

## 3 Presentation

**History** — ± family history

**Symptoms** — aesthetic > functional limitation

**Exam** — absent/short **central (3rd) ray** · ± absent radial digits · ± **syndactyly of ulnar digits** · ± feet

![Typical V-shaped cleft hand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-clinical-photo-george-tiller-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique **bilateral hands** + **feet** if involved

### Manske & Halikis classification (thumb web)

| Type | Web | Characteristics | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Normal** | Thumb space not narrowed | ![I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-i.jpg) |
| IIA | Mildly narrowed | Thumb space mildly narrowed | ![IIA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-ii.jpg) |
| IIB | Severely narrowed | Thumb space severely narrowed | ![IIB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-iib.jpg) |
| III | Syndactylized | Thumb + index syndactylized, web obliterated | ![III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-iii.jpg) |
| IV | Merged | Index ray suppressed, thumb web merged with cleft | ![IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-iv.jpg) |
| V | Absent | Thumb suppressed, ulnar rays remain, no thumb web | ![V](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6072-type-v.jpg) |

**Most accepted** — Manske & Halikis (TO)

**Spot diagnosis (TO)** — missing middle ray + **normal thumb–index web** → **type I**

## 5 Management

### Priorities

**Thumb web** — **greater priority** than central cleft

**Sequence** — thumb reconstruction **should not precede cleft closure** (compromises skin flaps)

**Cleft closure** — local tissue flaps from cleft + **close intermetacarpal space**

**Thumb/web options** — web deepening, tendon transfer, rotational osteotomy, toe-to-hand transfer

### Treatment by Manske type

| Type | Web surgery | Central cleft |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **None** (normal web, no impairment) (TO) | **Cleft closure + reduce intermetacarpal space** ± excise polydactylous bone (TO) |
| IIA | **4-flap Z-plasty** (TO) | Dorsal/palmar cleft flaps + reduce IM space |
| IIB | Cleft flaps transposed into thumb web | Close with web procedure; IM space reduced by **ligament reconstruction** |
| III | **Priority to web**: cleft flaps + thumb–index syndactyly release + skin graft | Local pedicle flaps; ligament reconstruction |
| IV | Usually none (less impairment); **MCP arthrodesis**, excise excess skin | — |
| V | **None**; consider **free toe-to-hand transfer** → converts to type IV | — |

**Conflict — type I** — **observation** for types I & IV (Orthobullets) · **no web surgery but cleft closure + IM reduction** for type I (TO) — read the options

**Operative indication (Orthobullets)** — types **IIA, IIB, III, V** → web + thumb + cleft reconstruction

### Prognosis

**Function** — depends on **1st web space involvement**

**Classic line** — **aesthetically displeasing, not functionally limiting**

## X vs Y

| | **Cleft hand** | **Symbrachydactyly** |
|---|---|---|
| Metacarpals | **Absent** | Present (rudimentary nubbins) |
| Shape | V | U ("atypical cleft") |
| Laterality | **Bilateral** | Unilateral |
| Feet | Involved | Not |
| Inheritance | Familial | Sporadic |

## How it's asked

**"Pedigree for cleft hand inheritance?"** → **autosomal dominant**\
✗ Wrong options: AR, X-linked recessive, X-linked dominant, mitochondrial

**"Missing middle ray, normal web — type and treatment?"** → **Manske type I: no web surgery; cleft closure + reduce intermetacarpal space** (TO)

**"Type IIA web surgery?"** → **4-flap Z-plasty** (TO)

**"Which comes first, thumb or cleft?"** → **thumb web has priority, but thumb reconstruction must not precede cleft closure**

**"Feature separating cleft hand from symbrachydactyly?"** → **absent metacarpals**, bilateral, feet involved

**"Associated syndrome?"** → **EEC syndrome**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Clasped Thumb

*Hand · 13 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition** — **flexion-adduction deformity of thumb** persisting **beyond 3rd–4th month** of life; often **bilateral**

**Sex** — **♂:♀ ≈ 2.5:1**

**Predisposing** — consanguinity, family history

### Pathoanatomy

**Key lesion** — **attenuation/absence of EPB** (commoner, mild cases) **or EPL, or both**

**Also** — 1st web contracture · adductor pollicis / 1st dorsal interosseous contracture · global MCP instability · abnormal MCP cartilage

### Genes & lab markers

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant**, variable expressivity; may be **sporadic**

### Associations

**Syndromes** — **arthrogryposis** · digitotalar dysmorphism · **Freeman-Sheldon** · X-linked **MASA**

**Lower limb** — **congenital vertical talus**, bilateral **CTEV**

**Upper limb** — flexion deformities of the four fingers

## 3 Presentation

**History** — persistent flexion-adduction **beyond 3–4 mo**, usually bilateral; family history

**Examination** — type of clasped thumb (passive extension/abduction possible?) + associated anomalies

![Congenital clasped thumb](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6085-congenital-clasped-thumb.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

### Tsuyuguchi classification

| Type | Features | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I — Supple** | Thumb **can be passively abducted & extended**; no other digital anomaly | ![Type I supple clasped thumb](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6085-tileshop-fcgi-3.jpg) |
| **II — With contracture** | **Cannot passively extend/abduct**; ± other digital anomalies | |
| **III — Rigid** | With **arthrogryposis** + marked soft-tissue deficit | |

## 5 Management

```
Congenital clasped thumb (all types)
├─ First line → SERIAL SPLINTING + STRETCHING 3–6 MO  (start ~6 mo of age)
│      └─ Type I with EPL or EPB present → good definitive result
└─ Fails / residual deficit → SURGERY (delay to 3–5 y)
       ├─ Type I–II, residual active extension deficit → EIP → EPL TRANSFER
       ├─ Type II–III, web deficit / MCP contracture → THUMB RECONSTRUCTION
       └─ Joint deformity not corrected by release + transfer → ARTHRODESIS
```

### First-line treatment – serial splinting

**All types** — **serial splinting of thumb 3–6 mo** = first line\
✗ Wrong options: observation (will not correct)

**Timing** — begin **~6 months** of age

**Good results** — **Type I** when **one of EPL/EPB present**

**Poor results** — Type I with **both EPL & EPB absent**; **Type II & III**

### Operative options

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **EIP → EPL transfer** | **Type I or II** with residual deficit of active extension |
| **Thumb reconstruction** | Failed nonop; **1st web soft-tissue deficit (Type III)**; **Type II/III with significant MCP contracture** |
| **MCP arthrodesis** | **Last resort**: severe deformity / MCP instability not overcome by skin release + transfer |

**Reconstruction age** — **3–5 years**

**Web widening** — dorsal rotational advancement flap · **4- or 5-flap Z-plasty**

**Web deepening** — release thenar origins from TCL · release 1st MCP capsule

**Tendons** — **FPL Z-lengthening** in forearm; **EPB + EPL absent → tendon transfer**; **isolated EPB absence → usually no transfer**

## 6 Complications

**List** — cosmetic appearance · **MCP instability** · reduced thumb function

## X vs Y

| | Clasped thumb Type I | Type II | Type III |
|---|---|---|---|
| Passive extension/abduction | Yes | No | No (rigid) |
| Association | None | ± digital anomalies | **Arthrogryposis** |
| Splinting result | **Good** | Poor | Poor |
| Usual surgery | EIP → EPL if residual lag | Web reconstruction ± transfer | Web reconstruction ± arthrodesis |

## How it's asked

**"8-mo-old, restricted passive thumb extension/abduction — first line?"** → **serial splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · web reconstruction · tendon transfer · MCP arthrodesis

**"Commonest deficient tendon?"** → **EPB**

**"Syndrome with rigid Type III?"** → **arthrogryposis**

**"Type I–II with residual active extension deficit?"** → **EIP → EPL transfer**

**"Age for reconstruction?"** → **3–5 y**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Radial Head Dislocation

*Hand · 14 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathoanatomy

**Incidence** — rare, **<1 per 100,000**

**Direction** — **posterior ~70%** > anterior 15% = lateral 15%

**Laterality** — **bilateral in majority**; unilateral congenital form can occur (Agnew)

**Pathoanatomy** — **failure of capitellum development** → loss of contact pressure → abnormal radial head → altered PRUJ → abnormal ulna

**Radius** — **bowed**, appears **too long vs ulna**

**Associations** — other congenital anomalies; **radial longitudinal deficiency**, **proximal radioulnar synostosis**; syndromes; familial

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — often **asymptomatic**, **painless**; radial head prominence (posterolateral)

**ROM** — limited, **especially extension & supination**; usually functional

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – congenital vs traumatic

| Clinical | Radiographic |
|---|---|
| **Bilateral** | **Dome-shaped (convex) radial head**, long narrow neck |
| Present at birth, familial | **Hypoplastic / absent capitellum** |
| Other congenital anomalies | **Short ulna / long radius**, bowed radius |
| **No trauma history** | Deficient trochlea, prominent medial epicondyle |
| **Irreducible by closed methods** | Grooving distal radius; anterior curvature of posterior ulnar outline |

![CRHD diagnostic criteria](../../MCQ/attachments/q5359-illc-criteria.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**Radiocapitellar line** — line along radius long axis should intersect capitellum on all views → misses = dislocation

**X-ray** — radial head **posterior to capitellum**; **convex "dome" radial head**; long, bowed radius

**Next step (suspected CRHD)** — **X-ray contralateral elbow** → confirms bilateral

![Posterior CRHD, convex radial head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6069-congenital-dx-of-radial-head.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
CRHD
├─ Asymptomatic / functional ROM → OBSERVATION
└─ Pain / restricted motion / cosmesis (usually adulthood) → RADIAL HEAD RESECTION
       (treat each elbow on its own symptoms)
```

### Diagnosis (bilateral, vs traumatic) & management – observation vs late excision

**First line** — **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: closed reduction · open reduction ± osteotomy · child-abuse workup without other findings

**Radial head resection** — adult, symptomatic → **↓ pain**, improves **forearm rotation**; no change in flex-ext or carrying angle (Bengard)

**Resection downside** — **>25% wrist pain** post-op

**Avoid open reduction** — high **redislocation** (even with ulnar/radial osteotomy or annular ligament reconstruction) — hypoplastic capitellum, dome head, long radius

**Avoid radial head arthroplasty** — redislocation (undeveloped capitellum, non-collinear head)

**Not interposition arthroplasty** — for ulnohumeral arthritis, not radial head disease

**Not physiotherapy** — no gain in pain/ROM

## X vs Y

| | **Congenital RHD** | **Traumatic RHD** |
|---|---|---|
| Laterality | **Bilateral** | Unilateral |
| Radial head | **Convex / dome** | Normal concave |
| Capitellum | **Hypoplastic** | Normal |
| Trauma Hx | None | Present |
| Closed reduction | **Difficult / irreducible** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Atraumatic nontender posterolateral elbow bump, dome radial head — next step?"** → **X-ray contralateral elbow**

**"7-y with convex radial head, vague fall, lacks 20° extension — treatment?"** → **Observation**\
✗ Wrong options: abuse workup · reduction

**"Bilateral CRHD, right painful + stiff, left minimal loss — plan?"** → **resect right radial head + nonop left**

**"Why not open reduction?"** → **redislocation**

**"Commonest direction?"** → **posterior**

**"Complication after radial head excision?"** → **wrist pain (>25%)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Radial Ulnar Synostosis

*Hand · 15 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Aetiology & associations (failure of segmentation, XXY)

**Embryology** — single cartilaginous anlage divides **distal → proximal** into radius + ulna in **7th week** in utero; proximal ends share common perichondrium

**Cause** — **failure of segmentation / differentiation** → proximal bony bridge\
✗ Wrong options: failure of formation · trauma · transverse forearm deficiency

**Demographics** — **♂ > ♀ (3:2)**; **~60% bilateral** (TO: "more than half")

**Family history** — **~20%**; familial cases **autosomal dominant**

**Associated syndromes (~30%)** — **Apert** · Carpenter · **arthrogryposis** · mandibulofacial dysostosis · **Klinefelter**

**Associated anomaly** — posterior **radial head dislocation**

### Genes & lab markers

**Chromosomes** — **sex chromosome duplication** → **Klinefelter 47 XXY** (most common chromosomal association)

| ✗ Distractor karyotype | Associations |
|---|---|
| 45 XO (Turner) | Malignant hyperthermia, sexual infantilism, scoliosis, **short 4th MC**, webbed neck |
| Trisomy 21 | C1–2, hip, patellofemoral instability, scoliosis |
| Trisomy 18 | Cardiac defects; 5% survive 1 y |
| Partial 15 deletion (Prader-Willi) | Obese hypotonia, insatiable appetite, hip dysplasia, scoliosis |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **painless**; usually **asymptomatic**, noticed by parents/teachers

**Age at diagnosis** — average **6 y** (unilateral may go unnoticed until adolescence)

**Exam** — **fixed forearm pronation** (average **30°**); **elbow flexion/extension preserved**; no active or passive rotation

**Compensation** — **shoulder abduction → for lost pronation** · **shoulder adduction → for lost supination** · wrist hypermobility

**Tasks** — keyboard/tabletop ← pronation deficit; eating, washing face, catching ball ← supination deficit

**Toddler clue** — rotates elbow in front of body to bring bottle to mouth; abducts shoulder to pick up blocks

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral forearm and elbow; **X-ray confirms**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · MRI · arthrocentesis (no extra info)

**Findings** — **proximal radioulnar synostosis** · **radius wide and bowed** · **ulna narrow and straight** · radial head ± dislocated/malformed

![Proximal radioulnar synostosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6071-ru-synostosis.jpg)

**Fall + fixed pronation** — synostosis is congenital, not from acute trauma 3 days ago

### Cleary & Omer classification

| Type | Synostosis | Radial head | |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Cartilaginous** (no osseous) | Normal, reduced | ![Type 1](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6071-type1.jpg) |
| 2 | Osseous | Normal, reduced | ![Type 2](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6071-type2.jpg) |
| **3** | **Long osseous** | **Hypoplastic, posteriorly dislocated** (TO) | ![Type 3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6071-type3.jpg) |
| 4 | **Short osseous** | **Mushroom-shaped, anteriorly dislocated** | ![Type 4](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6071-type4.jpg) |

**Cleary vs Wilkie (TO)** — Cleary & Omer = the better classification

### Wilkie classification (TO)

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| 1 | Medullary canals of radius + ulna joined; usually **bilateral**; radial head **not** dislocated |
| 2 | Radial head **dislocated** (anterior or posterior); usually **unilateral** |

## 5 Management

```
Congenital RU synostosis
├─ Asymptomatic / unilateral → observation
├─ ADL-limiting / pronation >60° / bilateral → derotational osteotomy (age 3–6 y)
│      └─ goal: functional position, NOT motion
└─ Goal = restore motion → excise + vascularised fascio-fat interposition
```

### Management – observation vs osteotomy/mobilisation indications

**Default** — **observation** (most cases; surgery rarely needed) — 14-mo-old with ↓ supination → **observe**

**Slight pronation** — well tolerated (keyboard position)

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Type |
|---|---|
| Deformity **limiting activities** (sport, hygiene, eating) | Absolute |
| **Pronation >60°** | Relative |
| **Bilateral** | Relative (✓ answer over 45° pronation, 30° supination, age <3, affected sibling) |

**Optimal age** — **3–6 y** (average ~5 y)

### Derotational osteotomy (procedure of choice)

**Goal** — **functional resting position**; does **not** restore motion

**Sites** — at synostosis (proximal diaphysis; narrow space → tightness, loss of correction, NV risk, **PIN palsy**) · **distal to synostosis at different levels** (distributes rotation → safer) · radius distal diaphysis alone

**Timing** — immediate · **delayed at 10 days** (Lin: percutaneous drill osteotomy + cast, manipulate in plaster at 10 d) (TO) · gradual with **Ilizarov** (**lowest NV complications**)

| Position | Orthobullets | OB explanation | TO |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Unilateral** | **0–30° pronation** | 30° pronation | 0–30° pronation |
| **Bilateral – dominant** | **0–15° pronation** | 10–20° pronation | 20° pronation |
| **Bilateral – nondominant** | **Neutral** | Neutral | Neutral |

**Old teaching** — nondominant in 10–15° (Orthobullets) / 20° (TO) **supination** → no longer recommended (keyboards, phones)

**Bilateral older OB goal** — one forearm modest pronation, other modest supination

### Synostosis excision (mobilisation)

**Goal** — restore active rotation; gain usually **slight**, unsatisfactory results

**Prevent recurrence** — **interpose material between radius and ulna**: **vascularised fascio-fat graft** (vascularised > free fat) · free/pedicled fat, muscle, Gelfoam, Micropore

**Do not prevent recurrence** — soft-tissue reconstruction (capsule, annular ligament, biceps) · post-op casting · post-op radiation (not described) · **anconeus interposition**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Recurrence of synostosis** | **~100%** with excision alone or **anconeus** interposition; **0%** (negligible, TO) with vascularised fascio-fat graft |
| **Loss of correction** | Casting after osteotomy → **15–20°** (Orthobullets) · up to 20° (TO, Lin method) |
| **Compartment syndrome** | Up to **36%**; corrections **>60°**; ± prophylactic fasciotomy |
| **Nerve palsy** | **PIN** (proximal osteotomy), AIN, radial; most resolve in 3 mo |

## How it's asked

**"Fixed pronation, delayed milestones, obese uncoordinated boy — karyotype?"** → **47 XXY**\
✗ Wrong options: 45 XO · trisomy 21/18 · 15 deletion

**"Proximal RU synostosis is caused by?"** → **failure of segmentation**\
✗ Wrong options: formation · trauma · transverse deficiency · always needs surgery

**"Indication for future surgery?"** → **bilateral involvement**\
✗ Wrong options: 45° pronation · 30° supination · age <3 · sibling

**"Excision to restore motion — step to prevent recurrence?"** → **interposition between radius and ulna**\
✗ Wrong options: soft-tissue reconstruction · radiation · casting

**"14-month-old, ↓ supination, abducts shoulder — next step?"** → **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · MRI · aspiration · reduction

**"Long osseous synostosis, hypoplastic posteriorly dislocated radial head — Cleary & Omer type?"** → **Type 3** (TO)

**"Short synostosis, mushroom radial head dislocated anteriorly?"** → **Cleary Type 4**

**"Shoulder abduction compensates for?"** → **loss of pronation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Trigger Thumb

*Hand · 16 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathoanatomy

**Prevalence** — **3 per 1,000** by age 1; **♂ = ♀**; **25% bilateral**

**Separate entity** — distinct from adult acquired trigger thumb (and from pediatric trigger finger)

**"Congenital" = misnomer** — usually **acquired / developmental**; not noted at birth; rare AD reports (variable penetrance)

**Pathoanatomy** — **thickened FPL** (collagen degeneration + synovial proliferation) → ↑ diameter vs **A1 pulley** → abnormal gliding

### Thumb pulleys

| Label (OB217410) | Structure | Release? |
|---|---|---|
| A | FPL tendon | No — cutting → loss of IP & MCP flexion |
| **B** | **A1 pulley** | **✓ site of triggering** |
| C | Av pulley | No |
| D | Oblique pulley | No — cutting → **bowstringing** |
| E | A2 pulley | No — no role |

![Thumb pulleys: B = A1](../../MCQ/attachments/q217410-a-trigger-thumb.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Typical** — **fixed flexion deformity of thumb IP joint** (not triggering), painless; rare trauma or family Hx

**Notta's node (TO)** — prominent **FPL tendon nodule** at **base of thumb / over MCP** = **pediatric trigger thumb**

**Exam** — loss of passive IP extension, **unchanged by wrist/MCP flexion**; no intrinsic IP contracture; NV intact; ± MCP hyperextension\
✗ Wrong options: FPL shortening

**Natural history** — triggering → **fixed contracture**; spontaneous resolution unlikely **after age 2**

![Notta's node outlined](../../MCQ/attachments/q4757-c-fig-c-nodule-new.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; imaging not required

**X-ray** — only if **trauma history** (AP/lateral hand ± thumb views) → **usually normal** (IP flexion, no osseous lesion)

## 5 Management

```
Pediatric trigger thumb
├─ Flexible, <2 y → passive extension exercises + intermittent extension SPLINTING
├─ Triggering (not locked), <1 y → OBSERVE ~6 mo
└─ Fixed >12 mo / locked 6 mo at age 2 / failed nonop → OPEN A1 PULLEY RELEASE
```

### Diagnosis (Notta's node) & A1 pulley release timing

**First line, flexible, 1-y-old (TO)** — **passive extension exercises + intermittent extension splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: release · exercises alone

**Splinting (TO)** — **more successful than observation / exercises alone**; IP hyperextension, block MCP hyperextension; **6–12 wk** (several months; not 30 days); high drop-out

| Treatment | Resolution |
|---|---|
| Passive exercises / observation <2 y (TO) | **30–60%** |
| Spontaneous after 2 y | **<10%** |
| Splinting + exercises (TO) | **50–60%** |
| Nonop after 3 y (OB) | **50%** |
| Baek: no treatment, 48 mo | 60% |
| **A1 release** | **65–95%** (Orthobullets) · **95%** (OB) |

**Delay to age 2** — **no sequelae**\
✗ Wrong options: permanent contracture · motor delay · hand dominance change (handedness ~4 y)

### Operative criteria

| Source | Indication for A1 release |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets / TO | **Fixed deformity >12 mo** or failed nonop |
| Shah | Fixed >12 mo; consider surgery **after 2 y** |
| Marek | **Locked for 6 mo at age 2 → surgery**; triggering <1 y → observe 6 mo |
| OB explanation | **>3 y** or failed splinting / stretching |
| Dittmer | **Stage IV → immediate surgery** (4.6× surgery risk); stage II–III observe ≥1 y |

**Technique** — **open** release (gold standard); transverse incision MCP flexion crease; **protect radial digital nerve**; watch nodule glide on passive IP extension

**Avoid percutaneous** — incomplete release, radial digital n. injury, FPL laceration; no advantage (GA needed anyway)

**Not indicated** — nodule resection · volar plate release · FPL lengthening · steroid injection (difficult, no benefit)

**6-y fixed IP flexion + nodule** — **complete A1 release**\
✗ Wrong options: no treatment (won't resolve)

![Intra-op A1 pulley](../../MCQ/attachments/q219838-b-a1.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Digital nerve injury** — high risk (close to tendon/A1)

**Bowstringing** — from **oblique pulley release**

**Others** — wound (scar contracture, abscess, infection), IP flexion deficit; Marek: no major complications/recurrence

## X vs Y

| | **Pediatric trigger thumb** | Camptodactyly | Clasped thumb | Blauth I hypoplasia |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Digit | **Thumb IP** | **Little finger** PIP | Thumb | Thumb |
| Nodule | **Notta's node** | — | — | — |
| Treatment | **A1 release** | Stretching | — | **Nonop** |

## How it's asked

**"Notta's node characteristic of?"** → **pediatric trigger thumb** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: RLD, synostosis, constriction ring

**"First treatment, flexible trigger thumb, 1-y girl?"** → **passive exercises + intermittent extension splinting** (TO)

**"2-y fixed IP flexion, nodule, splinted 6 mo — diagnosis & Rx?"** → **pediatric trigger thumb; open A1 release**\
✗ Wrong options: nodule resection · percutaneous

**"Delay surgery from 13 mo to 2 y causes?"** → **no sequelae**

**"6-y fixed thumb IP flexion + nodule?"** → **complete release of proximal annular (A1) pulley**

**"IP not passively extendable, unchanged with wrist flexion?"** → **A1 release**\
✗ Wrong options: FPL lengthening · volar plate · 30-day splint

**"Which structure to release (labelled pulleys)?"** → **B = A1**\
✗ Wrong options: oblique → bowstringing

**"Structure in intra-op photo?"** → **A1 pulley**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Constrictive Ring Syndrome (Streeter's Dysplasia)

*Hand · 17 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Synonyms** — amniotic band syndrome (ABS), Streeter's dysplasia, **early amnion rupture sequence**

**Incidence** — **1:15,000** (Orthobullets, OB) · **1:10,000** "rare" stated true (TO) — read the options

**Sex** — M = F

**Site** — **hands/fingers 80%**, >90% distal to wrist; distal > trunk; **central digits** (long 28%, ring 27%, index 23%); average **3 limbs**

**Leg bands (TO)** — mostly **zone 2** (knee–ankle); tibia/fibula fractures may occur at band level (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: zone 1

**Risk factors** — **low birth weight (<2500 g)**, **prematurity (<37 wk)**, maternal drugs, trauma in pregnancy, attempted 1st-trimester termination; abnormal gestational history **60%**\
✗ Wrong options: late gestation / high birth weight

**Mortality** — infant 4.6%, perinatal 12.7%

### Genes & inheritance

**Inheritance** — **sporadic, not hereditary**\
✗ Wrong options: AD/AR/X-linked

## 2 Mechanism

**Extrinsic theory (most accepted)** — ruptured amnion → fibrous strands **wrap limb circumferentially**

**Distal to band** — lymphatic + NV compression → **lymphoedema**, altered circulation, neuropathy, amputation

## 3 Presentation

### Associated conditions (clubfoot, cleft lip/palate) & sporadic inheritance

**Exam** — **normal anatomy proximal to ring**; band **perpendicular** to digit/limb axis

**No amputation** — look for secondary syndactyly, bony fusion, proximal sinus tracts

**Clubfoot** — **commonest associated foot condition**; **rigid**; casting poor (6% success, 77% need band Z-plasty first)

**Clubfoot rate** — 12–56% (OB) · up to 25% (OB) · ~⅓ (Foulkes) · 38% (Koskimies)

**Associated** — **clubfoot**, **cleft lip/palate**, craniofacial defects, **cardiac defects**, **encephalocele** (off-midline), **acrosyndactyly**, pseudarthrosis, LLD, hemihypertrophy, renal, haemangioma, **lymphoedema** (TO: non-orthopaedic)

**Anterolateral tibial bowing** — listed as a manifestation (OB) · "not associated / NF1" (OB, 2 questions) — read the options

**NOT associated** — **bronchopulmonary dysplasia** (prematurity) · **complex syndactyly** (TO) · polydactyly · vertical/oblique talus · tarsal coalition · pes planovalgus · cutis aplasia · renal aplasia · dural ectasia

**Syndactyly type (TO)** — **simple incomplete, fenestrated** (slits proximally), bones not fused

![Acrosyndactyly with fenestrations](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6082-acrosyndactyly.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Antenatal US** — diagnosis possible at **end of 1st trimester**; chorioamniotic membrane separation → bands

**Diagnosis** — clinical at birth

### Patterson classification

| Type | Description | Treatment | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | **Simple constriction ring** | Observe; **release if circulation compromised** | ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6082-type-1-abs.jpg) |
| II | Ring + distal deformity (hypoplasia, **lymphoedema**) | **Circumferential Z-plasties** | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6082-abs-photo2.jpg) |
| III | Distal fusion (**acrosyndactyly**) | **Syndactyly release** (early) | ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6082-abs-hand-2-photo.jpg) |
| IV | **Amputation** | Reconstruction (bone lengthening, web deepening) | ![Type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6082-abs-photo.jpg) |

## 5 Management

```
Constriction band
├─ Shallow / incomplete, no lymphatic compromise → OBSERVE
├─ Complete band ± lymphatic/vascular compromise → BAND EXCISION + Z-PLASTY
│      └─ NV compromise → urgent release
└─ Acrosyndactyly / amputation → release / reconstruction
```

### Treatment – band release & Z-plasty

**Indication** — **complete circumferential band interfering with lymphatic drainage → excision + Z-plasty** (one-stage possible)

**Trap** — incomplete bands not affecting lymphatics → do not resect

**Acrosyndactyly + distal oedema** — **early** release → better prognosis

**Clubfoot with band** — **Z-plasty band release before** clubfoot correction

**Follow-up** — to skeletal maturity (contracture, growth disturbance)

**Prognosis** — depends on band location & severity

## How it's asked

**"Streeter band of leg — EXCEPT"** → **most in zone 1** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ; zone 2

**"Not a feature of Streeter's dysplasia"** → **complex syndactyly** (TO)

**"Associated foot condition"** → **clubfoot (CTEV)**\
✗ Wrong options: vertical talus, coalition, equinovalgus

**"Associated EXCEPT"** → **bronchopulmonary dysplasia**

**"Inheritance"** → **sporadic**

**"Associated condition"** → **cleft palate**

**"Cleft palate + Ponseti-treated clubfoot + syndactyly — expect"** → **amniotic band syndrome of hand**

**"True statement"** → **complete band blocking lymphatics → excision + Z-plasty**\
✗ Wrong options: trunk > limbs · late gestation/high BW · tibial bowing

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cubital Tunnel Syndrome

*Hand · 18 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **2nd most common** upper-limb compressive neuropathy (after CTS); ~30/100,000/y

**Demographics** — **♂ > ♀** (♀ present younger); ↑ with age

**Associations** — cubitus varus/valgus, medial epicondylitis, burns, elbow contracture release

**Children** — rare; trauma or repetitive use; nonop works in up to 50%

### Anatomy – ulnar nerve course & cubital tunnel

**Ulnar nerve** — **medial cord (C8–T1)** → posteromedial to brachial artery → pierces IM septum at **arcade of Struthers 8 cm proximal** to medial epicondyle → behind medial epicondyle (cubital tunnel) → **between 2 heads of FCU** → between FCU & FDP → **superficial to TCL** at wrist

| Cubital tunnel | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Roof** | **FCU fascia + Osborne's ligament** (cubital tunnel retinaculum; medial epicondyle → olecranon) |
| **Floor** | **Posterior bundle (posterior oblique ligament) of MCL** + transverse band + capsule |
| Walls | Medial epicondyle + olecranon |

**✗ Floor distractors** — Osborne (= roof) · anterior/posterior band of anterior bundle UCL · transverse bundle

**MUCL bundles** — **anterior bundle** = primary valgus stabiliser (anterior band isometric; posterior band tight in flexion) · **posterior bundle** = greatest tension change, **tight in flexion**, **floor of tunnel** · transverse = no stability (doesn't cross joint)

### Compression sites (Osborne's ligament, anconeus epitrochlearis)

| Site (proximal → distal) | Note |
|---|---|
| **Arcade of Struthers** | Hiatus in medial IM septum, 8 cm proximal |
| Medial IM septum / medial head triceps | |
| Medial epicondyle | Nonunion, osteophyte |
| **Osborne's ligament–MCL** (cubital tunnel) | |
| **Anconeus epitrochlearis** | Accessory muscle medial epicondyle → olecranon, **replaces Osborne's** |
| **Two heads of FCU / aponeurosis** | = humeral head forms **ulnar border of flexor-pronator mass** |
| Deep flexor-pronator aponeurosis / FDS proximal edge | |

**Most common site** — **between 2 heads of FCU** (Orthobullets, OB) · **Osborne's ligament** (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Anconeus epitrochlearis** — ~**10% of population** · **13–30%** of surgically treated CuTS; static/dynamic compression; usually found at surgery → **excise + in situ decompression ± transposition**; intra-op: muscle over nerve posterior to medial epicondyle

![Anconeus epitrochlearis intra-op](../../MCQ/attachments/q219430-a-epitrochlearis.jpg)

**External causes** — fracture/medial epicondyle nonunion, osteophytes, **HO**, tumours, ganglia

| NOT ulnar nerve compression sites | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **Ligament of Struthers** (supracondylar process → medial epicondyle) | **Median** (pronator syndrome) |
| Supracondylar process (1%) | Median |
| **Lacertus fibrosus** | Median |
| **Arcade of Frohse** + FLEAS (fibrous bands over radial head, leash of Henry, ECRB edge, supinator) | **PIN / radial** |
| Ulnar artery thrombosis/aneurysm | Ulnar at **Guyon zone III** (no dorsal sensory loss) |

![Compression sites](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6021-cubital-tunnel.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Compression + traction** — elbow flexion ↑ tension; superficial nerve; **subluxation = ↑ traction**

**Exacerbating** — **phone use (flexion)**, repetitive flexion + valgus (throwers); **night symptoms** (sleeping flexed)

### Tardy ulnar nerve palsy (TO)

**Cause (TO)** — **nonunion of paediatric lateral condyle fracture → cubitus valgus → chronic stretch**; also medial epicondyle nonunion, **varus** malunion of supracondylar fracture, arthritis, cysts, recurrent dislocation, Monteggia

**Onset (TO)** — delayed; months to **up to 25 years** after injury; even minimal valgus can cause it

**Pathology (TO)** — tension neuritis + intraneural fibrosis; V-kinking, FCU fibrous band

**Treatment (TO)** — **surgical**: **anterior transposition** ± corrective osteotomy (osteotomy only if elbow complaint)

**Trap (TO)** — ulnar nerve supplies **adductor pollicis**, **not abductor pollicis brevis** (median)

## 3 Presentation

**Sensory** — paraesthesia **small + ulnar ring + ulnar dorsal hand**

**Motor (intrinsics)** — adductor pollicis, deep FPB, interossei, lumbricals 3–4 → **weak grasp** (MCP flexion) · **weak pinch** (up to 70% lost) · ↓ index abduction (1st DI)

**Inspection** — interosseous + **1st web atrophy**, ring/small **clawing**, **subluxation** over medial epicondyle on flexion–extension

### Clinical signs (Wartenberg, Froment)

| Sign | Finding | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| **Froment** | **Thumb IP flexion** during key pinch | **FPL (AIN/median)** compensates weak **adductor pollicis** |
| **Jeanne** | Thumb **MCP hyperextension** + adduction on key pinch | **EPL (radial)** compensates |
| **Wartenberg** | **Small finger abducted** | Weak **3rd palmar interosseous** + small lumbrical; **accessory slip of EDM (radial n.) to abductor digiti minimi tendon** |
| Masse | Flat palmar arch, lost ulnar hand elevation | Weak ODQ |
| **Pollock** | Can't flex DIP ring/small | Weak **FDP** (extrinsic → proximal lesion) |

![Froment sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6021-1-s2-0-s0749071211000904-gr3.jpg)

**Froment = which nerve?** — **ulnar** (cubital tunnel or Guyon's)

**Wartenberg wrong explanations** — ✗ Wrong options: tetanic ADM (ADM is ulnar too) · RCL insufficiency · unopposed FDP (→ clawing) · Martin-Gruber

**Wartenberg causes (TO)** — ulnar palsy (commonest) · **avulsion of 3rd volar interosseous insertion** · **cervical cord compression** · mild hemiparesis ("digiti quinti sign") · hemiplegic migraine \
✗ Wrong options: **median nerve palsy**

**Wartenberg (TO)** — unopposed EDM + EDC (radial) abduct small; more pronounced **in extension**

**Provocative** — **Tinel over cubital tunnel** · **elbow flexion test >60 s** · direct compression

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – electrodiagnostics

**EMG/NCS** — diagnosis + **prognosis**; **NCV <50 m/s across elbow**; ↓ SNAP + CMAP amplitude

**Repeat EDS** — not when clinical diagnosis clear; only for new inconsistent symptoms

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray elbow** — look for **osteophyte / HO** (→ changes procedure: medial epicondylectomy), cubitus valgus/varus, lateral condyle nonunion

### McGowan & Dellon classification

| Type | Findings |
|---|---|
| 1 | Subjective sensory; no objective 2PD loss or atrophy |
| 2A | Sensory + **weak pinch/grip, no atrophy** |
| 2B | Sensory + **atrophy**, intrinsic strength ≤3 |
| 3 | **Profound atrophy** + sensory disturbance |

### Ulnar tunnel (Guyon's canal) compression

**Dorsal ulnar sensory branch** — arises **proximal** to Guyon → **dorsal hand spared** in Guyon/distal lesions

| Guyon zone | Content | Deficit |
|---|---|---|
| I (proximal to bifurcation; palmar carpal ligament) | Mixed | Sensory volar ring/small + intrinsic weakness; dorsal spared |
| II (deep branch, hook of hamate) | Motor | **Intrinsic weakness only** |
| **III (superficial branch, pisohamate ligament)** | Sensory | **Volar ring/small numbness, normal motor**; ulnar artery thrombosis/aneurysm (**cyclist**) |

![Guyon zones](../../MCQ/attachments/q220010-illa-zones.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Cubital tunnel syndrome
├─ Mild → night extension splint 45° + NSAIDs + activity mod
│      └─ fails (~3 wk adequate trial) → surgery
├─ Stable nerve, idiopathic → in situ decompression
├─ Subluxing nerve in thrower / young / failed in situ / poor bed → anterior transposition
└─ Osteophyte/HO, thin patient with subluxation → medial epicondylectomy
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — NSAIDs, activity modification, **night splint 45° extension, forearm neutral**; effective **~50%**

**Not indicated** — **daytime splinting** · **cortisone injection** (nerve risk) · continuing night splint another 6 wk in thrower

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indications |
|---|---|
| **In situ decompression** | Failed nonop; **before denervation**; idiopathic, stable nerve |
| **Anterior transposition** (subcut / intramuscular / submuscular) | **Failed in situ** · **throwing athlete** · **poor nerve bed (tumour, osteophyte, HO)** · **unstable/subluxing nerve** (snapping), esp. young · tardy palsy |
| **Medial epicondylectomy** | Visible symptomatic subluxing nerve · **thin patient** · **osteophyte/HO** on X-ray |

### In situ decompression vs anterior transposition

**In situ = transposition outcomes** — **no difference** (motor or sensory); in situ **fewer complications** → choice for idiopathic

**Subluxation** — not a contraindication to in situ (Keiner) · but **throwers/snapping nerve → transposition**

**Two "snaps"** — ulnar nerve + **medial triceps** dislocating → transposition

**Subcutaneous transposition** — not for idiopathic (↑ complications, equal outcomes)

**In situ technique** — 4 cm incision between olecranon + epicondyle; release **Osborne's + FCU fascia** distally, triceps–IM septum fascia proximally; **avoid circumferential dissection** (devascularisation, hypermobility); endoscopic option

**Transposition technique** — circumferential dissection, **excise medial IM septum**, secure with subcut tissue / fascial sling / within or under flexor-pronator mass

![Ulnar nerve transposition with fascial sling](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6021-ulnar-nerve-transposition.jpg)

**Medial epicondylectomy** — oblique osteotomy; **preserve MCL insertion** (risk medial instability)

### Prognosis

**Good** — **80–90%** good if intermittent + no denervation; **ulnar nerve instability (subluxation) in young = good prognostic factor**

**Poor** — **intrinsic muscle atrophy** (strongest)

**NCV** — returns to baseline within ~2 wk; distal latency recovers better with early decompression

## 6 Complications

### Medial antebrachial cutaneous nerve injury

**MABC** — crosses field **~3 cm (3.1) distal** to medial epicondyle; minor branches at/proximal → **painful neuroma**, posteromedial elbow pain, **↓ sensation ulnar (anteromedial) forearm**

**Medial brachial cutaneous** — crosses **7 cm proximal** to epicondyle

| ✗ Deficit distractor | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Can't flex thumb IP | AIN |
| Can't extend thumb IP | PIN |
| Can't hold fingers abducted | Ulnar |
| ↓ radial forearm sensation | Lateral antebrachial cutaneous |

**Recurrence** — inadequate decompression, scarring, tethering at IM septum/FCU; **higher than after CTR**

**Transposition** — risk of **new point of compression**

## X vs Y

| | **Cubital tunnel** | **Guyon's canal (ulnar tunnel)** |
|---|---|---|
| Clawing | **Less** (FDP also weak) | More |
| Dorsal ulnar hand sensation | **Lost** | **Spared** |
| Extrinsics (FDP ring/small, FCU) | **Weak** | Normal |
| Tinel / flexion test | **Elbow** | Wrist |

| | **Cubital tunnel** | **C8 radiculopathy** |
|---|---|---|
| Sensory | Ring + small | C8 dermatome |
| Motor | Ulnar intrinsics only | **+ APB, FPB, OP, lateral lumbricals** (median C8) |
| Shoulder abduction | — | **Relieves** symptoms |

## How it's asked

**"Thrower, medial elbow pain, small-finger paraesthesia — structure?"** → **anconeus epitrochlearis**\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar process · ulnar artery · lacertus · arcade of Frohse

**"Ulnar nerve compression sites EXCEPT"** → **ligament of Struthers** (median)

**"Idiopathic CuTS failed splint/NSAIDs, no subluxation, no osteophyte — treatment & site?"** → **in situ release; FCU aponeurosis**

**"Floor of cubital tunnel?"** → **posterior bundle of UCL**\
✗ Wrong options: Osborne = roof

**"Froment sign + EMG compression proximal to wrist — release?"** → **border of flexor-pronator mass (FCU heads)**\
✗ Wrong options: FLEAS radial tunnel structures

**"Black-dot muscle over nerve behind epicondyle?"** → **anconeus epitrochlearis**

**"Wartenberg sign not caused by"** → **median nerve palsy** (TO)

**"Froment sign — nerve?"** → **ulnar**

**"Why is little finger abducted in ulnar palsy?"** → **accessory EDM slip to ADM tendon**

**"Surgery that minimises complications?"** → **simple in situ decompression**

**"In situ vs subcutaneous transposition with subluxation?"** → **no difference in outcome**

**"Good prognostic factor, adolescent gymnast?"** → **ulnar nerve subluxation**\
✗ Wrong options: atrophy = poor

**"Snapping medial elbow + Jeanne sign, ex-pitcher?"** → **decompression + anterior transposition**

**"Javelin thrower, two snaps, failed 6 wk splint?"** → **release + anterior transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: more splinting · daytime splint · cortisone

**"Painful paraesthesia near incision after transposition?"** → **medial antebrachial cutaneous** nerve

**"Nerve cut 3 cm distal to epicondyle — deficit?"** → **↓ ulnar forearm sensation**

**"Medial cord branch at risk 3 cm distal?"** → **MABC (label C)**

**"Cyclist, volar ring/small numbness, normal strength, dorsal spared?"** → **superficial ulnar branch at pisohamate ligament (zone III)**

**"False about tardy ulnar palsy nerve"** → **supplies APB** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# De Quervain's Tenosynovitis

*Hand · 19 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **stenosing tenosynovitis of 1st dorsal compartment** (APL + EPB)

**Incidence** — very common, ~1/1000/y

**Demographics** — **♀ > ♂ (6×, OB)**; **30–50 y** (Orthobullets) · 5th–6th decade (OB); **dominant wrist**

**Risk factors** — **overuse** (golf, racquet sports), post-traumatic, **postpartum / lactating** (classic: mother of 2-month-old)

**Occupation** — **overuse syndrome**; typist, bowler, cell-phone use (TO, OB) · **no causal link with occupational risk factors** (OB) — read the options

### Pathology

**Pathoanatomy** — thickened, swollen **extensor retinaculum** → ↑ tendon friction

**Not inflammatory** — **NOT an inflammatory process**; **mucopolysaccharide** accumulation (Orthobullets) · **myxoid degeneration** + sheath thickening (TO)

**Tendon mechanics (TO)** — typist's graph = **hysteresis / energy-dissipation curve** of cyclically loaded tendon

| Viscoelastic term (TO) | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Creep** | ↑ deformation under **constant load** |
| **Stress relaxation** | ↓ stress under **constant deformation** |
| **Hysteresis** | Unloading curve ≠ loading curve; area = energy lost as heat |
| Cyclic loading | Curve shifts right, hysteresis ↓ → reproducible = **pseudo-elasticity** |
| Toe region | **Un-crimping** of collagen fibrils (low stiffness) → linear → yield/failure |

### Anatomy – APL/EPB & injection site

| Dorsal compartment | Contents | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **APL, EPB** | **De Quervain's** |
| 2 | ECRL, ECRB | **Intersection syndrome** (1st/2nd crossing) |
| 3 | EPL | — |
| 4 | EIP, EDC | — |
| 5 | EDM | Vaughan-Jackson |
| 6 | ECU | Snapping ECU |

**Nerve** — APL & EPB innervated by **PIN**\
✗ Wrong options: radial n. proper · AIN · median · deep ulnar

**EPB** — **more dorsal** than APL · **thinner** · single tendon · **absent in 7%** · often in **own subsheath** (dorsal) → missed at release; separate EPB compartment ↑ failure of nonop

**APL** — **thicker**, usually **≥2 slips**, may have own fibro-osseous compartment

**Separate compartment for EPB or APL** — **24–34%** of wrists

**1st compartment** — **most numerous tendon variations** in upper limb

**Radial artery** — dives **deep** to 1st compartment distal to scaphoid\
✗ Wrong options: superficial to APL

**Not EPL** — 3rd compartment, never in 1st

![Extensor compartments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6026-extensor-compartments-moved.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — gradual **radial-sided wrist pain**, worse gripping/lifting with wrist neutral

**Tenderness** — 1st compartment **at radial styloid** / **1–2 cm proximal to radial styloid**

**Motion** — usually normal; pain with resisted radial deviation

**NV exam** — **normal**

| Test | Manoeuvre | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Finkelstein** | Examiner grasps thumb, quickly **ulnar-deviates** hand → pain at radial styloid | More indicative of **EPB > APL**; TO and OB also apply the name to the fist-around-thumb version |
| **Eichhoff** | Thumb clenched in fist + wrist ulnar-deviated → pain, **relieved when thumb extended** | Often confused with Finkelstein |

**Bowler/typist (TO)** — radial wrist pain ↑ by **thumb flexion + ulnar deviation** → **De Quervain's**\
✗ Wrong options: tennis/golfer's elbow · lunate AVN · UCL injury · scaphoid insufficiency #

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; advanced imaging not needed

**X-ray** — AP + lateral; **usually not indicated**; rule out **thumb CMC (basilar) arthritis**, **carpal arthritis**

**Ultrasound** — thickened retinaculum over 1st compartment tendons

**Differentials** — thumb CMC OA, intersection syndrome, FCR tendinitis

## 5 Management

```
De Quervain's
├─ First line → rest + NSAIDs + thumb spica splint
├─ Second step → steroid injection into 1st dorsal compartment
│      (near radial styloid; 50–80% success after 1–2)
└─ Failed nonop ~6 mo / severe → surgical release of 1st compartment
       └─ recurrence → re-explore for unreleased EPB subsheath
```

### Nonoperative

**Injection site** — **1st dorsal compartment near radial styloid**\
✗ Wrong options: CMC joint · carpal tunnel · A1 pulley · 1st/2nd crossing

**Steroid vs splint** — **steroid superior**; adding splint/NSAID after injection does not improve outcome

**Immobilisation** — high failure rate, **except lactating women** (nonop highly effective)

**TO conservative list** — activity modification, NSAIDs, ice massage, thumb spica, **steroid into APL/EPB sheath** → release if fails

### Surgical release

**Incision** — radial-based, proximal to wrist; transverse

**Release on DORSAL side** of sheath → prevents **volar subluxation** of tendons

**Protect** — **superficial radial sensory nerve**

**Release all** — APL slips + **EPB subsheath**

### Recurrence – unreleased EPB subsheath

**Classic** — recurrent pain, **+ Finkelstein**, tender 1st compartment, **– Tinel**, normal skin → **failure to decompress EPB subsheath**\
✗ Wrong options: neuroma: Tinel – · CRPS: no skin change · EPL/APB: not in 1st compartment

**Prognosis** — most resolve nonop; **high recurrence rate**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Superficial radial nerve injury / neuroma** | **+ Tinel**; no positive Finkelstein |
| **Incomplete release → recurrence** | **Unreleased EPB / separate APL compartment** |
| **Volar tendon subluxation** | Avoided by dorsal-side release |
| CRPS | Skin changes |

## X vs Y

| | **De Quervain's** | **Intersection syndrome** | Thumb CMC OA | Snapping ECU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Radial styloid | **More proximal + more dorsal** | CMC joint | Ulnar wrist |
| Structures | APL + EPB | **1st crossing 2nd** (APL/EPB over ECRL/ECRB) | Basal joint | ECU (6th) |
| Clue | Finkelstein + | Crepitus, pain on **resisted wrist extension** | **Grind test**, older | Snap pronation → supination |

## How it's asked

**"Postpartum radial wrist pain — inject where?"** → **1st dorsal compartment near radial styloid**

**"True during 1st compartment release?"** → **EPB more dorsal than APL**\
✗ Wrong options: EPB thicker · EPB multiple slips · radial artery superficial · EPL subsheath

**"Exam finding that localises to APL + EPB?"** → **radial pain on Finkelstein**\
✗ Wrong options: resisted extension = intersection · Phalen = CTS · grind = CMC OA

**"Recurrence 3 mo after release, Finkelstein +, Tinel –?"** → **unreleased EPB subsheath**

**"Nerve supplying affected tendons?"** → **PIN**

**"Bowler: pain ↑ thumb flexion + ulnar deviation?"** → **De Quervain's** (TO)

**"Typist, dorsolateral wrist pain + hysteresis curve?"** → **De Quervain's** (TO)

**"Which side of sheath to incise?"** → **dorsal** (prevents volar subluxation)

# Deep Space & Collar Button Infections

*Hand · 20 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

**Deep space infection** — **thenar** (**most commonly infected**) · hypothenar · **midpalmar** (rare)

**Collar button abscess** — **web space** abscess between fingers, usually from a **volar puncture**

### Anatomy – deep spaces

| Space | Boundaries |
|---|---|
| **Thenar** | Potential space **palmar to adductor pollicis**, dorsal to flexor tendons; fascial septum from midpalmar |
| **Midpalmar** | **Dorsal and radial to hypothenar space** |
| **Hypothenar** | Palmar to 5th MC, dorsal-radial to hypothenar fascia, ulnar to hypothenar septum |

![Deep spaces of the hand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6106-deep-spaces.jpg)

### Ulnar & radial bursae communications

**Ulnar bursa (TO)** — proximal continuation of **little finger (5th) sheath**

**Radial bursa (TO)** — proximal continuation of **thumb (FPL) sheath**

**Extent (TO)** — **2–3 cm above wrist**

**Communications (TO)** — ulnar ↔ **radial bursa** · ± **Parona's (forearm) space** · not **4th (ring) finger sheath**

## 2 Mechanism

**Collar button spread** — volar puncture → **dorsal through sinus in superficial transverse metacarpal ligament**; proximal spread **blocked by palmar aponeurosis**

**Organisms** — Staph & Strep; **CA-MRSA ~30%** of surgically treated hand infections, ↑ yearly; only independent risk factor **IV drug use**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — ± penetrating trauma; pain, swelling

| Space | Pain on flexion of |
|---|---|
| Thenar | **Thumb** |
| Hypothenar | **Small finger** |
| Midpalmar | **Middle, ring, small** |

**Thenar & midpalmar** — **loss of palmar concavity**

**Collar button** — web-space tenderness + **abduction of adjacent digits (V-sign)**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — if **foreign body** suspected

**MRI** — defines **extent** of infection

## 5 Management

```
Deep space / collar button infection
└─ I&D + IV antibiotics (standard of care)
       └─ Collar button → DORSAL + VOLAR longitudinal incisions, avoid web skin ± Penrose drain
```

### Collar-button abscess drainage

**Incisions** — **two (dorsal + palmar)**, **avoid web space** → prevents **scar contracture & loss of abduction**

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — Brunner incisions) · incision across web space · oral antibiotics alone

![Dorsal + volar incisions with Penrose drain](../../MCQ/attachments/q210196-illa-screen-shot-2018-05-23-at-8-54-23-pm.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Child, palmar abrasion, web space swelling, digits abducted — incisions?"** → **dorsal + palmar, avoiding web**\
✗ Wrong options: Brunner · web incision

**"Ulnar bursa does NOT communicate with"** → **4th finger sheath** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 5th sheath, Parona, radial bursa

**"Most commonly infected deep space"** → **thenar**

**"Pain with middle/ring/small flexion + loss of concavity"** → **midpalmar space**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Digital Artery Aneurysm

*Hand · 21 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — permanent dilation with **50% ↑ in normal diameter**

**Incidence** — rare; **<1 / 100,000**

| Type | Cause | Wall | Shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| **True aneurysm** | **Blunt** trauma | Weakened arterial wall | **Uniform** |
| **False (pseudoaneurysm)** | **Penetrating** trauma | Organised haematoma + fibrous wall | **Sac-like** |
| Non-traumatic | Inflammatory, atherosclerotic | — | — |

**Palmar arch pseudoaneurysm** — after laceration or **endoscopic CTR**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — slow-growing **painful mass** after hand trauma; **paraesthesia** (digital nerve compression)

**Pulsatile** — ~**50%**

**Digit** — **thumb > index > ring**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — usually normal; only if destructive lesion suspected

**Doppler US / CT angiography** — pre-op confirmation: size, location, **thrombus**, **collateral circulation**

**MRA** — false aneurysm of superficial palmar arch

![MRA: false aneurysm of superficial palmar arch](../../MCQ/attachments/q219-a-coronalmra.jpg)

**Differentials** — AV fistula, neurilemmoma, foreign body granuloma, ganglion, epidermoid cyst

## 5 Management

```
Digital / palmar artery aneurysm
├─ Small, asymptomatic → OBSERVATION + analgesia
├─ Symptomatic + adequate collaterals → EXPLORATION + LIGATION (prox & distal) / excision
└─ Symptomatic + inadequate collaterals → EXCISION + INTERPOSITION GRAFT
```

**Thrombin injection (US-guided)** — reported for more proximal hand/wrist lesions

**Repair** — end-to-end anastomosis or **autogenous vein/arterial interposition graft**

**Not appropriate** — aspiration, compression bandage, steroid injection, elevation/observation (symptomatic false aneurysm)

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — digital ischaemia, chronic pain

## How it's asked

**"Pulsatile palmar mass + paraesthesia after laceration, MRA shown — treatment?"** → **surgical exploration**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration, compression, steroid, observation

**"Blunt vs penetrating"** → **blunt = true (uniform) · penetrating = false (sac-like)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Digital Collateral Ligament Injury

*Hand · 22 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — rare, <1 per 100,000/yr

**Anatomy** — collaterals on lateral aspect of **DIP, PIP, MCP**; key to **pinch stability**

**Index RCL** — **critical for pinch**; deficient → MCPJ deviates **ulnarly** on pinch; 5.8 mm wide × 6.4 mm long

## 2 Mechanism

**Jammed finger** — dorsal/volar dislocation tears one or both collaterals; direct blow

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — joint pain, swelling, deformity; **pinch instability** after pain settles

**Exam** — varus/valgus stress; **endpoint** decides grade

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — PA, lateral, oblique ± **varus/valgus stress views**

**MRI** — if exam equivocal

![MRI collateral ligament](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6039-mri-collateral-lig.jpg)

### Index RCL grading

| Grade | Exam | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| I | Tender, stable | Immobilise **4–6 wk** |
| II | Laxity, **definite endpoint** | Immobilise **4–6 wk** → excellent |
| **III** | **No endpoint** = unstable | Early (<4 wk): **repair** (or strict immobilisation); late: **reconstruction / fusion** |

## 5 Management

```
Digital collateral injury
├─ Simple/partial tear → buddy tape 3 wk
├─ Complete tear (non-index-RCL) → buddy tape 6 wk
└─ Index MCP RCL, unstable → surgery
       ├─ early (<4 wk) → PRIMARY REPAIR
       └─ late → reconstruction / fusion (arthritic)
```

### Index MCP radial collateral ligament – repair

**Unstable index RCL** — **best outcome = early surgical repair**

**Traps** — RCL not key to pinch · ✗ Wrong options: stable → surgery (immobilise instead) · early vs late equivocal (late = poor) · early **reconstruction** (reconstruction = late, irreparable)

**Late presentation** — universally poor; chronic instability → OA

### Bowler's thumb (ulnar digital nerve neuropathy)

**Bowler's thumb (TO)** — **neuropathy of ulnar digital nerve of thumb** from friction of ball

**Pathology (TO)** — **perineural fibrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: true neuroma

**Presentation (TO)** — pain/tenderness **1st web space**, numbness/paraesthesia along medial thumb, tender small mass, hyperaesthesia

**Treatment (TO)** — conservative: rest, pressure relief, avoid activity; refractory → **neurolysis, neurectomy, transposition**

**Not (TO)** — thumb UCL injury · mallet thumb/index · 1st MC neck fracture

## How it's asked

**"Index MCP, no endpoint on ulnar stress, weak pinch — true?"** → **early surgical repair best**

**"Stable collateral injury"** → **immobilise / buddy tape**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery

**"Bowler's thumb is"** → **ulnar digital nerve neuropathy of thumb** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# DIP and PIP Joint Arthritis

*Hand · 23 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest hand OA** — **DIP > thumb CMC > PIP > MCP**

**DIP** — **highest joint forces in hand**; ≥60% of >60-y-olds show DIP OA

**PIP OA prevalence** — ~**17% ♀ · 13% ♂**

### Forms of arthritis

| Form | Key features |
|---|---|
| Primary OA – DIP | **Heberden nodes** (osteophytes), **mucous cysts**, nail ridging/splitting/deformity/loss of gloss |
| Primary OA – PIP | **Bouchard nodes**; contracture from ligament fibrosis |
| **Erosive OA** | DIP > PIP; **middle-aged ♀, 10:1**; intermittent inflammatory episodes; RA-like synovium but **not systemic**; self-limiting yet destructive |

![Heberden (DIP) & Bouchard (PIP) nodes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6055-herberden-nodes-and-bouchard-nodes.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Primary OA** — pain, deformity; PIP → **flexion contracture**, ↓ motion, swelling

**Mucous cyst** — dorsal DIP mass, proximal to nail fold; nail ridging/deformity; → draining sinus → **septic arthritis**

![Mucous cyst with nail ridging](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6055-mucous-cyst-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique hand

**OA** — joint space narrowing DIP/PIP, **dorsal osteophytes** (under mucous cyst)

**Erosive OA** — cartilage destruction, osteophytes, subchondral erosion → **"gull wing" deformity**

**Diagnosis** — history + exam + radiographs

## 5 Management

```
DIP / PIP arthritis
├─ Mild symptoms → observation + NSAIDs (erosive: splints)
├─ DIP debilitating pain / deformity → DIP FUSION (headless screw)
└─ PIP end-stage
       ├─ Index / small, young laborer, deformity, instability, poor bone → PIP FUSION
       └─ Long / ring, older, good bone, no deformity → SILICONE ARTHROPLASTY (volar)
```

### Mucous cyst – excision with osteophyte removal

**First line** — observation (**20–60% resolve**) (Orthobullets) · rarely resolve, usually progress → nail deformity (OB)

**Aspiration ± steroid** — **>40% recurrence**; 60% complete resolution (Rizzo)

**Surgery** — **excision of cyst + osteophyte (bone spur) removal** → **0% recurrence**, 100% resolution; key = **osteophytes MUST be debrided**

**Surgery indications** — significant pain, impending rupture, **repeated drainage**

**Not needed** — skin excision/graft (lesion independent of skin; flap controversial), nail plate removal, collateral/extensor resection, steroid injection, infectious workup

**DIP fusion instead** — **pain with forcible joint movement** + advanced arthritis

![Mucous cyst over DIP spur](../../MCQ/attachments/q711-b-spur.jpg)

### DIP arthrodesis

**Indication** — debilitating pain/deformity; **DIP OA of lesser digits** (e.g. ring DIP marked narrowing) → arthrodesis

**Headless compression screw** — **highest fusion rate**; nonunion 10% (Orthobullets) · union 95% (Kocak), 94–97% (Cox, Matsumoto)

| Digit | DIP fusion position |
|---|---|
| Index, long | **Extension** |
| Ring, small | **10–20° flexion** |

### DIP arthrodesis complication – screw length

| Screw error | Result |
|---|---|
| **Too long** | Prominent tip → **fingertip hypersensitivity** |
| Too wide | **Fracture, nailbed deformity** |
| Headless | ↓ prominence ✓ |
| Cannulated vs not | No difference |

### PIP arthroplasty (silicone, pyrocarbon) vs arthrodesis – indications & outcomes

| PIP option | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Collateral excision + volar plate release + osteophyte excision** | **Predominant contracture, minimal/no joint arthritis** |
| **Arthrodesis** | **Border digits (index, small)**; long/ring with **angulation/rotation, ligament instability, poor bone stock**; **young laborer** |
| **Silicone arthroplasty** | **Long & ring**, good bone stock, no deformity, older |
| Pyrocarbon arthroplasty | Needs **intact collaterals + volar plate** (unconstrained) |
| Resection arthroplasty | Recurrent infection, bone loss |
| Ray resection | Salvage only |
| Vascularised toe PIP transfer | Young; not in clotting disorder (factor V Leiden) |

**Index PIP** — relative CI to arthroplasty (**ulnar-deviating forces in key pinch**); arthroplasty → 4.3× complications, no ROM/pain gain vs fusion (Vitale)

**Long finger** — may also be a relative CI (Milone)

**Young + post-traumatic + rotation** — **arthrodesis** (rotation = collateral insufficiency)

**Silicone & collaterals (conflict)** — RCL should be intact for pinch (Orthobullets) · linked silicone **not dependent** on collaterals/volar plate → choice for **collateral-deficient ring PIP** (OB)

**Silicone outcomes** — **reliable pain relief (98%)**; **no significant ↑ ROM, grip, DASH**; arc ~55°; 60% deformation, 40% failure (Bales); **<60 y → ~25% revision**

**Approach + implant** — **silicone via volar approach → best ROM, least extensor lag, lowest revision (6% vs 18%** surface/dorsal; = 33%); **surface (metal-on-plastic) → highest revision**; dorsal = historic gold standard

**Pyrocarbon PIP** — **high complication rate** → rarely used; **↓ arc (57→31°)**; squeaking, loosening, dislocation, contracture; 34% reoperation, 51% subsidence, 36% unstable (Wagner)

**Pyrocarbon MCP** — **low complications**, mild ↑ arc (62→76°), pain relief; squeaking reported in both MCP & PIP

**PIP fusion technique** — headless screw (highest union), tension band, or plate

| PIP fusion angle | Value |
|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **Index 30° · long 35° · ring 40° · small 45°** |
| OB (radial → ulnar) | **40–55°** increasing |
| Arauz (index) | **0–20°** → natural pinch posture |

![Post-traumatic index PIP OA](../../MCQ/attachments/q211435-a-pipj.jpg)

### Erosive OA

**Treatment** — splints + NSAIDs (tolerable) → **fusion** for intolerable deformity (same angles)

## X vs Y

| | **Silicone PIP** | **Pyrocarbon PIP** | **Surface (metal-plastic)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Constraint | Linked, single piece | **Unconstrained** | — |
| Needs collaterals | No (OB) | **Yes** | — |
| Complications | Low | **High** | **Highest revision** |
| ROM | No significant gain | **↓** | Worse than volar silicone |

| Thumb / digit scenario (OB218081) | Best surgery |
|---|---|
| **Ring DIP marked narrowing** | **Arthrodesis** |
| Thumb CMC stage II | Trapeziectomy ± LRTI |
| Thumb CMC hypermobile, 14° hyperextension, mild OA | **Metacarpal osteotomy** |
| Index PIP contracture, no OA | Collateral excision / volar plate release |
| Ring PIP OA, stable | Silicone arthroplasty |

## How it's asked

**"Tender dorsal DIP mass + spur — best treatment?"** → **remove bone spur + cyst**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration >40% recurrence · fusion

**"Advantage of excision + debridement over aspiration?"** → **↓ recurrence**

**"Important component treating mucous cyst?"** → **excise DIP osteophytes**\
✗ Wrong options: skin · nail · collateral

**"Repeated drainage from DIP lesion — surgery?"** → **mass excision + osteophyte removal**

**"Middle finger PIP arthroplasty — approach/implant?"** → **silicone via volar: best ROM + lowest revision**

**"28-y index PIP post-traumatic OA + rotation?"** → **PIP arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: pyrocarbon (collaterals deficient) · toe transfer (factor V Leiden)

**"True about pyrocarbon arthroplasty?"** → **PIPJ: high complication rate**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ ROM · no squeaking

**"Best treated with arthrodesis?"** → **ring DIP OA with marked narrowing**

**"Best for PIP arthrodesis?"** → **44-y laborer, index PIP OA**

**"True of silicone PIP arthroplasty?"** → **no significant ↑ ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ grip/DASH · dorsal better

**"Fingertip hypersensitivity after DIP fusion?"** → **excessive screw length**\
✗ Wrong options: diameter → fracture/nailbed

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Dog and Cat Bites

*Hand · 24 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **dog (90%) > cat (10%) > human**; dog known to victim 90%

**Demographics** — ♂; **2–19 y**; **upper > lower extremity**

**Serious / fatal** — large aggressive dogs, small children, head & neck bites

### Mechanism by animal

| | **Dog** | **Cat** |
|---|---|---|
| Teeth | Blunt, powerful (>450 lb/in²) | **Small, sharp** → punctures **seal immediately** |
| Injury | Crush, avulsion, tear → **structural damage** (nerve, vessel, joint) | Penetrate **joints, bone, flexor sheath** → septic arthritis, OM |
| Infection risk | Lower | **Higher** |

### Pasteurella multocida & microbiology

**Polymicrobial** — most, with **>1 anaerobe**; median 5 isolates/culture

**Most common isolate (dog & cat)** — **Pasteurella** (gram-neg facultative anaerobic pleomorphic coccobacillus)

| | Dog | Cat |
|---|---|---|
| Pasteurella share | **50%** | **70–80%** (Orthobullets) · **75%** (OB) |
| Species | ***P. canis*** | ***P. multocida***, *P. septica* |
| Others | S. aureus, α-strep, Corynebacterium, Bacteroides, Moraxella, Neisseria | Similar |

**Pasteurella culture** — tell lab; special media; **~1 wk** to grow

**Cat Pasteurella** — intense pain + swelling within **48 h**

**Capnocytophaga canimorsus (dog)** — rare; fatal in **splenectomy/immunocompromised** (30–60% mortality); sepsis, endocarditis, meningitis, DIC

**Rabies** — rhabdovirus; dogs, raccoons, **bats**, foxes

### Infection risk factors

**Wound** — hand, foot, major joint; puncture or crush

**Host** — **delay >12 h**, **age >50**, steroids, **asplenia**, alcoholism, DM, vascular disease, oedema

## 3 Presentation

**History** — animal type, time since bite, comorbidities

**Exam** — puncture depth, crush, NV status, **joint penetration**; photograph wounds

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray indications** — crush, suspected fracture, **foreign body** (tooth)

**MRI** — not for flexor tenosynovitis (clinical Dx, delays treatment)

**Culture** — only if infected; **deep aerobic + anaerobic**\
✗ Wrong options: wound swab → skin flora

## 5 Management

```
Dog / cat bite
├─ All → irrigation (>150 mL saline, 18-19G) + tetanus ± rabies Ig/vaccine
├─ Cat / hand / deep / >8 h / immunocompromised / DM → AMOXICILLIN-CLAVULANATE
└─ Abscess / tenosynovitis / septic joint / FB / devitalised tissue → FORMAL I&D
```

**Antibiotics** — **amoxicillin/clavulanate** (covers P. multocida); alternatives cefuroxime, ceftriaxone

**Rabies prophylaxis** — any suspicion (unprovoked attack, bizarre behaviour) → **HDCV + human rabies Ig**; povidone-iodine irrigation if high risk

**Immobilise + elevate**

### Operative criteria

| Formal surgical debridement if |
|---|
| Crush / devitalised tissue, foreign body |
| Bites to **pulp, nail bed, flexor sheath, deep palmar spaces, joints** |
| **Tenosynovitis**, **septic arthritis**, abscess |

### Cat bite flexor tenosynovitis – drainage

**Cat bite + FTS (3 days, fever)** — **open I&D of flexor sheath** ± drain + pathogen-directed Abx

**Early / mild FTS** — catheter irrigation: incision **proximal to A1 + distal to A4**, 18G angiocatheter

**Severe / >1 wk / necrosis** — open debridement (mid-axial or Brunner) → amputation

**Nonop (splint, elevation, IV Abx)** — only if **<48 h** → I&D if no improvement in 12–24 h; **cat bites often need surgery**

**Avoid radial bursa irrigation for long finger** — long finger sheath **does not connect** to radial bursa

**Outcome** — stiffness possible despite prompt treatment

![Cat bite FTS, long finger](../../MCQ/attachments/q210115-a-screen-shot-2018-04-10-at-8-54-14-pm.jpg)

### Cat-scratch disease (Bartonella) – epitrochlear lymphadenitis

**Organism** — ***Bartonella henselae***

**Course** — self-limited **6–12 wk** untreated; papule/pustule at inoculation (25–60%) 3–10 d → lymphadenopathy 1–2 wk later

**Nodes** — **epitrochlear**, axillary, cervical, inguinal; tender, may suppurate → **soft-tissue tumour simulator** (medial elbow mass)

**Histology** — **lymphoid tissue + multiple necrotising granulomas** (only other granulomatous option = M. avium)

**Labs/imaging** — ↑ ESR/CRP; MRI mass + oedema in lymphatic drainage area; serum Bartonella test

**Diagnostic criteria (3 of 4)** — cat contact · positive CSD skin test · characteristic node lesions · negative workup for other lymphadenopathy

**Treatment** — **antibiotics** (azithromycin, ciprofloxacin, doxycycline)\
✗ Wrong options: chemo/radiation · observation

## X vs Y

| Organism | Association |
|---|---|
| **Pasteurella canis** | Dog bite |
| **Pasteurella multocida** | Cat bite |
| **Capnocytophaga** | Dog bite, asplenic → sepsis |
| **Bartonella henselae** | Cat scratch, epitrochlear node, granulomas |
| Borrelia burgdorferi | Lyme |
| Mycobacterium avium | Immunocompromised / HIV |

## How it's asked

**"Most common organism, dog & cat bites?"** → **Pasteurella**\
✗ Wrong options: Strep, Staph, Bacteroides, Moraxella

**"Cat bite 3 days ago, Kanavel signs, fever — next?"** → **open I&D of flexor sheath**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · swab · radial bursa catheter

**"9-y medial elbow mass, biopsy granulomas — next?"** → **antibiotics** (cat-scratch disease)

**"Epitrochlear mass, necrotising granulomas — organism?"** → **Bartonella henselae**

**"Antibiotic of choice for bite?"** → **amoxicillin-clavulanate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Dupuytren's Disease

*Hand · 25 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Who** — **white ♂ of northern European (Scandinavian/Celtic) descent**, 40–60 y; ♂ earlier & more severe

**Sex ratio** — **2:1** (Orthobullets) · **10:1** (TO sources) — read the options

**Genetics** — **autosomal dominant, variable penetrance**; sporadic commoner

**Digits** — **ring > small > middle > index**; joints **MCP > PIP > DIP**

**Bilateral** — **~45%** but **rarely symmetrical**

**TO EXCEPT** — "**index & middle most common, asymmetrical**" = false

**Risk factors** — **DM, alcohol (cirrhosis), smoking**, antiepileptics/epilepsy, HIV, TB, COPD

**NOT a risk factor (TO)** — **occupation with vibrating tools/earth movers** (not confirmed)

**Ectopic** — **Garrod knuckle pads** (dorsal PIP, 40–50%) · **Ledderhose** (plantar, 10–30%) · **Peyronie** (penis, 2–8%)

**Shoulder link (TO)** — adhesive capsulitis associated with DM, thyroid, **Dupuytren**

### Pathogenesis & diathesis (Garrod's pads)

### Genes & molecular markers

| Marker | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| **Inheritance** | **Autosomal dominant, variable penetrance** (sporadic commoner) |
| **Cell** | **Myofibroblast** — intracellular **α-actin**; linked by extracellular **fibronectin** |
| **Collagen** | **↑ type III : I**; nodules = **type III** (TO); residual cord = type I |
| **Cytokines** | **TGF-β1** (↓ myofibroblast apoptosis), β2, **bFGF, PDGF**, EGF, CTGF |
| Not involved | **CBFA-1** (osteoblast transcription factor) |
| CCH target | Lyses **I & III**; spares **IV** (vessel/nerve basement membrane) |

**Cell** — **myofibroblast** (intracellular **actin** along long axis; linked by extracellular **fibronectin**)

**Collagen** — **↑ type III : type I** ratio; **nodules deposit type III** (TO)

**Cytokines** — **TGF-β1** (also blocks myofibroblast apoptosis), bFGF, PDGF, EGF, CTGF

**NOT implicated** — **CBFA-1** (osteoblast transcription factor)

**Diathesis** — **age <50, ♂ white, bilateral, family history, ectopic (Garrod, Ledderhose, Peyronie)** → aggressive & recurrent

**Bad prognostic factors (TO)** — **family history, ♂, DM, smoking, alcohol, bilateral, knuckle pads, plantar nodules**

**NOT a bad prognostic factor (TO)** — **epilepsy** (not associated per Geoghegan)

**Garrod pads alone + mild contracture (MCP 5°)** — **observe**; **do not resect dorsal pads**

![Garrod knuckle pads](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6058-garrods.jpg)

### Cord anatomy – spiral cord, neurovascular displacement & natatory ligament

**Rule** — **bands = normal, cords = diseased**

| Cord | From | Contracture | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Pretendinous** | Pretendinous band | **MCP** | Palmar pits (superficial fibres) |
| **Central** | Distal pretendinous; inserts flexor sheath at PIP | **MCP** (± PIP) | Midline; **does not displace NV bundle** |
| **Spiral** | **Pretendinous band + spiral band + lateral digital sheet + Grayson lig** | **PIP** (TO) | **Displaces NVB central & superficial** |
| **Lateral** | Lateral digital sheet | PIP / DIP | — |
| **Retrovascular** | Dorsal to NVB; P1 → P3 | **DIP** (± hyperextension with lateral cord) | Displaces nerve palmar & toward midline |
| **Natatory** | Natatory lig | **Web space** | — |
| Isolated digital | — | PIP | — |

**Spared (TO)** — **Cleland ligament** · **transverse lig of palmar aponeurosis**

**Spiral cord position** — runs **dorsal to NVB proximally, volar distally**; cord **lateral & deep** to NVB → NVB pulled **central & superficial** → **at risk**

**Spiral cord predictors** — **PIP flexion contracture (PPV 77%)** · **interdigital soft-tissue mass (71%)** · **nodule distal to MCP**

**Ring finger radial digital nerve** — displaced **palmarly & ulnarly** (toward midline)

![Spiral cord components](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6058-contents-of-the-spiral-cord.jpg)

![Digital fascia (McFarlane)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6058-digital-fascia-mcfarlane-moved.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

| Luck stage | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Proliferative** | **Nodule**; hypercellular, large myofibroblasts, vascular, gap junctions; minimal matrix |
| **Involutional** | Myofibroblasts align along **tension lines**; ↑ type III collagen → contraction |
| **Residual** | **Acellular**; fibrocytes; dense collagen cord (type I predominant) |

**Surgery in proliferative stage** — ↑ stiffness & recurrence

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **painless** nodule at **distal palmar crease** → cord → fixed flexion MCP/PIP

**Exam** — palmar pits, skin blanching on extension, **Hueston tabletop test** + (can't lay palm flat)

**Nodule beyond MCP** — clue to **spiral cord** (nerve midline & superficial)

![Hueston tabletop test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6058-tabletop.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — not needed; only to exclude arthritis/bony cause of stiffness

**Ultrasound** — not needed; shows **thickened palmar fascia + nodules**

| Differential | Distinguishing clue |
|---|---|
| Locked trigger finger | **Painful, tender A1** |
| Pulley rupture | Bowstringing, trauma |
| Ulnar claw | MCP **hyperextension** + IP flexion, ulnar sensory loss |
| Volkmann | Post-compartment syndrome; wrist flexion, thumb adduction, **MCP extension**, IP flexion |

## 5 Management

```
Dupuytren
├─ Nodule only / no function loss / painful nodule → observe (± steroid for painful nodule)
├─ Palpable cord, MCP-predominant, wants to avoid surgery → CCH or needle aponeurotomy (equal at 1 y)
└─ MCP >30° or ANY PIP contracture (>15°), recurrence, wants lowest recurrence → partial fasciectomy
       └─ recurrent / diathesis / skin involved → dermofasciectomy + FTSG
```

### Operative criteria

| Treatment | Indication |
|---|---|
| Observation | Nodules only, no functional loss |
| Steroid injection | **Painful nodule** (✗ Wrong options: surgery for painful nodule) |
| **CCH** | Adult with **palpable cord**, MCP-predominant; max 2 cords/visit |
| **PNA** | Mild MCP contracture, **medically unfit / elderly** |
| **Partial fasciectomy** | **MCP ≥30°** · **any PIP (>15°)** · +ve tabletop · wants lowest recurrence |
| Total fasciectomy | Rare: diffuse, multiple joints, **recurrence** |
| **Dermofasciectomy + FTSG** | **Recurrent / diathesis** / skin involved |
| Arthrodesis / amputation | Salvage — severe small-finger PIP contracture |

**Surgical indication** — **MCP ≥30°** · **PIP any (>15°)** · positive tabletop

**NOT an indication (TO)** — **painful nodule**

**NOT a treatment (TO)** — **Hyalase injection** · **high-dose radiotherapy** (only **low dose <30 Gy** for early disease)

**Steroid injection** — painful nodule; recurrence 50%; fat atrophy, depigmentation, tendon rupture

### Collagenase & needle fasciotomy – mechanism, complications & outcomes

**CCH (Xiaflex)** — metalloprotease from *Clostridium histolyticum*; lyses **type I & III**

**Why NV safe** — **low activity vs type IV** (basement membrane of vessels/nerves)

**Technique** — 0.58 mg per cord; **max 2 cords/joints per visit**; manipulate 24–72 h; up to 3 injections/cord at 4-wk intervals

**Manipulation at day 1 vs day 7** — **equivalent correction** (pain, skin tears same)

**Meals & Hentz protocol (TO)** — 4-step manipulation after CCH: flex PIP + extend MCP → flex MCP + extend PIP → extend both → press residual cord; max 3 attempts; stop if skin tears; night splint up to 4 mo

**CCH-only side effects** — **pruritic rash, axillary lymphadenopathy**

**CCH major (1%)** — **flexor tendon rupture**, CRPS, pulley rupture

**Both CCH & PNA** — **skin tears** (from manipulation; ↑ with age & degree of correction)
