**CCH vs PNA** — **outcomes equal at 1 y** (90% full MCP extension); CCH costlier ($5,952 vs $1,540) & not fewer treatments

| Reintervention | PNA | CCH |
|---|---|---|
| 2 y | 24% | **41%** |
| 5 y | **61%** | 55% |

**Both better at MCP than PIP**; **PIP recurrence more severe**

**PNA** — office, 22–25 G needle; elderly/comorbid; risk **nerve/tendon injury**; recurrence up to 58%

### Fasciectomy – indications, technique & complications

**Partial (selective) fasciectomy** — **gold standard**; removes diseased tissue only; **lowest recurrence**; best for **severe PIP**

**Incisions** — Brunner zig-zag, V-Y, **sequential Z-plasty → allows skin closure**\
✗ Wrong options: recurrence

**McCash open palm** — transverse DPC wound left open → **↓ haematoma, ↓ stiffness**; slower healing

**Total/radical fasciectomy** — rarely; diffuse/recurrent; high complications, no ↓ recurrence

**Dermofasciectomy + FTSG** — recurrent/diathesis; **recurrence rare under graft**; grafts take even over NVB

**Dermofasciectomy vs fasciectomy** — **no proven difference** in ROM/recurrence/satisfaction

**Skoog (TO)** — selective fasciectomy of **pretendinous fibres only**

**Luck (TO)** — **subcutaneous fasciotomy** (3 puncture wounds; elderly/unfit)

**Staged** — mini-ex-fix across PIP 6 wk → fasciectomy

**Amputation** — severe small finger PIP contracture

**Do CTR on separate occasion** (TO)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / key fact |
|---|---|
| **Wound (dehiscence, necrosis, skin tear)** | **23% — commonest after fasciectomy** |
| Scar pain | 17% |
| Infection | **Fasciectomy 7% · PNA 1% · CCH 0%** |
| CRPS | 5.8%; ♀ |
| Flare reaction | 3.5%; ↓ by delaying splint to 1st visit |
| NV injury | **2–3% primary · 20% recurrent**; **spiral cord**; PNA |
| Haematoma | 2%; ↓ with McCash |
| CCH skin tear | 12% |

| Recurrence | Rate |
|---|---|
| **CCH** | ~50% at 5 y (35% in some stems) |
| **PNA** | ~50% at 3 y |
| **Fasciectomy** | **3.5–20%** at 4–5 y (long-term up to 50% TO) |

**Recurrence risk factors** — **PIP disease**, small finger, **diathesis**, PNA/CCH > fasciectomy

**NOT a recurrence risk factor (TO)** — **MCP joint contracture**

**PIP secondary structures** — volar plate, accessory collaterals, flexor sheath → may need release

## X vs Y

| | **CCH** | **Needle aponeurotomy** | **Partial fasciectomy** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Best for | MCP, palpable cord | MCP, elderly/unfit | **PIP, severe, recurrence-averse** |
| Recurrence | ~50% 5 y | ~50% 3 y | **Lowest** |
| Unique complication | **Rash, axillary nodes**, tendon rupture | Nerve/tendon injury | **Wound complications 23%** |
| Infection | **0%** | 1% | 7% |
| Cost | **Highest** | Lowest | — |

| Cord | Joint |
|---|---|
| Central / pretendinous | **MCP** |
| **Spiral** | **PIP** |
| Retrovascular | **DIP** |
| Natatory | **Web space** |

## How it's asked

**"Cause of PIP contracture?"** → **spiral cord** (TO)

**"Natatory involvement?"** → **web space contracture**

**"Structure spared?"** → **Cleland ligament** (TO)

**"Spiral cord relative to NVB?"** → **lateral & deep** (NVB pushed central & superficial)

**"Collagen in nodules?"** → **type III** (TO)

**"CCH spares NV because?"** → **type IV**

**"CCH degrades?"** → **I & III**

**"Not implicated in pathogenesis?"** → **CBFA-1**

**"Not a bad prognostic factor?"** → **epilepsy** (TO)

**"Not a recurrence risk factor?"** → **MCP contracture** (TO)

**"Not a risk factor?"** → **vibrating tools occupation** (TO)

**"Not true"** → **painful nodules = indication for surgery** (TO) · **high-dose radiotherapy** (TO) · **index/middle most common** (TO)

**"Not a treatment"** → **Hyalase** (TO)

**"Meals & Hentz protocol"** → Dupuytren (CCH manipulation)

**"Skoog & Luck"** → Dupuytren (TO)

**"Garrod pads + 5° MCP"** → **observe**

**"CCH manipulation delayed 1 wk"** → **equivalent correction**

**"CCH vs PNA at 1 y"** → **equivalent**

**"Only after CCH"** → **rash + axillary lymphadenopathy**

**"Recurrence after PNA, MCP 35° + PIP 40°, lowest recurrence — commonest complication?"** → **wound dehiscence/skin necrosis**

**"Finding favouring fasciectomy over CCH"** → **degree of PIP contracture**

**"Z-plasty helps with"** → **skin closure**

**"Dermofasciectomy better for?"** → **none — no proven difference**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Epidermal Inclusion Cyst

*Hand · 26 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Nature** — painless, **benign, slow-growing** soft-tissue tumour; months–years after trauma

**Rank** — **3rd most common hand tumour**

**Demographics** — **♂ > ♀**; **3rd–4th decade**

**Site** — **distal phalanx / fingertip**

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathogenesis** — **penetrating injury** drives **keratinising epithelium** into subcutis or bone → **epithelium-lined cyst filled with keratin**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **painless fingertip mass**; erythematous painful lesions rare

**Exam** — flesh/yellow/white; **well-circumscribed, firm, slightly mobile**; superficial, tethered to skin

**vs ganglion** — **firmer**, **does not transilluminate**

**Large lesion near IP joint** — ↓ ROM

**Digital nerve compression** — ↓ 2-point discrimination

![Fingertip epidermal inclusion cyst](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6087-epidermal-inclusion-cyst-clinical-photograph.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique of digit/hand

**X-ray** — soft-tissue mass; **lytic distal phalanx lesion** if erodes bone → mimics malignancy/infection

**Biopsy** — before excision **if lytic distal phalanx lesion** (exclude neoplasm/infection)

![Intraosseous epidermal inclusion cyst, distal phalanx](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6087-interosseous-epidermal-inclusion-cyst.jpg)

### Histology

**Gross** — thick **white keratinous** material

**Micro** — **keratin-filled cyst lined by epithelial cells**

**Differentials** — tophaceous gout, foreign-body granuloma, sebaceous cyst, **pyogenic granuloma**, GCT tendon sheath, ganglion, enchondroma, glomus

## 5 Management

**Observation** — **not recommended**

**Marginal excision** — **remove entire capsule** → **curative**, low recurrence

**Bone erosion** — curettage + bone graft; amputation rarely (advanced destruction)

**Indications** — diagnosis, pain, loss of function, cosmesis

**Malignant transformation** — **never reported**

## 6 Complications

**List** — wound problems, infection, digital neurapraxia, recurrence (**low even with bone involvement**)

## X vs Y

| | **Epidermal inclusion cyst** | **Pyogenic granuloma** | Glomus tumour | GCT tendon sheath | Enchondroma |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Growth | **Slow**, after penetrating injury | **Rapid**, after repeated trauma | Rare, benign | Benign nodular | Intramedullary |
| Look | Flesh/white, firm, tethered | **"Beefy red", bleeds on bumping**, ≤2 cm | Subungual | Nodule on tendon sheath | Bone lesion |
| Histology | Keratin, epithelial lining | **Collarette around lobules of endothelial cells** | Glomus body | — | Chondrogenic |
| Treatment | Marginal excision | **Excision + silver nitrate cautery** (office); 85% resolve, mean 1.6 Rx | — | — | — |

**Pyogenic granuloma** — benign **vascular** lesion; commonest **head & neck**; slight ♂; recurrence least with surgical excision

![Pyogenic granuloma of ring finger](../../MCQ/attachments/q211677-a-52a.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Rapid, bleeding vascular finger mass after repeated trauma, excise + silver nitrate?"** → **pyogenic granuloma**\
✗ Wrong options: epidermal inclusion cyst · GCT · glomus · enchondroma

**"Fingertip mass after penetrating injury, lytic distal phalanx?"** → **epidermal inclusion cyst → biopsy, then marginal excision**

**"Histology?"** → **keratin-filled cyst with epithelial lining**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Extensor Tendon Compartments

*Hand · 27 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Dorsal compartment contents

| Dorsal wrist compartment | Contents |
|---|---|
| **1** | **APL, EPB** |
| **2** | **ECRL, ECRB** |
| **3** | **EPL** only (✗ Wrong options: ECRL) |
| **4** | **EDC, EIP, PIN** (floor) |
| **5** | **EDM** |
| **6** | **ECU** |

![Six dorsal wrist compartments](../../MCQ/attachments/q3522-illa-extensor-cpts2.jpg)

**PIN** — **only nerve** in the dorsal compartments; consistently at **base (floor) of 4th**

**Septa** — 1/2 radial surface thickest; **3/4 septum thinnest**; **5th compartment lowest resistance to failure** (Iwamoto)

**Extensor retinaculum** — check rein against **bowstringing** → **repair** at surgery (Palmer)

**PIN ± AIN block** — PIN alone: **1 cm ulnar to proximal Lister's tubercle**; PIN + AIN single injection: 1 cm ulnar + 3 cm proximal to Lister's (Grutter, 100% accurate)

### Number of compartments (hand)

**Hand compartments (TO)** — **10**

| Hand compartment (TO) | Number |
|---|---|
| Dorsal interossei | 4 |
| Palmar (volar) interossei | 3 |
| Thenar + hypothenar | 2 |
| Adductor pollicis | 1 |
| **Total** | **10** |

**11th (TO)** — some authors add **mid-palmar** compartment

## How it's asked

**"Compartment incorrectly paired with contents?"** → **3rd: EPL + ECRL** (ECRL is in 2nd)

**"Only nerve in dorsal compartments?"** → **PIN, floor of 4th**

**"How many compartments in the hand?"** → **10** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Extensor Tendon Injuries

*Hand · 28 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & anatomy

**Commonest digit** — **long finger**; commonest zone **VI**

**Extensor mechanism** — EDC → **central slip** (PIP extension) + **2 lateral bands** (DIP extension)

**PIP flexion** — central slip tensed, lateral bands sublux volar + slacken → DIP lax

**Lateral band restraints (TO)** — **triangular ligament** prevents volar subluxation; retinacular ligaments limit dorsal

**Juncturae tendinum** — link adjacent EDC → partial/temporary extension despite zone V cut (masks injury)

![Juncturae tendinum](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6028-junctura.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

| Zone | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| I | **Forced flexion of extended DIP** |
| II | Dorsal laceration / crush |
| III | Hyperflexion, blunt/penetrating trauma, **volar PIP dislocation**, rotation of flexed PIP (condyle buttonholes through triangular lig.) (TO) |
| V | **Fight bite**; **sagittal band rupture ("flea flicker")** = forced extension of flexed digit, long finger |

## 3 Presentation

**Zone I** — can't extend DIP (mallet)

**Zone III / boutonnière (TO)** — **PIP flexed, DIP hyperextended, can't flex DIP**; may appear **10–14 d** after injury

**Zone V** — extensor lag; **radial sagittal band** (stronger) rupture → tendon subluxes **ulnar**, MCP held flexed, no active extension

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Zones of extensor tendon injury

| Zone (finger / thumb) | Level & key fact | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | DIP / thumb IP (EPL) → **mallet finger** | ![Bony mallet](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6028-xray-lateral-moved.jpg) |
| II | Middle phalanx / thumb P1 (EPL) | |
| **III** | **PIP (central slip)** / thumb MCP → **boutonnière** | ![Boutonnière](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6028-clinical-photo-surgery-missouri-moved.jpg) |
| IV | Proximal phalanx / thumb MC; **adhesions common** | |
| **V** | **MCP** / thumb CMC → **fight bite, sagittal band** | ![Sagittal band rupture MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6028-mri-hand-axial-t1-shows-sagital-band-rupture-moved.jpg) |
| **VI** | Metacarpal; **commonest**; nerve/vessel injury likely | |
| VII | Wrist → **repair retinaculum** (bowstringing); immobilise **wrist 40° ext + MCP 20° flex, 3–4 wk**; adhesions common | |
| VIII | Distal forearm; penetrating; nerve injury; immobilise **elbow flexion + wrist extension** | |

### Central slip tests

| Test | Method | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Elson** (most reliable, before deformity) | **PIP flexed 90° over table**, extend PIP against resistance | **DIP rigid** (normal = DIP **supple**) |
| Modified Elson (TO) | Both digits PIP fully flexed, middle phalanges back-to-back | Injured DIP extends better |
| **Boyes** (TO) | Extend PIP, actively flex DIP | **Can't flex DIP** — **late** sign |

### Tightness tests

| | **Intrinsic tightness** | **Extrinsic tightness** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **Crush → interossei/lumbrical fibrosis** | **Extensor adhesions** (e.g. MC fracture) |
| PIP passive flexion ↓ when | **MCP extended** (Bunnell) | **MCP flexed** |
| Improves with | MCP flexion | **MCP extension** |
| Treatment | Interosseous tenotomy | **Tenolysis / fractional extensor lengthening** ± MC osteotomy |

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + lateral digit → exclude **bony mallet** / avulsion

**MRI axial T1** — sagittal band rupture

## 5 Management

```
Extensor tendon injury
├─ Laceration <50% + can extend against resistance → PROTECTED EARLY MOTION
├─ Laceration >50% (any zone) → TENDON REPAIR
└─ Closed injury → SPLINT the joint of the zone
       ├─ Zone I mallet → DIP extension splint 6 wk
       ├─ Zone III central slip → PIP extension splint, DIP free
       └─ Zone V sagittal band → MCP extension splint 4-6 wk
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Laceration >50% width** | Repair |
| **Bony mallet + P3 volar subluxation** | CRPP through DIP / extension-block pinning; **ORIF if >50% articular surface** |
| **Fight bite to MCP** | **Immediate I&D**, loose/delayed closure; cover **Eikenella** |
| Chronic / irreparable | Reconstruction (2-stage) |
| **Chronic EPL rupture** | **EIP → EPL transfer** |

### Mallet (zone I) splinting

**DIP extension splint** — acute (<12 wk), nondisplaced bony mallet, chronic (>12 wk) if **supple + congruent**; **full-time 6 wk** + part-time 4–6 wk; avoid hyperextension (skin necrosis); keep PIP free; noncompliance common

### Central slip injury – Elson test & PIP extension splinting

**Acute closed central slip** — **PIP extension splint, DIP free to move (active DIP flexion)**; **6 wk** full-time + 4–6 wk part-time (Orthobullets, OB) · **5 wk** (OB) · **4–6 wk round the clock + nights** (TO)

**Why DIP motion** — prevents **oblique retinacular ligament contracture** / adhesions

✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — full-time DIP splint (mallet), MCP extension splint (sagittal band), **dorsal blocking splint** (dorsal PIP dislocation), immobilising DIP

**Surgery (TO)** — only complete rupture/transection/bony avulsion after failed conservative trial (Doyle, Snow, Aiche)

**Chronic boutonnière >6 wk (TO)** — stiff/arthritic/RA → **PIP arthrodesis**; supple → lateral band relocation, **terminal tendon tenotomy (Fowler/Dolphin, not central slip)**, modified Littler

**Central slip reconstruction** — graft, extensor turndown, lateral band mobilisation, TRL, FDS slip

**Pseudo-boutonnière (TO)** — PIP flexion contracture **without DIP hyperextension**; after **dorsal PIP dislocation / volar plate injury**; can't passively extend PIP


### EPL rupture – EIP transfer

**Drummer's palsy (TO)** — **EPL tendon**; chronic tendinitis from repetitive palmar flexion + ulnar deviation; rubs on **Lister's (dorsal) tubercle** → attenuation/rupture

**Drummer's treatment (TO)** — conservative / activity modification; rupture → **EI to EPL transfer**

**EPL rupture after DRF** — **nondisplaced/minimally displaced DRF**; ~**3 mo**; **nutritional compromise + attrition** → **EIP → EPL transfer** or graft\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair · EPB · observation

### ECU tendinitis/subluxation

**ECU (extensor carpi ulnaris)** — tennis forehand; **dorsoulnar pain** on **supination + wrist flexion + ulnar deviation**; X-ray normal

**Subluxation** — ECU retinaculum ruptures → tendon leaves sheath **in supination**, returns in pronation

**Treatment** — immobilisation; surgery if refractory

### Rheumatoid extensor ruptures (Vaughan-Jackson, EDM)

**Commonest RA tendon rupture (TO)** — **EDM / EDQ** (extensor); **FPL** (flexor)

**Vaughan-Jackson (TO)** — **EDQ first → EDC small → ring → middle → index**; **ulnar → radial**; final stage of **caput ulnae** (dorsal ulnar head subluxation)

**Mechanism (TO)** — attrition on raw dorsal ulnar head + invasive tenosynovitis + ischaemia

**EPL in RA (TO)** — ruptures at **Lister's tubercle** (tortuous course)

**Mannerfelt (TO)** — **FPL** rupture in carpal tunnel over **scaphoid / radial osteophyte**

**Clinical (TO)** — painless "drop finger"; no active MCP extension; passive extension holds (tenodesis) → distinguish from MCP dislocation, **radial nerve palsy**

**Bone work first (TO)** — **Darrach** (low-demand) · **Sauvé-Kapandji** (younger) + dorsal synovectomy; wrist stabilisation if severe radial deviation

| Ruptured tendons (TO) | Reconstruction |
|---|---|
| 1 (EDQ) | Side-to-side to **ring EDC** |
| 2 (EDQ + EDC small) | Side-to-side to **middle EDC** |
| 3 (small, ring, middle) | **EIP transfer** |
| 4 (small → index) | **FDS ring** or **ECRL** transfer |

**Post-op (TO)** — dynamic extension splint 4–6 wk

### Repair technique & reconstruction

**Strength** — **number of strands crossing repair** > grasping loops; 4–6 strands → early active motion; epitendinous optional

**Weakest** — **post-op day 6–12**; fails at **knots**

**Reconstruction** — 2-stage: **silicone rod → graft at 3–4 mo**; grafts: **palmaris longus** (commonest; absent 15%), plantaris (absent 19%, longer), toe extensor

**Tenolysis** — adhesions with ↓ flexion; wait **>3 mo** + full passive ROM; not with procedures needing immobilisation

### Extensor laceration rehabilitation – relative motion splint

**SAM (short-arc motion)** — after **zone III repair**; ↑ arc, ↑ DIP motion, ↓ therapy time; **4 mm excursion**

**Relative motion (yoke) splint** — injured MCP in **relative hyperextension**; **zones IV–VII**

**Yoke benefits** — early active ROM, ↓ adhesions, ↓ ADL interference, adjacent digits free, **high compliance**, earlier RTW; **NOT "better at limiting motion"**

![Yoke splint](../../MCQ/attachments/q210121-b-screen-shot-2019-02-19-at-6-38-51-pm.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Adhesions** | ↓ flexion; **zones IV & VII**, older pts → **tenolysis at 3–6 mo** |
| **Rupture** | **5%**; days **7–10**; revision or reconstruction |
| **Swan neck** | Prolonged DIP flexion, dorsal lateral bands, PIP hyperextension → **Fowler central slip tenotomy**, SORL reconstruction |
| **Boutonnière** | Central slip + volar lateral bands → splint/serial cast → **terminal extensor tenotomy**, PIP volar plate release |

## X vs Y

| | Boutonnière | Pseudo-boutonnière | Swan neck |
|---|---|---|---|
| PIP | Flexed | **Flexion contracture** | **Hyperextended** |
| DIP | **Hyperextended** | Normal / passive motion | Flexed |
| Cause | **Central slip** (volar PIP disloc.) | **Volar plate** (dorsal PIP disloc.) | Lateral bands dorsal |
| Tenotomy | **Terminal tendon** | — | **Central slip (Fowler)** |

## How it's asked

**"Drummer's palsy involves?"** → **EPL tendon** (TO)

**"Thumb won't extend 3 mo after nondisplaced DRF — next?"** → **EIP → EPL transfer**

**"Tennis forehand, dorsoulnar pain on supination/flexion/ulnar deviation?"** → **ECU tendon**

**"DIP hyperextended, can't flex DIP, PIP flexed — injury?"** → **EDC central slip** (TO)

**"Exam finding in traumatic boutonnière?"** → **PIP flexed, resisted extension → DIP rigid (Elson)**

**"Central slip nonop — which active motion?"** → **DIP flexion**

**"Positive Elson, initial plan?"** → **PIP splint 6 wk, DIP free**

**"Relative motion splint benefits EXCEPT?"** → **better at limiting digit motion**

**"↓ PIP flexion with MCP flexed, improves with MCP extension — Dx & Rx?"** → **extrinsic tightness → tenolysis / fractional lengthening**

**"Most common tendon rupture in RA hand?"** → **EDM** (TO)

**"Vaughan-Jackson first tendon & direction?"** → **EDQ, ulnar → radial** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Extremity Flap Reconstruction

*Hand · 29 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions & indications

**Flap** — tissue unit transferred **with its own vascular supply**

**Pedicle** — vascular portion; usually 1 artery + ≥1 veins

**Indication** — exposed **bone, tendon, cartilage, implant**\
✗ Wrong options: STSG/FTSG on exposed tendon/bone/prosthesis (no viable bed, poor gliding)

### Reconstructive ladder

| Rung (simple → complex) |
|---|
| Primary closure |
| Secondary closure / secondary intention (± NPWT) |
| Skin graft |
| **Local flap** |
| Regional / distant flap |
| Free tissue transfer |

**Rule** — choose the **lowest rung** that covers the defect (local before distant or free)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Blood supply classification

| Type | Supply | Indication |
|---|---|---|
| **Axial pattern** | Single named AV pedicle | Length:width **>2:1** |
| **Random pattern** | Microcirculation, no named pedicle | Length:width **<2:1** |
| Venous | Veins as arterial inflow + outflow | |

### Tissue type classification

| Type | Note |
|---|---|
| Cutaneous | Skin + subcut |
| Fascial | Fascia only (temporoparietal) |
| **Muscle** | Needs **skin graft** or musculocutaneous (composite); **no motor nerve → atrophy to 50%** |
| Bone | **Free fibula (peroneal a.) → diaphysis** · **free iliac crest (deep circumflex iliac) → metaphysis** |
| Composite | e.g. radial forearm (fasciocutaneous) |

### Mobilisation classification

| Type | Use |
|---|---|
| **Local** | Tissue **adjacent** to defect |
| Distal random | Non-contiguous site; surrounding tissue inadequate; ratio <2:1 |
| Distal axial | Surrounding tissue inadequate; ratio >2:1 |
| **Free** | Local/distant tissue insufficient |

### Mathes & Nahai muscle vascular classification (TO)

| Type | Supply | Examples | |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | **Single** major pedicle | **Gastrocnemius**, TFL, rectus femoris | ![Type I](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17137948-1.png) |
| II | **One dominant + minor** pedicles | **Soleus**, vastus lateralis, **gracilis**, peroneus brevis/longus | ![Type II](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17137948-2.png) |
| III | **Two dominant** pedicles (different regional arteries) | **Rectus abdominis**, gluteus maximus, serratus anterior, semimembranosus | ![Type III](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17137948-3.png) |
| IV | **Segmental**, multiple similar-calibre pedicles | **Sartorius**, tibialis anterior, EDL | ![Type IV](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17137948-4.png) |
| **V** | **Single dominant + multiple secondary segmental** | **Latissimus dorsi**, pectoralis major | ![Type V](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17137948-5.png) |

## 5 Management

```
Leg defect over tibia (by third)
├─ Proximal 1/3 / knee / TKA → gastrocnemius rotational flap
│      └─ medial (anteromedial, larger) or lateral (smaller)
├─ Middle 1/3 → soleus / hemisoleus flap
└─ Distal 1/3 → free flap (or reverse sural)
       └─ also proximal/middle if gastroc/soleus damaged
```

### Gastrocnemius flap – sural artery supply; knee & tibia coverage

**Pedicle** — **medial / lateral sural arteries** (branches of **popliteal**); single source 85%; **Mathes I**

**✗ Gastroc distractors** — genicular arteries · anterior/posterior tibial · peroneal · saphenous

**Medial head** — **medial + midline** defects, proximal 1/3 tibia; **preferred for anteromedial TKA wounds** (larger, more mobile)

**Lateral head** — **lateral** defects; smaller, less excursion; lateral sural artery

**Reach** — defects **up to 15 cm** from knee joint; fascial pie-crusting or proximal release (island) → **+5 cm**

**TKA wound necrosis / PJI with exposed prosthesis** — **gastrocnemius flap first**\
✗ Wrong options: latissimus/gracilis free (higher on ladder, donor morbidity) · STSG/FTSG

![Medial gastrocnemius flap](../../MCQ/attachments/q12-illa-medial-gastroc.jpg)

**Soleus supply** — PT (medial) + peroneal (proximal) + popliteal trunk branches (Orthobullets) · **peroneal proximally, PT distally** (OB) · **PT medially, peroneal distal/lateral** (OB) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: anterior tibial perforators

**Free flap** — distal 1/3 tibia; **gracilis = most common free muscle donor** (**medial femoral circumflex a.**, anterior division obturator n.)

**ALT free flap** — **lateral femoral circumflex a.**\
✗ Wrong options: popliteal

### Upper-extremity flaps – lateral arm, radial forearm, latissimus & groin flaps (pedicles)

| Flap | Pedicle | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral arm** | **Posterior radial collateral a.** (branch of **profunda brachii**), 3–5 septocutaneous perforators in lateral IM septum | Max width 8 cm, pedicle 8 cm; supine; primary donor closure; **posterior antebrachial cutaneous n. sacrificed** (forearm hypoaesthesia); protect **radial n.**; osteo/sensate/fascial variants |
| **Radial forearm** (rotational) | Radial a. | Local flap for dorsal hand/forearm when **PIA disrupted** by dorsal wound |
| Posterior interosseous | PIA (septum EDM–ECU) | not if dorsal wound disrupted septum |
| **Latissimus dorsi** myocutaneous | **Thoracodorsal a.** (branch of **subscapular**) | **Large defects around elbow**; Mathes V (TO) |
| **Groin** (axial, distant) | **Superficial circumflex iliac a.** | Mainstay UE coverage (thumb amputation); **LFCN at risk** (pierces iliac fascia medial to ASIS) |

**Radial collateral a.** — runs with radial n. in spiral groove → pierces lateral IM septum → anterior + **posterior** radial collateral (posterior to septum)

**✗ Lateral arm distractors** — brachial, recurrent interosseous, posterior ulnar recurrent, middle collateral, radial recurrent, anterior radial collateral

**Dorsal hand wound (MC + radius fx, exposed tendon/bone)** — **radial forearm rotational flap** (local) > groin (distant) > free lateral arm (free)

![Groin flap](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6059-groin-flap.jpg)

### Bone flaps – free fibula (peroneal artery)

**Free fibula** — **peroneal a.** (1.5–4 mm) + 2 venae; pedicle 4–6 cm; ± skin paddle; keep distal **5–6 cm** (syndesmosis); defects **>6 cm** (trauma, infection, congenital, AVN) → 10 cm radius defect

| Bone flap | Pedicle | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Free fibula** | **Peroneal** | Long diaphyseal defects |
| Free iliac crest | Deep circumflex iliac | Metaphyseal |
| **Medial femoral condyle** | **Longitudinal br. descending geniculate** | Scaphoid nonunion |
| Medial femoral trochlea | Transverse br. descending geniculate | Scaphoid proximal pole nonunion |
| Distal radius vascularised graft | **1,2-intercompartmental supraretinacular a.** (radial a.) | Scaphoid AVN/nonunion |
| Lateral arm osteo | PRCA | Small bone only |

**Vascularised bone nonunion** — up to **32%** without additional bone graft

### Moberg flap limitations in the thumb

**Moberg** — volar advancement; medium defects **1.8–3 cm**; >1.5 cm → ↑ dehiscence, **parrot-beak nail**, ↓ padding → modifications (V-Y, Z-plasty, Burrow triangles, **O'Brien island + graft**)

**3.5 cm pulp defect** — **Moberg contraindicated**: needs **IPJ flexion >45° → IP stiffness**

**Large thumb pulp options** — **Foucher / Holevich FDMA flap** · **Littler neurovascular island** · **free great toe pulp**

### Ray transposition after digit amputation

**Problem** — central ray loss → objects fall through gap + **scissoring**

| Ray amputated | Gap closure |
|---|---|
| **Middle** | **Index metacarpal transposition** (straight or step-cut; K-wire or plate) |
| **Ring** | **Little (small) metacarpal transposition** |
| Benign, ligaments intact | Suture deep transverse intermetacarpal ligaments / remove MC base fully |

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — ring→long transposition leaves new gap) · DTML suture impossible if resected with **tumour** · 2nd toe transfer = multiple digit / thumb loss · iliac crest graft = thumb reconstruction

![Index metacarpal transposition](../../MCQ/attachments/q4518-illa-transposition.jpg)

### Negative pressure wound therapy settings

**Most efficient pressure** — **125 mmHg** → 4× blood flow; granulation ↑63% continuous, **↑103% intermittent**; <50 or >250 → ↓ granulation

**NPWT ≠ coverage** — temporary; no infection reduction vs soft dressing (**WOLFF 2018**)

### Timing

**Free transfer** — **within 7 days** of severe UE trauma → ↓ complications (Orthobullets) · coverage **within 7 days of definitive fixation** (OB) — orthoplastic team

**Fasciocutaneous vs muscle flaps** — equivalent bone healing, salvage, infection

## 6 Complications

### Flap failure as infection predictor

**Strongest predictor of wound infection** — **flap failure**; 2nd = **time from definitive fixation to flap coverage**\
✗ Wrong options: age · temporary ex-fix, time from debridement weaker

| Failure | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Arterial insufficiency** | **Immediate return to OR** |
| **Venous congestion** | Loosen dressings, remove sutures → OR if unrelieved |

**Donor site** — cosmesis, graft pain, **seroma → aspirate** (excise if encapsulated)

## How it's asked

**"Type IIIB proximal 1/3 tibia — medial gastroc dominant supply?"** → **sural artery**\
✗ Wrong options: genicular · AT · PT · saphenous

**"Sural artery supplies which flap?"** → **gastrocnemius**\
✗ Wrong options: soleus

**"Lateral defect over revision TKA, exposed deep tissue?"** → **gastrocnemius flap**\
✗ Wrong options: lat dorsi/gracilis free · skin graft

**"Anteromedial TKA wound — label for dominant supply?"** → **medial sural artery**\
✗ Wrong options: popliteal · geniculates

**"Correct flap–artery pair?"** → **lateral gastroc – lateral sural**\
✗ Wrong options: hemisoleus–AT · medial gastroc–PT/peroneal · ALT–popliteal

**"Latissimus dorsi vascular pattern?"** → **single dominant + secondary segmental (Mathes V)** (TO)

**"Lateral arm flap perforators arise from?"** → **posterior radial collateral artery**

**"Dorsal hand wound, exposed tendon/bone, dorsal forearm wound — coverage?"** → **radial forearm rotational flap**\
✗ Wrong options: STSG · PIA flap · groin · free lateral arm

**"Groin flap artery & nerve at risk?"** → **superficial circumflex iliac a.; LFCN**

**"Latissimus flap for posterior elbow defect — artery?"** → **thoracodorsal**\
✗ Wrong options: subscapular · circumflex scapular

**"10 cm radius defect — anastomosis artery?"** → **peroneal** (free fibula)

**"3.5 cm thumb pulp — contraindicated flap?"** → **Moberg** (IP stiffness)

**"Middle ray resected with DTML — prevent scissoring?"** → **index metacarpal transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: ring transposition · ligament suture

**"Most efficient NPWT pressure?"** → **125 mmHg**

**"Strongest predictor of infection after flap for open tibia?"** → **flap failure**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Felon

*Hand · 30 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – paronychia

**Most common hand infection (TO)** — **acute paronychia**\
✗ Wrong options: felon, whitlow, subungual

**Felon share** — **15–20%** of hand infections

**Felon site** — **thumb > index** (TO)

**Felon (TO)** — infection of **subcutaneous tissue of distal pulp**

**Paronychia (TO)** — infection of nail fold (eponychium); ***S. aureus***; poor nail hygiene; risk: DM, steroids, chemo, immunocompromise, manual labourers, farmers

| Paronychia stage (TO) | Treatment |
|---|---|
| No pus point | **Antibiotics** |
| Abscess one side of nail | **I&D (single incision)** |
| Pus to opposite side + under nail | **Second incision + remove proximal third of nail** |

![Incisions for acute paronychia](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141521-1.jpg)

### Anatomy & pathophysiology

**Pulp** — closed sac; **fibrous vertical septae** periosteum → epidermis (stabilise pinch/grasp); eccrine sweat glands

**Blood supply** — digital arteries give **nutrient branch to epiphysis before entering pulp** → epiphysis spared

**Pathophysiology** — ↑ pressure in micro-compartments → **pulp "compartment syndrome"** → diaphysis ischaemia > skin → **diaphyseal necrosis/sequestration** before spontaneous skin decompression

## 2 Mechanism

**Penetrating** — glucose needlestick, splinters

**Local spread** — from paronychia

**No injury** — **50%** (contamination via eccrine glands)

| Organism | Setting |
|---|---|
| ***S. aureus*** | **Most common** |
| Gram −ve | Immunosuppressed |
| ***Eikenella corrodens*** | **Diabetic nail-biters** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — severe **throbbing pain**; tense, tender pulp

**Swelling limit** — **not proximal to DIP crease** unless sheath/joint involved

![Felon](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6102-felon-presentation.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; X-ray only if trauma (fracture/FB); no MRI, no labs

**Differentials** — **herpetic whitlow**, paronychia, glomus tumour, mucous cyst, psoriasis, **tophaceous gout**

**Herpetic whitlow (TO)** — **HSV-1**; distinction from felon mainly by **history**; **24–72 h burning prodrome** → erythema → **clear vesicles** coalescing around nail folds; turbid not purulent; **pulp NOT tense**; persists ~2 wk, resolves over next 1 wk

## 5 Management

```
Felon
├─ Early, no drainable abscess → ORAL ABX + observation
└─ Abscess (most cases) → BEDSIDE I&D + IV ABX, heal by SECONDARY INTENTION
       ├─ superficial / FB / visible drainage → VOLAR LONGITUDINAL (midvolar)
       └─ deep, no FB, no drainage → MID-LATERAL (non-pressure side)
```

### Incision & drainage technique

**Placement** — over **point of maximal tenderness**; **distal to DIP crease** (avoid contracture & sheath extension)

**Mid-lateral side** — **ulnar side digits 2–4**; **radial side thumb & small**

**Avoid** — **fish-mouth** (unstable pulp, **vascular compromise**) · double longitudinal / transverse (digital n./a. injury) · **proximal probing** (seeds flexor sheath)

**Debridement** — **break up septa**; avoid sheath & DIP joint; Gram stain + culture **before Abx**; gauze wick

**Closure** — **secondary intention**; soaks from 24–48 h; anti-*S. aureus* cover\
✗ Wrong options: primary or delayed primary

![Volar midline incision](../../MCQ/attachments/q5677-illb-volar-midline.jpg)

![Fish-mouth incision — avoid](../../MCQ/attachments/q5677-illc-volar-fishmouth-incision.jpg)

### Diabetic hand infection – renal failure & amputation

**Diabetic triad** — poor wound healing + neuropathy + vascular disease → ↑ infection, multiple procedures

**↑ Amputation rate** — **renal failure** · deep **polymicrobial / Gram −ve** infection\
✗ Wrong options: insulin dependence, retinopathy, neuropathy, Gram +ve

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — **fingertip compartment syndrome** · **flexor tenosynovitis** · **osteomyelitis / diaphyseal sequestration** · **DIP septic arthritis** · digital tip necrosis

## X vs Y

| | **Felon** | **Herpetic whitlow** (TO) | Paronychia |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Distal pulp | Pulp / nail folds | Nail fold |
| Cause | *S. aureus* | **HSV-1** | *S. aureus* |
| Pulp | **Tense** | **Not tense** | — |
| Lesion | Abscess | Clear vesicles, turbid fluid | Pus at fold |
| Treat | **I&D** | No I&D (self-limiting ~3 wk) | Abx ± I&D |

## How it's asked

**"Most common hand infection"** → **acute paronychia** (TO)

**"Midvolar pulp abscess, failed oral cephalosporin — next step?"** → **I&D, midline volar incision, secondary closure**\
✗ Wrong options: fish-mouth · IV Abx alone · colchicine

**"Diabetic hand infection — ↑ amputation with"** → **renal failure**

**"Felon in diabetic nail-biter"** → ***Eikenella***

**"Mid-lateral incision side, index finger"** → **ulnar**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Fingertip Amputations & Finger Flaps

*Hand · 31 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Goals

**Goals** — **sensate** tip · **durable** tip · **bone support for nail growth**; improper Rx → **stiffness**

**Reconstructive ladder** — primary closure → secondary intention → STSG → FTSG → random local flap → axial local → island local → distant random → distant axial → free tissue

### Fingertip anatomy

| Structure | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Eponychium** | Dorsal skin proximal to nail (covers nail fold) |
| Paronychium | Lateral nail folds |
| **Hyponychium** | Keratin plug under distal free nail edge |
| **Lunula** | White proximal nail; **divides sterile from germinal matrix** |
| **Sterile matrix** | Distal; **nail adheres** here |
| **Germinal matrix** | Proximal; **90% of nail growth** |

![Fingertip anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6060-fingertip-anatomy.jpg)

### Digital arterial arches

**Proper digital aa.** — radial + ulnar communicate via **proximal, middle, distal transverse arches**

**Middle transverse arch** — at **C3 pulley**, deep to flexors; supplies digital aa. between PIP & DIP → **required for retrograde digital artery flap**

## 3 Presentation

**Mechanism** — avulsion, laceration, crush

**Exam** — **laceration geometry** (transverse / dorsal oblique / volar oblique) guides Rx; **exposed bone?**; flexor/extensor involvement

**X-ray** — AP + lateral for **bony involvement**

## 5 Management

```
Fingertip amputation
├─ No bone/tendon exposed, <2 cm loss → SECONDARY INTENTION
│      └─ >2 cm loss, no bone → FTSG (hypothenar)
├─ Child, even with exposed bone → SECONDARY INTENTION (ointment + dressing)
└─ Exposed bone/tendon (adult)
       ├─ can rongeur without losing nail support → REVISION AMPUTATION + primary closure
       └─ can't → FLAP by region
```

**Not replantation** — not indicated for distal fingertip amputation

### Fingertip amputation – healing by secondary intention (children, small defects)

**Indications** — adult/child, **no bone or tendon exposed, <2 cm skin loss**; **children with exposed bone**

**6-y-old, exposed P3** → **no change: ointment + dressing**\
✗ Wrong options: homodigital island · thenar · volar advancement · V-Y

**6-y-old, lawn mower, 6 mm distal to lunula, contaminated, no bone, <1 cm** → **I&D + soft dressing + review within 1 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: replant · DIP revision · Moberg · Z-plasty

**Adult 1 cm defect, no bone, sterile matrix intact** → **local wound care alone**\
✗ Wrong options: primary closure · revision · V-Y · digital island

**Child >1 cm soft-tissue loss** — controversial: V-Y vs dressings

**Technique** — irrigation + soft dressing → **day 7–10 water–peroxide soaks** → heals **3–5 wk** (Orthobullets) · ~4 wk (Neustein) · semiocclusive 2–8 wk (Germann)

**Revision amputation outcomes** — better **static 2PD & arc of motion** than local flaps; commonest complaint **cold intolerance**

### FTSG & revision amputation

**FTSG** — **hypothenar** donor, closed primarily; bolster (cotton ball) 7 d → ROM after 7 d

**Avoid STSG** — **contractile, tender, less durable**

**No skin graft on bare tendon without paratenon** → adhesions

**Revision amputation steps** — **ablate remaining nail matrix** (avoid nail remnants/cysts) · tendon insertions lost → **disarticulate DIP** · **transect digital nerves & tendons proximally** (traction neurectomy) · volar skin over bone to dorsal

### Flap selection by region

![Flap options by region](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6060-hand-master-moved.jpg)

| Region | Flap(s) |
|---|---|
| Tip – **transverse / dorsal oblique** | **V-Y advancement**, digital island artery |
| Tip – **volar oblique** | **Cross-finger (>30 y)** · **thenar (<30 y)** · digital island artery |
| Nail bed sterile matrix + eponychial loss | **Reverse cross-finger** |
| Volar proximal finger | Cross-finger (>30 y), axial flag (from long finger) |
| Dorsal proximal finger & MCP | Reverse cross-finger, axial flag |
| **Volar thumb** | **Moberg <2 cm** · **FDMA >2 cm** · **neurovascular island up to 4 cm** |
| **Dorsal thumb** | **FDMA (Kite)** |
| **1st web space** | **Z-plasty 60°**, **posterior interosseous fasciocutaneous** (if >75%) |
| **Dorsal hand** | **Groin flap** (superficial circumflex iliac a.) |

### Advancement flaps – V-Y & volar (Moberg) advancement

**V-Y** — **transverse / dorsal oblique** tip (more dorsal than volar loss); can't advance over phalanx to cover eponychium

**Transverse ring tip, exposed bone** → **shorten P3 + nail bed removal + V-Y** (nail bed removal prevents **hook nail**)\
✗ Wrong options: PIP amputation · cross-finger · groin · thenar

**Moberg (volar advancement)** — **volar thumb pulp ≤2 cm**

**Island volar advancement (thumb)** — divide all but NV bundles → covers up to **3.5 cm** · up to 4 cm by elevating whole volar thumb skin; IP crease → nail bed\
✗ Wrong options: <2.5 cm options

**Thumb defect size source note** — neurovascular island **up to 4 cm** (Orthobullets) · island volar advancement **2.5–3.5 cm** (OB4508)

| Flap | Thenar | Cross-finger |
|---|---|---|
| Indication | **Volar oblique, index/long, <30 y** | Volar oblique / volar proximal, **>30 y** |
| Pros | **Cosmesis**, more fat/bulk, colour match, primary donor closure | **Less stiffness** |
| Cons | **PIP flexion contracture** (finger sutured to palm 2–3 wk), **donor tenderness**, limited size | — |
| Contraindications | **RA, Dupuytren's, age >30, DJD** | — |

**Thenar positioning** — flex **MCP & DIP** maximally to ↓ PIP flexion

### Cross-finger & reverse cross-finger flaps

**Cross-finger** — full-thickness; **volar** loss distal to PIP with **intact tenosynovium**; hinge adjacent to defect

**IVDU ring finger volar defect after abscess I&D (exposed tendon)** → **cross-finger flap**

**Reverse cross-finger (RXFF)** — hinge **opposite** defect; dermis level (below follicles, above veins); **subcutaneous (adipofascial) flap turned over + FTSG**; donor paratenon left; divide **2–3 wk**; flap **5 mm wider** than defect; no graft over germinal matrix

**RXFF indications** — **eponychial fold loss + exposed extensor**, **sterile matrix loss + bare P3**, tendon without paratenon, dorsal PIP (boutonnière/burn), total nail-complex avulsion, burns

**RXFF donor** — **dorsum of middle/proximal phalanx of adjacent finger**\
✗ Wrong options: distal phalanx (too little tissue, endangers donor germinal matrix) · over DIP/PIP (skin too thin)

**Blood supply RXFF** — dorsal cutaneous branches of proper digital a.

**Index total nail complex avulsion alternative** — cross-thumb-to-index flap

**Other dorsal options (wrong here)** — toe nail bed graft (nail bed only, not eponychium) · volar cross-finger (rare, distal thumb) · heterodigital reversed NV island (extensive defects, alternative to microsurgery)

![Reverse cross-finger flap](../../MCQ/attachments/q5438-illa-illustration-a.jpg)

### First dorsal metacarpal artery (Kite) flap for thumb defects

**Indication** — **dorsal thumb** with exposed tendon/bone (no paratenon/tenosynovium); also volar thumb >2 cm, web space

**Vessel** — **FDMA, branch of radial a. at snuffbox**; **antegrade**, axial pattern; over index MC; ± 2nd DMA for large defects

**Flap** — island from dorsoproximal index (over MCP/P1) + radial nerve branches + 1st DI fascia; pedicle **up to 7 cm**; covers **up to 3 × 5 cm**; donor FTSG; sensate via dorsal digital nerve branches

**2 × 4 cm dorsal thumb, exposed tendon & bone** → **FDMA**\
✗ Wrong options: cross-finger · Moberg · FTSG · V-Y

**Dorsal thumb avulsion after EPL repair/transfer** → **Kite flap**\
✗ Wrong options: VAC · wet-to-dry

![Kite flap](../../MCQ/attachments/q8837-illa-kite-flap.jpg)

| Flap | Vessel / flow |
|---|---|
| **FDMA (Kite)** | **First dorsal MC a., antegrade** |
| Dorsoulnar/radial collateral (thumb) | Retrograde, distal thumb |
| Posterior interosseous | PIA, antegrade or retrograde; web space, dorsal hand, elbow |
| Homodigital / heterodigital NV island | Digital a., antegrade or reverse |
| **Retrograde digital artery** | **Needs middle transverse arch** |
| Axial flag | Dorsal MC aa. |
| Groin | Superficial circumflex iliac a. |

### Homodigital retrograde island flap

**Retrograde flow digital artery flap** — pulp, dorsal & lateral defects **P3 → PIP**; requires **patent digital a. + middle transverse anastomotic arch**\
✗ Wrong options: axial flag · groin · PIA · RXFF

**Digital island artery** — **best axial pattern flap**; straight, dorsal oblique or volar oblique tip

### Z-plasty principles

| Z-plasty angle | Gain in length |
|---|---|
| 30° | **25%** |
| 45° | **50%** |
| **60°** | **75%** |

**Use** — scar contracture revision (1st web burn, volar finger scar), prophylaxis; **60° = standard**\
✗ Wrong options: 25° limbs · STSG · Moberg · V-Y

![60° Z-plasty](../../MCQ/attachments/q219790-c-zplasty-60.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Nail bed injury – germinal matrix

**Split nail** — **germinal matrix** scar → excise scar + matrix graft (adjacent finger / 2nd toe)\
✗ Wrong options: eponychium · hyponychium · lunula · sterile matrix

**Nail inclusion cyst** — incomplete germinal matrix ablation at revision · or eponychial fold not splinted open → scars to sterile matrix

**Nail bed repair** — remove plate, repair (chromic), splint eponychial fold (nail/foil)

**Hook nail** — **tight tip closure / pulp overtightened** + **insufficient bony support** → prevent by nail bed removal when shortening

![Hook nail deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6060-hook-nail-deformity.jpg)

### Complications – digital neuroma

**Symptomatic neuroma** — <10%; **highest risk: avulsion mechanism** and **index finger** (nerve density)

**Not risk factors** — single digit, proximal level, ED vs theatre, small finger

**Flap failure** — inadequate arterial flow (**vasospasm → anastomotic thrombosis**) or venous outflow

**Thenar flap** — **recipient PIP flexion contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: flap failure · colour mismatch · motor branch injury · recipient sensitivity (donor tenderness)

## X vs Y

| | **Cross-finger** | **Reverse cross-finger** |
|---|---|---|
| Defect | **Volar** | **Dorsal** (eponychium, nail bed, bare extensor) |
| Hinge | Adjacent to defect | **Opposite** defect |
| Tissue | Full-thickness skin + fat | Subcutaneous turnover + FTSG |

| | **Moberg** | **FDMA (Kite)** | NV island |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thumb defect | Volar **<2 cm** | **Dorsal**, volar >2 cm | Volar up to 4 cm |

## How it's asked

**"Child, fingertip amputation, exposed bone?"** → **ointment + dressing (no flap)**

**"Child, contaminated tip, no bone, <1 cm?"** → **I&D, soft dressing, review in 1 wk**

**"Adult 1 cm defect, no bone, sterile matrix intact?"** → **local wound care**

**"Transverse tip, exposed bone?"** → **shorten P3 + nail bed removal + V-Y**

**"Moberg flap suits?"** → **volar thumb defect**

**"Largest defect for island volar advancement?"** → **2.5–3.5 cm**

**"Thenar flap candidate?"** → **28 y, volar oblique index defect**\
✗ Wrong options: 66 y · RA · dorsal · thumb

**"Volar finger defect with exposed tendon, 29 y IVDU?"** → **cross-finger flap**

**"Commonest complication of thenar flap?"** → **recipient PIP flexion contracture**

**"Nail + eponychium avulsed, bare extensor?"** → **reverse cross-finger flap**\
✗ Wrong options: FTSG · toe nail bed · volar cross-finger

**"RXFF for index sterile matrix loss — donor?"** → **dorsum of middle phalanx of middle finger**

**"Dorsal thumb defect with exposed tendon/bone?"** → **FDMA (Kite) flap**

**"Blood supply of Kite flap?"** → **antegrade, first dorsal metacarpal artery**

**"Flap needing middle transverse anastomotic arch?"** → **retrograde flow digital artery flap**

**"75% lengthening of web scar?"** → **two-flap Z-plasty, 60°**

**"Split nail after revision amputation?"** → **germinal matrix**

**"Neuroma after revision amputation — biggest risk?"** → **avulsion mechanism**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Flexor Carpi Radialis Tendinitis

*Hand · 32 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — rare; <1 / 100,000

**Risk** — **repetitive wrist flexion**: **golf, racquet sports**, manual labour

**Primary** — stenosing tenosynovitis in fibro-osseous tunnel

**Secondary** — scaphoid fracture/cyst, DRF, **STT arthritis**, thumb CMC arthritis

### Anatomy – FCR tunnel & insertion

**Muscle** — **bipennate**

**Sheath** — origin → trapezium; no fibrous sheath distal to trapezium

**Tunnel entry** — **proximal border of trapezium**

| Tunnel wall | Structure |
|---|---|
| Radial | Body of trapezium |
| Palmar | Trapezial crest, TCL |
| Ulnar | Retinacular septum (separates from carpal tunnel) |
| Dorsal | Septum reflection on trapezium |

| Zone | Tendon fills | Prone to |
|---|---|---|
| **Within tunnel** | **90%** (on rough trapezium) | Constriction, tendinitis, **attrition, rupture** |
| Proximal to tunnel | 50–65% | Irritation from **osteophytes** |

**Insertion** — 1–2 mm slip → trapezial crest; **80% → 2nd MC**; **20% → 3rd MC**

![FCR tunnel](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6033-tunnel.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **volar radial wrist**; tender along FCR at distal wrist crease

**Provocative** — **resisted wrist flexion** · resisted radial deviation

**No** — numbness/tingling

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical ± **MRI**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — normal in primary; secondary: healed scaphoid/DRF, STT/CMC arthritis, exostosis

**MRI (T2)** — **↑ fluid/signal around FCR sheath**; ganglion, scaphoid cyst

![T2 MRI fluid in FCR sheath](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6033-fcr-mri.jpg)

**Diagnostic injection** — LA along FCR sheath relieves pain

**Differentials** — thumb CMC OA, scaphoid cyst, ganglion, De Quervain's, CTS

## 5 Management

```
FCR tendinitis
├─ First line → REST / PT / ACTIVITY MODIFICATION, splint, NSAIDs
│      └─ fails (e.g. 6 wk) → FCR SHEATH STEROID INJECTION (not into tendon)
└─ Recalcitrant → SURGICAL RELEASE of FCR sheath
```

### FCR sheath injection

**Technique** — near, **not into tendon** (rupture risk)

**Outcome** — effective in **primary**; fails in **secondary** (osteophytes)

### Surgical release

**Approach** — volar longitudinal, proximal to wrist crease → proximal thenar eminence; reflect thenar muscles radially

**Technique** — open sheath distal forearm → trapezial crest; in tunnel **incise along ulnar margin**; mobilise tendon from groove (release trapezial insertion)

**Nerves at risk** — **palmar cutaneous br. median** · **lateral antebrachial cutaneous** · **superficial radial**

## 6 Complications

**Disease** — **FCR attrition/rupture**

**Surgery** — cutaneous nerve injury · deep palmar arch · **FPL** (lies superficial to FCR) · **FCR laceration within tunnel**

**Poor prognosis** — overuse, **workers' comp**, **no response to injection**, long duration

## How it's asked

**"Golfer, volar-radial wrist pain, T2 fluid round FCR, failed 6 wk PT — next?"** → **FCR sheath injection**\
✗ Wrong options: more PT · CTR · SRN decompression · FCU debridement

**"Provocative test"** → **resisted wrist flexion**

**"FCR main insertion"** → **2nd metacarpal base**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Flexor Pulley System

*Hand · 33 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Pulley anatomy – annular/cruciform sequence, A2 & A4, thumb oblique pulley

**Finger sequence** — **A1, A2, C1, A3, C2, A4, C3** (prox → distal); 5 annular + 3 cruciate

**Joint pulleys** — **A1, A3, A5** → arise from **palmar plates** of MCP, PIP, DIP

**Bone pulleys** — **A2** = periosteum **proximal half of proximal phalanx** · **A4** = **midportion of middle phalanx**

**Most important** — **A2 + A4** → prevent **bowstringing**; minimum to preserve/reconstruct in flexor tendon surgery

**Ideal** — a pulley **proximal and distal to each joint**; preserve/reconstruct **≥3 pulleys**

**Cruciate (C1–C3)** — thin, collapsible; prevent sheath collapse/expansion; let annular pulleys **approximate in flexion**

**Trigger finger** — **A1** most commonly involved

![Finger pulleys](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6004-finger-pulleys-2.jpg)

### Thumb pulleys

**Thumb sequence** — **A1 – Av – oblique – A2** (2 annular + Av + oblique)

**Oblique pulley** — **most important in thumb**; analogue of finger C1 position; full FPL excursion, prevents bowstringing

**Oblique origin** — proximal half of proximal phalanx (Orthobullets) · ulnar side of PP base → radial side of DP base, 4 mm wide (Bayat, OB)

**A1 (thumb)** — at MCP volar plate; ~6 mm; **radial digital nerve closest (2.7 mm)** · ulnar 5.4 mm

**A2 (thumb)** — 5–10 mm (Orthobullets) · 8 mm (Bayat); contributes **least to arc of motion**

**Bowstringing rule** — if **A2 intact**, cutting **A1 OR oblique** alone → no bowstringing; cutting **both A1 + oblique** → **bowstringing**

**Restore kinematics (A2 intact)** — repair **oblique OR A1**

**Bayat caveat** — once **A1 + Av** both cut, oblique can no longer prevent bowstringing

| Av pulley type | Image | Feature |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | ![Type I Av](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6004-type-i-av-pulley.jpg) | Transverse, parallel to A1, **gap** to A1 |
| **II** | ![Type II Av](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6004-type-ii-av-pulley.jpg) | **No gap** to A1 |
| **III** | ![Type III Av](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6004-type-iii-av-pulley.jpg) | **Triangular/oblique**, fibres converge radially |

## 2 Mechanism

### Pulley rupture – climbers, grading & sequelae

**Mechanism** — **extending force on flexed (crimped) digit**: **rock climbers**, Brazilian Jiu-Jitsu, baseball, lifting crimped; or laceration

**Climbers** — 75% get UE injury; **30–50%** of these = closed pulley injury

**Thumb pulley rupture** — rare; e.g. after **steroid injection** for FPL tenosynovitis → pain, clicking, ↓ ROM

**Sequential ruptures** needed for bowstringing; isolated rupture → early functional rehab

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Closed injury** — **MRI or US** to diagnose

**MRI sign** — **volar subluxation of flexor tendon (FPL) away from phalanx** (normally apposed)

**Clinical** — visible **bowstringing**; FPL intact (bowstringing needs tendon fixed at both ends → not FPL avulsion)

![Thumb FPL bowstringing after oblique pulley rupture](../../MCQ/attachments/q5681-a-fig-a.jpg)

### Schöffl classification

| Grade | Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| I | **Pulley strain** | Nonop |
| II | **Complete rupture A4** or **partial A2/A3** | Nonop, **taping** |
| III | **Complete rupture A2 or A3** | Nonop |
| **IV** | **Multiple ruptures** (A2/A3, A2/A3/A4) or single A2/A3 + **lumbrical/collateral lig trauma** | **Surgical reconstruction** |

**Outcome (Schöffl)** — 92% nonop returned to previous climbing level

## 5 Management

```
Pulley injury
├─ Grade I–III → NONOP (taping, functional rehab)
└─ Grade IV → PULLEY RECONSTRUCTION (not repair)
       ├─ A2 (proximal phalanx) → 3 LOOPS around bone, DEEP to extensor
       └─ A4 (middle phalanx) → 2 LOOPS around bone, SUPERFICIAL to extensor
```

### Pulley reconstruction

**Goal** — prevent bowstringing → **flexion contracture** & ↓ ROM; preserve/reconstruct ≥3 pulleys, **A2 important**; A4 may be sacrificed acutely (unclear)

**Thumb** — reconstruct **oblique pulley alone** = enough (tendon graft)

**First step** — **excise all scar dorsal to flexor tendon**

**Graft** — **extensor retinaculum** (synovialised → **least gliding resistance**) · excised tendon · PL/plantaris · FDS · allograft

| Technique | Examples | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Around-the-bone (encircling)** | Bunnell single loop · **Okutsu triple loop** | **Biomechanically strongest** (triple loop) |
| Non-encircling | Kleinert ever-present rim · Karev belt loop · Lister extensor retinaculum · Doyle-Blythe PL through volar plate · Pulvertaft weave | Weaker |

**Hemostasis** — prevents adhesions; check excursion after reconstruction

![Okutsu triple loop](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6004-okutsu-triple-loop.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Untreated grade IV** — **bowstringing → painful flexion contracture**

**Encircling reconstruction** — **phalangeal fracture** (most worrisome) · stiffness · persistent bowstringing (inadequate tension, **scar left dorsal to tendon**)

## X vs Y

| Effect | Cause |
|---|---|
| **Bowstringing → flexion contracture** | Neglected multi-pulley (A2–A4) injury |
| **Quadriga** | FDP **repaired short** → flexor lag of adjacent digits |
| **Lumbrical plus** | FDP injury **distal to lumbrical** → paradoxical IP extension on MCP flexion |

## How it's asked

**"Annular pulleys arising from palmar plates"** → **A1, A3, A5**

**"Proximal → distal sequence"** → **A1, A2, C1, A3, C2, A4, C3**

**"Two most important pulleys"** → **A2 and A4**

**"Thumb, A2 intact — repair which to restore kinematics?"** → **oblique or A1**

**"Thumb clicking after FPL steroid injection, bowstringing on MRI"** → **pulley rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: FPL avulsion, RCL, gout

**"Grade II pulley injury"** → **complete A4 rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: A2/A3 complete = III; multiple = IV

**"Laceration through A2–A4, tendons intact, not reconstructed — sequel?"** → **flexion contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: quadriga, lumbrical plus

**"Strongest reconstruction"** → **Okutsu triple loop**

**"Graft with least gliding resistance"** → **extensor retinaculum**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Flexor Tendon Injuries

*Hand · 34 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & tendon nutrition

**FDP** — flexes **DIP**; common belly; **index/long = AIN**, **ring/small = ulnar**

**FDS** — flexes **PIP**; independent bellies; median n.; **absent to small finger in 25%**

**FPL** — AIN; **most radial** structure in carpal tunnel

**FCR** — inserts base 2nd MC; **median nerve lies immediately ulnar to FCR** at DRUJ level (deep FCR laceration → median nerve injury)

**FCU** — pisiform, hook of hamate, base 5th MC; ulnar n.

**Camper chiasm** — level of **proximal phalanx**; FDP passes through split FDS

**Finger pulleys** — **A2 & A4 (from periosteum) = most important vs bowstringing**; **A1, A3, A5 from volar plate** (MCP, PIP, DIP); C1–C3 collapsible

**Thumb pulleys** — **A1, Av, A2 + oblique**; **oblique (+ A1) most important vs bowstringing**; A2 contributes least

**Pulley venting** — **25% of A2** and **100% of A4** can be incised with little deficit

**Nutrition in zone II (sheathed)** — **synovial diffusion = primary**\
✗ Wrong options: vincula

**Outside sheath** — direct perfusion: vincula, bony insertion, reflected sheath vessels, longitudinal palmar vessels

![Pulley system of the finger](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6031-shows-the-pulley-system-of-the-digital-flexor-sheath.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Cause** — volar laceration ± digital NV injury; closed avulsion (jersey finger)

**Healing pathways** — **intrinsic** (tenocytes) · **extrinsic** (sheath/inflammatory cells → **adhesions**)

| Phase | Days | Histology | Strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inflammatory | 0–5 | Cellular proliferation | None |
| Fibroblastic | 5–28 | Disorganised collagen | Increasing |
| Remodelling | >28 | Linear collagen | Tolerates AROM |

**Weakest repair** — **post-op day 6–12**; fails at **knots**; **gap >3 mm** → failure

**Early motion** — ↓ adhesions, ↑ excursion, better healing biology (esp zone II)

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – disrupted tenodesis cascade

**Resting cascade** — involved digit lies **extended** out of cascade

**Tenodesis** — passive **wrist extension → digits flex**; **DIP stays extended = FDP cut**; PIP stays extended = FDS+FDP

**Extensor injury** — loss of digit extension with passive **wrist flexion**

**Isolated tests** — FDP: block PIP, flex DIP · FDS: hold other fingers extended, flex PIP

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — associated fracture; **bony FDP avulsion** (Leddy III large fragment caught at **A4**)

**Ultrasound** — suspected (partial) laceration, retracted stump level

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Flexor zones (no man's land)

| Zone | Boundaries | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Distal to FDS insertion | **Jersey finger**; FDP only |
| **II** | FDS insertion → **A1 / distal palmar crease** | **"No man's land" (Bunnell 1918)** (TO); **FDS + FDP in same sheath**; adhesions |
| **III** | A1 → distal edge TCL (palm) | **NV injury** → worse; good results otherwise; ± A1 release |
| **IV** | **Carpal tunnel** | Adhesions; repair TCL **lengthened** if bowstringing |
| **V** | Proximal to carpal tunnel (forearm) | NV injury |
| **Thumb T I–III** | TI distal to IP · TII IP→MCP · TIII thenar | ↑ re-rupture; early motion does not improve long-term; **avoid TIII incision (recurrent motor br.)** |

![Flexor zones](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6031-flexor-zones.jpg)

### Jersey finger – FDP avulsion

**Mechanism** — forced DIP extension during grip; **ring finger** most common

**Big bony fragment** — **Leddy type III**, caught at **A4** (stays distal)

**Stem** — can't flex DIP after grabbing jersey/sliding headfirst → **profundus insertion rupture**

## 5 Management

```
Flexor tendon laceration
├─ <60% width, no triggering → trim frayed edges + early protected ROM (no repair)
├─ 60–75% or ≥50% with triggering → EPITENDINOUS repair of cut edge only
└─ Complete → core (4–6 strand) + running epitendinous, within 2–3 wk, early motion
       └─ child / non-compliant → 4-strand + cast 3–4 wk
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Criterion → action |
|---|---|
| Partial laceration | **<60%**, no trigger → no repair · **60–75%** or ≥50% + trigger → epitendinous · **>75%** → repair |
| Complete laceration | Core (**4–6 strands** for early active motion) + running epitendinous |
| Timing | **Within 3 wk** (2 wk ideal); FDP avulsion past ~3 wk → retracted; >6 wk pulleys collapse |
| Re-rupture | Scar **<1 cm** → resect + repair · **>1 cm** → graft (1-stage if sheath intact, 2-stage if collapsed) |
| Reconstruction | **Supple skin, sensate digit, vascularity, full passive ROM** |
| Single-stage graft | Pristine sheath + full ROM |
| Tenolysis | **>3 mo**, stable soft tissue, **full passive ROM**, active ≪ passive |
| Immobilise (✗ Wrong options: early motion) | **Children**, non-compliant |

### Partial lacerations – trimming vs epitendinous repair

**<60% (e.g. 40–50%), no trigger** — **no repair**; trim edges + **early protected ROM** (repaired partial tendons can be weaker)

**Up to 75% partial** — **epitendinous suture of cut edge alone** prevents gap >2 mm; no core (adds bulk), no circumferential needed

**Complete laceration** — always **core + epitendinous**

### Repair biomechanics – core strands, suture purchase, FDS slip & rehab protocol strength

**Strength ∝ number of core strands crossing repair** (linear) — strongest single factor\
✗ Wrong options: suture size · sheath repair

**4–6 strands** — needed for **early active motion**

**Purchase** — **10 mm** from cut edge (> 5 mm)

**Other** — larger calibre ↑ strength; **locking loops ↓ gap**; **dorsal core** stronger

**Epitendinous** — **simple running** (↓ gliding resistance; not Silfverskiöld); +20% strength; ↓ gap; ↓ bulk

**Sheath repair** — no clinical difference

**Zone II FDS** — repair it (↑ strength, outcomes); **repairing 1 slip → better gliding** than both

**Timing** — **within 3 wk** (2 wk ideal); retraction later

**Incisions** — cross creases transversely/obliquely (Brunner); **never longitudinal**

| Rehab protocol | Force / excursion | How |
|---|---|---|
| **Duran** | Low / low | **Patient-assisted** passive flexion, active extension |
| **Kleinert** | Low / low | **Rubber-band dynamic** passive flexion, active extension |
| **Synergistic (Mayo)** | Low / **high** | **Active wrist motion** → greatest excursion |
| **Early active** (place & hold) | Moderate / high | Needs **4–6 strands** |
| Immobilisation | — | **Children, non-compliant**: wrist & MCP flexed, IPs extended |

**2-strand repair** — OK for Duran/Kleinert/synergistic; **not for early active motion**

### Paediatric flexor tendon injuries

**Child (e.g. 4 y) zone II** — **4-strand core + cast 3–4 wk** (wrist 35° flex, MCP 60–70°, IP extended; above elbow)

**Differences from adults** — **poor therapy cooperation**; delayed presentation commoner (may need staged); Botox may aid rehab

### Wide-awake (WALANT) repair

**Anaesthesia** — **lidocaine + adrenaline** tumescent; **no tourniquet, no sedation**

**Adrenaline in digit** — **safe** (up to 1:1000); necrosis was **procaine**; antidote **phentolamine** (1 mg in 5 mL)

**Doses** — 7 mg/kg lido; 1% + 1:100,000 if <50 mL; 0.5% + 1:200,000 if 50–100 mL; 0.25% + 1:400,000 if 100–200 mL; P1/P2 2 mL, P3 1 mL, palm 10–15 mL

**Primary goal** — **active testing of repair** by awake patient (gaps, gliding through A2/A4)

**Benefits** — debulk on the spot, **vent A2 / divide A4** → **↓ late tenolysis**; facilitates early active motion

**Post-op** — immobilise 3 days → **half-fist 45/45/45** active midrange

**✗ EXCEPT traps** — "adrenaline contraindicated" · "brachial block + sedation" · "forearm tourniquet" · "surgeon passively flexes" · "doesn't reduce tenolysis"

### Late reconstruction – two-stage grafting, FDS transfer & failed repair

**Re-rupture** — 15–25%

```
Re-rupture after repair
├─ scar <1 cm → resect scar + primary repair
└─ scar >1 cm → graft
       ├─ sheath intact (paediatric catheter passes) → single-stage graft (palmaris longus)
       └─ sheath collapsed/scarred → staged (Hunter rod)
```

**Advance FDP >1 cm** — avoid → **quadrigia** & contracture

**Reconstruction prerequisites** — **supple skin, sensate digit, adequate vascularity, full passive ROM of joints**

**Hunter (two-stage)** — stage 1 silicone rod → pseudosheath; stage 2 at **≥3 mo** graft through **mesothelium-lined pseudosheath** (Pulvertaft proximal)

**Paneva-Holevich** — stage 1 rod + FDS–FDP loop in palm; stage 2 FDS reflected distally; **intrasynovial graft**, one tenorrhaphy healing at a time

**Graft choice** — **palmaris longus** (commonest; absent 15%) · plantaris (absent 19%, longer) · toe extensors · EIP · FDS

**Pulleys** — reconstruct one **proximal & distal to each joint**; do pulleys first

**Tenolysis after reconstruction** — **>50%**

**Chronic FDP avulsion (4 mo), full passive ROM, palm mass** — **two-stage grafting** (pulleys collapse >6 wk; no direct repair after ~3 wk)

**Chronic FPL laceration (4 mo), full passive IP** — **FDS ring transfer to FPL** (single stage) · alternative IP fusion

**Active silicone rod** — fatigues → not permanent

### Attritional ruptures (rheumatoid, prominent plate)

**Mannerfelt (TO)** — **FPL rupture** in carpal tunnel from **scaphoid (STT) spur**; **most common flexor rupture in RA**

**Treatment** — **palmaris longus graft** (✗ Wrong options: primary repair (attritional)) or FDS transfer; debride osteophyte; IP fusion alternative (✗ Wrong options: MCP fusion)

**Vaughan-Jackson (TO)** — extensor ruptures **ulnar → radial** (EDM/EDC small first) from **distal ulna** (caput ulnae)

**After volar DRF plate** — **FPL attrition** (plate **distal to watershed** / prominent) → **remove plate + FPL reconstruction** (graft or FDS4)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Adhesions** | **Most common**; zone II; tenolysis at **3–6 mo** if passive ≫ active ROM |
| Re-rupture | 15–25%; days 6–12 weakest |
| Joint contracture | Up to 17% |
| **Quadrigia** | FDP over-advanced/tethered → adjacent fingers can't fully flex |
| **Lumbrical plus** | FDP lax/long (graft too long, FDP cut distal to lumbrical) → paradoxical PIP extension on flexing |
| Swan-neck, trigger finger | — |

**Tenolysis requirements** — soft tissues stable (>3 mo), **full passive ROM**; preserve A2/A4

## X vs Y

| | Quadrigia | Lumbrical plus |
|---|---|---|
| FDP | **Too short / tethered** | **Too long / lax** |
| Sign | Adjacent fingers lack full flexion | IP extension on attempted flexion |

| | Mannerfelt | Vaughan-Jackson |
|---|---|---|
| Tendon | **FPL** (± FDP index) | **EDM, EDC small → radial** |
| Bony cause | **Scaphoid / STT spur** | **Distal ulna** |

| Finger | Most important pulleys |
|---|---|
| Fingers | **A2, A4** |
| Thumb | **Oblique** (+ A1) |

## How it's asked

**"No man's land?"** → **zone II** (TO)

**"Zone II, 40–50% laceration, no triggering"** → **trim + early ROM, no repair**

**"75% laceration with triggering, least suture"** → **epitendinous of cut edge only**

**"Managed by epitendinous alone"** → **~70% partial FDP**

**"Greatest effect on strength"** → **number of core strands**

**"Best load to failure + gliding"** → **10 mm purchase + simple running epitendinous**

**"2-strand repair — NOT strong enough for"** → **early active motion**

**"Zone II true statement"** → **repair of 1 FDS slip improves gliding**

**"Zone II nutrition"** → **synovial diffusion**

**"Median nerve immediately ulnar to"** → **FCR**

**"DIP stays extended with passive wrist extension"** → **FDP**

**"4-y-old zone II"** → **4-strand + cast 4 wk**

**"WALANT true"** → **lido + adrenaline safe in digit** · **primary goal** → **active testing of repair**

**"Re-rupture, 1.8 cm scar, sheath intact"** → **excise + palmaris longus graft**

**"Two-stage reconstruction true"** → **supple skin, sensate, vascular digit**; tenolysis >50%

**"Jersey finger 4 mo, palm mass"** → **two-stage graft**

**"Chronic FPL 4 mo"** → **FDS transfer**

**"RA, can't flex thumb IP"** → **Mannerfelt (FPL) → PL graft**

**"Pop 12 mo after volar plate, can't flex thumb IP"** → **remove plate + FPL reconstruction**

**"Baseball slide, big fragment on X-ray"** → **FDP insertion (Leddy III)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Frostbite

*Hand · 35 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Demographics** — **♂:♀ 10:1**; **30–50 y**; homeless, outdoor workers

**Distribution** — upper limb 20% · **lower limb 47%** · both 30% · head/face 3%

**Most important host risk** — **alcohol abuse** (63% of admissions); homelessness

**Other host** — mental illness, PVD, neuropathy, DM, malnutrition, chronic illness, prior cold injury, **African descent** (Caucasians better cold-induced vasodilatation)

**Smoking** — ↓ NO + ↑ fibrinogen/platelets → **thrombosis**

| Environmental factor | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Temperature | Risk **low > −10°C**; **high < −25°C** |
| Wind at −18°C | Freeze in **1 h at 10 mph** · **10 min at 40 mph** |
| Altitude | >17,000 ft |
| Contact | Water, ice, **metal** (↑ severity) |

### Spectrum of cold injury

| Injury | Key features |
|---|---|
| **Frostnip** | Mildest; superficial skin only, **no dermal damage**; reversible |
| **Chilblain (pernio)** | **Nonfreezing, dry**; burning, pruritus; resolves 2 wk; chronic vasculitis in young/middle-aged ♀ |
| **Trench (immersion) foot** | **Nonfreezing, wet**, <10°C; military |
| **Frostbite** | Freezing → tissue necrosis → amputation |
| Hypothermia | Core temp ↓; can be fatal |

## 2 Mechanism

| Limb phase | Events |
|---|---|
| **I Cooling/freezing** | Vasoconstriction → **"hunting response"** (cyclic AV shunting q10 min); **intracellular ice** → membrane rupture; **extracellular ice** → sludging + **intracellular dehydration** |
| **II Rewarming** | Crystals melt; **endothelium permeable** → blisters/oedema; **avoid refreezing** |
| **III Progressive injury** | Inflammation, thrombosis, necrosis; **↓ PGE2**; **↑ PGF2α + thromboxane B2** |
| IV Resolution | Healing ± sequelae or gangrene |

**Numbers** — sensory nerve dysfunction **−10°C**; extracellular ice **−2 to −15°C**

**Hypothermia** — CBT <35°C; shivering stops at **30–32°C** → rigidity, mimics death → **"no one is dead until warm and dead"**

## 3 Presentation

**Hypothermia grades** — mild **32–35°C** · moderate **28–32°C** · severe **<28°C**; brady, AF/VF, resp acidosis, coma

**Blisters** — form **6–24 h after rewarming**; degree only defined **after rewarming**

**Severe hand injury signs** — no Doppler signal, **absent cap refill**, dark purple digits, **haemorrhagic blisters** → bone scan / angiography

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification

| Degree | Findings | Newer class / prognosis |
|---|---|---|
| 1st | **Central white area + surrounding erythema** | Superficial – good |
| 2nd | **Clear/cloudy blisters** within 24 h | Superficial – good |
| 3rd | **Haemorrhagic blisters** (painless) / black eschar | **Deep – poor** |
| 4th | Tissue necrosis | **Deep – poor** |

![Frostbite degrees by tissue depth](../../MCQ/attachments/q219990-illa-screenshot-2024-12-24-at-7-33-58-pm.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**Tc-99m bone scan** — assess severity: **1st at day 2** (absent uptake = poor prognosis, not necessarily necrosis); **2nd at day 5**

| Day-5 blood/bone pool | Action |
|---|---|
| Normal | Expectant |
| Diminished | Observe ± early debridement |
| **Absent** | **Early debridement / amputation**; tPA candidate |

**MRI T2** — ↑ signal in necrotic muscle; not the initial test

![Bone scan algorithm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12105-bone-scan-algorithm.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Frostbite
├─ Field → pad/splint, prevent refreezing; rewarm only if warmth can be maintained
├─ ED → warm IV fluids, tetanus, NSAIDs, RAPID REWARM 40-42°C water bath
│      └─ <24 h, no flow on bone scan, 2nd/3rd degree → IV tPA
└─ Necrosis → wait for demarcation (1-3 mo) → LATE DEBRIDEMENT / AMPUTATION
```

### Prevention – insulated footwear

**Most reliable prevention** — **thermal insulation footwear** / reduce shoe heat loss (toes: little muscle → little heat)

✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — hard-soled shoes (comfortable shoes ✓), industrial toe-caps, compression stockings, high-protein diet, warm drinks, triple socks, carbo-loading

### Rewarming

**Water bath** — **40–42°C**; **30 min** (Orthobullets) · **15–30 min** (Golant); mild antibacterial

**End point** — skin **pliable and red-purple**

**Avoid 50°C** → thermal burn · lower temp → ↓ survival · **avoid freeze-thaw cycles**

**Hypothermia rewarming** — mild: external (passive/active; active → **"afterdrop"**) · moderate/severe: **internal core** (warm O₂/IV, lavage, bypass, CAVR ~1°C/15 min); avoid alcohol/sedatives

### Acute treatment – blister care & thrombolysis

| Blister | Management |
|---|---|
| **Clear** (superficial) | **Debride + topical aloe vera** (↓ PGF2α, TXB2) |
| **Haemorrhagic** (deep) | **Drain/aspirate, leave skin intact** → prevents dermal desiccation |
| Intact | Leave, dry gauze |

**Liquid nitrogen finger, haemorrhagic blister** — **drain, leave roof intact**\
✗ Wrong options: HBO · full debridement + flap · MRI · wet-to-dry

**IV tPA (tissue plasminogen activator)** — **within 24 h** → **↓ digital amputation**; indication: **no flow on bone scan**, **2nd/3rd degree**\
✗ Wrong options: superficial

**Unconscious in snow, cold blue digits, no cap refill, 2PD >15 mm** — **tPA**\
✗ Wrong options: diltiazem · amlodipine [Raynaud's] · collagenase [Dupuytren] · diclofenac

| tPA contraindications | |
|---|---|
| General | **Alcoholic** (head injury bleed), active bleeding, ICH/surgery <3 mo, **concurrent trauma**, major surgery <14 d, aneurysm/AVM, bleeding diathesis, pregnancy, labile HTN |
| **Cold-related** | **>24 h cold exposure**, **warm ischaemia >6 h**, **multiple freeze-thaw** |

**Other adjuncts** — LMW dextran, anticoagulants; **HBO anecdotal**; IV Abx only if infected

**Rehab** — whirlpool hydrotherapy, PT/OT

### Delayed debridement/amputation after demarcation

**Timing** — **after demarcation, 1–3 months** → **"frostbite in January, amputate in July"**\
✗ Wrong options: days · weeks

**Before demarcation, no sepsis** — **moist dressings + observation** (like burns; much may be superficial)

**Immediate surgery only** — **escharotomy** (circumferential constriction) · **fasciotomy** (compartment syndrome)

**Sympathectomy** — ↓ pain duration + time to demarcation; **no ↓ extent of necrosis**

![Frostbite hand at 12 h](../../MCQ/attachments/q5743-a-fig-a.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Adult | Detail / treatment |
|---|---|
| **Persistent pain** | **50%** (intolerable 15%) |
| **Cold intolerance** | — |
| **Vasospasm** | **Raynaud's**, colour change, **hyperhidrosis** → CCB, vasodilators, β-blockers, late sympathectomy |
| Neuropathy | ↓ NCV → decompression (CTR) |
| **Frostbite arthropathy** | Subchondral bone loss, osteopenia, contractures **DIP > PIP** → arthroplasty |

| Child | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Premature physeal closure** | **1–2 y after exposure** (chondrocyte injury) |
| Late (>10 y age) | Joint laxity, angular deformity, **short digits**, excess skin, DJD → physeal arrest, osteotomy, arthrodesis |

**Severity ↑** — **alcohol intoxication**, skin contact with metal/ice, high wind chill

## X vs Y

| | Chilblain | Trench foot | Frostnip | Frostbite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Freezing | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Wet/dry | **Dry** | **Wet** | — | — |
| Tissue loss | No | ± | No (reversible) | Yes |

## How it's asked

**"Most reliable way to reduce toe frostbite?"** → **thermal insulation footwear**

**"Most important measure hiking in extreme cold?"** → **reduce thermal heat loss from shoes**

**"Liquid nitrogen, haemorrhagic blister — next?"** → **drain, leave overlying skin intact**

**"Found in snow bank, absent cap refill — medication?"** → **tPA** (within 24 h)

**"When to debride/amputate?"** → **after demarcation, 1–3 months**

**"Rewarming bath temperature?"** → **40–42°C**\
✗ Wrong options: 50°C

**"Stranded days, gangrenous toes, no sepsis — next morning?"** → **moist dressings + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: amputation

**"Growth problem after childhood frostbite?"** → **premature physeal closure 1–2 y later**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ganglion Cysts

*Hand · 36 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & sites

**Commonest** — **most common hand/wrist mass (60–70%)**\
✗ Wrong options: 40%

**Age** — 20s–40s (TO); trauma history ≥10% (TO)

**Lower limb** — 15–20% of all ganglia; ⅓ of these around **knee**

| Site | % | Origin |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal carpal** | **70%** (Orthobullets) · 60–70% (TO) | **Scapholunate ligament / SL articulation** (most common stalk); over 3rd/4th compartments, between EDC & EPL |
| Volar carpal | 20% (Orthobullets) · 13–20% (TO) | Radiocarpal or **STT** (Orthobullets, OB) · **radioscaphoid–SL interval > scaphotrapezial > metacarpotrapezial** (TO, in that order) |
| Volar retinacular (flexor sheath) | 10% | Herniated tendon-sheath fluid; A1/A2 pulley |
| **Dorsal DIP (mucous cyst)** | — | DIP OA, **Heberden's nodes** |

**Children** — **volar > dorsal (1.2:1)**; higher ♀:♂

### Pathology & origin

**Causes** — trauma, **mucoid degeneration**, **synovial herniation**

**Contents** — **mucin** from tendon sheath or joint; clear yellow viscous gel

**Lining** — **no true epithelial lining**; **no true synovial lining**, fibrous layer (Orthobullets, OB4424) · "**synovial cells with mucin accumulation**" (OB8790 answer) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: epithelial

**Histology** — **mucin-filled space with occasional spindled fibroblasts**

**Associations (volar)** — median/ulnar nerve compression; hand ischaemia (vascular occlusion)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — usually asymptomatic; cosmesis; may wax & wane

**Exam** — **firm, well circumscribed**, **transilluminates**; fixed to deep tissue, **not fixed to skin**

**Volar wrist** — **Allen's test** (radial artery adjacent)

![Dorsal wrist ganglion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6086-ganglioncysts-fig1.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray | **Normal** |
| **MRI** | Not routine; well-marginated, **homogeneous fluid signal: T1 low, T2 high**; diagnostic if uncertainty remains |
| **Ultrasound** | **Homogeneous anechoic**, well-defined; **differentiates cyst from aneurysm**/vascular malformation; guides aspiration away from artery |
| Biopsy | Not routine; specimen always sent post-op |

**Most definitive confirmation (OB)** — **needle aspiration + cytology** (excludes myxoid neoplasm)\
✗ Wrong options: observe 1 y · gadolinium MRI · X-ray · manual rupture

![Ganglion MRI: homogeneous T2-bright mass](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6086-wrist-ganglion-mri.jpg)

### Histology vs MRI of look-alikes

| Lesion | Histology | MRI |
|---|---|---|
| **Ganglion** | **Mucin + spindled fibroblasts** | **T1 low / T2 high** |
| GCT tendon sheath | **Uniform stromal + giant cells** / histiocytes with giant cells | **Low T1 + low T2**; recurs |
| Spindle-cell lipoma | Mature fat + spindle cells | High T1 + T2 |
