| Nerve sheath tumour | Fibroblasts + Schwann, mast cells, lymphocytes | T1 hypo / T2 hyper |
| Pyogenic granuloma | Lobular vascular proliferation + inflammation | T1 iso to muscle / T2 high |
| Abscess | PMNs | — |

## 5 Management

```
Ganglion
├─ Child → observation (± splint)
│      (76–80% resolve within 1 y)
├─ Adult, asymptomatic → observation (1st line)
├─ Adult dorsal, wants Rx → aspiration (2nd line; avoid volar — radial artery)
└─ Severe sx / NV compression / recurrence → marginal excision of stalk + capsule cuff
```

### Recurrence rates

| Treatment | Recurrence |
|---|---|
| Closed rupture | High (home remedy) |
| **Aspiration** | **50%** (Orthobullets, OB4424) · **40–60%** (OB211501); repeat aspiration success only **30–50%** (TO) |
| Observation | Recurrence 50–60% (OB) |
| **Open excision** | **15–20%** (volar higher than dorsal) |
| Arthroscopic excision | **Lower** than open (OB) |
| Intralesional excision | High |

**TO adjuncts** — steroid after aspiration / hyaluronidase before aspiration → **do not improve success**

### Surgical excision

**Gold standard (TO)** — surgical excision of **cyst + pedicle + cuff of joint capsule**; recurrence from missed tortuous capsular ducts

**Margin** — **marginal excision**\
✗ Wrong options: wide · intralesional

**Arthroscopic (TO)** — viewing **6R**, working **3-4** (through ganglion); excise root; if stalk not found → resect wide dorsal capsule

**Mucous cyst** — excise cyst + **resect DIP osteophyte**

### Paediatric ganglia – observation & spontaneous resolution

**Resolution** — **76% within 1 y** (Orthobullets, OB217981) · **79%** (OB2880) · nearly 80% (OB211160) → **observe**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · biopsy · MRI · excision

**Aspiration in children** — recurrence 13–85%, worse if off tendon; excision recurrence 6–35%

**Volar palm base of index, 3 y** — tendon-sheath ganglion → **reassure, will resolve**

### Special locations – retinacular cyst, mucous cyst, Guyon's canal, proximal tibiofibular joint

**Retinacular cyst** — **2–8 mm, pea-sized**, from **A1/A2 pulley**, at **MCP flexion crease**; **does not move with tendon**; aspiration may work; excise with small portion of sheath/pulley

**Mucous cyst** — DIP ganglion in **OA** (dorsal osteophyte), **nail deformity** → excise + debride osteophyte\
✗ Wrong options: inclusion cyst · synovial sarcoma · metastasis · felon

![Mucous cyst with nail groove](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6086-mucous-cyst.jpg)

**Guyon's canal ganglion** — ulnar to hook of hamate → ulnar motor palsy (**Froment**, **adductor pollicis sharp waves**, no numbness) → **excise**; ulnar **nerve dorsal + ulnar to artery**; DDx hypothenar hammer (jack-hammer)

**Proximal tibiofibular ganglion, recurrent** — **PTFJ arthrodesis = lowest recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: interposition arthroplasty · phenol / liquid nitrogen (ABC adjuvants) · aspiration

## 6 Complications

**Aspiration** — infection (rare), NV injury

**Excision** — infection, **radial artery injury (most common NV)**, **SL ligament injury**, stiffness, recurrence

## How it's asked

**"Dorsal wrist ganglion stalk origin?"** → **scapholunate ligament** (TO, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: STT · radioscaphoid–SL interval · CMC

**"True of this dorsal wrist mass?"** → **lacks a true epithelial lining**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration low recurrence · STT origin · wide excision

**"Best way to confirm dorsal wrist ganglion?"** → **needle aspiration + cytology**

**"Volar wrist ganglion — resection & histology?"** → **marginal excision; synovial cells with mucin**\
✗ Wrong options: histiocytes + giant cells = GCT

**"Histology of post-sprain dorsal wrist mass?"** → **mucin-filled space with spindled fibroblasts**

**"10-year-old, transilluminating dorsal wrist mass?"** → **observation**

**"Counsel parents of child with volar finger-base cyst?"** → **will likely resolve with observation**

**"True of paediatric ganglion?"** → **>75% resolve within 1 y**

**"True of volar wrist ganglion management?"** → **open excision ~20% recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration 33% · 40% of masses · open better than arthroscopic

**"DIP mass + nail deformity + dorsal osteophyte?"** → **mucous cyst**

**"Ganglion in Guyon's canal — nerve position?"** → **ulnar nerve ulnar and dorsal to ulnar artery**

**"Inconsistent with retinacular cyst?"** → **moves with the tendon**

**"Recurrent proximal tibiofibular ganglion — lowest recurrence?"** → **PTFJ arthrodesis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Giant Cell Tumor of Tendon Sheath

*Hand · 37 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Synonyms** — pigmented villonodular tumour of tendon sheath · **tenosynovial giant cell tumour (TGCT)**

**Nature** — **benign** nodular tumour from synovial lining of tendon sheath; idiopathic; **no metastasis, no malignant transformation reported**

**Epidemiology** — **2nd commonest hand soft-tissue tumour after ganglion** · **commonest SOLID soft-tissue mass** in hand · 3rd–5th decade

**Site** — **palmar radial 3 digits near DIPJ**; also foot/ankle tendon sheaths (dorsum, web space)

**GCTTS vs PVNS** — **histologically identical**; GCTTS almost always **extra-articular** · PVNS diffuse, intra-articular, larger joints

### Genes & lab markers

**Histology** — **multinucleated giant cells**, sheets of **polygonal/histiocyte-like mononuclear cells**, **hemosiderin-laden** & **lipid/foam** macrophages, bland fibrous/spindled stroma, lymphocytes

**Hemosiderin** — present, typically **less than PVNS**

**Gross** — **lobulated, multicoloured: yellow-brown**, red, grey

**CSF1R** — CSF1R inhibitors for **diffuse-type TGCT** when surgery too morbid\
✗ Wrong options: routine after excision

![GCTTS histology: giant cells and hemosiderin](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6092-gctts-histo.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — slowly enlarging mass (months–years); pain with activity / **shoe wear** (foot); mechanical block, ↓ joint flexion

**Exam** — **firm nodular mass, does not transilluminate** (transilluminates = ganglion); may be fixed to bone

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — soft-tissue swelling; **pressure erosion of bone** in up to **5%**

**US** — relation to tendon; homogeneously **hypoechoic**; **internal vascularity**

**MRI** — **modality of choice**; **↓ signal on T1 AND T2** (hemosiderin; Orthobullets, OB) · OB213042/OB219922: T1 iso/hypo, T2 may be **hyperintense**, enhances — read the options

![MRI: low-signal GCTTS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6092-gctts-mri.jpg)

**Differential** — ganglion (cystic, transilluminates), PVNS, desmoid, fibroma/fibrosarcoma, glomangioma

## 5 Management

```
GCTTS
├─ Primary → MARGINAL EXCISION (complete, not piecemeal)
│      └─ excised → ROUTINE FOLLOW-UP
├─ Local recurrence → REPEAT EXCISION
└─ Diffuse, unresectable → CSF1R INHIBITOR
```

**Not indicated** — radical/wide excision, ray resection, chemotherapy, routine radiation (lower recurrence reported but wound complications/fibrosis)

### Local recurrence after excision

**Commonest complication of incomplete excision** — **recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: metastasis · malignant degeneration · pathological fracture · infection

**Recurrence rate** — **5–50%** (Orthobullets) · 4–44% (Lanzinger) · 27% at mean 27 mo (Reilly)

| Recurrence risk factors |
|---|
| **Incomplete/piecemeal excision** |
| **Poorly encapsulated / diffuse**, satellite lesions |
| **Intra-articular** extension, deep to volar plate |
| **Flexor/extensor tendon** involvement |
| **Intraosseous involvement / osseous erosion** |
| Adjacent **degenerative joint disease** |
| **Distal location** (DIP finger / IP thumb) |
| Not: age, sex, size, volar/dorsal, histology |

## X vs Y

| | **GCTTS** | Ganglion | Epithelioid sarcoma | Gouty tophus | Synovial sarcoma |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Key | Solid, yellow-brown, giant cells + hemosiderin | **Transilluminates**, cystic | **Commonest hand sarcoma**; epithelioid nodules, central necrosis/ulceration | **White chalky**, negatively birefringent crystals | **Commonest foot sarcoma**; mineralised 30%, heterogeneous MRI |

## How it's asked

**"Painful firm foot mass with shoe wear, GCTTS histology — true?"** → **marginal resection**\
✗ Wrong options: like GCT of bone · transilluminates · metastasis · radical excision

**"Enlarging hand mass, multicoloured lobulated, giant cells?"** → **GCTTS**\
✗ Wrong options: ganglion · synovial sarcoma

**"Foot web-space mass, hypointense T1 & T2, brown specimen?"** → **GCTTS**\
✗ Wrong options: melanoma · neuroma

**"Finger mass eroding phalanx cortex, yellow-brown, hemosiderin?"** → **GCTTS**\
✗ Wrong options: epithelioid sarcoma · tophus

**"Commonest complication of incomplete excision?"** → **recurrence**

**"After marginal excision — next step?"** → **routine follow-up**\
✗ Wrong options: wide re-excision · radiation · chemo

**"Intraosseous, poorly encapsulated — counsel?"** → **↑ local recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: CSF1R inhibitor · radiation

**"Histologically identical to?"** → **PVNS**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Gymnast's Wrist (Distal Radial Physeal Stress Syndrome)

*Hand · 38 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — up to **25% of non-elite gymnasts**; radiographic physeal stress changes in **up to 85%** of gymnasts studied

**Dose-response** — training intensity correlates with ulna–radius length discrepancy

**Load** — distal radius bears **80% of axial load** on the extended wrist

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathophysiology** — wrist used as weight-bearing joint → supraphysiological repetitive load → **physeal inflammation** → microtrauma → **premature closure of distal radial physis** → **secondary ulnar overgrowth**

**Associations** — **distal ulnar overgrowth**, **positive ulnar variance**

**Commonest injuring skills** — front/back handsprings, saltos/flips

### Distal radial physeal stress – growth arrest & Madelung deformity

**Growth arrest risk in gymnasts** — **distal radius** > scaphoid, distal humerus, clavicle, shoulder

**Upper-limb weight bearers** — stress fractures occur **distal to the elbow**

**Physeal bar** — forms in **ulnar aspect of distal radial physis** → relative ulnar overgrowth → ulnocarpal abutment → **Madelung deformity** (depends on growth remaining)

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — distal radial *overgrowth*) · compression pseudofractures (Looser zones = osteomalacia/rickets) · radiocapitellar instability (nursemaid's) · ulnar longitudinal deficiency (club hand)\
✗ Wrong options: — radius is short

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — chronic wrist pain, usually **radial-sided**

**Exam** — **tenderness over distal radius**, ± swelling, ↓ flexion/extension from pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical + radiographs

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray (AP + lateral)** | **Widened distal radial physis with ill-defined, sclerotic borders**; chronic → **positive ulnar variance** |
| MRI (chronic / non-responders) | **Paraphyseal oedema**, early physeal bridging, radial bone bruise |
| CT | **Physeal bar** (ulnar side of distal radial physis) |

![Widened distal radial physis](../../MCQ/attachments/q2198-illa-ap-gymnast-wrist.jpg)

![Positive ulnar variance after physeal arrest](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6052-ap-ulnar-variance.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Gymnast's wrist
├─ First line → NSAIDs + REST + IMMOBILISATION 3–6 WK
└─ Premature physeal closure
       ├─ Small bar → PHYSEAL BAR RESECTION
       └─ Large closure (~50% physis) → ULNAR EPIPHYSIODESIS ± SHORTENING ± RADIAL OSTEOTOMY
```

**Prevention** — proper technique, wrist guards/braces, ↓ repetitions during growth spurts, shoulder flexibility + thoracic mobility, core stabilisation

**Return to play** — pain resolved, normal wrist function, completed progressive rehab, proper technique

**Prognosis** — good with early treatment

## 6 Complications

**Premature distal radial physeal closure** — ulnar overgrowth → ulnocarpal abutment → **Madelung deformity**, DRUJ instability\
✗ Wrong options: PRUJ

## How it's asked

**"Gymnast — which UE stress fracture most likely causes growth arrest?"** → **distal radius**

**"Physeal bar on CT, lost to follow-up — at risk of?"** → **Madelung deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: distal radial overgrowth · Looser zones

**"X-ray finding?"** → **widened physis, ill-defined sclerotic borders**

**"First-line treatment?"** → **rest, NSAIDs, immobilisation 3–6 wk**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# High-Pressure Injection Injuries

*Hand · 39 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare; **1 in 600** hand traumas

**Typical patient** — **young male laborer**; paint, grease, solvents, diesel

**Commonest digit** — **non-dominant index finger** (>50%; 84% non-dominant hand in Bean series)

### Pathophysiology

**Force** — **3,000–10,000 PSI**, up to 400 MPH

**Skin breach** — **100 PSI** breaks skin\
✗ Wrong options: 80 PSI

**4 components** — initial injury → **chemical irritation** → inflammation → **secondary infection**

**Spread** — along planes of least resistance = **neurovascular bundles**\
✗ Wrong options: flexor sheath

**Vascular occlusion** — local soft-tissue necrosis, thrombosis, ischaemia → compartment-syndrome-like picture

## 2 Mechanism

### Injected substance & amputation risk

| Substance | Destructiveness |
|---|---|
| **Water, air** | Least; non-toxic → only ones allowing nonop trial |
| **Latex / water-based paint** | Low |
| Grease, chlorofluorocarbon | Intermediate; aggressive debridement restores reasonable function |
| **Oil-based paint** | Highly inflammatory → chronic inflammation → amputation despite early surgery |
| **Organic solvents** (paint thinner, diesel, jet fuel, gasoline, oil) | **Highest amputation risk, up to 50%** |

**Most soft-tissue damage long term** — **oil-based paint**

**Highest amputation risk** — **paint thinner / organic solvents** (some authors: primary amputation)

**Temperature** — **high temperature → worse**\
✗ Wrong options: room temperature

**Organic solvents & infection** — may ↓ infection (do not support bacterial growth)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — document **time since injury** (average ~9 h to physician) + **material injected**

**Entry wound** — **benign-looking**, minimal/no pain; only **1 in 4** present to ED initially

**Entry wound size** — does not correlate with severity (>3 cm wound not prognostic)

**Later** — pain + paresthesias; finger **bloated, oedematous, tense, pale, cold**

![Benign-looking entry wound, index finger](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12104-high-injection.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**: history + inspection

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — show spread of **radio-opaque paint/dyes**; often unremarkable

![Radiograph: radio-opaque paint tracking](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12104-radiograph.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
High-pressure injection
├─ Paint / grease / solvent / oil → emergent OR debridement + IV antibiotics + tetanus
│      └─ repeat I&D 24–72 h; admit
└─ Water or air → admit, IV antibiotics, tetanus, elevation, observe
       └─ compartment syndrome / worsening pain → emergent debridement of entire zone + fasciotomy
```

### Management – urgent debridement, antibiotics & decompression

**First line (even 3 days late)** — **immediate debridement in the OR**\
✗ Wrong options: bedside I&D with digital block · IV then oral abx · amputation · clinic referral

**Paint thinner, swollen finger** — **surgical debridement & lavage**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · corticosteroids · neutralising agent · antibiotics alone

**Neutralising agents** — only for **hydrofluoric acid** or chemo extravasation

**Steroids & infection** — do not affect amputation rate

**Water injection + ↓ 2PD, swelling, erythema into palm, can't move** — **emergent operative decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: outpatient because water is non-toxic · because perfused · admit + IV abx only

**Water injection observed → escalating pain** — compartment syndrome → **immediate debridement of entire zone of injury**\
✗ Wrong options: entry site only · cultures · cold compress · amputation

**Antibiotics** — **give before surgery**, don't delay; broad-spectrum **gram + and gram −**

**Analgesia** — **no digital block** and **no ice** (↓ perfusion)

**Nonop care** — tetanus, parenteral abx, elevation, early mobilisation, compartment monitoring; indicated only for **air, water, chicken vaccine**

**Nonop outcome** — **50%** ultimately need surgery; delay → ↑ reoperation & complications

**Approach** — **Bruner** palmar or **midaxial** incisions → full exposure of zone

**Technique** — wide debridement, **decompress compartments**, open tendon sheaths, remove all material, saline irrigation, **repeat I&D 24–72 h**

**Post-op** — **admit** for observation, possible second debridement/fasciotomy

### Prognostic factors

**Worst prognostic variable (MCQ)** — **delay in surgical treatment**

| Factor | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Time to treatment** | Debride **<6 h** → ↓ amputation & complications; **>10 h** → ↑ amputation |
| Force of injection | ↑ pressure → worse |
| Volume injected | ↑ volume → worse |
| **Composition** | Solvents / oil-based paints worst |
| Temperature | Hot → worse |
| Location | Tendon sheath involvement, proximal spread → worse |

![Debridement of injection injury](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12104-high-pressure.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Amputation** — **48%** of finger injuries; ~50% with oil-based paint / organic solvents; Hogan series 30%

**Infection** — **42% positive cultures, mostly polymicrobial**; delay ↑ risk; necrotic tissue = culture medium

**Long term** — residual pain, stiffness, ↓ sensation, difficulty returning to work

![Oil injection injury](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12104-oil-injection-injury-moved.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Most soft-tissue damage long term?"** → **oil-based paint**\
✗ Wrong options: water, latex, grease, CFC

**"Highest amputation risk?"** → **paint thinner** (organic solvent)

**"Worst prognostic variable?"** → **delay in surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: wound >3 cm · grease · room temp

**"Presents 3 days later, first-line?"** → **immediate OR debridement**

**"Paint thinner, swollen finger — next step?"** → **surgical debridement & lavage**

**"Water gun, ↓ 2PD, can't move finger?"** → **emergent operative decompression**

**"Observed water injection, now severe pain?"** → **debride entire zone of injury**

**"True statement?"** → **antibiotics before surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: 80 PSI · digital block · tracks flexor sheath · no timing difference

**"Pressure that breaks skin?"** → **100 PSI**

**"Which may be managed nonop?"** → **water, air**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hook of Hamate Fracture

*Hand · 40 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **2–4% of carpal fractures**; ♂:♀ 2:1; often missed (mean **4 wk** to diagnosis)

**Sports** — **golf, baseball, hockey**, racquet sports; **non-dominant hand** in batters

### Anatomy

**Articulations** — 4th & 5th MC, capitate, triquetrum

**Flexor retinaculum** — hook = palpable attachment

**Deep motor branch ulnar n.** — lies **under the hook**

**Blood supply** — radial + ulnar foramina at base; **ulnar vessel absent 29%** → high nonunion

| Guyon's canal border | Structure |
|---|---|
| Roof | Superficial palmar carpal ligament |
| Floor | Deep flexor retinaculum, hypothenar muscles |
| Ulnar | Pisiform, pisohamate lig. |
| Radial | **Hook of hamate** |

**Not a border** — **capitate**

**Ulnar tunnel zones** — 1 (motor + sensory) & 2 (motor): **ganglion, hook of hamate fracture**; 3 (sensory): **ulnar artery thrombosis**

## 2 Mechanism

**Direct blow to volar proximal palm** — grounding golf club, **checking a bat**, fouling a pitch, racket/oar strike; shear from ring/small flexors; FOOSH

**Associated** — **bipartite hamate** (smooth cortices = normal variant, not fracture) · ring/small **flexor tendonitis/rupture** · **ulnar neuropathy in Guyon** (often motor) · other carpal fracture

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **ulnar wrist pain** (commonest), hypothenar pain, pain with tight grip; ± ring/small paraesthesia

**Exam** — **hook tenderness (80% sensitive)**; ↓ ulnar deviation, ↓ grip; Tinel at Guyon; intrinsic weakness

**Hook of hamate pull test** — ulnar deviation + resisted DIP flexion ring/small → pain (70% sens)

**Ulnar nerve clue** — **dorsal ulnar sensation normal** → lesion at wrist\
✗ Wrong options: cubital tunnel

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| PA/lateral | 10% sensitivity; PA: **absent "eye sign"** (cortical ring) |
| **Carpal tunnel view** | **Best/most sensitive radiograph**, **first test** (40%) |
| Semi-supinated oblique | Adjunct |
| **CT** | Confirms if X-rays negative (92%; missed if cuts >1 mm) — **next step** |
| **MRI** | **Most accurate** (100%) with high suspicion |
| Bone scan | Confirms with CT (OB) · not typically indicated (OB) — read the options |
| ✗ Wrong option: clenched fist | SL ligament |
| ✗ Wrong option: MRA | Hypothenar hammer |

![Carpal tunnel view](../../MCQ/attachments/q1425-illb-radiograph-carpal-tunnel-view.jpg)

![Axial CT hook fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6035-hook-of-hamate-fracture-axial-ct.jpg)

### Milch classification

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Hook** fracture (commonest): I-I avulsion · I-II middle · I-III base |
| II | Body fracture: IIA coronal · IIB transverse |

## 5 Management

```
Hook of hamate fracture
├─ Acute nondisplaced, low demand → ulnar gutter cast 6 wk
│      (nonunion 40–50%, mostly painless)
├─ Chronic symptomatic / nonunion / ulnar neuritis / tendon irritation → EXCISION
├─ High-level athlete → EXCISION (RTP ~6 wk)
└─ Acute displaced, can't lose grip → ORIF (countersunk headless screw)
```

### Treatment – fragment excision & return to play

**Excision** — **surgical treatment of choice**; fastest return; **RTP 6 wk** (some 3 mo)

**Indications** — chronic symptomatic nonunion, **ulnar neuritis**, flexor tendon irritation/grating, high-level athlete

✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — Guyon release alone, carpal tunnel release, pisiform excision, cast for chronic nonunion\
✗ Wrong options: tendon

**Technique** — incision along **ulnar border of ring finger**; release Guyon; **subperiosteal** removal (protect deep motor br.)

**Excision cost** — small finger FDP strength ↓ **10–15%**; FDP shifts 5 mm ulnar

**ORIF** — ~100% union, theoretically better grip; screws **countersunk** (deep motor br.); neurolysis if neuritis

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | **Commonest**, ~50%; motion + poor supply |
| **Ulnar neuritis** | 20% → excision |
| **Closed rupture ring/small flexors** | **Hook nonunion** (fulcrum); rare |
| Weak grip | Excision of large fragment |
| Recurrence | Pro baseball; prevent with extraperiosteal excision |

**Other closed ruptures** — volar plate DRF → **FPL** · closed DRF → **EPL** · scaphoid spur → radial flexors

## How it's asked

**"Baseball player, hypothenar pain + ulnar paraesthesia — view?"** → **carpal tunnel view**

**"Chronic golfer wrist pain, initial test"** → **carpal tunnel view**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan, EMG, MRA

**"Mechanic, interossei weak, dorsal sensation normal, X-ray"** → **hamate nonunion**

**"Not a border of Guyon's canal"** → **capitate**

**"Rower, oar into palm, carpal tunnel view"** → **hook of hamate fracture**

**"Pro baseball, 1 yr hypothenar pain, CT fracture — Rx"** → **hook excision**

**"Best literature outcome / RTP"** → **surgical excision**

**"Ulnar nerve sx + grating fingers 6 mo after FOOSH"** → **excision of hook**

**"Return to play after excision"** → **6 wk**

**"True of CT finding"** → **excision → excellent RTP**\
✗ Wrong options: normal variant · APB weakness · high union nonop

**"Closed small-finger flexor rupture from"** → **hook of hamate nonunion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Human Bite

*Hand · 41 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — ~2% of bite wounds; **3rd after dog & cat**; ♂

**Location** — **dorsal 3rd/4th MCP** = **"fight bite"**

### Pathoanatomy

**Mechanism** — clenched fist → tooth → **MCP capsule penetrated** → fist opens → bacteria **trapped under extensor tendon/capsule**

**Also** — direct bite (child biting child)

**Associated** — **extensor tendon laceration** (missed — proximal retraction)

### Microbiology – polymicrobial & Eikenella

**Most common isolate** — **polymicrobial** (~4 isolates/culture; saliva >600 species)

**Commonest single organisms** — **α-haemolytic strep (S. viridans)** + **S. aureus** (S. aureus = overall most common pathogen)

**Eikenella corrodens** — **facultative anaerobic gram-neg rod**; **characteristic of human bites** (rare in dog/cat); 7–29% (Orthobullets) · 17–30% / ~⅓ (OB)

**Eikenella resistance** — **aminoglycosides, 1st-gen cephalosporins, clindamycin**

| Bite | Infection rate | Signature organism |
|---|---|---|
| Dog | 5–15% | Pasteurella canis, Capnocytophaga |
| Cat | **30–50%** | Pasteurella multocida |
| **Human** | High | **Eikenella corrodens** |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — punch to mouth, often **concealed** → treat as fight bite until proven otherwise; delayed presentation

**Exam** — small **transverse, irregular** dorsal MCP wound; erythema, warmth, ± pus; pain on passive MCP ROM; **check extensor function**; volar side & NV usually spared

![Fight bite over 3rd MCP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6104-fight-bite.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **tooth fragment (FB)** + fracture (MC head)

**Culture** — not in ED (contaminated); **deep aerobic + anaerobic in OR**

## 5 Management

```
Fight bite
├─ All fight bites / joint or sheath involved → I&D + IV AMPICILLIN-SULBACTAM
│      └─ discharge → PO AMOX-CLAVULANATE 5-7 d
└─ PIP joint bite → SERIAL DEBRIDEMENT + prolonged Abx
```

**I&D** — debride wound + **joint capsule**; **leave open**; Gram stain + culture

**MCP approach** — **tendon-splitting** (Shewring)

**IV Abx** — cover **Staph, Strep, gram-neg** → **ampicillin/sulbactam**; alternatives: cephalosporin, gentamicin + penicillinase-resistant penicillin, **penicillin + metronidazole**

**Outcome** — MCP injuries satisfactory/good; **PIP injuries → 42% poor**

## X vs Y

| | Human bite | Dog / cat bite |
|---|---|---|
| Characteristic organism | **Eikenella corrodens** | **Pasteurella** |
| Also | S. viridans, S. aureus, anaerobes | Capnocytophaga (dog), Corynebacterium, Moraxella |
| Typical site | Dorsal MCP (fist) | Any (cat → joints/sheath) |
| Oral Abx | Amox-clav | Amox-clav |

## How it's asked

**"Fight bite — most common culture result?"** → **polymicrobial**\
✗ Wrong options: Eikenella alone · Pasteurella

**"Organism more frequent in human than animal bites?"** → **Eikenella corrodens**\
✗ Wrong options: Capnocytophaga, P. canis, S. aureus

**"IV antibiotic for fight bite?"** → **ampicillin-sulbactam**

**"Eikenella resistant to?"** → **clindamycin, 1st-gen cephalosporins, aminoglycosides**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hypothenar Hammer Syndrome

*Hand · 42 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & anatomy

**Definition** — post-traumatic digital ischaemia from **ulnar artery thrombosis / aneurysm at Guyon's canal**

**Demographics** — rare (<1/100,000); **♂:♀ 9:1**; 40s–50s

**Location** — unilateral, dominant **ring ± small** finger; less often index/middle; **thumb spared**

**Risk** — **vibrating tools** (carpenters, mechanics, jackhammer, construction); baseball catchers, mountain biking, golf, volleyball, karate, MMA

**Anatomy** — distal 2 cm: ulnar artery **directly anterior to hook of hamate**, covered only by palmaris brevis, fat, skin; divides at Guyon's exit → deep branch + superficial palmar arch

**Ulnar artery** — **most common site of true and false aneurysms** in the hand; aneurysm = permanent dilatation **≥50%** of normal

### Pathology – true aneurysm vs thrombosis & recurrence

**Mechanism** — single or **repetitive blunt impact** on hypothenar eminence → **hook of hamate acts as an anvil** → thrombosis or aneurysm → **distal embolisation** → ulcers, gangrene

**Hook of hamate fracture** — may precipitate ulnar artery aneurysm (catcher)

| | **True aneurysm** (HHS) | False / pseudoaneurysm |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **Blunt** (repetitive) trauma | **Penetrating** trauma |
| Wall | **All arterial layers**, true endothelial lining | No endothelial lining |
| Shape | **Gradual uniform dilatation** | Haematoma organises → recanalises → false lumen |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain hypothenar eminence + **ring finger**, **cold sensitivity**, paraesthesia ulnar 2 digits (± ulnar nerve compression)

**Signs** — **hypothenar callus**, tenderness, blanching, mottling, cyanosis, pallor, **ulnar fingertip ulcers**, **splinter haemorrhages**, **pulsatile mass** if aneurysm

**Allen's test** — **positive with occlusion**; **negative with aneurysm** (patent); may show incomplete palmar arch

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**First-line** — **Doppler US** + **digital brachial index (DBI)**\
✗ Wrong options: MRA first

**Angiography (CTA / MRA / conventional)** — **mandatory for diagnosis** (Orthobullets); contrast arteriography = **gold standard** (Hui-Chou)

**Angiogram signs** — **"corkscrew" tortuous ulnar artery**; **occlusion or aneurysm at hook of hamate**

**Carpal tunnel view** — hook of hamate fracture

![Angiogram: ulnar artery aneurysm at the hook of hamate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6097-aneurysm-angio.jpg)

### Digital brachial index criteria

| DBI | Meaning / action |
|---|---|
| **0.85–1.25** | Normal |
| **>0.7** | Minimal symptoms, viable digit → **no surgery** / ligation acceptable |
| **<0.7** | **Reconstruction** |
| **<0.5** | Severe symptoms, **digital gangrene** |

**Differential** — **Raynaud's** involves the **thumb** (HHS spares it)

## 5 Management

```
Hypothenar hammer syndrome
├─ Thrombosis, no aneurysm
│      ├─ <2 wk → endovascular fibrinolysis
│      └─ >2 wk / asymptomatic / no threat → nonop (80% success)
└─ Aneurysm / DBI <0.7 / multi-digit ischaemia / failed nonop → excision + reconstruction
       └─ DBI >0.7 → ligation (Leriche) acceptable
```

**Nonop** — smoking cessation, avoid trauma, vascular consult; **80% success**

### Treatment – resection & arterial reconstruction

**Operative criteria** — **DBI <0.7** · **thrombosis with aneurysm** · ischaemia multiple digits · recurrent symptoms after nonop

**Aneurysm** — **always surgical**: **excision of segment + reconstruction ± vein graft** → prevents emboli, removes painful mass, relieves ulnar nerve, removes vasospasm trigger

**Graft** — **reversed interpositional vein graft** (saphenous, forearm, foot dorsum) or arterial (thoracodorsal, SIEA, descending LFCA); gap usually too large for direct repair

**Not for aneurysm** — fibrinolysis (acute thrombosis without aneurysm only) · **systemic thrombolysis** (never) · **stenting** (stenosis, not thrombosis) · warfarin · CCB / abstinence alone

**Ligation** — **contraindicated with incomplete palmar arch**

**Ligation vs repair vs vein graft** — **no outcome difference** (Demetri)\
✗ Wrong options: "ligation superior"

**Outcomes** — vein graft patency **77% at 2 y** (Chloros); **78% occluded at ~10 y** yet patients satisfied (Endress)

## 6 Complications

**Most common post-op complaint** — **persistent cold intolerance**\
✗ Wrong options: digital ischaemia

| Vein-graft failure | Time | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **Acute** | **<1 mo** | **Thrombosis** (patency 98% → <88% in 1st month) |
| **Subacute** | **1–18 mo** | **Intimal hyperplasia** = vascular smooth muscle migration + proliferation at anastomosis (30–50% fail) |

**Not failure causes at 6 mo** — graft resorption, recurrent aneurysm, hamate nonunion compression, thrombosis (acute only)

## X vs Y

| | **HHS** | **Raynaud's** |
|---|---|---|
| Side | Unilateral, dominant | Bilateral |
| Digits | Ring ± small, **thumb spared** | Includes **thumb** |
| Cause | Blunt trauma, hook of hamate anvil | Vasospasm |
| Angio | Corkscrew, occlusion/aneurysm at hamate | — |

## How it's asked

**"Jackhammer worker, hypothenar callus, pulsatile mass — true statement?"** → **gradual dilatation of all arterial layers (true aneurysm)**

**"DBI that necessitates reconstruction?"** → **<0.7**\
✗ Wrong options: 0.85

**"First-line test?"** → **Doppler US + DBI**\
✗ Wrong options: MRA

**"Most common complication after surgery?"** → **persistent cold intolerance**

**"Pathophysiology of blunt-trauma aneurysm?"** → **true endothelial lining, uniform dilatation**\
✗ Wrong options: recanalised false lumen

**"Vein graft failed at 6 mo — why?"** → **intimal hyperplasia (smooth muscle migration/proliferation)**\
✗ Wrong options: thrombosis = acute

**"HHS with aneurysm and ischaemia — next step?"** → **excision + reconstruction ± vein graft**\
✗ Wrong options: fibrinolysis · warfarin · CCB

**"Aneurysm thrombosed, ulcer, DBI 0.4, incomplete arch?"** → **ulnar artery reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: ligation · stenting · thrombolysis

**"Acute thrombosis <2 wk without aneurysm?"** → **endovascular fibrinolysis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Intersection Syndrome

*Hand · 43 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & anatomy

**Definition** — inflammation where **1st compartment (APL, EPB) muscle bellies cross over 2nd compartment (ECRL, ECRB) tendons**; aka crossover tendinitis; = stenosing tenosynovitis of **2nd compartment**

**Site** — dorsoradial forearm **~5 cm (4–6) proximal to wrist**

**Demographics** — rare; ♂ = ♀ (Orthobullets) · **more in men, dominant hand, younger, shorter symptoms vs DQT** (Sato)

**Risk** — **rowing**, canoeing, skiing, horse riding, weight lifting, racket sports

| Compartment | Contents | Eponymous condition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | APL, EPB | **De Quervain's** |
| **2** | **ECRL, ECRB** | **Intersection syndrome** |
| 3 | EPL | — |
| 4 | EIP, EDC | — |
| 5 | EDM | Vaughan-Jackson |
| 6 | ECU | Snapping ECU |

![Intersection of 1st compartment over ECRL/ECRB](../../MCQ/attachments/q213017-illa-is-tendon-picture.jpg)

| Tendon | Insertion |
|---|---|
| **ECRL** | **Base of 2nd MC** |
| EPB | Dorsal base thumb proximal phalanx |
| EPL | Dorsal base thumb distal phalanx |
| APL | Radial base 1st MC |
| EIP | Extensor hood index MCP |

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **repetitive wrist extension** → friction at crossover → tenosynovitis

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — dorsal forearm/radial wrist pain, worse with pronation–supination

**Signs** — tenderness dorsoradial forearm ~5 cm proximal; **crepitus / squeak / popping** with **resisted wrist extension + thumb extension**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — not required (unremarkable)

**MRI** — only if unclear; fluid-sensitive (STIR, FS PD, T2) → **peritendinous oedema around 1st + 2nd compartments** (most characteristic); tendinosis, muscle oedema, loss of comma shape

![MRI: peritendinous fluid at the intersection](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6032-intersec.jpg)

**Differentials** — De Quervain's, Wartenberg's

## 5 Management

```
Intersection syndrome
├─ First line → rest + wrist splint + steroid into 2nd compartment
└─ Recalcitrant → surgical release + debridement of 2nd compartment
```

### Second dorsal compartment (ECRL/ECRB) – injection & release

**Injection** — into **2nd dorsal compartment (ECRL + ECRB)**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st = DQT

**Surgery** — rare; release **2nd compartment** ~**6 cm proximal to radial styloid** (Orthobullets) · **5–6 cm proximal to wrist** (OB) → decompresses **ECRL (inserts base 2nd MC)**

## X vs Y

| | **Intersection syndrome** | **De Quervain's** |
|---|---|---|
| Compartment | **2nd (ECRL, ECRB)** | **1st (APL, EPB)** |
| Site | **~5 cm proximal** to wrist, dorsoradial forearm | Radial styloid |
| Patient | Younger, men, dominant hand, rowers | ♀ >40, **pregnant / lactating / peripartum** |
| Sign | Crepitus, resisted wrist + thumb extension | Finkelstein |
| Injection | 2nd compartment | 1st compartment |

## How it's asked

**"Rower, tender 5 cm proximal to wrist, inject which compartment?"** → **ECRL + ECRB**\
✗ Wrong options: APL/EPB · EPL

**"Compartment + structures affected?"** → **compartment 2; ECRB + ECRL tendons**\
✗ Wrong options: muscle bellies · compartment 1

**"Release decompresses tendon inserting on?"** → **base of 2nd MC (ECRL)**\
✗ Wrong options: thumb P1 base = EPB · P2 base = EPL · 1st MC = APL · index hood = EIP

**"Peripartum woman with radial wrist pain?"** → **De Quervain's**\
✗ Wrong options: intersection

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Intrinsic Minus Hand (Claw Hand)

*Hand · 44 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Etiology

**Definition** — **MCP hyperextension + PIP/DIP flexion** from **strong extrinsics vs deficient intrinsics**

**Ulnar nerve palsy** — cubital tunnel, ulnar tunnel

**Median nerve palsy** — Volkmann's ischaemic contracture, **leprosy**, crush hand not splinted intrinsic-plus

**Others** — **Charcot-Marie-Tooth** (HMSN), **compartment syndrome of hand**

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism – strong extrinsics & deficient intrinsics

**Loss of intrinsics** — lose MCP flexion + IP extension

**Strong EDC** — unopposed **MCP extension**; not a significant PIP extensor (central slip force mostly from interossei)

**Strong FDP + FDS** — unopposed **PIP & DIP flexion**

**Crush hand splinted in MCP extension** — ↑ extrinsic flexor tension → **PIP flexion** (claw); splint **intrinsic-plus**

**Hand compartment syndrome** — most consistent sign: **tense swollen hand in intrinsic-minus position**; Rx fasciotomy; full function unlikely after ischaemic contracture

![Pathoanatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6010-shows-pathoanatomy.jpg)

| Deformity | Pathoanatomy |
|---|---|
| **Claw / intrinsic minus** | **Strong extrinsics + deficient intrinsics** |
| Intrinsic plus | **Spastic intrinsics + weak extrinsics** → MCP flexion, IP extension |
| Swan neck | **Volar plate laxity** + dorsal lateral band tethering → PIP hyperext, DIP flexion |
| **Lumbrical plus** | **FDP laceration distal to lumbrical origin** → paradoxical IP extension on flexing |
| **Quadriga** | **Adhered FDP** (one finger) → flexion lag adjacent digits |

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — MCP hyperextension + IP flexion; ↓ grasp, grip, pinch

**Ulnar palsy** — worst in **ring & little** (ulnar lumbricals); index/middle less (median lumbricals)

![Claw hand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6010-claw-hand-moved.jpg)

### Ulnar nerve palsy – claw, ulnar paradox & Bouvier test

**Low ulnar (wrist) cut (TO)** — **clawing ring + little** (lumbricals 3–4), **can't abduct/adduct** (interossei; can't abduct middle finger), **can't pinch** (adductor pollicis), thumb hyperextension

**Ulnar supply in hand (TO)** — deep br.: ADM, FDM, ODM, **adductor pollicis**, **deep head FPB**, **all interossei** (PAD/DAB), **medial 2 lumbricals**; superficial br.: palmaris brevis

**Lumbricals (TO)** — arise from FDP → dual supply like FDP

**Ulnar paradox (TO)** — **higher lesion → less clawing**; due to **FDP** (ulnar half) paralysis at/above elbow; distal lesion spares FDP → more claw\
✗ Wrong options: FDS, FCU, FCR

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Bouvier test

**Bouvier (TO)** — guides **claw correction**; block MCP hyperextension (~10° flexion) → watch active PIP extension

| Result | Meaning | Surgery |
|---|---|---|
| **Positive** (PIP extends; assisted angle 0) | **Simple claw**; extensor mechanism intact | Any procedure **preventing MCP hyperextension** → **static** (MCP capsulodesis, pulley advancement) |
| **Negative** (PIP stays flexed) | **Complex claw** | Needs extensor augmentation / flexor or joint correction |

| Negative Bouvier subtype (TO) | Cause | Clue |
|---|---|---|
| Passive positive | **Stretched central slip** (long-standing) | — |
| Passive negative, tenodesis positive | **Long flexor shortening** | PIP extends with **wrist flexed** |
| Passive negative, tenodesis negative | **Stiff PIP** (volar plate / collateral contracture) | No extension even with wrist flexion |

## 5 Management

```
Claw hand (progressive, ↓ QoL)
├─ Bouvier positive (simple) → STATIC: capsulodesis / pulley advancement / tenodesis
└─ Bouvier negative (complex) → active tendon transfer + extensor/flexor/joint correction
```

**Goal** — **prevent MCP hyperextension**

## How it's asked

**"Crush hand splinted in MCP extension — deformity?"** → **PIP flexion**

**"Claw after crush/compartment syndrome — pathoanatomy?"** → **strong extrinsics + deficient intrinsics**\
✗ Wrong options: spastic intrinsics = intrinsic plus

**"Ulnar paradox due to"** → **FDP** (TO)

**"Wrist cut: claw ring/little, can't pinch, can't abduct middle — nerve?"** → **ulnar** (TO)

**"Bouvier test helps correction of"** → **claw deformity** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Intrinsic Plus Hand

*Hand · 45 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **MCP flexion + PIP & DIP extension** from **spastic/contracted intrinsics** vs weak extrinsics

**Pathoanatomy** — tight interossei + lumbricals → MCP flexion, IP extension; **weak EDC** (no MCP extension balance); **weak FDS/FDP** (no IP flexion balance)

## 2 Mechanism

**Trauma** — crush, vascular injury, **compartment syndrome** (e.g. IVDU overdose); oedema/haematoma trapped by interosseous fascia → fibrosis

**Rheumatoid** — MCP dislocation + ulnar deviation → spastic intrinsics

**Neurologic** — **TBI, CP, CVA, Parkinson's**

**Other** — repetitive grip (carpenter), prolonged immobilisation, ischaemia, iatrogenic

## 3 Presentation

### Intrinsic tightness (Bunnell test) & posture

**Symptom** — difficulty **gripping large objects** (e.g. hammer)

**Posture** — MCP flexion, PIP + DIP extension

![Intrinsic plus posture](../../MCQ/attachments/q217357-illa-plus.jpg)

**Bunnell (intrinsic tightness) test** — **positive = ↓ passive PIP flexion with MCP extended** vs MCP flexed → **intrinsic tightness**

| Finding | Source of stiffness |
|---|---|
| PIP flexion **↓ with MCP extended** | **Intrinsic tightness** |
| PIP flexion **↓ with MCP flexed** (more with MCP extended) | **Extrinsic tightness** (extensor adhesions) |
| PIP motion unchanged by MCP position | **Joint capsule** contracture |

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — **quadriga** (flexion lag adjacent to repaired FDP)) · **lumbrical plus** (paradoxical PIP extension on active flexion) · central slip rupture (boutonnière)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; X-ray not required (normal)

## 5 Management

```
Intrinsic plus hand
├─ Mild → passive stretching / OT, orthoses, botulinum toxin
├─ Some intrinsic function (spastic) → proximal muscle slide
└─ Severe MCP + IP, fibrotic intrinsics → distal intrinsic release
```

**Proximal muscle slide** — **subperiosteal elevation of interossei** → lengthens MTU

**Distal intrinsic release** — resect intrinsic tendon **distal to transverse fibres** (spares MCP flexion)

**Release needed (Espiritu)** — extensor hood index 59%, middle 65%, ring 26%, small 33%

## X vs Y

| | **Intrinsic plus** | **Intrinsic minus (claw)** |
|---|---|---|
| MCP | **Flexion** | **Hyperextension** |
| PIP / DIP | **Extension** | **Flexion** |
| Imbalance | Spastic intrinsics, weak extrinsics | Strong extrinsics, deficient intrinsics |

## How it's asked

**"Passive PIP flexion possible with MCP flexed but not extended?"** → **intrinsic tightness (positive Bunnell)**\
✗ Wrong options: extrinsic tightness · quadriga · lumbrical plus

**"Crush injury, same Bunnell finding — stiffness from?"** → **intrinsic musculature**\
✗ Wrong options: PIP/MCP capsule

**"Posture of intrinsic plus hand after compartment syndrome?"** → **MCPJ flexion with PIPJ & DIPJ extension**\
✗ Wrong options: MCP extension + IP flexion = intrinsic minus

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Jersey Finger

*Hand · 46 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **FDP avulsion** from base of distal phalanx (**zone I**); aka rugby/rugger jersey finger

**Ring finger** — **75%**; ring tip 5 mm more prominent in grip (~90%) → ↑ pull-away force

**FDP innervation** — ulnar n. + AIN

**Zone I** — distal to FDS insertion

**Blood supply** — **vincula**: short (breve) near insertion · long (longum) from FDS chiasma region; palm retraction shears **both** → tendon necrosis

**Quadriga basis (TO)** — FDP of long, ring, little share **common muscle belly**

## 2 Mechanism

**Forced DIP extension during max FDP contraction** — grabbing a jersey/flag in a tackle

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis, retraction level & urgency

**Rest** — finger in **slight extension** at DIP vs cascade

**Key sign** — **no active DIP flexion**; MCP/PIP normal

**Palpable mass** — retracted tendon in palm / distal palmar crease (Type I) — MRI can show level

**Paradoxical PIP extension** — on attempted flexion

![Jersey finger: loss of DIP flexion in ring finger](../../MCQ/attachments/q1132-a-jersey-finger.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — may show **avulsion fragment** (level of fragment = level of retraction)

**MRI** — level of proximal retraction

### Modified Leddy & Packer classification

| Type | Retraction / fragment | Vincula | Repair window | Image |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Into palm** (lumbrical origin, ≥A1); no fragment | **Both ruptured** → **most threatening** (TO) | **7–10 d** | ![Types](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6015-types-of-jesey-finger.jpg) |
| **II** | **PIP level** (A3 / Camper's chiasma), ± small fragment; **most common** (TO) | Short ruptured, **long intact** | Several weeks (Orthobullets) · **6 wk** (OB) · up to **3 mo** reported (TO) | |
| **III** | **Large fragment** held at **DIP / A4 pulley** (Orthobullets, TO) · held at **A2** in one TO stem — read the options | **Both intact** → best prognosis | Weeks; **ORIF** (screw, K-wire, pull-out wire) | ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6015-jerseybony2.jpg) |
| **IV** | **Double avulsion**: fragment + tendon off fragment → tendon usually into palm (Smith 1981) | As I/II | **ORIF fragment first, then tendon** | ![Type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6015-type-4.jpg) |
| V | Avulsion + **comminuted distal phalanx** (Va extra-, Vb intra-articular) (Al-Qattan 2001) | Minimal damage (TO) | Fix if possible, else tendon-to-bone ± DIP stabilisation | |

**Urgency depends on** — **degree of tendon retraction**\
✗ Wrong options: presence or size of fragment · PIP ROM · athletic level

## 5 Management

```
Jersey finger (all need surgery)
├─ Acute (<3 wk; ideally <1 wk) → tendon repair / reinsertion (button or anchor)
│      └─ bony (III/IV) → ORIF fragment (IV: + tendon reinsertion)
├─ Chronic (>3 mo) + full DIP PROM → TWO-STAGE TENDON GRAFT
└─ Chronic (>3 mo) + stiff DIP → DIP ARTHRODESIS
```

**No fracture on X-ray (TO)** — Type I/II → **immediate surgical repair**\
✗ Wrong options: splint in extension/flexion · observe months

**No nonop** — extension block splint, buddy taping, early ROM → no DIP function

### Acute repair technique – advancement limit & anchor placement

**Fixation** — direct repair or reinsertion with **dorsal (nail) pull-out button** or **suture anchor** (anchors replacing buttons: button irritation) (TO)

**Advancement limit (TO)** — **≤1 cm** (excursion balance of common FDP belly) → >1 cm = **quadriga** / DIP flexion contracture

**2.5 mm advancement** — **acceptable**

**Anchor** — **45° retrograde** (distal-volar → proximal-dorsal) in **proximal metaphysis** of distal phalanx\
✗ Wrong options: perpendicular · dorsal cortex breach · antegrade (more gap) · articular surface

**DIP pinning** — optional, not required

**No DIP arthrodesis acutely** — repair instead

**Do not delay** — don't finish 8-week season before repair

**Rehab** — **Duran** (patient-assisted passive) or **Kleinert** (dynamic splint passive)

**After repair** — check **symmetric resting cascade** (TO: over-tension → quadriga)

### Chronic injury – two-stage grafting

**Chronic (>3 mo) + full PROM** — **excise palmar mass + 2-stage grafting** (Hunter: silicone rod → pseudosheath → PL/plantaris graft)

**Avoid single-stage graft** — adhesions in fibro-osseous tunnel

**Avoid active silicone rod** — not effective

**No direct repair late** — too much tension, tendon won't reach

**DIP arthrodesis** — chronic + **stiff** DIP; young guitarist with supple finger → **grafting**

**Outcome** — reconstruction poorer than acute repair, stiffness

## 6 Complications

**Quadriga** — over-advancement (>1 cm) → adjacent fingers' flexion & grip ↓

**DIP flexion contracture** — over-advancement

## X vs Y

### Differential – central slip injury

| | **Jersey finger** | **Central slip / boutonnière** (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| PIP | Flexes normally | **Flexed, can't extend** |
| DIP | **Can't flex** | **Hyperextended, can't flex** |
| Test | No active DIP flexion with PIP blocked | **Elson** (rigid DIP = +), modified Elson, Boyes (late) |
| Acute Rx | **Surgery** | **PIP extension splint 4–6 wk**, DIP free |

**Boutonnière pathology (TO)** — central slip rupture → lateral bands volar (transverse retinacular lig. contract) → PIP flexion + DIP hyperextension; may appear **10–14 d** after injury

**Chronic boutonnière (TO)** — supple: **terminal tendon tenotomy (Fowler/Dolphin)**, lateral band relocation, Littler; stiff/RA/arthritic: **PIP arthrodesis**

**Pseudo-boutonnière (TO)** — PIP flexion contracture **without** DIP hyperextension

| Other diagnoses (wrong) | Finding |
|---|---|
| FDS avulsion | PIP flexion lost |
| Distal extensor (mallet) | DIP extension lost |
| Sagittal band | MCP extensor subluxation |

## How it's asked

**"Football tackle, can't flex DIP, X-ray normal — Rx"** → **immediate surgical repair** (TO)

**"Most threatening Leddy-Packer type"** → **Type I** (TO)

**"Large fragment trapped at pulley, ring finger, 5 d"** → **Type III**, vincula intact (TO)

**"Ring finger after tackle, can't flex DIP — Dx"** → **FDP avulsion**

**"Rugby, 1 wk, distal palm tender, can't flex DIP"** → **acute tendon repair**

**"Urgency of fixation depends on"** → **degree of tendon retraction**

**"Max advancement to avoid quadriga"** → **1 cm** (TO)

**"Correct statement about repair"** → **2.5 mm advancement acceptable**

**"Suture anchor position"** → **45° retrograde in proximal metaphysis**

**"5 mo, palmar mass, full PROM"** → **excise mass + 2-stage grafting**

**"Guitarist, 6 mo, supple DIP"** → **two-stage flexor reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: arthrodesis

**"DIP hyperextended can't flex, PIP flexed"** → **central slip injury** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Kienbock's Disease

*Hand · 47 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition – lunate osteonecrosis

**Definition** — **avascular necrosis of the lunate** → progressive wrist pain, abnormal carpal motion

**Osteochondrosis, crushing type (TO)** — Kienböck = **lunate**\
✗ Wrong options: scaphoid, radial head, capitulum

| Osteochondrosis (TO) | Site |
|---|---|
| Köhler | Navicular |
| **Kienböck** | **Lunate** |
| Panner | Capitulum |
| Scheuermann | Vertebral ring epiphysis |
| Calvé | Vertebral central nucleus |
| Thiemann | Phalangeal epiphyses |
| Freiberg | Metatarsal heads |
| Iselin *(traction)* | 5th MT base |
| Osgood-Schlatter *(traction)* | Tibial tuberosity |
| Sever *(traction)* | Calcaneal apophysis |
| Sinding-Larsen-Johansson *(traction)* | Inferior pole patella |
| OCD *(splitting)* | — |

**Typical patient** — **♂ 20–40 y**, often **manual labourer** (TO); history of trauma; **dominant** hand

**Lunate ossification** — **2–4 y**

### Aetiology & risk factors (ulnar negative variance, vascular pattern)

**Multifactorial** — repetitive microtrauma + altered biomechanics + ↑ intraosseous pressure + ulnar negative variance → **"all of the above"**

**Ulnar negative variance** — ↑ **radiolunate contact stress**; **most predominant risk factor** (TO); up to **78%** of cases (TO)

**↓ Radial inclination** — ↑ radiolunate pressure; risk ↑ as inclination ↓ → **not increased radial inclination** (TO)

**Lunate morphology (TO)** — small lunate · **type I (triangular, no medial facet)** = weaker trabeculae → risk; type II/III square

**Vascular (TO)** — **fewer penetrating arteries** (esp. volar from palmar radiocarpal arch) → ↑ risk; sparse intraosseous collaterals

**Venous congestion** — ↑ **intraosseous pressure (>40 mmHg vs capitate)**

**Medical conditions** — scleroderma, sickle cell, SLE, corticosteroids

**NOT a cause** — **increased carpal height** (carpal height ↓ in Kienböck)

**Ulnar variance associations** — **negative** → Kienböck + ulnar impingement · **positive** → TFCC tear, ulnar abutment, SL dissociation, triquetral arthrosis

**Measuring ulnar variance** — PA, **shoulder 90° abduction, elbow 90°**, neutral rotation; **more positive in pronation**, negative in supination

![Ulnar variance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-ulnar-variance.jpg)

### Anatomy – lunate blood supply

| Pattern | Fact |
|---|---|
| Y | — |
| X | — |
| **I** | **31%** of Kienböck patients (Orthobullets/OB); **highest AVN risk** (also TO) |

**Single palmar supply** — **25%** of lunates (TO) → ↑ osteonecrosis risk

**Capitate** — supply also poor → AVN possible

![I-pattern lunate supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-i-pattern-of-blood-supply-to-lunate.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **dorsal wrist pain**, **activity-related** / worse with grip loading; insidious, usually no trauma event (TO)

**Exam** — ± swelling, **radiocarpal tenderness**, ↓ flexion–extension arc, **↓ grip strength**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lat, oblique) | Normal (stage I) → **sclerosis** (II) → **collapse** (III) → carpal OA (IV) |
| **MRI** | **Best for early disease / IOC** (TO); **↓ T1 signal**; rules out ulnar impaction |
| **CT** | **Most sensitive for staging / early collapse**; extent of necrosis, trabecular destruction, geometry |
| ✗ Wrong option: bone scan | Non-specific |
| ✗ Wrong option: US, angiography | Not used |
| ✗ Wrong option: clenched-fist AP | SL interval |

**Stage IIIA → IIIB** — **radioscaphoid angle >60°** (fixed scaphoid flexion)

![Kienböck MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-keinbocksmri-figure3.jpg)

### Staging (Lichtman) & imaging

| Stage | Findings | Treatment | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | X-ray normal, **MRI changes** | **Immobilisation + NSAIDs** (cast (TO)) | ![I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stage-1.jpg) |
| **II** | **Lunate sclerosis**, normal shape | **Ulnar −ve → joint levelling** · **ulnar neutral/+ → radial wedge osteotomy or STT fusion** · core decompression · revascularisation | ![II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stage-2.jpg) |
| **IIIA** | **Collapse**, no scaphoid rotation, carpal height preserved | **Same as II** | ![IIIA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stage-3a.jpg) |
| **IIIB** | Collapse + **fixed scaphoid flexion**, ↓ carpal height | **PRC, STT fusion or SC fusion** | ![IIIB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stage-3b.jpg) |
| IIIC (TO) | Collapse + **coronal fracture** | — | — |
| **IV** | **Adjacent intercarpal OA** | **Wrist fusion, PRC, limited intercarpal fusion** (± TWA) | ![IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stage-4.jpg) |

**Lichtman** — X-ray + MRI based; low interobserver variability (TO)

### Articular grading for advanced disease (TO, Bain–Begg)

| Grade | Nonfunctional surfaces | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | None | Joint levelling · forage ± graft · VBG |
| 1 | Proximal lunate | RSL fusion · PRC · osteochondral graft |
| 2a | Proximal lunate + lunate facet radius | **RSL fusion** |
| 2b | Proximal + distal lunate | PRC · lunate replacement · capitate lengthening |
| 3 | 3 surfaces (capitate preserved) | Hemiarthroplasty (SC fusion if radial column intact) |
| 4 | All 4 | Total wrist fusion / TWA (SC fusion if radial column intact) |

**Synovectomy** — all patients (TO)

## 5 Management

```
Kienböck – by stage
├─ I → immobilisation + NSAIDs
├─ II / IIIA → lunate unloading or revascularisation
│      ├─ ulnar NEGATIVE → radial shortening (± VBG)
│      └─ ulnar neutral / positive → radial wedge osteotomy · STT fusion · capitate shortening
├─ IIIB → PRC · STT fusion · SC fusion
└─ IV → wrist fusion · PRC · limited intercarpal fusion
```

```
Practical approach (TO)
├─ Age <15 → nonop >12 mo → epiphysiodesis
├─ Age >70 → nonop 6–12 mo → arthroscopic debridement
├─ Lunate only (B) → unloading (−ve UV radial / +ve UV capitate shortening) or reconstruction (VBG / osteochondral graft)
└─ Wrist affected (C) → SC fusion · PRC · RSL fusion · advanced OA → wrist fusion
```

**Treatment categories (TO)** — core decompression · joint levelling / unloading · revascularisation (distal radius / pronator quadratus VBG) · salvage (carpectomy, partial fusion)

### Early stages – radial shortening, core decompression & vascularised bone graft

**Stage II, ulnar −ve (TO)** — **VBG + radial shortening**\
✗ Wrong options: STT fusion · PRC · VBG + radial wedge

**Radial shortening osteotomy** — **treatment of choice** stage II ulnar minus; **extra-articular**; more evidence than ulnar lengthening

**Failed immobilisation stage II** — → **radial shortening**\
✗ Wrong options: continue cast · PRC · STT · fusion

**DRUJ (Tolat type II)** — remodelling after shortening **without arthritis** → not a contraindication

**Ulnar shortening** — ✗ wrong option: **worsens** (↑ radiolunate force)

**Distal radius core decompression** — stage I–IIIA; **local vascular healing response**

**Not Kienböck options** — TFCC repair, SL reconstruction, lunate chondroplasty; lunate excision / implant **rarely used**

**Limited intercarpal fusion** — before total wrist arthrodesis

**Partial fusions** — STT, **capitate shortening ± capitohamate**, SC → stage II (neutral/+), IIIA/IIIB; must address **DISI**

**VBG** — stage I–IIIB; best in stage III; long-term data lacking

**VBG options** — pisiform · distal radius on **pronator quadratus** pedicle · dorsal metacarpal artery branches · **4+5 ECA**

**4+5 ECA advantage** — **largest arc of rotation** · large diameter · **ulnar location** (✗ Wrong options: radial) · **dorsal** approach (✗ Wrong options: volar) · antegrade flow not unique

**After VBG** — **2–3 mo immobilisation / unloading** (STT/SC pinning or ex-fix) required

![4+5 ECA graft](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-surgical-technique-for-4-5-eca-graft.jpg)

### Radiolunate contact stress

| Procedure | ↓ Radiolunate stress |
|---|---|
| STT fusion | 3% |
| SC fusion | 12% |
| Capitohamate fusion | 0% |
| **Ulnar lengthening 4 mm** | **45%** |
| **Radial shortening 4 mm** | **45%** |
| Capitate shortening + CH fusion | **66%** (but ↑ radioscaphoid 26%) |

### Advanced disease – STT fusion/pinning

**Temporary STT pinning** — **adolescent** with Kienböck + progressive pain → ↓ radiolunate, ↑ radioscaphoid load\
✗ Wrong options: radial club hand, scaphoid fracture, hypoplastic thumb, gymnast's wrist

**IIIB** — PRC (also lunate excision + STT/SC fusion); STT superior to PRC in some studies

**IV** — wrist fusion (remove arthritic joint) ; TWA long-term data lacking

![STT pinning](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6050-stt-pinning.jpg)

### Paediatric Kienböck – conservative care

**Skeletally immature, stage I** — **short arm cast + NSAIDs (4 wk)**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression + ulnar shortening · PRC · STT pinning/fusion

**Nonop outcome (adult)** — majority progress → surgery

### Other carpal osteonecrosis (capitate) – salvage

**Carpal AVN frequency** — scaphoid (trauma), lunate (idiopathic) > **capitate** > hamate > trapezium > trapezoid > triquetrum > pisiform

| Eponym | Bone |
|---|---|
| Kienböck | Lunate |
| **Preiser** | **Scaphoid** |
| Köhler | Navicular |

**Capitate AVN causes** — trauma, **retrograde palmar-dominant supply** (like scaphoid), hypermobility / **ligamentous laxity**, steroids, vibration, repetitive extension

**Capitate AVN treatment** — cast first

| Group | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Pre-collapse, no OA (young) | Decompression / **VBG** (4+5 ECA local; **free MFC flap**) → best preserves vascularity & anatomy |
| Collapse, no OA | Proximal pole excision + interposition · **pyrocarbon** resurfacing · graft · fusion |
| Collapse + OA | **Midcarpal fusion** (salvage), SCL / 4-corner fusion |

**Not for capitate AVN** — **PRC** (radius articulates with dysvascular capitate) · TWA · radial wedge osteotomy · curettage + non-vascularised graft · implant arthroplasty in teen

**Carpal coalition** — **LT** commonest (1%, 60% bilateral; West African descent)

## X vs Y

| | **Kienböck** | Capitate AVN | Ulnar impaction |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bone | Lunate | Capitate proximal pole | Ulnar lunate / triquetrum |
| Variance | **Negative** | — | **Positive** |
| MRI | ↓ T1 lunate | Proximal pole capitate cystic change | MRI rules it out (Orthobullets) |
| Key Rx | Radial shortening (ulnar −ve) | VBG / midcarpal fusion | Ulnar shortening |

## How it's asked

**"NOT a risk factor"** → **↑ radial inclination** (TO)

**"All are etiologies EXCEPT"** → **↑ carpal height**

**"Cause of Kienböck"** → **all of the above** (multifactorial)

**"Negative ulnar variance on X-ray — at risk of"** → **lunate collapse**\
✗ Wrong options: TFCC tear, ulnar abutment, SL dissociation

**"Kienböck involves"** → **lunate** (TO)

**"Most sensitive study to stage, sclerosis no collapse"** → **CT**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan, US, angiography

**"Collapsed lunate + flexed scaphoid, PRC advised"** → **IIIB** (radioscaphoid >60°)

**"MRI sclerosis + negative ulnar variance — ideal Rx"** → **VBG + radial shortening** (TO)

**"Procedure inciting local vascular healing"** → **distal radius core decompression**

**"Stage II failed immobilisation"** → **radial shortening osteotomy**

**"Accepted Rx after failed immobilisation"** → **radius core decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar shortening, TFCC repair

**"T1 low lunate, ulnar minus, no OA"** → **radial shortening**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar shortening

**"4+5 ECA main advantage"** → **large arc of rotation**

**"Temporary STT pinning indicated for"** → **adolescent Kienböck**

**"7-y-old stage I"** → **short arm cast + NSAIDs 4 wk**

**"Jackhammer, MRI proximal capitate change — diagnosis"** → **capitate AVN**\
✗ Wrong options: Preiser, Köhler

**"Capitate AVN, best restores vascularity / native anatomy"** → **vascularized bone graft**

**"Capitate AVN pre-collapse, failed cast"** → **4+5 ECA VBG**\
✗ Wrong options: PRC · 4CF

**"Capitate AVN salvage"** → **midcarpal fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: PRC, TWA

**"Carpal bone ossifying at 2–4 y removed in PRC for osteonecrosis"** → **lunate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ligaments of the Fingers

*Hand · 48 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Retinacular ligaments

![Oblique retinacular ligament](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6003-obretlig.jpg)

**Oblique retinacular ligament (Landsmeer)** — links PIP & DIP; **volar to PIP axis, dorsal to DIP axis**; prox phalanx (lateral volar) → terminal extensor

**ORL biomechanics** — PIP flexion → relaxes → DIP flexes; PIP extension → tightens → DIP extends

**ORL tight** — rest DIP extended + PIP flexed; **can't flex DIP with PIP extended**, can after PIP flexed

**ORL pathology** — contracture → **boutonnière**; **ORL reconstruction → swan neck** treatment

**Transverse retinacular ligament** — flexor sheath at PIP → lateral bands; **attenuation → swan neck** (dorsal bands); contracture + triangular lig. attenuation → **boutonnière**

**Triangular ligament** — dorsal, distal to PIP; prevents lateral band subluxation; contracture → swan neck

| Tightness test | MCP extended | MCP flexed |
|---|---|---|
| **Intrinsic tightness** | ↓ PIP flexion | ↑ PIP flexion |
| **Extrinsic (extensor) tightness** | ↑ PIP flexion | ↓ PIP flexion |
| **ORL tightness** | DIP flexion only after PIP flexed | — |

### Digital cutaneous ligaments

| | **Cleland's** ("C" = ceiling) | **Grayson's** ("G" = ground) |
|---|---|---|
| Relation to digital nerve | **Dorsal** | **Volar** |
| Dupuytren's | **Not involved** | Involved |

![Cleland's and Grayson's ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6003-cleland-grayson.jpg)

### Expansion hood & MCP ligaments

**Central slip** — base of middle phalanx → **PIP extension**

**Lateral band** — distal phalanx → **DIP extension**; lumbricals, EI, interossei insert

**Lumbrical** — **volar** to deep transverse MC ligament · **interossei dorsal** to it

**MCP cam** — "snoopy head" MC head → collaterals **loose in extension, tight in flexion**

| MCP collateral part | Shape/position | Tight in | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Proper** | Cord, dorsal; MC posterior tubercle → P1 base | **30° flexion** | Stress in 30° flexion |
| **Accessory** | Fan, volar; MC head → volar plate & DTML | **Extension** | Stress in extension |

**RCL vs UCL** — RCL more horizontal

**Deep transverse MC ligament** — joins 2nd–5th MC heads at volar plates; prevents splaying

**Natatory ligament** — most superficial; resists abduction; web space

**Sagittal bands** — volar plate → extensor; centralise extensor over MCP

**Volar plate** — prevents hyperextension; **checkrein ligaments** attach to P1; loose in flexion, tight in extension; thumb sesamoids

## 5 Management

### PIP contracture release sequence

```
Volar PIP flexion contracture release (test extension after each step)
├─ 1 → retract flexors, release CHECKREIN ligaments
├─ 2 → ACCESSORY COLLATERAL + VOLAR PLATE
├─ 3 → PROPER COLLATERAL off proximal phalanx
└─ no active extension → extensor tenolysis
```

**After checkreins** — **accessory collateral ligament + volar plate**\
✗ Wrong options: proper collateral · dorsal capsule · extensor

## How it's asked

**"Released after checkrein ligaments in PIP contracture?"** → **accessory collateral ligament + volar plate**

**"Ligament dorsal to digital nerve, spared in Dupuytren's?"** → **Cleland's**

**"MCP proper collateral tight in?"** → **30° flexion**

**"Transverse retinacular ligament attenuation?"** → **swan neck**

**"Lumbrical relation to DTML?"** → **volar**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lumbrical Plus Finger

*Hand · 49 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy – lumbricals

| Lumbrical | Pennation | Nerve | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1st | **Unipennate** | **Median** | Radial side FDP2 |
| 2nd | **Unipennate** | **Median** | Radial side FDP3 |
| 3rd | **Bipennate** | **Ulnar** | FDP3 & FDP4 |
| 4th | **Bipennate** | **Ulnar** | FDP4 & FDP5 |

**Insertion** — all on **radial** side of extensor expansion (lateral bands)

**Commonest digit** — **middle finger** (2nd lumbrical)

**Why middle** — FDP 3/4/5 share a **common muscle belly**; distal FDP3 cut → FDP shifts **ulnarly** (pull of bipennate 3rd lumbrical) → tightens 2nd lumbrical → amplifies effect

**Why not index** — **independent FDP belly**; can fist with FDS2 alone → avoids paradoxical extension

![Lumbrical origins and innervation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6017-lumbricals.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Cause – amputation at DIP level

**Definition** — **paradoxical IP (PIP + DIP) extension** on attempted finger flexion

**Pathoanatomy** — lumbrical arises from FDP → FDP divided distal to lumbrical origin → FDP contraction pulls lumbrical → pulls **lateral bands** → IP extension

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| **FDP disruption distal to lumbrical origin** | **Most common**: transection or **avulsion** (jersey finger) |
| **DIP amputation** | Distal to central slip insertion → PIP extends on flexion |
| Amputation through **middle phalanx shaft** | |
| **"Too long" tendon graft** | Excess flexor graft length |

**✗ DIP amputation distractors** — central slip rupture, swan neck, boutonniere, **quadrigia** (FDP advanced >1 cm → adjacent digit flexion weakness, common muscle belly)

![Pathoanatomy of lumbrical plus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6017-lp.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — volar digital laceration (FDP transection) or axial traction on flexed digit (FDP avulsion)

**Symptom** — one digit **sticks out / catches on clothes** when gripping or making a fist

**Exam** — paradoxical IP extension with grip ("**fingers extend while holding a beer can**")

**Diagnosis** — clinical: one digit's IPs extend when fisting all fingers

![Lumbrical plus finger on grip](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6017-lumbrical-plus.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Lumbrical plus finger
├─ Minimal symptoms → observe
├─ FDP laceration, repairable → tenodesis FDP to terminal tendon / reinsert to distal phalanx
└─ FDP retracted / segmental loss → lumbrical release at base of flexor sheath (palm)
```

**Tenodesis / reinsertion** — for FDP lacerations; **do not suture flexor–extensor mechanisms over bone**

**Lumbrical release** — **transect in palm at base of flexor sheath** → abolishes paradoxical PIP extension

**No acute routine release** — phenomenon not consistent enough

**Contraindication** — **do not divide lumbricals 1 & 2 with ulnar nerve palsy** (interossei also lost → no IP extensors left)

## X vs Y

| | **Lumbrical plus** | **Quadrigia** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | FDP too loose / cut distal to lumbrical, long graft, DIP amputation | FDP **too tight** (advanced >1 cm) |
| Sign | Paradoxical IP extension on fisting | Adjacent fingers can't fully flex, weak grip |
| Fix | Lumbrical release in palm | Release tethered FDP |

## How it's asked

**"Complication of DIP-level amputation?"** → **lumbrical plus finger**\
✗ Wrong options: quadrigia, swan neck, boutonniere, central slip rupture

**"Paradoxical PIP extension on making a fist?"** → **lumbrical plus**

**"Where to release the lumbrical?"** → **palm, base of flexor sheath**

**"Most common finger?"** → **middle (2nd lumbrical)**

**"Don't release lumbricals 1 & 2 when?"** → **ulnar nerve palsy**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lunate Dislocation (Perilunate dissociation)

*Hand · 50 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & categories

**Incidence** — rare, high energy; **missed in ~25%** at first presentation (Herzberg 25%, Dunn 27.5%)

**Carpal instability class (TO)** — **CIC** (complex) = **perilunate dislocations & fracture-dislocations** → intrinsic (SL, LT = dissociative) + extrinsic (midcarpal capsule = non-dissociative) ± fractures\
✗ Wrong options: pure LT dissociation · Colles dorsal tilt · RA ulnar translocation

**Perilunate dislocation** — **lunate stays in fossa**, carpus dislocates (usually dorsal)

**Lunate dislocation** — lunate forced volar (or dorsal), carpus aligned → lunate usually **into carpal tunnel**

**Perilunate types** — transscaphoid-perilunate · perilunate · transradial-styloid · transscaphoid-transcapitate-perilunate

**Most common variant** — **dorsal trans-scaphoid perilunate fracture-dislocation** (OB, TO)

| Arc | Injury |
|---|---|
| **Lesser arc** | **Purely ligamentous** (rapid force) |
| **Greater arc** | Ligament + fractures of radius, ulna or carpus (slower force: transradial styloid, transscaphoid, transcapitate) |

![Greater vs lesser arc](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-greater-and-lesser.jpg)

### Ligament anatomy

**SLIL / LTIL** — interosseous; major proximal-row stabilisers; SL prevents **DISI**, LT tear → **VISI**

**Space of Poirier** — weak volar area **between RSC and long radiolunate** (OB) · between radiocapitate and LRL (TO); over capitolunate joint\
✗ Wrong options: between dorsal intercarpal and radiotriquetral

**Short radiolunate (SRL)** — **remains intact**, tethers lunate to radius\
✗ Wrong options: first ligament injured

**Dorsal intercarpal** — typically remains intact

**Ligament of Testut (RSL)** — neurovascular conduit, little structural role

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism – Mayfield progression

**Position** — **wrist extension + ulnar deviation + intercarpal supination**; axial load, FOOSH, fall from height

**Ligament order** — **SL → capitolunate → LT → dorsal radiocarpal** → lunate rotates + dislocates\
✗ Wrong options: SL → LT → CL · DRC first

**First ligament injured** — **scapholunate**\
✗ Wrong options: SRL

**Bony sequence (Mayfield)** — **scaphoid extension → scaphoid failure (space of Poirier opens) → distal row dissociation → triquetral hyperextension → LT failure → dorsal dislocation of carpus**

**Reduction (Mayfield)** — intercarpal **pronation, radial deviation, palmar flexion**

![Progressive perilunar instability: clockwise ligament disruption](../../MCQ/attachments/q219974-illa-progression-of-lig-disruptions.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — acute swelling, pain, deformity

**Acute CTS** — **~25%** (Orthobullets) · **25–50%** (OB) · **25–45%** (TO) · 24–45% (Stanbury) · 50% (Dunn) — read the options; highest in **Mayfield IV**

**Vascular exam** — rarely abnormal; DRUJ injury uncommon

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **PA + lateral**; lateral = **most diagnostic** (TO)

| View | Sign |
|---|---|
| PA | **Break in Gilula's arcs** (3 arcs), lunate–capitate **overlap**, **↓ carpal height**, crowded carpus, SL diastasis |
| PA | **"Piece-of-pie" sign** = triangular lunate (palmar rotation) |
| Lateral | **Loss of collinearity radius–lunate–capitate**; SL angle >70° |
| Lateral | Perilunate: lunate in fossa, capitate dorsal · Lunate: **"spilled teacup"** lunate volar, capitate drops into fossa |
| CT | Fracture lines (trans-scaphoid/capitate), planning (TO) |
| MRI | **Not required** / doesn't aid diagnosis (OB); ligament extent (TO) |

**Radial styloid fracture** — avulsion of radiocarpal ligaments

![Lateral: spilled teacup](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-spilled-teecup-sign.jpg)

### Mayfield classification

| Stage | Orthobullets / OB | TO wording | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **SL dissociation** | SL dissociation / **scaphoid fracture** / rotation | ![Mayfield 1](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-mayfield-1.jpg) |
| **II** | + **lunocapitate** disruption | Lunocapitate dislocation through **space of Poirier** / perilunate dislocation | ![Mayfield 2](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-mayfield-2.jpg) |
| **III** | + **LT** disruption = "perilunate" | LT disruption / triquetrum dislocation | ![Mayfield 3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-mayfield-3.jpg) |
| **IV** | **Lunate dislocated** from fossa (usually volar) → **median n. compression**; DRC fails | Lunate extruded volarly into carpal tunnel | ![Mayfield 4](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6045-mayfield-4.jpg) |

**Stage I (TO)** — **"not always a scaphoid fracture"** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: LT injury · lunate extruded · RSC detachment

**Stage II (TO)** — capitate–lunate joint through **space of Poirier**

**Stage IV ligament** — **dorsal radiocarpal** fails (Orthobullets/OB) · long radiolunate shown disrupted (OB219974 illustration) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
Perilunate / lunate dislocation
├─ Acute → emergent closed reduction + splint (neuro exam before & after)
│      └─ then ORIF + ligament repair ± CTR (all <8 wk)
│             (worsening / persistent ACTS → emergent CTR + ORIF)
├─ Chronic >8 wk → proximal row carpectomy
└─ Chronic + degenerative change / failed PRC → total wrist arthrodesis
```

### Acute management – urgent reduction, neurological exam & missed diagnosis

**Next step** — **urgent closed reduction + splint**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · MR arthrogram · bone scan

**Before reduction** — **neurological exam** (repeat after)\
✗ Wrong options: vascular · Allen · MRI · DRUJ

**Technique** — finger traps, elbow 90°, **5–10 lb for 15 min** → **wrist extension + traction → flexion with dorsal pressure on carpus** (volar pressure on lunate for lunate dislocation); sugar-tong splint

**Benefit of emergent reduction** — ↓ **median nerve** damage, ↓ **cartilage** damage, ↓ AVN (TO)

**Median nerve (Budoff)** — usually static, non-progressive → no emergent release; **delayed onset / progressive CTS → emergent CTR + ORIF**

**Closed treatment definitive** — **never**; universally poor, redislocation

### Definitive ORIF ± carpal tunnel release

**Timing** — all acute <8 wk; surgery can wait **~4 d** for swelling (Budoff) · standard **within 7–10 d** (TO); **delay >3 wk** worsens outcome (TO)

**Lunate volar + median symptoms** — **ORIF via dorsal + volar approaches + extended CTR**\
✗ Wrong options: CR + cast · CRPP · ORIF without CTR · PRC acute

| Approach | Gives | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| Dorsal (Lister's) | Proximal row + midcarpal exposure, SL/LT repair | not for CTR |
| Volar (extended CT) | **CTR**, close space of Poirier rent, volar ligaments | — |
| Combined | Easier reduction, distal scaphoid, CTR | Swelling, devascularisation, ↓ flexion/grip |

**Steps** — fix fractures (scaphoid headless screw) · **SL repair with suture anchors** · protect with K-wires (SL, LT, scaphocapitate) or cerclage · LT repair optional

**Post-op** — thumb spica → cast **≥6 wk** (Orthobullets); **K-wires out 8–12 wk** (TO)

### Chronic/late injuries – proximal row carpectomy & RSC ligament

**Chronic (>8 wk)** — **PRC**; arthrodesis only if PRC fails / degenerative\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF: cartilage loss + contracture · more splint/cast

**PRC stability** — capitate head in lunate fossa + **preserve RSC** → prevents **ulnar translocation**\
✗ Wrong options: SRL · SL · RSL

**Radial styloidectomy** — **≤4 mm** to preserve RSC; **6 mm destabilises** (Nakamura)

**PRC approach** — dorsal (scaphoid, triquetrum) + volar (lunate + median decompression)

## 6 Complications

### Post-operative lunate ischaemia – observation

**Transient lunate ischaemia** — radiodense lunate in **12.5%**, **1–4 mo** post-injury → **observation** (benign, self-limiting; no Kienböck progression)

**Not Kienböck treatments** — STT pinning (adolescent), capitate shortening (Lichtman II–III), PRC (advanced), cast (stage I)

| Outcome | Figure |
|---|---|
| Post-traumatic arthritis | **40–60%** (TO); **56% at 6 y** (Herzberg); well tolerated, does not correlate with symptoms |
| Wrist motion loss | **30–50%** (TO) |
| Grip | **70–80%** of contralateral (TO) |
| Persistent paraesthesia | 17.5% (Dunn) |
| Other | Scaphoid nonunion/AVN (trans-scaphoid), lunate AVN, full function unlikely |

## X vs Y

| | **Perilunate dislocation** | **Lunate dislocation** |
|---|---|---|
| Mayfield | III | **IV** |
| Lunate | **In fossa** | **Volar, in carpal tunnel** |
| Capitate | Dorsal to lunate | Back in line with radius |
| Lateral | Capitate off lunate | **Spilled teacup** |
| ACTS | Less | **Highest** |

## How it's asked

**"True of Mayfield stage I?"** → **not always a scaphoid fracture** (TO)

**"CIC is best represented by?"** → **perilunate dislocations and fracture-dislocations** (TO)

**"Mayfield II disrupts?"** → **capitolunate joint through space of Poirier** (TO)

**"Sequence of perilunate injury (bony)?"** → **scaphoid extension → scaphoid failure → distal row dissociation → LT failure → dorsal carpal dislocation**

**"First ligament injured?"** → **scapholunate**\
✗ Wrong options: SRL · DRC

**"Order of ligament failure?"** → **SL → CL → LT → DRC**

**"Perilunate + thumb/index paraesthesia — next step?"** → **urgent closed reduction + splint**

**"Before reduction obtain?"** → **neurological exam**

**"True statement?"** → **missed in ~25%**\
✗ Wrong options: ACTS rare · SRL first · MRI aids · Poirier dorsal

**"Volar lunate + hand numbness — definitive?"** → **ORIF dorsal + volar + extended CTR**
