**"Stage IV + median numbness — definitive?"** → **ORIF + carpal tunnel decompression**

**"PRC — preserve which ligament?"** → **radioscaphocapitate**

**"Perilunate missed 8 wk ('sprain'), limited ROM?"** → **PRC**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · arthrodesis

**"Radiodense lunate 2 mo after ORIF?"** → **observation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lunotriquetral Ligament Injury & VISI

*Hand · 51 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — LT injury **less common than SL** injury

### LT ligament – volar fibres strongest

**Shape** — **C-shaped intrinsic** ligament: dorsal + palmar transverse parts + weak proximal membranous (fibrocartilaginous) part

| Ligament | Strongest part | Yield strength (Berger) |
|---|---|---|
| **LT** | **Volar** (thickest; transmits triquetral extension moment) | Volar **301 N** vs dorsal 121 N |
| **SL** | **Dorsal** | Dorsal **260 N** vs volar 118 N |

**Dorsal LT** — most important **rotational constraint** (but weaker)

**Dorsal radiocarpal (radiotriquetral) ligament** — extrinsic; **secondary restraint to VISI**; loss → lunate flexes easily

**Volar long & short radiolunate** — extrinsic; torn in advanced injury

**Short radiolunate** — stays attached to lunate in perilunate dislocation; not involved in LT tear

![Volar ligaments of the carpus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6042-volar-ligaments-of-the-carpus.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — wrist **hyperextension** or extension + **radial deviation**

**Kinematics** — LT torn → scaphoid flexes lunate unopposed; triquetrum extends → **lunate flexes with scaphoid**

**VISI** — **LT ligament + dorsal radiotriquetral + volar radiolunate** injury; a **CID** pattern

**VISI in normal wrists** — may occur with **ligamentous laxity**\
✗ Wrong options: DISI (always pathological)

### Rocking lunate

**Rocking lunate (TO)** — **tear of both SL + LT ligaments**\
✗ Wrong options: SL alone · LT alone · + capitolunate

**Lunate balance (TO)** — lunate held between scaphoid (flexion) and triquetrum (extension) via SL & LT; lose one → dominated by the other

| (TO) | SL disruption | LT disruption |
|---|---|---|
| Scaphoid | Flexes | — |
| Lunate | Follows triquetrum → **extends** | Loses triquetral pull → **flexes** |
| SL angle (normal <60°) | **↑** | **↓** |

**Chronic ulnar impaction** — **ulnar positive variance** → long ulna impacts triquetrum → degenerative LT tear ± **TFCC** tear

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **ulnar-sided wrist pain**, worse with **pronation + ulnar deviation** (power grip); painful snap with ulnar deviation, pronation, axial load

| Test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **LT shuck / ballottement** | Hold lunate, shuck triquetrum dorsal–palmar | Pain, crepitus, laxity |
| **Kleinman shear** | Stabilise radiolunate, load triquetrum AP (forearm neutral) | Pain or **clunk** |
| LT compression | Triquetrum displaced ulnarly during radioulnar deviation | Pain |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | **Volar-flexed lunate → SL angle <30°** (normal **47°**) = **VISI** |
| Lateral | **Capitolunate angle >15°** (zigzag; normally co-linear) |
| **AP** | **Break in Gilula's arc**; proximal translation of triquetrum ± LT overlap; often **no LT widening** (unlike SL) |
| AP clenched fist | Incongruent LT articulation |
| **Arthroscopy** | Diagnostic when X-rays normal |

![VISI: SL angle <30° on lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6042-sl-angle-lateral-new-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
LT ligament injury
├─ Initial / acute stable → observe / cast (better if acute)
├─ Acute instability → CRPP (multiple K-wires) + ligament repair ± dorsal capsulodesis
└─ Chronic instability → LT fusion
       └─ due to ulnar positive variance → arthroscopic LT debridement + ulnar shortening
```

**Cast** — more successful when acute; only **37%** operated (Atkinson & Watson)

**Acute** — BLB autograft reconstruction & LT fusion **out of favour** acutely

**LT fusion** — chronic; complication **nonunion**

## X vs Y

| | **SL injury** | **LT injury** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | Commoner | Rarer |
| Pain | Dorsoradial | **Ulnar** |
| Instability | **DISI** (always pathological) | **VISI** (may be laxity) |
| SL angle | **>60°** (↑) | **<30°** (↓) |
| AP X-ray | SL widening | Gilula break, no widening |
| Stronger part | **Dorsal** | **Volar** |
| Test | Watson scaphoid shift | Shuck / Kleinman shear |

## How it's asked

**"Strongest portion of the injured ligament (VISI, positive ballottement)?"** → **volar fibres of LT**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal LT, dorsal SL

**"Rocking lunate results from?"** → **SL + LT tear** (TO)

**"SL angle in VISI?"** → **<30°** (normal 47°)

**"LT disruption effect on SL angle?"** → **decreases** (TO)

**"Chronic LT tear with ulnar positive variance?"** → **arthroscopic debridement + ulnar shortening**

**"Chronic LT instability?"** → **LT fusion** (risk nonunion)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Macrodactyly (local gigantism)

*Hand · 52 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **nonhereditary congenital digit enlargement**; all tissues (skin, subcut fat, **nerve, joint, bone**) (TO)

**Incidence** — very rare; no known risk factors

**Laterality** — **90% unilateral** (Orthobullets + TO)

**Multiple digits** — **70%** involve >1 digit; majority >1 digit (TO)

**Commonest digit** — **index** > long > thumb > ring > small; hand or foot

**Distribution** — follows **nerve territory**; **median nerve** commonest

### Genes & lab markers

**Genetics** — **somatic (post-zygotic) gain-of-function PIK3CA mutation**, mTOR pathway (TO) · "no genetic correlation known" (Orthobullets) — read the options

### Associations & growth pattern (lipofibromatous hamartoma)

**Lipofibromatous hamartoma of median nerve** — seen in **macrodactyly** (TO); **adult homolog**\
✗ Wrong options: syndactyly, polydactyly, idiopathic CTS

**Median nerve in carpal tunnel** — enlarged in majority → **significant CTS** (TO)

**Most common associations (TO)** — **neurofibromatosis**, hemangiomatosis, **congenital lipofibromatosis**

**Other associations** — **Proteus**, **Bannayan-Riley-Ruvalcaba**, **Maffucci**, **Ollier**, **Milroy** (Orthobullets + TO)

**Foot pattern (TO)** — overgrowth **greatest plantarly and distally** → convex sole, dorsiflexed toes\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal

**Bone involvement (TO)** — usually limited to **phalanges**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — asymmetry at birth or over time, pain, ↓ use, cosmesis; foot: pain, **shoe fitting** (TO)

**Exam** — thick **fibrofatty** tissue; ↓ ROM from soft-tissue constraint

![Macrodactyly of multiple digits](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6080-macrodactyly.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — biplanar hand views → **enlarged phalanges** ± joint malalignment, angled phalanges

**CT / MRI** — not typically needed; **angiography** only for surgical planning

![Radiograph: enlarged phalanges](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6080-f21-large.jpg)

### Functional classification

| Type | Feature | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|
| **Static** | Present at birth; grows **linearly** with other digits | Asymmetry **does not worsen** |
| **Progressive** | Less obvious at birth; **disproportionate growth** | Asymmetry **worsens** |

## 5 Management

```
Macrodactyly
├─ Mild, 1–2 digits, not progressive → observe
├─ Single digit, length issue → epiphysiodesis when = same-sex parent's digit
│      └─ circumferential overgrowth → debulking (often incl. digital nerve) at physeal closure
├─ Thumb / multiple digits / severe deformity → osteotomy + shortening
└─ Massive / non-reconstructable / severe recurrence → (ray) amputation
```

### Epiphysiodesis

**Timing** — when digit reaches **adult length of same-sex parent's same digit** (father for a boy) (TO + OB)\
✗ Wrong options: now · debulk now · ray amputation · osteotomy at maturity

**Level (TO)** — **proximal & middle phalanges** (± metacarpal) → stops **longitudinal** growth

**Most common approach** — epiphysiodesis for single digit

**Debulking (TO)** — for **circumferential** growth; removes enlarged digital nerve + skin/subcut; done **at time of physeal closure** unless massive

**Angulation (TO)** — osteotomy, can be at level of physeal closure → combining procedures ↓ number of operations

**Avoid nerve resection** — hypertrophic nerve resection fails to prevent overgrowth (Ishida)

**Avoid compression wrapping**

**Ray amputation (TO)** — massive bone + soft-tissue enlargement; **procedure of choice for severe recurrence** after debulking/reduction syndactyly; "often required" = ✓ true (TO)

**Foot options (TO)** — amputation, ray amputation, epiphyseal ablation, osteotomies, nerve stripping, defatting

**Post-op** — soft-tissue care, early ROM

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — **digital stiffness**, chronic pain / oedema; recurrence

## How it's asked

**"10-y boy with macrodactyly — advice?"** → **epiphysiodesis of PIP when finger = father's** (TO)

**"Best intervention for proportional finger?"** → **epiphysiodesis when finger reaches father's adult length**\
✗ Wrong options: nerve resection

**"All except — macrodactyly?"** → **max overgrowth dorsal & distal** (false — it is plantar & distal) (TO)

**"Lipofibromatous hamartoma of median nerve seen in?"** → **macrodactyly** (TO)

**"Commonest digit?"** → **index**

**"Severe recurrence after debulking?"** → **ray amputation** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Madelung's Deformity

*Hand · 53 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **adolescent females**; commonly **bilateral**; ♀ > ♂ (TO); common in **gymnasts**

**History (TO)** — described by Malgaigne, then **Otto Madelung 1878**

### Aetiology – volar-ulnar physis & SHOX (Léri-Weill)

**Cause** — **impaired growth of the volar (palmar) & ulnar part of the distal radial physis**\
✗ Wrong options: physeal mismatch radius/ulna · DRUJ fusion · nutritional · unrecognised trauma

**Physis asked** — **ulnar volar physis of distal radius**\
✗ Wrong options: radial dorsal · ulnar dorsal · radial volar · distal ulna

**Vickers ligament** — thick **radiolunate** band (volar), tethers lunate to distal radius **proximal to the physis** → restricts ulno-palmar growth; 0.5–1.0 cm diameter; ± anomalous palmar radiotriquetral ligament

**Result** — **↑ radial inclination** (coronal) + **↑ volar tilt** (sagittal) + **ulnocarpal impaction**; **dorsal subluxation of distal ulna** (TO)

### Genes & lab markers

| Item | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Inheritance** | **Autosomal dominant** (Orthobullets, TO) · SHOX on X & Y → **sex-linked dominant** (OB) — read the options |
| **Gene** | **SHOX** (short stature homeobox), **pseudoautosomal region / tip of sex chromosomes** (TO) |
| **Léri-Weill dyschondrosteosis** | SHOX mutation → **mesomelic dwarfism + bilateral Madelung** |
| Other associations (TO) | **Turner syndrome**, achondroplasia |
| Madelung-like (TO) | **Multiple hereditary exostoses**, **Ollier disease** |

**SHOX gene distractors (TO)** — radial club hand → **Sonic hedgehog** · CPT → **NF1** · Blount → no named gene

**Other dysplasias (OB)** — cleidocranial → **CBFA-1** (AD) · pseudoachondroplasia → **COMP** chr 19 (AD) · Ellis-van Creveld → **EVC** (AR) · achondroplasia → **FGFR3** (AD)

## 3 Presentation

**Course** — **asymptomatic until late childhood/adolescence**; deformity **painless**, worsens with growth; advanced → pain limits function (TO)

**Symptoms** — **ulnar impaction**, **median nerve irritation**

**Exam** — **ulnar + volar displacement of hand**, prominent distal ulna, **restricted forearm rotation**, family history +

![Clinical Madelung deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6070-madelungs-deformity.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | Undergrowth of **volar-ulnar corner of radius**; **↑ radial inclination**, **↑ volar tilt**; **lunate uncovered**; carpus **wedge-shaped with lunate at apex**, subluxed ulnar + palmar (TO) |
| X-ray (TO) | ↑ radius–ulna width; **positive ulnar variance**; **↓ carpal angle**; triangular distal radial epiphysis; carpus migrates proximally |
| X-ray | ± proximal synostosis |
| **MRI** | **Thick Vickers ligament** distal radius → lunate |

![Madelung radiograph: increased radial inclination and volar tilt](../../MCQ/attachments/q3147-illa-madelung-deformity.jpg)

![T1 MRI: thick Vickers ligament](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6070-t1-mri-of-the-wrist-showing-a-thick-ligament-from-the-distal.jpg)

### Vender & Watson classification (TO)

| Type | Example |
|---|---|
| Post-traumatic | Physeal injury (e.g. gymnast's wrist) |
| **Dysplastic** | **Dyschondrosteosis**, diaphyseal achalasia (as printed in TO) |
| **Genetic** | **Turner syndrome** |
| Idiopathic | — |

## 5 Management

```
Madelung's deformity
├─ Asymptomatic → OBSERVATION  (stop repetitive wrist loading if painful)
├─ Skeletally immature, pain / ↓ ROM → PHYSIOLYSIS + VICKERS LIGAMENT RELEASE
│      └─ marked deformity → + DOME OSTEOTOMY ± ULNAR SHORTENING
└─ Skeletally mature → RADIAL OSTEOTOMY ± ULNAR PROCEDURE
       └─ painful DRUJ instability → DRUJ ARTHROPLASTY (controversial)
```

### Operative options

| Procedure | Indication / note |
|---|---|
| **Physiolysis + Vickers release** (volar approach; bar resection + fat graft) | Pain / ↓ ROM in immature; **83% radiographic improvement**, no ROM loss (OB); prophylactic release in mild cases — efficacy unknown |
| **Radial corrective (dome) osteotomy ± ulnar shortening** | Pain, ↓ ROM, cosmesis, function; dome corrects coronal + sagittal; severe → staged distraction ex-fix |
| Adolescents (TO) | **Physiolysis + ligament resection + dome osteotomy** preferred |
| Mature (TO) | Ligament resection + dome osteotomy · closing wedge + ulnar shortening (**Milch**) · opening wedge · + **Darrach** · + **Sauvé-Kapandji** |
| DRUJ arthroplasty | Painful DRUJ instability + ↓ pronosupination; controversial |

**Acute/recent cases (TO)** — dorsiflexion in **full arm plaster 4 wk**

## 6 Complications

**List** — incomplete physiolysis / premature arrest · violation of radiocarpal/ulnocarpal joint · under-correction · recurrence · osteotomy nonunion · **continued ulnar impaction if radial osteotomy alone**

## X vs Y

| | Madelung | Reverse Madelung |
|---|---|---|
| Ulnar variance | **Positive** | Negative |
| Physeal deficit | Volar-ulnar distal radius | — |
| Carpal adaptation | Adaptive **VISI** (↑ volar tilt) | — |

## How it's asked

**"Inheritance pattern?"** → **autosomal dominant** (TO)

**"SHOX mutation → which deformity?"** → **Madelung** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: radial club hand · Blount · CPT

**"SHOX sex-linked mutation → which dysplasia?"** → **Léri-Weill dyschondrosteosis**

**"Cause of Madelung?"** → **impaired growth of volar-ulnar distal radial physis**

**"Which physis is disrupted?"** → **ulnar volar physis of distal radius**

**"Tethering structure?"** → **Vickers ligament** (radiolunate)

**"Radial osteotomy alone — complication?"** → **continued ulnar impaction**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Mallet Finger

*Hand · 54 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — disruption of **terminal extensor tendon distal to DIP** (tendinous or bony avulsion); "baseball / drop finger"; extensor **zone 1** (Kleinert & Verdan)

**Demographics** — young–middle-aged ♂, older ♀; work/sport (ball sports)

**Digits** — **long (commonest) > ring > small**, dominant hand (Orthobullets, TO) · "ulnar three fingers" (TO)

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest** — **sudden forced flexion** (impaction) of extended fingertip → DIP hyperflexion avulses tendon

**Bony mallet** — **axial load** on extended finger → P3 abuts P2 head

**Less common** — dorsal sharp/crush laceration

**Untreated** — terminal tendon retracts → central slip overpulls P2 → **swan neck** (PIP hyperextension + DIP flexion) (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — painful swollen DIP; fingertip rests **~45° flexion**; **no active DIP extension** (TO)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + lateral finger: **bony avulsion at dorsal P3 base** or normal bones (tendinous); **assess volar subluxation of P3** (key decision)

**US** — not needed; loss of tendon motion, tears, fluid at insertion

![Bony mallet on lateral X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6014-xray-lateral-moved.jpg)

### Doyle classification

| Type | Description | |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Closed ± small dorsal avulsion fracture | ![Doyle classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6014-doyle-classification.jpg) |
| II | Open (laceration) | |
| III | Open, deep abrasion with skin + tendon loss | |
| **IV** | Mallet fracture: **A** physeal (child) · **B** 20–50% articular · **C** >50% articular | |

### Wehbe & Schneider classification (TO)

| Type | | Subtype | Articular surface |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **No DIP subluxation** | 1 | **<1/3** |
| **2** | **DIP subluxation** | 2 | **1/3–2/3** |
| **3** | Epiphyseal/physeal | 3 | **>2/3** |

**Most used** — Wehbe & Schneider (TO)

## 5 Management

```
Mallet finger
├─ Tendinous / bony, P3 NOT subluxed → FULL-TIME DIP EXTENSION SPLINT (6–8 wk)
│      └─ chronic, supple, congruent → still SPLINT TRIAL
├─ Volar subluxation of P3 (even after splinting) → CRPP (extension block pinning) / ORIF
└─ Painful stiff arthritic DIP → DIP ARTHRODESIS
       └─ swan neck → Fowler central slip tenotomy / lateral band or FDS tenodesis
```

### Nonoperative – continuous DIP extension splinting (bony & tendinous, delayed, residual lag)

**Treatment of choice (TO)** — **extension splinting of DIP only**; **no PIP immobilisation**; DIP in extension / slight hyperextension\
✗ Wrong options: PIP extension block · buddy strap

**Partial articular avulsion, no subluxation (TO)** — **splint distal phalanx in extension**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF

**Displaced fragment but joint reduced** — still **full-time DIP extension splint** (surgery ↑ complications)

**Regimen** — **24 h/day** (✗ Wrong options: 12 h), **6–8 wk** (✗ Wrong options: 2–4 wk); PIP free; then night splint

| Splint duration | Source |
|---|---|
| 6–8 wk full-time, then **4 wk nocturnal** (controversial) | Orthobullets |
| **6 wk** full-time, then **night 2–6 wk** | TO |
| **Bony 6 wk · tendinous ≥8 wk** | OB218821 |
| Bony 6–8 wk · tendinous **10–12 wk** | OB219900 |

**Splint details** — **volar** splint fewer complications than dorsal; avoid hyperextension (skin necrosis); swan-neck posture → add **dorsal blocking PIP splint**; stack splint; flexion exercises from 6 wk; extra night splinting no proven benefit

**Delayed presentation** — splint trial still works: **chronic >12 wk** supple, congruent, non-arthritic (Orthobullets) · **>4 wk** without fixed deformity or fragment >30% (TO) · 1 month (OB) → **chronic >6 wk ≠ surgery** (TO)

**Compliance** — key to success

**Outcome** — op = nonop for satisfaction & lag; ~80% satisfactory

**Residual lag <10°** — up to **40%** after op or nonop → **no functional deficit** (TO, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: dexterity loss · arthritis · swan neck/boutonnière

### Operative indications – volar subluxation & pinning; swan-neck risk

| Indication | Source |
|---|---|
| **Volar subluxation of P3 = absolute** | Orthobullets, OB |
| >50% articular surface · >2 mm articular gap/step-off · open injury (relative) | Orthobullets, TO |
| **All open injuries + large fragment >30% with DIP subluxation** | TO |
| Chronic failing splint trial | TO, OB |
| Chronic >12 wk without contracture, segmental loss → **tendon reconstruction** (~50% complications) | Orthobullets |
| Painful stiff arthritic DIP → **arthrodesis** | All |

**Fragment threshold conflict** — **>50%** (Orthobullets) · **>30% with subluxation** (TO) — read the options

**Bony mallet not fixed at earliest** (TO) — splint first; re-X-ray in splint before deciding

**Subluxed after closed reduction + splint** → **reduction and pinning**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat splint · PIP+DIP splint · fusion in young

**~50% articular + volar subluxation (athlete)** → **CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: play in cast · fusion · fragment excision + dermatotenodesis · 12 wk splint

**Extension block pinning sequence (TO, Ishiguro)** — **DIP flexion → K-wire through extensor tendon into middle phalanx (dorsal block) → DIP extension (reduces fragment) → K-wire across DIP**

![Extension block pinning](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6014-dorsal-blocking.jpg)

**Pinning hierarchy (TO)** — simple K-wire < **dorsal extension block (Ishiguro)**

**ORIF options** — dorsal midline; pins, dorsal blocking pin, screw, plate, suture-button

**Tendon repair** — direct repair + trans-articular pin; tenodermodesis; spiral ORL reconstruction

**Swan neck correction (TO)** — **Fowler central slip tenotomy**, **lateral band tenodesis**, FDS tenodesis; mild may correct with DIP treatment alone

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Extensor lag** | <10° in 40%, no deficit; **1 mm terminal tendon lengthening → 25° lag** |
| ↓ DIP flexion | **1 mm shortening**; adhesions |
| **Swan neck** | Terminal retraction → central slip overpull + **volar plate & transverse retinacular lig. attenuation** → dorsal lateral bands → PIP hyperextension; **triangular ligament contracture maintains** (TO) |
| Skin (maceration, ulcer, nail) | **~70%** with splinting; dehiscence/infection after surgery |

![Swan neck after untreated mallet](../../MCQ/attachments/q218113-d-figure-d-png.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Mallet → swan neck** | Boutonnière | Lumbrical plus |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Terminal tendon | **Central slip** + triangular lig. attenuation, volar lateral bands | FDP distal to lumbrical origin |
| PIP | Hyperextension | Flexion | Paradoxical extension |
| DIP | Flexion | Hyperextension | — |

## How it's asked

**"True regarding mallet finger?"** → **extension splinting of DIP only** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PIP + DIP · fix bony mallet early · >6 wk = surgery

**"Partial articular avulsion, no subluxation?"** → **splint distal phalanx in extension** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: buddy splint · ORIF · PIP extension block

**"Sequence of extension block pinning?"** → **DIP flexion → wire through tendon into P2 → DIP extension → wire across DIP** (TO)

**"Non-displaced bony mallet, 2 days?"** → **DIP extension splint 6–8 wk**

**"15 y, displaced fragment, joint reduced?"** → **full-time DIP extension splint**

**"Tendinous mallet regimen?"** → **24 h/day DIP splint for 6–8 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: 12 h · 2–4 wk · PIP

**"7° lag after 8 wk splint — outcome?"** → **no functional effect**

**"Bony mallet, 1 month, no subluxation?"** → **trial of extension splint 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: pinning · screw · fusion

**"Subluxed P3 after closed reduction + splint?"** → **reduction and pinning**

**"Untreated mallet risks which deformity?"** → **swan neck**\
✗ Wrong options: boutonnière · lumbrical plus · Dupuytren · scissoring

**"~50% fragment + volar subluxation, athlete?"** → **CRPP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# MCP Dislocations

*Hand · 55 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare, **<1/100,000/y**; less common than IPJ dislocations

**Direction** — **dorsal** most common

**Digit** — **index** > **thumb**

**Associated** — base P1 / MC head fractures in up to **50%**

### Anatomy

**Osteology** — condyloid; **cam-shaped MC head**; shallow concave P1 base; sesamoids in volar plate (thumb: FPB, APB)

**Proper collateral** — **primary stabiliser**; dorsal MC head → volar P1 base; **tight in flexion**

**Accessory collateral** — volar to proper → volar plate; **tight in extension**

**Volar plate** — thick fibrocartilage distally, membranous proximally; **A1 pulley attaches to it**; attaches to **deep** transverse MC ligament\
✗ Wrong options: superficial

**Dorsal capsule** — weak, thin, loose

**Sagittal bands** — keep extensor central during MCP flexion\
✗ Wrong options: interposed

## 2 Mechanism

**Dorsal** — FOOSH → **hyperextension** → **volar plate avulsed from MC neck/head**\
✗ Wrong options: hyperflexion · rotation

**Volar** — hyperextension or hyperflexion

## 3 Presentation

| | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Simple dorsal** | **P1 hyperextended** on MC head, **PIP flexed** |
| **Complex dorsal** | **Bayonet** P1 (dorsal to MC shaft), **skin dimpling proximal palmar crease**, palpable MC head in palm, digit in extension deviated toward central digits, can't flex MCP |
| **Volar** | **Extensor lag**, dorsal skin depression proximal to P1 base |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, **lateral (best view)**, oblique

**Complex signs** — **joint space widening** (volar plate interposed) · **sesamoid in joint = diagnostic of complex**

![Lateral X-ray: dorsal MCP dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6115-lat-moved.jpg)

### Classification

| Direction | Mechanism | |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal** (most common) | Hyperextension | ![Dorsal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6115-dorsal-dislocatiob.jpg) |
| Volar | Hyperextension / hyperflexion | ![Volar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6115-volar-dislocation.jpg) |

| Complexity | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Simple** (subluxation) | **No interposition**; volar plate draped over MC head; P1 base touches MC head | **Closed reduction** |
| **Complex** (complete) | **Volar plate ± sesamoid interposed** dorsal to MC head | **Open reduction** |

## 5 Management

```
MCP dislocation
├─ Simple → CLOSED REDUCTION
│      └─ fails / complex / delayed → OPEN REDUCTION
└─ Complex (sesamoid in joint, widening, dimpling) → OPEN REDUCTION (dorsal approach, split volar plate)
```

### Complex dorsal MCP dislocation – volar plate interposition & reduction manoeuvre

**Commonest block** — **volar plate**\
✗ Wrong options: flexor tendon · extensor · lumbrical · collaterals · 1st DI · natatory lig.

**Cadaver (Afifi)** — only **volar plate division** allows reduction; flexors move ulnar, lumbricals radial, radial digital nerve superficial

**Dorsal reduction manoeuvre** — **wrist flexion** (relax intrinsic & extrinsic flexors) + **direct dorsal-to-volar (distal & volar) pressure on P1 base** (same for thumb, index, small)

**Avoid longitudinal traction** — pulls volar plate into joint → **converts simple → irreducible complex**\
✗ Wrong options: wrist extension tightens noose

**Traction source conflict** — avoid traction (Orthobullets, OB) · "gentle" traction with dorsal pressure (OB219826) — read the options

**Volar reduction** — pressure on volar P1 with MCP flexed → immobilise **30° flexion 2 wk** → AROM in dorsal blocking splint

**After dorsal reduction** — early ROM + dorsal blocking splint

**Noose (dorsal complex)**

| Digit | Radial side | Ulnar side |
|---|---|---|
| **Index** | **Lumbrical** | **Flexor tendons** |
| Thumb | Intrinsics | **FPL** |
| Small | Flexors + lumbrical | ADM + FDM |

![Index noose: lumbrical and flexor tendon](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6115-buttonhole.jpg)

**Volar dislocation blocks** — **dorsal capsule**, avulsed volar plate / collateral ligament, **juncturae tendinum (4th–5th EDC) in small finger**, **1st DI in thumb**

| Open approach | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal** (midline, split extensor/capsule; thumb EPL–EPB interval; **split volar plate longitudinally**) | **↓ NV injury**, easy **MC head fracture fixation**, 14 min shorter, no 2nd approach | — |
| Volar (oblique, skin only; release **A1**) | Better volar plate access | **NV bundle at risk** (radial digital n. tented), hard osteochondral fixation; 42% needed 2nd dorsal approach |

### CMC fracture-dislocations – fixation

**Mechanism** — axial load (punch), hyperflexion; ~1% of hand/wrist injuries; often **5th ± 4th**, dorsal **hamate fracture**

| Scenario | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **All 5 CMC fracture-dislocations** | **Closed vs open reduction + internal fixation** (✗ Wrong options: cast · splint) |
| Open contaminated | External fixation |
| **Acute 4th CMC fracture-dislocation** | **CRPP** (✗ Wrong options: IM fixation · fusion · splint) |
| **Subacute >2 wk, still dislocated** | **Open reduction + fixation** (scar blocks closed reduction) |
| Acute (<3 d) isolated ulnar CMC dislocation | Closed reduction + splint can work (Zhang, Storken) |
| Severely comminuted MC base | CMC arthrodesis |
| Chronic painful | Fusion / arthroplasty |

**4th CMC fracture-dislocation** — **contraindicates 5th CMC suspension arthroplasty** (ECU slip)

## 6 Complications

**Stiffness** — injury trauma, prolonged immobilisation

**Post-traumatic arthritis / osteonecrosis** — repeated closed attempts, prolonged dislocation, traumatic open reduction

**Premature physeal closure** — rare

## X vs Y

| | **Simple dorsal** | **Complex dorsal** |
|---|---|---|
| Posture | P1 hyperextended, PIP flexed | Bayonet, P1 parallel dorsal |
| Palm | — | **Skin dimpling** |
| X-ray | Congruent contact | **Sesamoid in joint**, widening |
| Treatment | Wrist flexion + dorsal pressure | **Open (dorsal), split volar plate** |

## How it's asked

**"Irreducible dorsal index MCP — interposed structure?"** → **volar plate**

**"Mechanism + cause of failed closed reduction?"** → **hyperextension → volar plate entrapped dorsal to MC head**\
✗ Wrong options: flexor–lumbrical buttonhole

**"Simple dorsal MCP subluxation — next?"** → **wrist flexion + distal/volar pressure on P1**\
✗ Wrong options: axial traction · surgery first

**"Reduction manoeuvre for 5th MCP / thumb MCP dorsal dislocation?"** → **dorsal pressure on P1 with wrist flexed**\
✗ Wrong options: wrist extension · longitudinal traction

**"True of interposed structure?"** → **A1 pulley attaches to it**\
✗ Wrong options: prevents bowstringing (A2/A4) · superficial TML · midline tracking (sagittal band)

**"Cross-section — which structure blocks?"** → **volar plate**\
✗ Wrong options: DTML, sagittal band, proper collateral, dorsal capsule

**"Fracture-dislocation of all 5 CMC joints?"** → **closed vs open reduction + internal fixation**

**"4th/5th CMC dislocation seen at 3 wk?"** → **open reduction + fixation**

**"Acute 4th CMC fracture-dislocation?"** → **CRPP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# MCP Joint Arthritis

*Hand · 56 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency in hand** — **DIP > thumb CMC > PIP > MCP** (MCP least common)

**Cause** — mostly **inflammatory** (RA); primary OA rare (**6.1% ♂, 1.4% ♀**; OB: 1–5%); post-traumatic rare

**Sex** — OA & post-traumatic → **men**, younger · inflammatory → **women**; 4th–5th decade

**Risk** — intra-articular fracture, dislocation, rheumatic disease

**Trend** — ↓ incidence with **biologics**

### Anatomy

**Joint** — diarthrodial; MCP = **77% of total finger flexion arc**; ADLs need ~35° (50%)

**Volar plate** — ↑ congruence, prevents hyperextension

**Deep transverse MC ligament** — links **2nd–5th MC heads** at volar plate

| Collateral component | Tight in |
|---|---|
| **Accessory** | **Extension** |
| **Proper** | **30° flexion** |

**Thumb MCP** — 2 sesamoids; **adductor pollicis → ulnar sesamoid**; **FPB → radial sesamoid**; most thumb motion at **CMC**

### Genes & lab markers

| Disease | Mediator | Lab / aspirate |
|---|---|---|
| **RA** | **Neutrophil** proteases, ROS; synovial hypertrophy | **RF, anti-CCP** |
| **Gout** | MSU → **↑ IL-1β** | **Needle-shaped, strongly negatively birefringent** (yellow parallel) |
| **CPPD** | Ca pyrophosphate in synovium | **Rhomboid, weakly positively birefringent** |
| **SLE** | **Interferon-inducible genes** ↑ | **ANA** |
| **Haemochromatosis** | Iron → ↑ IL-1R | **Transferrin saturation, ferritin** |

## 3 Presentation

**OA** — pain, deformity · **inflammatory** — waxing/waning, other joints · **erythema** → gout/CPPD · **periarticular tophi** → gout

**RA fingers** — **ulnar deviation** (also secondary to **radial wrist deviation**) · synovitis attenuates dorsal capsule → **volar translation of proximal phalanx** · volar capsule laxity → **swan neck**

**RA thumb** — EPL displaced ulnar & volar → no active MCP extension · MCP flexion + IP hyperextension → **boutonnière** · **90/90 deformity** · CMC adduction → secondary MCP hyperextension · **gamekeeper's deformity** (UCL attrition) → **first web contracture**

![Thumb boutonnière: MCP flexion, IP hyperextension](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob423154-this-is-a-clinical-image-depicting-a-boutonniere-deformity-s.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique hand

| Disease | X-ray sign |
|---|---|
| **OA** | Narrowing, osteophytes, subchondral sclerosis, cysts |
| **RA** | **Bone loss dorsal base of proximal phalanx**; volar subluxation, ulnar drift |
| **Gout** | **Punched-out "rat-bite" erosions, overhanging edges**, sclerotic margins |
| **SLE** | **Jaccoud's** (deforming **non-erosive**) · erosive = **"rhupus"** |
| **Haemochromatosis** | **Hook-like osteophytes radial 2nd & 3rd MC heads**; symmetrical narrowing |
| **CPPD** | **2nd–5th MCP**, radial hook osteophytes |

### Larsen radiographic grading (RA)

| Grade | Finding |
|---|---|
| 0 | No change |
| I | Slight; periarticular swelling |
| II | Erosions + definite narrowing |
| III | Medium destruction; joint space poorly defined |
| IV | Severe destruction, collapse |
| V | Mutilating; articular surfaces gone |

## 5 Management

```
MCP arthritis
├─ Mild → NSAIDs, DMARDs, splint, steroid injection
└─ Failed 3–6 mo nonop
       ├─ Thumb MCP → ARTHRODESIS 20–25° flexion + slight pronation
       └─ Index–small MCP → ARTHROPLASTY (silicone / pyrocarbon)
```

**Nonop** — injections **temporary**; intrinsic-plus MCP blocking splint + dexterity exercises ↑ ROM; topical NSAID if GI/renal compromise

**Perioperative DMARDs** — **continue methotrexate & sulfasalazine**; cyclosporine/tacrolimus if severe; **stop biologics** (operate at end of dosing cycle)

### Thumb MCP arthrodesis position

**Gold standard** — **arthrodesis** (silastic arthroplasty = option); well tolerated as motion at CMC & IP

**Position** — **20–25° flexion + slight pronation** → **key pinch** (OB, Orthobullets) · "15° pronation" (Orthobullets technique)

**Advanced TMC disease** — ↑ flexion to **20–40°** offloads TMC

| Fixation | Key numbers |
|---|---|
| K-wire | **83–100% fusion**; ↓ pinch/dexterity; **16.7% complications** (nonunion, **EPL rupture**) |
| **Tension band** | **95% success**; ↓ infection/nonunion vs K-wire |
| Plate | Union **11.8 wk**; positioned 10–15° |
| IM screw | Mean 18°, union **10 wk**, 96% pain relief; MC head fracture, cut-out |

**Immobilise** — 4–6 wk / until union

### Finger MCP arthrodesis

**Indication** — index in **high-demand / young with instability**; otherwise **arthroplasty preferred** (motion)

| Finger | Fusion flexion |
|---|---|
| Index | **20–25°** |
| Long | **30°** |
| Ring | **35°** |
| Small | **40°** |

**Index union** — 84.6% at 11.6 wk

### Rheumatoid MCP arthroplasty

**Indication** — **severe finger MCP arthritis / fixed deformity** (ulnar drift, volar subluxation) → **MCP arthroplasty** = least extensor lag, best ulnar drift correction\
✗ Wrong options: tenosynovectomy · soft-tissue realignment alone · resection arthroplasty

**Soft tissue** — release ulnar collateral, tension radial; repair sagittal band, **centralise extensor**; intrinsic release/transfer (swan neck)

**Results** — arc **27–60°**; extensor lag **9–22°**; ~12° loss by 63 mo; pinch ↑ (index radialised)

![Swanson silicone implants](../../MCQ/attachments/q3613-illa-swanson-implants.jpg)

### Implant contraindications (pyrocarbon vs silicone)

| | **Silicone** | **Pyrocarbon** | Metal-plastic SRA |
|---|---|---|---|
| Design | **One-piece linked hinge**, spacer; fibrous capsule; 30° preflexed | **Unlinked, non-constrained** surface replacement; no ingrowth | Porous Ti MC + cemented PE |
| Stability from | **Implant itself** | **Collateral ligaments + volar plate** | Soft tissues |
| Best for | **Erosion / deformity (RA)** | **Milder, no deformity; OA** | — |
| Survival | 63–97% | RA 81% (10 y), 70% (16 y); OA > inflammatory | RA 95% / 85% (10/20 y); non-inflammatory 76% (10 y), satisfaction 72% |

**Pyrocarbon contraindication** — **deficient collateral ligaments**

**Not contraindications** — deficient radial sagittal band, radial lateral band, MC head subchondral bone, intermetacarpal ligaments

**Thumb MCP arthroplasty** — RA with fixed deformity or limited IP/CMC motion; silastic survival **83% at 5.8 y**; bone change 4%, fracture 2%

## 6 Complications

**Nonunion (thumb fusion)** — **9%**; risk **tension band**, smoking, DM → revision + distal radius graft

**Infection** — **0.1–1%**; ***S. aureus***; risk DMARDs, RA, DM → superficial 2 wk Abx · deep I&D + removal

**Instability** — RA: **SRA 31%, pyrocarbon 19%**; **pyrocarbon highest acute dislocation**; revision 71% success → **revise to silicone**

**Pyrocarbon** — ↑ failure & recurrent deformity in **inflammatory** arthritis

## How it's asked

**"RA, fixed MCP ulnar drift — least extensor lag at 1 y?"** → **MCP arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: tenosynovectomy, EIP→EDQ, resection

**"Thumb MCP fusion position"** → **20–25° flexion**

**"Thumb MCP fusion — flexion & rotation"** → **20° flexion + slight pronation**\
✗ Wrong options: supination · 40°

**"Pyrocarbon vs silicone contraindication — true?"** → **deficient collaterals contraindicate pyrocarbon**

**"Thumb vs fingers"** → **thumb = arthrodesis; index–small = arthroplasty**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Metacarpal Fractures

*Hand · 57 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **40% of all hand injuries**; most common hand injury

**Who** — **men 10–29 y**

**Site** — **neck = commonest site**; **5th MC = commonest bone**

### Anatomy

**Arch** — palmar concave; **1st, 4th, 5th = mobile borders**; **2nd + 3rd = stiff central pillar** (index most firmly fixed)

**CMC mobility** — 2nd/3rd CMC stiff → **less tolerance of angulation** in index/long

**Transverse (deep) intermetacarpal ligament** — **primary restraint to shortening** of isolated 3rd/4th shaft fractures; stays intact in shaft fracture\
✗ Wrong options: rotation · MCP/CMC stability · fracture prevention

| Tendon | Insertion | Deforming role |
|---|---|---|
| ECRL / ECRB | Base MC II / III | — |
| **ECU** (PIN) | **Base MC V** | **Pulls 5th MC base dorsal + proximal** |
| APL | Base MC I + trapezium | Bennett/Rolando |
| Opponens pollicis / digiti minimi | MC I / MC V | — |

**Interossei** — 3 palmar + 4 dorsal arise from MC shafts

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — direct blow / punch; rotational + axial load; high energy → **multiple fractures**

**Associations** — open wounds (tendon, NV injury), **compartment syndrome** (crush, multiple fractures)

### Boxer's fracture

**Definition (TO)** — **transverse or short oblique fracture of the 5th MC neck** (less often 4th)\
✗ Wrong options: head · shaft

**Facts (TO)** — **most common MC fracture**; punching with clenched fist; **apex dorsal angulation**; closed reduction + cast or K-wire

## 3 Presentation

**Fight bite over MCP** — **open until proven otherwise**; dorsal wounds over MC fractures almost always open

**Malrotation** — line up fingernails in partial + full flexion, compare sides → **scissoring/overlap**; best assessed after block

![Assessing malrotation in flexion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6037-metacarpal-rotation.jpg)

**Shortening** — compare knuckles with other hand

**Base deformity** — suspect **CMC dislocation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Best for |
|---|---|
| PA + lateral | All |
| **ER oblique** (supinated) | **4th/5th CMC** fracture-dislocation |
| **IR oblique** (pronated) | **2nd/3rd CMC** |
| **Brewerton** | **MC head** fractures |
| **Roberts** | **Thumb CMC** |
| CT | Inconclusive CMC injury, multiple CMC dislocations, complex head fractures; **after reduction** of 5th CMC injury |

**CMC fracture-dislocation clues** — MC shortening on AP; dorsal base subluxation / dorsal hamate fragments on lateral

### Acceptable angulation & shortening; extensor lag

| Digit | Shaft angulation (Orthobullets/OB) | Shaft angulation (TO) | Neck angulation | Shortening |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Index & long | **10–20°** | **>10°** → fix | **10–15°** | 2–5 mm |
| Ring | **30°** | **>25°** → fix | **30–40°** | 2–5 mm |
| Little | **40°** | **>45°** → fix | **50–70°** | 2–5 mm |

**Conflict** — ring 30° / little 40° (Orthobullets, OB) · **ring 25° / little 45°** (TO) — read the options

**Shortening** — **2–5 mm acceptable** (Orthobullets, OB) · fix if **>5–6 mm** (TO)

**Shortening effect** — **7° extensor lag per 2 mm** (linear, not digit-dependent) + **8% ↓ power per 2 mm** (↓ interosseous force; intrinsics 40–90% of grip)

**Shortening >2–5 mm** — **MCP extension lag + ↓ grip** (loss of extensor tension)

**Apex dorsal angulation (index/long)** — **pseudoclawing** (MCP hyperextension, PIP extension lag) because stiff 2nd/3rd CMC cannot compensate; reduce if >10°

**Rotation** — overlap, no extensor lag, no pseudoclaw

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Malrotation** | **Any scissoring** / >10° (TO) — **absolute**, least tolerated |
| Angulation | Beyond table limits |
| Shortening | >5–6 mm (TO) |
| **Open** fracture | Any |
| **Intra-articular** | Any (head: **no articular step acceptable**) |
| **Multiple** MC fractures | Any — loss of adjacent support |
| Associated CMC dislocation, extensor lag (TO) | Any |

**Not indications** — comminution alone, manual-labour occupation, ring sagittal angulation 15° (TO)

## 5 Management

```
Metacarpal fracture
├─ Stable, no rotation, within limits → NONOP
│      └─ 5th neck → BUDDY TAPE + SOFT WRAP, OPTIONAL FOLLOW-UP
├─ Rotation / open / intra-articular / beyond limits → CRPP or ORIF
└─ Multiple MC fractures → ORIF ALL
```

### Nonoperative care – buddy taping & early return

**Cast** — **MCP 70–90° flexion**, PIP free, **4 wk**

**Jahss reduction** — MCP 90° flexed, dorsal push through proximal phalanx, stabilise shaft

**5th neck, no rotation (<50–70°)** — **buddy taping 4–5 + soft wrap**, **optional follow-up** → better satisfaction, **earliest RTW**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar gutter splint/cast · CRPP

**Athletes, minimally displaced spiral ring MC** — ORIF: **no faster union, but faster return to play**; no better grip/ROM

### Rotational deformity – scissoring as fixation indication

**Strongest fixation indication** — **scissoring of digits with flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: 20° 4th neck angulation (30° OK) · 4 mm shortening · comminution · occupation

**Untreated multiple MC shaft fractures** — rotational malunion → **digits overlap in fist** → corrective osteotomy

**Not MC fracture sequelae** — sagittal band rupture (can hold passive extension) · loss of key pinch / Wartenberg / ulnar numbness (ulnar neuropathy)

### Operative fixation – multiple fractures, ORIF & intramedullary screws

**Multiple shaft fractures** — **ORIF**; **fix all three**\
✗ Wrong options: casting → stiffness · ex-fix binds extensors · removable splint → shortening

**Techniques**

| Fixation | Fact |
|---|---|
| CRPP | Antegrade from base or retrograde via collateral recess; remove pins **4 wk** |
| **Lag screws** | Long spiral/oblique; ≥2 screws |
| **Dorsal plate** | **Transverse**; cover with periosteum; extensors less adherent at MC level → low adhesion |
| **IM headless screw** | **Only axially stable** (transverse) patterns; corrects angulation/rotation; not for oblique/shortened/comminuted (**least amenable = long oblique axially unstable**); unstable if fracture length >2× shaft diameter |
| Head ORIF | Dorsal approach, split extensor or release/repair sagittal band; screws in collateral recess / headless / K-wires; hardware not proud |
| Severe head comminution | Ex-fix or **MCP arthroplasty**; late arthritis → fusion |

**Post-op** — **early motion critical**; head fracture commonest complication = **stiffness**

**Neck ORIF** — difficult (little distal bone)

### Fifth CMC fracture-dislocation – deforming forces & pinning

**Incidence** — **<1% of hand trauma**; often **missed**; 5th ± 4th; axial load on clenched fist

**Deforming force** — **ECU** (insertion base 5th MC) → **innervated by PIN**\
✗ Wrong options: radial (ECRL only) · ulnar · AIN

**Acute** — **closed reduction + splint first** → **CT after reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: CT first · immediate CRPP/ORIF

**Reduction lost at 1 wk** — **CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat reduction · discontinue immobilisation · arthrodesis

**Delayed / irreducible** — **open reduction + pinning**; **comminuted intra-articular 5th base + displaced 4th shaft (>30°)** → **fix both**

**Arthrodesis** — chronic / painful post-traumatic arthritis only

**Dorsal ulnar approach** — **dorsal branch of ulnar nerve** (from **medial cord**), arises **~6 cm proximal to ulnar styloid** → retract (neuroma)

![4th/5th CMC fracture-dislocation](../../MCQ/attachments/q211369-illa-cmcj.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**List** — **stiffness** (commonest, head) · malunion (rotation → scissoring; angulation → pseudoclaw; shortening → extensor lag) · tendon irritation (plate) · arthritis

## X vs Y

| | Shortening | Apex dorsal angulation | Malrotation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sign | **MCP extensor lag**, ↓ grip | **Pseudoclawing** (index/long) | **Scissoring / overlap** |
| Tolerance | 2–5 mm | Digit-specific | **None** |

| | Shaft | Neck |
|---|---|---|
| Angulation tolerance | Less | **More** |
| Little finger | 40° (45° TO) | **50–70°** |
| Ring | 30° (25° TO) | **30–40°** |

## How it's asked

**"Fixation indications EXCEPT?"** → **15° sagittal angulation in ring finger** (TO)

**"Extensor lag + ↓ grip after closed treatment — cause?"** → **5 mm shortening**

**"Effect of shortening?"** → **7° extensor lag per 2 mm**

**"Role of transverse intermetacarpal ligament?"** → **limits shortening**

**"Little finger neck — max acceptable angulation?"** → **50–70°**

**"Index/long healed with deformity — pseudoclawing cause?"** → **30° apex dorsal angulation**

**"Strongest indication to fix 4th neck?"** → **scissoring with flexion**

**"Multiple MC shafts untreated — complaint?"** → **digits overlap in fist**

**"Buddy taping + optional follow-up?"** → **angulated 5th MC neck without rotation**

**"ORIF vs nonop in athlete?"** → **same union time, faster return to play**

**"Fastest RTW for 5th neck <50°?"** → **buddy taping 4th–5th**

**"Multiple MC shaft fractures (polytrauma) — definitive?"** → **ORIF**

**"Three MC fractures from airbag?"** → **fix all three**

**"Least amenable to IM headless screw?"** → **oblique, axially unstable, shortened shaft**

**"Comminuted 5th base + angulated 4th, 2 wk old?"** → **fix both**

**"4th/5th CMC redislocated at 1 wk?"** → **CRPP**

**"Deforming muscle — nerve?"** → **ECU — PIN**

**"Dorsal ulnar approach nerve — from?"** → **medial cord** (dorsal branch ulnar n.)

**"Acute 5th base fracture-dislocation — next step?"** → **closed reduction + splint** (then CT)

**"Boxer's fracture is?"** → **5th MC neck fracture** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Nail Bed Injury

*Hand · 58 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — fingertip injuries = **most common hand injury in ED**; **♂:♀ 3:1**

**Mechanism** — crush between objects, closing door, saw, snowblower, hammer

**Associated** — **DIP fracture/dislocation**, **distal phalanx fracture**

### Anatomy

| Structure | Definition |
|---|---|
| Perionychium | Nail + nail bed + surrounding skin |
| Paronychium | Lateral nail folds |
| Hyponychium | Skin distal + palmar to nail |
| Eponychium | Dorsal nail fold (proximal) |
| Lunula | White proximal nail |
| **Sterile matrix** | **Distal to lunula**, adheres to nail |
| **Germinal matrix** | **Proximal to sterile**; **most nail production**; extensor insertion **1.2–1.4 mm proximal** |

![Fingertip anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6109-fingertip.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP, lateral, oblique** of finger → rule out **distal phalanx fracture**; child: **physeal separation (Seymour)**

### Injury types

| Type | Finding | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Subungual haematoma <50%** | Crush; blood under nail | **Trephination** (sterile needle / electrocautery) |
| **Subungual haematoma >50%** | | **Nail removal, I&D, nail bed repair** |
| **Laceration** | Nail intact + haematoma >50% | Nail removal + repair; tetanus + antibiotics |
| **Avulsion, minimal matrix loss** | Higher energy; ± DP fracture | Repair ± fracture fixation (**reduce fracture**); tetanus + antibiotics |
| **Avulsion/crush, significant matrix loss** | | Repair + **STSG or nail matrix graft** (adjacent injured finger or **2nd toe**) ± fixation |

![Subungual haematoma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6109-subungual-hematoma.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Nail bed injury
├─ Subungual haematoma <50% → trephination
├─ Haematoma >50% / laceration → nail removal + nail bed repair
└─ Avulsion ± DP fracture → repair + reduction ± fixation
       └─ significant matrix loss → matrix graft (toe / adjacent finger)
```

### Repair technique – tissue adhesive & Seymour fracture

**Nail** — remove → **soak in Betadine** during repair

**Suture** — **6-0 or smaller absorbable**

**Dermabond (2-octylcyanoacrylate)** — RCT (Strauss, level 1): **only advantage = faster procedure**; no better cosmesis, function, pain, infection (all equal)

**Splint eponychial fold** — original nail, aluminium, or non-adherent gauze (prevents fold adhesion)

**Seymour fracture** — child, nail torn **transversely beneath eponychium** + **juxta-epiphyseal distal phalanx physeal separation** (nail bed trapped) → **I&D + physeal reduction + nail bed repair + immobilisation**\
✗ Wrong options: splint alone · home Betadine soaks · I&D + splint without reduction · pinning first

**Early repair** — superior to secondary correction (91% good–excellent in children); outcome depends on **germinal matrix** injury

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Hook nail** | Matrix advanced for coverage **without bony support** | **Remove nail + trim matrix to level of bone** |
| **Split nail** | **Matrix scarring** | **Excise scar + replace matrix** (± graft) |

![Hook nail](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6109-hook-nail.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Dermabond vs suture for nail bed — advantage?"** → **faster procedure**\
✗ Wrong options: cosmesis · function · pain · infection

**"Child, nail torn under eponychium + DP physeal fracture — next step?"** → **I&D, reduction, nail bed repair, immobilisation** (Seymour)

**"Subungual haematoma <50%, nail intact?"** → **trephination**

**"Hook nail treatment?"** → **trim matrix to level of bone**

**"Split nail cause?"** → **matrix scar** → excise + matrix graft

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Nerve Conduction Studies

*Hand · 59 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Uses

**Components** — **NCV + EMG** (= EDS)

**Uses** — localise **compression**/neuropathy; **UMN vs LMN**; root vs plexus vs peripheral; **severity & prognosis** (neurapraxia good; axonotmesis/neurotmesis poor); denervation/reinnervation; **worker's compensation**/secondary gain

**Indications** — CTS, cubital tunnel, cervical & lumbar radiculopathy, shoulder nerve dysfunction (winging)

**Pretest probability** — EDS most useful when nerve injury **likely** (severity, location, prognosis); low probability + nonspecific sx → inconclusive/misleading; adjunct after Hx + Ex

**Fibres tested** — **large myelinated** motor + sensory (position, vibration, some light touch)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Nerve conduction velocity – measures

![NCV technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6113-nerve-conduciton-velocity.jpg)

**Stimulus** — **0.1–0.2 ms square wave**; motor studies add a 2nd proximal stimulus (cancels NMJ delay)

**NCV** — **distance ÷ latency** (cathode → recording electrode); NCV ∝ 1/latency

**Latency** — stimulus onset → response onset (incl. NMJ transmission + muscle propagation)

**NCV determinants** — **myelin thickness**, internode distance, temperature, age

| Age | NCV (% adult) |
|---|---|
| Newborn | **50%** |
| 1 y | **75%** |
| 5 y | **100%** |

**Amplitude** — baseline → negative peak (mV) = **number of functioning axons/muscle fibres**

**Duration** — spread of CVs; ↑ in acute demyelination

**Late responses (proximal lesions, e.g. GBS)** — **F-wave**; **H-reflex** (Iα at knee → soleus, **S1 root**; tibial/sciatic neuropathy)

### Demyelination – increased latency

**Demyelination** — **↑ latency** + **↓ conduction velocity**; amplitude normal/↓; evoked response absent/prolonged\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ amplitude · normal evoked response · unchanged CV/latency

**Velocity vs latency** — velocity **less specific** than latency; **<52 m/s abnormal**

**Clinical focal demyelination** — ↓ DTRs, ↓ vibration, intermittent pain/paraesthesia; **no motor strength/sensibility loss**

### Interpreting NCS in carpal tunnel syndrome

**Abnormal for CTS (Orthobullets NCS note)** — **distal sensory latency >3.2 ms** · **motor latency >4.3 ms**

**Mild–moderate CTS** — **↑ latency + ↓ NCV, normal amplitude** = focal demyelination, axon number intact

**Severe/chronic CTS** — **↓ amplitude** (SNAP/CMAP) = axonal loss from ischaemia → surgery less predictable

**Prognosis** — mild–moderate + temporary relief from steroid injection → **CTR predictably relieves**

**EDS in CTS** — not needed for diagnosis; only EDS grades mild → severe; ambiguous cases, outcome prediction

**SNAP vs CMAP** — sensory nerve action potential vs compound motor action potential

### Findings on NCV

| Condition | Latency | Velocity | Amplitude | Evoked response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Normal | N | UL **>45 m/s** · LL **>40 m/s** | N | N |
| **Axonal** | N | N | **↓** | Prolonged |
| **Demyelinating** | **↑** | **↓** | N/↓ | Absent/prolonged |
| Anterior horn cell | N | N | ↓ | N or polyphasic, prolonged |
| Myopathy | N | N | ↓ | N |
| NMJ | N | N | ↓ | N |
| Neurapraxia – proximal / distal to lesion | Absent / **N** | Absent / **N** | Absent / **N** | Absent / **N** |
| Axonotmesis – proximal / distal | Absent / Absent | Absent / Absent | Absent / Absent | Absent / N |
| Neurotmesis – proximal / distal | Absent / Absent | Absent / Absent | Absent / Absent | Absent / Absent |

### Electromyography

**Purpose** — root vs nerve vs muscle; acute vs chronic; reinnervation; nerve continuity

**Insertional activity** — **↑ (>300–500 ms) = early denervation** (polymyositis, myotonia, myopathy); **↓ = prolonged denervation** (fibrosis)

**Normal spontaneous** — **end plate potentials & spikes**

**Fibrillations** — **single** muscle fibre; denervated fibre membrane oscillation; appear **3–5 wk** after lesion; also muscular dystrophy

**Fasciculations** — **group** of fibres; **ALS**, spinal muscular atrophy, polio, syringomyelia; "**bag of worms**"

**Other abnormal** — positive sharp waves, complex repetitive discharges, myokymic discharges

| Condition | Insertional | Spontaneous | Minimal activity | Interference |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Normal | N | Silent | Bi/triphasic | Complete |
| **Axonal neuropathy** | ↑ | **Fibs / PSW** | Bi/triphasic | Incomplete |
| **Demyelinating neuropathy** | N | **Silent** | Bi/triphasic | Incomplete |
| Anterior horn cell | ↑ | Fibs/**fasciculations**, PSW | **Large polyphasic** | Incomplete |
| Inflammatory myopathy | ↑ | Fibs, myotonia | **Small polyphasic** | Early |
| Noninflammatory myopathy | N | N | Small polyphasic | Early |
| NMJ disorder | N | N | Bi/triphasic (↓ amplitude/duration) | Early/N |
| **Neurapraxia** | N | **Silent** | None | None |
| Axonotmesis / neurotmesis | ↑ | **Fibs / PSW** | None | None |

## X vs Y

| | **Demyelination** | **Axonal loss** |
|---|---|---|
| Latency | **↑** | N |
| Velocity | **↓** | N |
| Amplitude | N (or ↓) | **↓** |
| EMG | Silent | Fibs, PSW |
| CTS grade | Mild–moderate | Severe |
| CTR outcome | Predictable | Less predictable |

| | Fibrillation | Fasciculation |
|---|---|---|
| Unit | **Single fibre** | **Group of fibres** |
| Classic | Denervation (3–5 wk) | ALS, anterior horn disease |

## How it's asked

**"Demyelination on NCS?"** → **increased latency**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ amplitude · unchanged velocity

**"↑ latency, ↓ NCV, equal amplitude + transient injection relief — counsel?"** → **mild–moderate CTS; surgery predictably relieves**

**"Amplitude reflects?"** → **number of functioning axons**

**"NCV of 1-year-old?"** → **75% of adult**

**"Fasciculations seen in?"** → **ALS / anterior horn disease**

**"Fibrillations appear after?"** → **3–5 weeks**

**"H-reflex tests?"** → **S1 root**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Peripheral Nerve Injury & Repair

*Hand · 60 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 16.9 / 100,000 / yr; 2.6% of UE trauma, 1.2% of LE trauma; ~3% of UE/hand injuries

**Demographics** — **♂ 80%**, age 32–39

**Risk factors** — penetrating / high-energy trauma, crush, displaced fractures

**Commonest UE nerve injury (OB)** — **ulnar nerve**, working-age males

### Nerve microanatomy (epineurium, perineurium, endoneurium)

| Layer | Covers | Function / MCQ fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Epineurium** | Group of fascicles → whole nerve | **Cushions against external pressure**; collagen + elastin; focus of surgical repair |
| **Perineurium** | **Individual fascicle** | **Tensile strength + elasticity**; blood–nerve barrier; limits diffusion (protects from oedema) |
| **Endoneurium** | **Individual axon** (+ Schwann cell, myelin) | Forms **Schwann cell tube**; nourishes axon; **must be intact for full recovery** |
| Myelin | Axon | Schwann cells; ↑ conduction velocity; saltatory at **nodes of Ranvier** |

**Cross-section labels (OB)** — A axon · B myelin · C epineurium · **D perineurium** · E endoneurium · F node of Ranvier

**✗ distractors** — fibronectin/laminin (ECM, directional branching), N-cadherin (adhesion, growing axons)

**Neuron** — cell body <10% of mass (metabolic centre); axon conducts; dendrites receive

**Blood supply** — extrinsic vessels in loose tissue + intrinsic plexus in epi-, peri-, endoneurium

![Nerve cross-section with fascicles](../../MCQ/attachments/q5434-illa-with-fascicel.jpg)

### Fibre types

| Fibre | Diameter | Myelin | Speed | Carries |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 10–20 µm | Heavy | Fast | Touch (Aβ) |
| **Aδ** | Thin | Myelinated | Medium-fast | **Pain + temperature** (TO) — sharp, first pain |
| B | <3 µm | Moderate | Medium | Autonomic |
| **C** | <1.3 µm | None | Slow | Pain — dull, delayed (TO) |

### Sensory receptors

| Receptor | Adaptation | Senses | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Merkel** | **Slow** | Pressure, texture, low-freq vibration | **Static 2PD** |
| Meissner | Rapid | Light touch | Moving 2PD |
| Pacinian | Rapid | **High-freq vibration**, rapid indentation (~1 mm ovoid) | Vibration |
| Ruffini | Slow | Skin stretch | |

## 2 Mechanism

**Stretch** — **8% elongation → ↓ microcirculation 50%**; **15% → ↓ 80%**; e.g. stingers, suprascapular n. in volleyball, CPN after TKA valgus correction

**Compression** — **30 mmHg → paresthesia**, ↑ latency; **60 mmHg → complete conduction block**; endoneurial oedema → fibrosis → ↓ gliding

**Chronic compression** — conduction change from **Schwann cell proliferation + apoptosis**; Schwann cells ↑ VEGF\
✗ Wrong options: Wallerian degeneration · axon transection · cell body apoptosis

**Compressive neuropathy (cubital tunnel, NCV 31 m/s)** — **neurapraxia of ischaemic origin**\
✗ Wrong options: axonotmesis · endoneurial disruption

**Laceration** — sharp better than crush; ends retract

| Classic injury | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Anterior shoulder dislocation | Axillary |
| **Holstein-Lewis** (distal 1/3 humerus), Saturday night palsy, extension SCH | Radial |
| Distal humerus ORIF, flexion SCH, OR positioning | Ulnar |
| **Extension SCH**, prominent olecranon TBW pins (anterior ulnar cortex) | AIN |
| Posterior hip dislocation | Sciatic |
| TKA valgus correction | CPN |
| Percutaneous tibial plating, holes 11–13 | Superficial peroneal |

### Regeneration physiology

**Wallerian degeneration** — **distal** to injury (TO); axoplasm + myelin degraded by **phagocytes**; proximal reaction = primary/retrograde degeneration (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Schwann cells

**Sequence** — distal Wallerian → Schwann cells proliferate, line basement membrane → **proximal budding after ~1 month** → **antegrade** growth **1 mm/day**\
✗ Wrong options: distal → proximal · neurotrophic factors direct phagocytosis

**Advancing Tinel** — ~**1 inch/month** (TO); **most reliable sign of recovery**

**Guidance** — contact guidance (Schwann basal lamina), neurotropism, neurotrophic factors

**Order of recovery** — **sympathetic → pain → temperature → touch → proprioception → motor**; loss in reverse; **motor first lost, last to return**

**Myelin** — disrupted before axon

## 3 Presentation

**Diagnosis** — clinical (injury + deficit); EMG/NCS mainstay; exploration confirms traumatic injury

**Radial palsy (Holstein-Lewis) last to recover** — **wrist extension**\
✗ Wrong options: elbow extension: spared at this level

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Injury classification (Seddon / Sunderland)

| Seddon | Sunderland | Damaged | Wallerian | Tinel advances | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Neurapraxia** | **1** | Focal demyelination (ischaemia); axon intact | No | No (no motor march; simultaneous proximal + distal recovery) | **Complete**, days–6 wk |
| Axonotmesis | **2** | **Axon**; endo-, peri-, epineurium intact | Yes | Yes | **Full** (intact endoneurial tube) |
| Axonotmesis | **3** | Axon + **endoneurium**; peri-/epineurium intact | Yes | Yes | **Most variable**, incomplete |
| Axonotmesis | **4** | Axon + endo- + **perineurium**; **only epineurium** intact | Yes | No | **Neuroma-in-continuity**; poor without surgery |
| Neurotmesis | **5** | All layers (transection) | Yes | No | None without repair |
| — | **6 (Mackinnon)** | Mixed degrees in one nerve | | | Mixed; neuroma in continuity (TO) |

**Sunderland 2 = axon disrupted, endoneurial tube intact** (TO); regeneration because **endoneurium** intact\
✗ Wrong options: epineurium, perineurium, myelin, Schwann cell

**Tinel progression** — **Sunderland II & III** (TO)

**Neurotmesis range** — Sunderland **5** (Orthobullets classification) · **3rd–5th** (Orthobullets treatment section, OB214026) — read the options

**Full recovery prevented by** — **endoneurial injury = neurotmesis** (structure A in figure)

![Seddon / Sunderland table](../../MCQ/attachments/q4659-illa-nerve-injury-table-1-1.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

**High-resolution US** — dynamic, superficial nerves; localises entrapment, cut ends, neuromas

**MRI / MR neurography** — brachial plexus; **better for muscle denervation**, deep structures, static resolution

### Sensory receptors & electrodiagnostics

| Study | Finding | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **NCS** | ↑ latency, ↓ velocity, ↓ SNAP/CMAP | Large myelinated fibres; compression/demyelination |
| **EMG** | **Fibrillations, positive sharp waves** | **Acute denervation** (axonotmesis / neurotmesis) |
| EMG | Fasciculations, myokymia | Neurogenic |
| EMG | CRDs, myotonic discharges | Myopathy |
| **Polyphasic MUPs** | | **Early reinnervation** (TO) |
| Chronic MUPs (large amplitude, long duration) | | Established reinnervation (TO) |
| ↓ SNAP & CMAP | | Axonal loss (quantitative) (TO) |
| **↓ / absent insertional activity** | | **Poor potential for surgical reinnervation** (TO) |
| ↓ recruitment | | Loss of innervated MEPs (TO) |

**Thresholds** — DSL **>3.2 ms**, DML **>4.3 ms** abnormal; velocity **<52 m/s** (Orthobullets) · **<50 m/s across elbow** (OB); amplitude ↓ 50% vs contralateral

**SNAPs affected before CMAPs**; EMG normal until late

**Cubital tunnel NCS** — **prolonged distal sensory latency** + ↓ velocity\
✗ Wrong options: fibrillations, PSW, fasciculations = EMG · ↑ velocity

**Neurapraxia** — NCV slowing/conduction block, **no fibrillations**

**EMG value** — often only objective evidence (workers' comp)

## 5 Management

```
Nerve deficit
├─ Closed injury / neurapraxia / Sunderland 1–2 → observe + serial EMG (baseline 3–4 wk)
│      └─ no recovery by 3 mo → explore
├─ Sharp / iatrogenic / vascular injury / progressive → early exploration + repair (7–14 d)
└─ Gunshot with transection → repair at 1–3 wk (zone of injury declares)
```

```
Gap after resecting to healthy fascicles
├─ Tension-free → primary epineurial (or fascicular) repair
├─ Small sensory gap (≤20 mm) → collagen conduit / processed allograft
└─ >5 cm or motor/mixed → autograft (sural; cable if size mismatch)
```

### Surgical repair

**Repair window** — best **7–14 d**; unpredictable after **6 mo**; reinnervation by **18 mo** goal; time limit **18 mo**

**Epineurial repair** — resect proximal neuroma + distal glioma to **"mushrooming" fascicles**; align with epineurial vessels; **tension-free**; gain length by transposition/neurolysis

**Fascicular repair** — perineurial sutures; outcome **= epineurial**; ↑ scarring; matching by topography, **stimulation (proximal: sensory in awake pt; distal: motor before Wallerian)**, histology

**Direct muscular neurotization** — stump into muscle; ↓ neuroma; **TMR, RPNI** for amputees

### Timing of repair after ballistic injury

**GSW with transected radial nerve** — definitive repair **1–3 wk** after injury\
✗ Wrong options: at initial debridement · at fracture fixation · after 3 mo · wait for neuroma

**GSW without transection / closed humerus fracture** — observe **3 mo** (mostly neurapraxia); GSW brachial plexus → observe

### Repair options – conduit, allograft & autograft by gap length

| Option | Gap | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Collagen conduit** | **≤20 mm** (Orthobullets, OB) · <3 cm (OB4644) · tubular wrap <1 cm (OB219234) | **Type I collagen** (= **ligaments**; not cartilage/NP II, basement membrane IV, vessels III); relies on **fibrin clot**; nutrient + neurotrophic access; **8 mm digital gap → as good as autograft** (✗ Wrong options: PGA, GTMC, silicone, end-to-end under tension) |
| **Decellular allograft** | Sensory **<3 cm**; effective up to **7 cm** | **Scaffold (microtubules, laminin) for host Schwann cell migration**; no living cells, no immunosuppression; not yet for motor/mixed |
| Non-processed allograft | | Needs immunosuppression — rarely used |
| Vein conduit | Digital | not >5 cm, not superior |
| **Autograft** | **>5 cm (50 mm)** | **Gold standard**; graft **≥10% longer** than gap; sural (palm level), MABC, LABC, terminal PIN; **cable** for mismatch; donor morbidity |

**Quality of recovery** — drops with gaps **>5 mm**

**Segmental defect, motor recovery** — **nerve autograft** best\
✗ Wrong options: allograft, conduits, vein

![Nerve graft reconstruction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6066-post-recon.jpg)

### Prognosis – nerve, level & order of recovery

**Favourable** — **younger age (most important)**, **distal level (2nd)**, sharp / stretch injuries

**Unfavourable** — older age, proximal level, crush/blast, delay, **ulnar nerve**, concomitant bone/soft-tissue injury, graft length

**Best motor recovery after graft (<6 mo)** — **radial**\
✗ Wrong options: median, ulnar, tibial, peroneal

**Ulnar** — poor outcomes (worse than tibial, radial, femoral, MC)

✗ Wrong options: **Prognostic factor** — **type of autograft** (sural vs saphenous); ✓ time to repair, level, length, rehab

**High median laceration + 6 cm sural cable graft — poorest recovery** — **thumb opposition** (intrinsics; endplates survive ~**12 mo**)\
✗ Wrong options: wrist flexion, finger flexion, FPL, sensation

**Critical period of delay (TO)**

| Nerve | Critical period |
|---|---|
| **Radial** | **15 mo** |
| PIN | 9 mo |
| Ulnar high / low | 9 / 15 mo |
| Median high / low | 9 / 12 mo |
| Sciatic | 12–15 mo |
| CPN | 12 mo |

### Nerve transfers – AIN to ulnar motor (SETS)

**Indication** — proximal injury: deliver axons before **motor endplate** loss; priority shoulder abduction/ER, elbow flexion, hand

| Goal | Transfer |
|---|---|
| Shoulder abduction + ER | **Spinal accessory → suprascapular** |
| Shoulder abduction | **Medial triceps branch (radial) → axillary** (Leechavengvong) |
| Elbow flexion | **Oberlin**: FCU fascicle (ulnar) → biceps branch (MC); double Oberlin adds FCR/FDS fascicle (median) → brachialis |
| Ulnar intrinsics | **Terminal AIN (from PQ) → deep motor ulnar, supercharged end-to-side (SETS)** |

**SETS purpose** — **maintain finger abduction/adduction** (interossei) while repaired ulnar nerve regrows; **motor only**\
✗ Wrong options: sensation

**Proximal forearm ulnar laceration** — **ulnar repair + end-to-side AIN → deep motor branch**\
✗ Wrong options: end-to-end: requires dividing deep branch · repair alone

**SETS contraindication** — **absent preop CMAPs** (endplates gone)\
✗ Wrong options: scarce fibs, intrinsic atrophy = indication, ↓ SNAP, present CMAP

**Martin-Gruber** — 15%; some intrinsic function spared in high ulnar injury

**Success** — ≥ M3 (many M4); easier rehab than tendon transfer; nerve transfer within **6 mo**, tendon transfer any time\
✗ Wrong options: cortical re-education

### Tendon transfers for irreparable palsy (radial, ulnar claw, peroneal)

**Principles** — supple joints first; synergistic; similar power (cross-sectional area); straight line of pull; one tendon one function; **lose one MRC grade**; better <30 y, distal

| Smith excursion rule | cm |
|---|---|
| Wrist flexors / extensors | **3** |
| EDC, FPL, EPL | **5** |
| FDS, FDP | **7** |

| Palsy | Function | Transfer |
|---|---|---|
| **Radial** | Wrist extension | **PT → ECRB** |
| Radial | Thumb extension | **PL (or FDS IV) → EPL** |
| Radial | Finger MCP extension | **FCU → EDC (Jones)** · FCR → EDC (Brand) · FDS → EDC (Boyes) |
| **Median** | Opposition | **EIP → APB (Burkhalter)** · **FDS ring (Riordan)** · **PL → APB (Camitz)** (TO) |
| Median | Thumb IP flexion | **BR → FPL** |
| Median | Index flexion | Side-to-side FDP |
| Ulnar | Simple claw (**+ Bouvier**) | **Zancolli lasso** (FDS around A1), MCP volar plate capsulodesis, A1/A2 release |
| **Ulnar** | **Complex claw (− Bouvier)** | **FDS → lateral bands** (or FCR + grafts) |
| Ulnar | Ring/small flexion | Side-to-side FDP to median FDP |
| Shoulder ER | | Lower trapezius → infraspinatus |
| Elbow flexion | | Steindler flexor-pronator advancement; pec major, lat, triceps |
| **CPN foot drop** | Dorsiflexion | **Tibialis posterior → dorsum of foot** |

**Camitz in long-standing CTS (TO)** — **PL → APB** via same incision\
✗ Wrong options: Burkhalter, Riordan, Jones

**Bouvier test** — block MCP hyperextension → active IP extension? **Yes = simple claw** (static procedure) · **No = complex claw** (dynamic FDS → lateral bands)

**Radial palsy >12 mo, no conduction** — **PT → ECRB, FCU → EDC, PL → EPL**\
✗ Wrong options: neurolysis, grafting, AIN → ECRB / FCR → PIN nerve transfers

**Radial palsy natural history** — 85–90% recover in 2–3 mo; EMG at 3 mo

**CPN palsy after THA, 6 mo, brace intolerant, supple** — **tibialis posterior transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat decompression · arthrodesis: fixed deformity/arthritis · BKA

## 6 Complications

### Neuroma after digital amputation

**Neuroma** — disorganised proximal regrowth; amputees up to **30%** (Orthobullets)

**Digital amputation** — **<10%** symptomatic (6.6–7.8%); **>½ need revision within 1 yr**; rate higher than after neurorrhaphy\
✗ Wrong options: improves spontaneously · revision after 1 yr

**Symptoms** — sharp/burning pain, cold intolerance, pressure hypersensitivity

**Treatment** — gabapentin/antidepressants, phenol/botox, rehab → resection, **TMR**, **RPNI**

**Grafting** — donor neuroma; allograft rejection

### Metabolic neuropathy – vitamin B12

**B12 deficiency** — **peripheral sensory neuropathy** (✓ check B12); **↓ DTR**, Babinski, fatigue\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ DTR · inability to whistle = FSHD · hydrophobia = rabies

## X vs Y

| | **Neurapraxia** | **Axonotmesis** | **Neurotmesis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wallerian | No | Yes | Yes |
| EMG fibs/PSW | No | Yes | Yes |
| Advancing Tinel | No | Yes (II, III) | No |
| Recovery | Complete, simultaneous | Variable, proximal → distal | Needs repair |

| | **Simple claw** | **Complex claw** |
|---|---|---|
| Bouvier | Positive | Negative |
| Extensor mechanism | Intact | Stretched |
| Procedure | Static: capsulodesis, Zancolli lasso, pulley release | **Dynamic: FDS → lateral bands** |

## How it's asked

**"Cushions nerve against external pressure?"** → **epineurium**

**"Cross-section labels A–F?"** → **axon, myelin, epineurium, perineurium, endoneurium, node of Ranvier**

**"Item D in nerve cross-section?"** → **perineurium**

**"Axon disrupted, endoneurial tube intact — Sunderland?"** → **2** (TO)

**"Wallerian degeneration is?"** → **distal to severed nerve** (TO)

**"Advancing Tinel in which Sunderland?"** → **II and III** (TO)

**"Sunderland 2 regenerates because intact?"** → **endoneurium**

**"Cubital tunnel, slowed NCV — classify?"** → **neurapraxia, ischaemic origin**

**"Regeneration after axonotmesis?"** → **proximal budding → antegrade migration**

**"Chronic compression conduction change from?"** → **Schwann cell proliferation + apoptosis**

**"Injury preventing full recovery?"** → **endoneurium (A); neurotmesis**

**"8 mm digital gap — as good as autograft?"** → **collagen conduit**

**"Segmental defect — best motor recovery?"** → **nerve autograft**

**"Conduit collagen = primary collagen of?"** → **ligaments (type I)**

**"Decellular allograft purpose?"** → **scaffold for host Schwann cell migration**

**"Gap requiring autograft over conduit?"** → **50 mm**

**"Critical period, radial nerve?"** → **15 months** (TO)

**"Best motor regeneration after graft?"** → **radial nerve**

**"NOT prognostic after repair?"** → **type of autograft**

**"Holstein-Lewis palsy — last to return?"** → **wrist extension**

**"High median 6 cm graft — poorest recovery?"** → **thumb opposition**

**"GSW, transected radial nerve — repair when?"** → **1–3 weeks**

**"Contraindication to SETS?"** → **absent preop CMAPs**

**"Purpose of AIN → ulnar SETS?"** → **maintain finger abduction/adduction**

**"Proximal forearm ulnar laceration — best early hand function?"** → **ulnar repair + end-to-side AIN transfer**

**"Long-standing CTS, opponensplasty?"** → **Camitz (PL → APB)** (TO)

**"Claw, negative Bouvier?"** → **FDS → lateral bands**

**"Radial palsy 2 yr after humerus ORIF?"** → **PT → ECRB, FCU → EDC, PL → EPL**

**"Persistent foot drop after THA, brace intolerant?"** → **tibialis posterior transfer**

**"Fibres for pain + temperature?"** → **A delta** (TO)

**"Polyphasic MUPs?"** → **early reinnervation** (TO)

**"Slowly adapting, static 2PD?"** → **Merkel**

**"Cubital tunnel NCS finding?"** → **prolonged distal sensory latency**

**"B12 deficiency causes?"** → **peripheral sensory neuropathy**

**"Neuroma after digital amputation — counsel?"** → **<10%; those affected need surgery before 1 yr**
