# Phalanx Dislocations

*Hand · 61 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**PIP joint** — **most commonly injured joint in hand**; **dorsal > volar**

**DIP** — usually dorsal or lateral; volar rare; often **open** (tight envelope)

**Mechanism** — direct blow; fracture-dislocations by **avulsion** or **impaction shear**

### Anatomy

| Stabiliser | PIP | DIP |
|---|---|---|
| Proper collateral | Proximal phalanx condyle → volar base P2 | P2 condyle → volar base P3 |
| Accessory collateral | → volar plate | → volar plate |
| Dorsal | **Central slip** (dorsal base P2) | Terminal extensor (dorsal base P3) |
| Volar tendon | FDS (volar shaft P2) | FDP (volar base P3) |
| **Volar plate** | **Checkrein ligaments** proximally; resists **hyperextension** | no checkreins; resists hyperextension |

**Collaterals** — resist radial/ulnar deviation

## 3 Presentation

**Skin puckering** — soft-tissue interposition

**Stability tests** — lateral stress in **full extension** (secondary stabilisers) and **30° flexion** (collaterals); hyperextension (volar plate); **Elson** (central slip); full active ROM = stable

**Collateral grades** — I pain, no laxity · II laxity, firm endpoint · III no endpoint

**Neuro** — traction neurapraxia of adjacent digital nerves

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, oblique

**Dorsal "V sign"** — dorsal joint widening = **subtle dorsal subluxation of P2** → unstable

![V sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6038-v-sign.jpg)

### PIP dislocation types

| Type | Lesion | Reduction | After reduction |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal simple** | Hyperextension + axial load; collaterals torn, **volar plate** sheared ± small avulsion; P2 touches condyles | Volar force + flexion (often by patient) | Stable → **buddy tape 3–6 wk** · unstable → **extension block splint** |
| **Dorsal complex** | **Bayonet**; P2 off condyles; **volar plate blocks** traction | Hyperextend then palmar force | Open (dorsal approach between central slip & lateral band) if fails |
| **Volar simple** | **Central slip rupture** | Flexion + dorsal force at P2 base | **PIP full extension splint**, DIP + MCP free |
| **Volar rotatory** | **One collateral torn**, condyle **buttonholes between central slip & lateral band** | **MCP + PIP 90° flexion + traction** (relaxes lateral band) | Open via **dorsolateral** approach if fails |
| **Lateral** | One collateral + partial volar plate | Closed | Buddy tape; block = **lateral band** |

**Blocks to reduction** — closed dorsal PIP: **volar plate** · open dorsal PIP: **FDP** · lateral: **lateral band** · closed dorsal DIP: **volar plate** (open: FDP) · volar DIP: **terminal tendon**

**Volar PIP splint duration** — **6–8 wk** (Orthobullets) · **4–6 wk** (OB) · **6 wk** (OB) — read the options

![Rotatory volar PIP dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6038-pip-volar-dl-rotatory.jpg)

### Hastings classification (PIP fracture-dislocation, % P2 articular surface)

| Type | % | Stability | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | **<30%** | Stable | ![Hastings](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6038-hastings-classification.jpg) |
| II | **30–50%** | Tenuous | |
| III | **>50%** | Unstable | |

**Commonest osseous injury with dorsal PIP fracture-dislocation** — **middle phalanx volar (palmar) lip fracture**

**Volar fracture-dislocation** — hyperflexion → central slip → **dorsal lip** avulsion

**Pilon** — comminuted dorsal + volar lips (axial load)

## 5 Management

### Dorsal PIP dislocation/fracture-dislocation – extension block splinting

**Primary goal** — **maintain joint reduction → congruent gliding arc**\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic articular reconstruction · volar plate reattachment · immobilise to union

**Best predictor of outcome** — **congruent gliding joint** > early motion > anatomic surface

**Avoid hinging** — subluxation → poor outcome

| Operative threshold | Source |
|---|---|
| **<40%** stable → closed + splint; **>40%** unstable → CRPP/ORIF | Orthobullets |
| **<25%** + reduces in flexion → EBS; **>25%** or incomplete reduction → surgery; **>50%** → open/VPA | OB |
| Stable <30% buddy · tenuous 30–50% EBS if reduced in <30° flexion · unstable >50% surgery | OB (Hastings) |

**Extension block splint (EBS)** — stable reduced dorsal **fracture**-dislocation <40%; block **~40°**, reduce **10°/wk** over 3 wk, active flexion within splint

**Redislocates only in full extension, <25% involvement** — **EBS 3 wk**

**Avoid cast 6 wk** — stiffness

```
Dorsal PIP fracture-dislocation
├─ Stable, small fragment → buddy tape + early motion
├─ Tenuous, reduces in flexion → EXTENSION BLOCK SPLINT
└─ Unstable (>40–50%, V sign despite splint)
       ├─ reconstructable fragment → ORIF / extension block pinning
       ├─ pilon / comminuted → DYNAMIC DISTRACTION EX-FIX
       ├─ young, ~50%, not fixable → HEMI-HAMATE
       └─ chronic → volar plate arthroplasty / arthrodesis
```

### Unstable/chronic fracture-dislocations – volar plate arthroplasty, hemi-hamate & ORIF

**Acute >50% volar rim, V sign at 30° flexion, young athlete** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: buddy · EBS · VPA in young acute

**Volar plate arthroplasty** — acute unstable or **chronic** dorsal FD (>40% comminuted volar surface, e.g. neglected 5 wk); excise fragments, trough, advance plate, dorsal sutures

**VPA must release** — **collateral ligaments** (else hinging)

**Volar exposure** — resect **proximal C2, entire A3, distal C1** pulleys

**Hemi-hamate** — **non-vascularised osteochondral autograft with hyaline cartilage** from **hamate**; young, ~50% P2 base, chronic/irreducible\
✗ Wrong options: MFC, iliac crest, fibula, distal radius

**Older sedentary chronic** — **PIP arthrodesis**

**Intra-articular proximal phalanx condylar fracture with step** — **ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: buddy, splint, arthrodesis in young

![Hemi-hamate reconstruction](../../MCQ/attachments/q218169-illa-illustration-a.jpg)

### Volar PIP dislocation – central slip injury & reduction/splinting

**Most often injured (TO)** — **central slip**\
✗ Wrong options: sagittal bands, lumbrical, juncturae

**Dorsal PIP (TO)** — volar plate injured → early ROM after reduction

**Volar PIP Rx** — **closed reduction + full-time PIP extension splint**; DIP + MCP free (lateral band motion)\
✗ Wrong options: EBS = dorsal

**Untreated** — **boutonnière** (3 wk later: can't extend PIP → central slip)

**Open central slip repair** — subacute/chronic developing boutonnière

**Rotatory volar** — no in-line traction (noose tightens) · no hyperextension · not always open

### DIP dislocations & fracture-dislocations

**Reduction** — traction + dorsal pressure on P3 + flexion; I&D if open

**Splint** — dorsal: slight flexion 2–3 wk if unstable · volar: **extension 6 wk**

**Irreducible closed dorsal DIP** — **volar plate** entrapment → open reduction\
✗ Wrong options: central slip, rotatory, collateral, MCP natatory/transverse ligament

**Open reduction** — after 2 failed attempts; FDP repair if attached to volar fragment

**>40% unstable** — VPA · comminuted without soft-tissue loss — arthrodesis · with NV/soft-tissue loss — amputation

**Tuft fracture** — no specific Rx

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Seen in |
|---|---|
| **Pseudoboutonnière** (PIP flexion contracture) | Commoner after volar; usually resolves with therapy |
| **Swan neck** | Dorsal (volar plate injury) |
| **Boutonnière** | Volar (central slip) |
| Extensor lag | Volar |
| DIP stiffness | DIP |
| Mallet | Volar DIP dislocation |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest osseous injury with dorsal PIP fracture-dislocation"** → **P2 volar lip fracture**

**"Which X-ray best treated by dorsal extension block splint?"** → **stable reduced dorsal PIP fracture-dislocation <40%**

**"Stable dorsal FD, no hinging — next step"** → **EBS + active ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 6 wk · VPA

**"Redislocates in full extension, <25% involved"** → **EBS 3 wk**

**"Most important predictor of outcome"** → **congruent gliding joint**

**"Primary objective"** → **maintain joint reduction**

**"Intra-articular proximal phalanx fracture with step-off"** → **ORIF**

**"Neglected 5-wk dorsal FD, >40% comminuted volar surface"** → **volar plate arthroplasty**

**"Structure excised in VPA"** → **collateral ligament**

**"Chronic irreducible PIP FD, osteochondral autograft donor"** → **hamate**

**">50% volar rim, V sign despite 30° flexion splint"** → **ORIF**

**"Irreducible closed DIP dislocation — block"** → **volar plate**

**"Most often injured in volar PIP dislocation"** → **central slip** (TO)

**"Volar PIP dislocation — initial Rx"** → **closed reduction + full-time extension splint**

**"Boutonnière 3 wk after reduced volleyball injury — structure"** → **central slip**

**"Rotatory volar PIP — reduction manoeuvre"** → **MCP + PIP 90° flexion + traction**

**"Post-reduction volar PIP splinting"** → **PIP full extension, DIP + MCP free**

# Phalanx Fractures

*Hand · 62 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — commonest skeletal injuries; **10% of all fractures**

**Sex** — **♂:♀ 2:1**

**Location** — **distal > middle > proximal phalanx**; **small finger** commonest (38% of hand fractures)

**Mechanism by age** — 10–29 y sports · 40–69 y machinery · >70 y falls

**Association** — distal phalanx # → **nail bed injury**

### Anatomy

**Parts** — P3: tuft, shaft, base · P1/P2: head, neck, shaft, base

**Insertions** — terminal extensor → dorsal base P3 · **FDP → volar base P3** · central slip → dorsal base P2 · **FDS → volar shaft P2**

**FDP origin** — **anteromedial proximal ulna** (upper ¾ ulna, olecranon/coronoid, IOM)\
✗ Wrong options: epicondyles; lumbricals arise from FDP tendons

**IP joint** — hinge; dynamic stability from pinch/grip compression; passive from **proper + accessory collaterals**

**Digital NV** — dominant artery on side **closer to midline**; nerve **volar** to artery

### Deforming forces – interossei & central slip (apex volar)

| Fracture | Proximal fragment | Distal fragment | Apex |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P1 shaft (transverse)** | **Flexed by interossei** | **Extended by central slip** | **Volar** |
| P2 distal to FDS insertion | Flexed by FDS | — | **Volar** |
| P2 proximal to FDS insertion / P2 base | Extended by central slip | Flexed by FDS | **Dorsal** |
| P2 with long intact FDS | — | — | No angulation |
| P2 neck | — | — | Volar |
| **Seymour (P3 epiphysis)** | Terminal tendon on proximal epiphysis | FDP on distal fragment | Dorsal (**mallet look**) |

**Proximal fragment flexor** — **interossei** (Orthobullets, most OB) · "lumbrical insertions" (OB217355) — read the options

**P1 base** — transverse # in **proximal 6–9 mm** more stable (collaterals, capsule, intrinsics)

**Reduction manoeuvre** — **flex MCP** → extensor mechanism acts as tension band (**intrinsic-plus**)

![Deforming forces in phalanx fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6114-deforming-forces.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — swelling, deformity (angular, **rotation**, shortening), **open wounds**

**Scissoring** — rotational component; assess via **tenodesis**

**NV** — 2-point discrimination; cap refill <2 s

![Scissoring = malrotation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6114-scissoring.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **PA, lateral, oblique** of digit (orthogonal)

**X-ray** — P1 apex volar; P2 apex volar (distal to FDS) / dorsal (proximal to FDS)

**CT** — articular displacement, comminution

### Condylar (head) fractures — P1 & P2

| Type | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| I | **Stable**, undisplaced | ![Condylar classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6114-condylar-fracture-classification.jpg) |
| II | **Unstable unicondylar** | |
| III | **Unstable bicondylar / comminuted** | |

### Shaft & base patterns

| Bone | Patterns |
|---|---|
| P1 | Shaft: short oblique, **long oblique**, **spiral**, transverse · base: extra-articular, intra-articular lateral base |
| P2 shaft | Transverse, short oblique, long oblique, spiral |
| **P2 base – volar** | Hyperextension/axial → **volar plate avulsion**; unstable if **>40%** articular surface (Orthobullets) |
| P2 base – dorsal | Hyperflexion → **central slip avulsion** |
| P2 base – lateral | Collateral avulsion |
| **P2 pilon** | Complete articular (axial load; dorsal + volar cortex) → **unstable in all directions** |
| P3 tuft | **Crush**; stable (nail plate + pulp); **nail matrix/pulp laceration** |
| P3 shaft | Transverse, longitudinal |
| P3 base | **Usually unstable**: shear (axial, **>20%** surface) or avulsion (terminal tendon / FDP) |

### PIP fracture-dislocation stability (OB)

| Volar lip (P2 base) involvement | Stability |
|---|---|
| **<30%** | Stable → closed reduction |
| **30–50%** | Tenuous; depends on post-reduction stability |
| **>50%** | **Unstable** → surgery |
| Needs **>30° PIP flexion** to hold | Unstable regardless of size |

**Conflict** — volar base unstable if **>40%** (Orthobullets) · **>30% / >50%** bands (OB) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
P1 / P2 extra-articular
├─ <10° angulation + <2 mm shortening + no rotation → buddy tape / splint 3 wk → aggressive ROM
├─ >10° · >2 mm · ANY rotation · open → CRPP or ORIF
└─ Unstable pattern (spiral, long oblique, comminuted) → fixation
       └─ needs early ROM (e.g. musician) → ORIF (lag screws ± plate)
```

### Operative criteria

| Bone | Nonoperative | Operative |
|---|---|---|
| **P1 & P2** | Extra-articular **<10° angulation, <2 mm shortening, no rotation**; undisplaced intra-articular | **>10° angulation**, **>2 mm shortening**, **any rotation**, displaced intra-articular, unstable/irreducible, **grossly contaminated open** |
| **P3** | Most: closed reduction ± splint (nail matrix may block reduction) | Displaced/irreducible shaft · **dorsal base >25%** · **displaced volar base with large FDP fragment** · nonunion · nail bed injury → remove nail, repair bed, replace nail (keeps eponychial fold) |

**EXCEPT trap** — **5° apex dorsal** angulation not an indication (atypical, <10°)

### Operative indications & fixation methods

| Pattern | Fixation |
|---|---|
| Transverse / short oblique P1 | **Two 0.045-in K-wires longitudinally through flexed MCP (metacarpal head)** or PIP; single wire risks rotational malunion |
| Very proximal P1 | **Eaton-Belsky** pinning through metacarpal head |
| **Long oblique / spiral P1** | **Open reduction + lag screws (1.3 mm > 1.5 mm), radial approach**; CRPP ≈ lag screws long term |
| Oblique extending to condyles | **Lag screw** fixation |
| **Unicondylar / bicondylar head** | Unstable → **ORIF** |
| Comminuted bicondylar / comminuted shaft | **Plate**, radial approach (↓ adhesions) |
| P2 | Crossed K-wires, extension-block pinning, collateral recess pinning, mini-fragment, volar plate arthroplasty, **hemi-hamate (>50% articular)** |
| P3 | Longitudinal/crossed K-wires, extension-block, lag screw |
| Intramedullary screw | Shaft #s; not too-proximal P1 |

**Plate** — **allows early ROM** (main reason for ORIF) but **higher complications** (52% in Guerrero; hardware removal, tenolysis/capsulotomy 30%) than CRPP/lag screw; one RCT: plate via mid-lateral → ↑ TAM

**Distal phalanx nonunion ORIF** — **high risk of symptomatic implant** → removal

### Nonoperative care – immobilisation & early motion

**Position** — **intrinsic-plus (MCP flexion)** → prevents MCP stiffness\
✗ Wrong options: MCP + IP extension

**Duration** — **3 wk immobilisation → ROM** (Orthobullets, OB) · buddy-tape 4 wk (Meals, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: immediate ROM without immobilisation → displaces

**Nonop P1 in young** — <60% active motion maintained

### Intra-articular fractures & avulsions (pilon, volar lip)

| Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Bony mallet | Extension splint 6–8 wk |
| DIP fracture-dislocation, small articular piece | Closed reduction ± splint |
| Undisplaced P3 # | None / Alumafoam for comfort |
| **Unicondylar P1 head** | **ORIF** |
| Dorsal PIP fracture-dislocation, congruent + stable 30–90° | **Extension-block splint** |
| Dorsal PIP # -dislocation, subluxed | Extension-block pinning / pin volar fragment / ORIF |
| Large volar articular piece | **ORIF** (buttress plate / screws) |
| **Pilon P2 base** | **Dynamic external fixator** (✗ Wrong options: static) |
| **Unstable dorsal PIP subluxation, comminuted volar lip / malunion with arthrosis** | **Hemi-hamate arthroplasty** (needs intact dorsal cortex) |
| Volar fracture-dislocation (dorsal lip #) | Extension splint, CRPP, transfixion, mini/hook plate, screws |

**HHA not for** — pilon or dorsal lip # (dorsal cortex not intact)

![Pilon fracture of P2 base](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6114-mp-base-pilon.jpg)

### Paediatric phalangeal neck/condylar fractures

**Malunion blocking subcondylar fossa** (7 y, PIP 40° arc, stable) → **ostectomy** of subcondylar fossa\
✗ Wrong options: observe: P1 physis proximal → no remodelling · osteotomy: AVN of small fragment · stretching/dynamic splint: bony block

## 6 Complications

### Loss of motion

**Most common complication** (incl. after ORIF PIP fracture-dislocation) — risk: prolonged immobilisation, intra-articular #, extensive dissection → aggressive therapy → surgical release

### Malunion & shortening – PIP extensor lag

**Apex volar P1 malunion** — effectively **shortens extensor** → **PIP extensor lag**\
✗ Wrong options: MCP motion · PIP flexion · scissoring without rotation

| Number | Effect |
|---|---|
| **1 mm P1 shortening** | **12° PIP extensor lag** |
| **16° apex volar** | **10° extensor lag** |
| <15° apex volar | Loss of extension only |
| >25° apex volar | Flexion + extension lost |

**Site → lag** — **P1 shortening → PIP lag**; P2 distal to FDS → DIP lag; P2 proximal to FDS no PIP lag; MC neck no lag

**Treatment** — symptomatic → **corrective osteotomy at malunion** (preferred) · metacarpal osteotomy (limited) · soft-tissue release

**Nonunion** — **<2%**, atrophic, bone loss/NV compromise → resection, graft, plate; ray amputation/fusion

## X vs Y

| | P1 shaft | P2 distal to FDS | P2 proximal to FDS / base |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apex | **Volar** | Volar | **Dorsal** |
| Malunion lag | **PIP** | DIP | No PIP lag |

| | CRPP | Lag screws | Plate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pattern | Transverse/short oblique; long oblique | **Long oblique / spiral** | Comminuted, bicondylar |
| Early ROM | Less | ✓ | ✓ |
| Complications | Low | Low | **Highest** (stiffness, hardware) |

## How it's asked

**"Cause of apex palmar P1 deformity?"** → **central slip on distal fragment + interossei on P1 base**\
✗ Wrong options: swan neck · boutonnière · intrinsic fibrosis

**"Interossei and central slip deform P1 how, respectively?"** → **proximal flexion, distal extension**

**"True of ORIF of distal phalanx nonunion?"** → **high risk of symptomatic implant**

**"Optimal fixation, long oblique P1?"** → **open reduction + 1.3 mm lag screws, radial approach**

**"Oblique P1 into condyles, can't move PIP/DIP?"** → **reduction + lag screw**

**"P1 operative indications EXCEPT?"** → **5° apex dorsal angulation**

**"Long oblique P1 with rotation — best outcome, fewest complications?"** → **CRPP**\
✗ Wrong options: plate: more complications

**"Indication for surgery after reduced P1 fracture?"** → **5° rotational deformity**

**"Why plate instead of cast/CRPP in a violinist?"** → **need for early ROM**

**"Undisplaced transverse phalanx fracture — treatment?"** → **immobilise with MCP flexed**

**"Reduced P1 fracture, no rotation/shortening/angulation?"** → **short immobilisation, ROM at 3 wk**

**"17° volar malunion — which movement lost?"** → **PIP extension**

**"Untreated angulated P1 fracture — sequela?"** → **loss of PIP extension**

**"Shortened healing causing PIP extensor lag — which bone?"** → **proximal phalanx**

**"Child, PIP motion blocked by subcondylar malunion?"** → **ostectomy**

**"Acute pattern best for ORIF?"** → **unicondylar phalangeal head fracture**

**"Dynamic external fixator — which fracture?"** → **pilon fracture of P2 base**

**"Origin of tendon avulsing P3 volar base?"** → **anteromedial proximal ulna (FDP)**

**"Commonest complication of ORIF of PIP fracture-dislocation?"** → **loss of motion**

**"Unstable dorsal PIP subluxation — hemi-hamate for which film?"** → **dorsal fracture-dislocation with large volar lip fragment**\
✗ Wrong options: pilon · dorsal lip · pure dislocation

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Physical Exam of the Hand

*Hand · 63 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Digital nerve anatomy

**Median nerve after TCL** — splits into smaller **lateral** + larger medial portion

**Lateral portion** — **recurrent motor branch** + **3 proper palmar digital nerves**: radial thumb, ulnar thumb, **radial index** → **one common branch off median nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: radial nerve origin for any of them

| Thumb–index pattern (Jolley) | % |
|---|---|
| Type 1: radial digital thumb + common digital to 1st web (→ radial index + ulnar thumb) | **69** |
| Type 2: common digital to thumb (→ radial + ulnar thumb) + proper radial index | 6 |
| Type 3: **trifurcation** of 3 proper digital nerves | 25 |

**Nerve vs artery** — **digits: nerve VOLAR to artery** ("it hurts before it bleeds"); **palm: nerve DORSAL** (superficial arch volar)

**Radial digital n. index (Lourie)** — at intersection of central index line with **proximal palmar crease**

**Dorsal branches** — proper digital n. at base of P1 → dorsal branch → supplies dorsum of distal phalanx (joins superficial radial)

## 3 Presentation

### Inspection & palpation

| Finding | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Erythema / white / blue-purple / black spots | Cellulitis / arterial insufficiency / venous congestion / melanoma |
| Trophic change (hair, sweat) | Sympathetic derangement |
| **Thenar atrophy** | **Median** (CTS) |
| **Interossei atrophy** | **Ulnar** (cubital tunnel, cervical radiculopathy) |
| Subcutaneous atrophy | Post-steroid injection |
| **Cascade sign** | Flexed fingers converge on **scaphoid tubercle**; non-convergence → **malrotation** |
| Warm / cool | Infection / vascular |

### Normal range of motion

| Joint | Arc |
|---|---|
| MCP | 0–85° |
| PIP | 0–110° |
| DIP | 0–65° |
| Wrist | 60° flexion · 60° extension · **50° radioulnar deviation arc** |

### Neurovascular exam

| Nerve | Motor test |
|---|---|
| Radial | **Thumb IP extension** against resistance |
| Median – recurrent motor | **Palmar abduction** of thumb |
| Median – AIN | Thumb IP + index DIP flexion (**OK sign**) |
| Ulnar | **Cross fingers** / abduct against resistance |

**Vascular** — radial + ulnar pulse, **Allen's**, capillary refill; **sensation** — 2-point discrimination

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Special tests – wrist & DRUJ

| Test | Pathology |
|---|---|
| **Watson (scaphoid shift)** | **SL instability** — thumb on distal pole, radial→ulnar deviation; dorsal pain/**clunk** |
| **Reagan (LT ballottement)** | **LT instability** — pisotriquetral unit vs lunate; laxity + pain |
| **Kleinman shear** | LT instability |
| **Lichtman** | **Midcarpal instability** — axial load, radial→ulnar deviation; **clunk in ulnar deviation** |
| TFCC grind / **ulnocarpal abutment** | TFCC tear / ulnar impingement — ulnar deviation + axial load; pain, pop/click |
| **Fovea sign** | TFCC or **ulnotriquetral ligament split tear** |
| **ECU snap** | ECU instability |
| **Piano key** | **DRUJ instability** |

### Special tests – hand

| Test | Pathology / technique |
|---|---|
| **Grind** | **Thumb CMC** arthritis — axial load + rotate 1st MC; pain, crepitus, instability |
| **Finkelstein** | **De Quervain** — fist over thumb + ulnar deviation; pain 1st compartment |
| **FDP test** | Hold MCP + PIP extended → flex DIP |
| **FDS test** | Hold other fingers fully extended (blocks FDP) → tested finger flexes PIP |
| **Gamekeeper's** | **Thumb UCL** — radial stress in flexion + extension; **>30° laxity** or gross vs other side |
| **Tinel** (CTS) | Percuss distal palmar crease midline → median paraesthesia |
| **Phalen** (CTS) | Gravity wrist flexion, **max 2 min** |
| **Froment** (ulnar) | Paper key pinch → **thumb IP flexes** |
| **Wartenberg** (ulnar) | Fingers adducted + extended → **small finger drifts into abduction** |
| **Jeanne** (ulnar) | Key pinch → **thumb MCP hyperextension** |

![Froment sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6008-froment.jpg)

### Intrinsic tightness test (Bunnell)

**Technique** — passively flex PIP with **MCP extended**, then with **MCP flexed**

**Intrinsic tightness (positive)** — **↓ PIP flexion with MCP extended** (lateral bands tensioned); PIP flexes easily with MCP flexed

**Extrinsic tightness** — ↓ PIP flexion with **MCP flexed**; flexes easily with MCP extended

![Bunnell intrinsic tightness test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6008-intrin.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Functional position of the hand (TO)

**Functional position (TO)** — **wrist 20° extension · MCP 70° flexion · fingers 10° flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: wrist neutral / flexed · MCP 20°

| Position of safe immobilisation (POSI) (TO) | Range |
|---|---|
| Wrist | **10–45° extension** |
| MCP | **60–90° flexion** |
| IP joints | Slight flexion → full extension |
| Thumb | **45° abduction** |

## X vs Y

| | **Intrinsic tightness** | **Extrinsic tightness** |
|---|---|---|
| PIP flexion with MCP **extended** | **↓** | Easy |
| PIP flexion with MCP **flexed** | Easy | **↓** |

## How it's asked

**"Positive intrinsic tightness test?"** → **↓ PIP flexion with MCP extended**

**"Numb both sides of thumb + radial index — nerve origin?"** → **one common branch off median nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: radial nerve

**"Digital nerve relation to artery in the finger?"** → **volar** (dorsal in palm)

**"Functional position of wrist and hand?"** → **wrist 20° extension, MCP 70° flexion, fingers 10° flexion** (TO)

**"Clunk in ulnar deviation under axial load?"** → **midcarpal instability (Lichtman)**

**"Piano key sign?"** → **DRUJ instability**

**"Gamekeeper's positive threshold?"** → **>30° laxity**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# PIN Compression Syndrome

*Hand · 64 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — compressive neuropathy of **PIN** (deep motor radial n.) → extensor compartment weakness, **no sensory loss**

**Incidence** — ~3/100,000/yr; manual labourers, ♂, bodybuilders

**Causes** — repetitive pronosupination microtrauma · trauma (Monteggia, radial head) · **space-occupying (lipoma = commonest lesion, ganglion)** · **RA radiocapitellar synovitis** · iatrogenic

### Anatomy – arcade of Frohse, innervated muscles & recovery order

**Origin** — radial n. splits at **anterior radiocapitellar joint**, just proximal to supinator

**Course** — between 2 heads of **supinator**, around **radial neck**, over APL origin → posterior interosseous membrane → **floor of 4th compartment** (dorsal wrist capsule sensation)

**Cutaneous** — **none**

| PIN innervates | Not PIN |
|---|---|
| **Supinator, EDC, EDM, ECU, APL, EPB, EPL, EIP** (± ECRB) | **ECRL, BR** (± ECRB) = radial n. proper; **APB** = median recurrent motor |

**EMG fibrillations** — **EPL / supinator / APL**\
✗ Wrong options: any set with ECRL, BR, APB

**Last to recover** — **EIP** (most distal)

| 5 sites of compression | Note |
|---|---|
| Fibrous bands anterior to radiocapitellar jt | Between brachialis & BR |
| **Leash of Henry** | Recurrent radial vessels at radial neck |
| **ECRB** edge | Medio-proximal |
| **Arcade of Frohse** | Proximal edge superficial supinator — **commonest spontaneous site** |
| Distal supinator edge | |

**Not lateral intermuscular septum** — compresses **radial nerve** proper, not PIN

![Radial tunnel compression sites](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6023-radial-tunnel-syndrome-moved.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — insidious; forearm/wrist pain (site-dependent; just distal to lateral epicondyle = arcade); **weak finger MCP, thumb, wrist extension**

**Wrist** — extends with **radial deviation** (ECRL intact, ECU lost); can't extend in neutral/ulnar deviation

**Thumb** — **can't extend thumb** (EPL)

**Preserved** — PIP extension (intrinsics, ulnar) · thumb flexion (AIN) · sensation

**Resisted supination** — ↑ pain

**Tenodesis** — **normal**\
✗ Wrong options: tendon rupture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray / MRI** — not routinely needed; **MRI** to locate a **mass** + plan resection

**EMG** — level of compression, rule out other neuropathy

### Differential – radial tunnel syndrome & rheumatoid extensor rupture

| Condition | Key finding |
|---|---|
| **PIN syndrome** | Pain distal to lateral epicondyle (mobile wad) + **extensor weakness** + **intact sensation** |
| **Radial tunnel syndrome** | Mobile wad pain, **no motor deficit** |
| **RA extensor tendon rupture** | **Abnormal tenodesis** |
| **RA PIN palsy** | **Peri-elbow synovitis** → normal tenodesis, can't initiate or hold extension |
| **Sagittal band rupture** | Can't initiate extension from fist but **can maintain** after passive extension |
| Wartenberg | Superficial radial n. → dorsal thumb numbness, normal motor |
| C7 radiculopathy | Sensory loss, triceps + wrist flexor weakness |
| Intersection syndrome | 1st/2nd compartment crossing pain |

**Others** — brachial plexus, peripheral neuropathy

## 5 Management

```
PIN compression
├─ All → rest, splint, NSAIDs, activity modification
│      └─ tender distal to lat epicondyle, no mass → injection 3–4 cm distal
├─ Mass on imaging → SURGICAL DECOMPRESSION
└─ Symptoms >3 mo despite nonop → SURGICAL DECOMPRESSION
```

**Approach** — **anterolateral elbow** (commonest) or posterior

**Release** — brachialis–BR bands → leash of Henry → ECRB edge → arcade of Frohse + distal supinator

**Outcomes** — variable; spontaneous motor recovery **75–97%** (non-traumatic); improves up to **18 mo**

## 6 Complications

**Neglected** — fibrosis of PIN muscles → **tendon transfers**

**Chronic pain**

## How it's asked

**"EMG fibrillations in PIN compression"** → **EPL / supinator / APL**

**"Commonest site of spontaneous PIN entrapment"** → **arcade of Frohse**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral intermuscular septum

**"Lesion at arcade of Frohse — exam finding"** → **can't extend thumb**\
✗ Wrong options: can't flex thumb · PIP extension lost

**"Last muscle to recover"** → **EIP**

**"RA, can't extend fingers, normal tenodesis, wrist radially deviates"** → **peri-elbow synovitis compressing PIN**

**"Exam corroborating PIN syndrome"** → **pain distal to lateral epicondyle, extensor weakness, intact sensation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pisiform Fracture

*Hand · 65 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & anatomy

**Incidence** — **<1% of carpal fractures**; rare, often missed

**Associated** — **50% isolated**, 50% with other carpal or distal radius fractures

**Pisiform** — pea-shaped **sesamoid within FCU**; most **ulnar & palmar** bone of proximal row; stabilises ulnar column (prevents triquetral subluxation)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **direct impact / FOOSH**; or sudden **FCU contraction**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **ulnar-sided wrist pain** after fall, ↓ grip

**Exam** — **hypothenar tenderness** + swelling; rule out other carpal / DR injuries

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | PA, lateral, ER oblique, carpal tunnel; **best seen on ER oblique or carpal tunnel view** (routine views miss it) |
| **CT** | Delineates pattern, guides treatment; shows nonunion |
| MRI | Suspected fracture with negative X-rays → **marrow oedema** |

![Pisiform fracture on CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6112-fracture.jpg)

**Differential** — hamate body fracture · **hook of hamate fracture** · FCU tendonitis

## 5 Management

```
Pisiform fracture
├─ Acute → SHORT ARM CAST 30° FLEXION + ULNAR DEVIATION, 6–8 WK
└─ Severely displaced symptomatic / painful nonunion → PISIFORMECTOMY
```

### Symptomatic nonunion – pisiform excision

**Cast position** — **30° wrist flexion + ulnar deviation**, 6–8 wk (relaxes FCU)

**Painful nonunion / pisotriquetral incongruity / arthritis** — **pisiform excision**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF pisiform · hook of hamate excision · more splinting · SL repair · LT fusion

**Outcome** — reliable pain relief; **no loss of wrist flexion strength**, grip or motion (75% complete relief)

## 6 Complications

**List** — malunion · **nonunion** · chronic ulnar wrist pain · ↓ grip

## X vs Y

| | Pisiform fracture | Hook of hamate fracture |
|---|---|---|
| Best view | ER oblique / carpal tunnel | Carpal tunnel / CT |
| Symptomatic nonunion | **Pisiform excision** | Hook excision |
| Bone | Sesamoid in FCU | Radial border of Guyon's canal |

## How it's asked

**"Ulnar wrist pain 6 mo after fall, pisiform nonunion on CT — treatment?"** → **excision of pisiform**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF pisiform

**"Hypothenar pain 9 mo, splint failed, displaced pisiform fracture?"** → **pisiform excision**

**"Best view?"** → **ER oblique / carpal tunnel view**

**"Cast position?"** → **30° flexion + ulnar deviation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Poland Syndrome

*Hand · 66 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Triad** — **unilateral chest wall hypoplasia (absent sternocostal head of pectoralis major)** + ipsilateral hand/forearm hypoplasia + **symbrachydactyly**

**Incidence** — **1 in 32,000** live births; **♂ 3×**; **right side ~75%**; sporadic (no family history)

**Cause** — **subclavian artery hypoplasia / disruption in utero**

**Syndactyly link** — Poland = **10% of syndactyly** cases

### Associations – syndactyly & absent pectoralis major

**Most common hand malformation** — **symbrachydactyly** = short central digits (absent/short **middle phalanx**) + syndactyly ± nubbins

![Symbrachydactyly](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6077-symbrachy.jpg)

**Syndactyly type** — **simple complete** of short digits (Orthobullets) · usually **simple incomplete** (Ireland) — read the options; can involve all digits ± thumb

| Syndactyly associated | Not associated |
|---|---|
| **Poland** (chest wall + limb hypoplasia) | **Tay-Sachs** |
| **Apert** (AD, craniosynostosis, complex syndactyly) | |
| **Holt-Oram** (heart–hand) | |
| **Carpenter** (acrocephaly) | |

**Not seen in Poland** — **polydactyly**, **macrodactyly**, **lower-limb** anomalies (pes planus, accessory navicular)

## 3 Presentation

**Chest** — hypoplasia/absence pec major, pec minor, deltoid, serratus anterior, external oblique, latissimus; **breast/nipple aplasia**; hypoplastic ribs/scapula; **Sprengel**; scoliosis; **dextrocardia**; renal/GU anomalies

**Hand / forearm** — syndactyly, hypoplastic/absent MC or phalanges, absent flexors/extensors, carpal coalition, **radioulnar synostosis** (no forearm rotation), nail agenesis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CT chest** — **absent pectoralis major** / unilateral chest wall hypoplasia

**X-ray hand** — hypoplastic digits, short/absent middle phalanges, malformed MCs, absent carpal ossification

![CT chest: absent pectoralis major](../../MCQ/attachments/q214241-c-fig-c-chest-in-poland.jpg)

**✗ CT distractors** — **pectus excavatum** (Marfan), **pectus carinatum**, rib osteochondroma

**Classification (Catena)** — severity ↑ with more proximal upper-limb involvement

## 5 Management

**Syndactyly release** — most patients, **early (by ~1 y)**; some need revision releases or 3-finger hand

**Technique** — complete release → **skin graft** needed; **one side of a digit at a time** (vascular safety); local commissure flap + interdigitating **zigzag dorsal/palmar flaps**

**Other** — **first web release** (opposition), toe-to-hand transfer; chest wall reconstruction / muscle transfer near **sexual maturity**

**Outcome** — function improves but **hand stays hypoplastic**; deficits persist vs idiopathic syndactyly (Kramer)

## 6 Complications

**After release** — skin graft failure, excessive tension, poor flap planning, **digital artery injury**, **web creep**, nail deformity

## X vs Y

| | **Poland** | **Apert** |
|---|---|---|
| Syndactyly | **Simple**, short central digits | **Complex**, bilateral |
| Other | Absent pec major, unilateral | **Craniosynostosis**, AD |
| Side | Unilateral (R) | Bilateral |

## How it's asked

**"Syndactyly associated EXCEPT?"** → **Tay-Sachs**\
✗ Wrong options: Apert, Poland, Holt-Oram, Carpenter

**"Hand malformation most associated with Poland?"** → **symbrachydactyly**\
✗ Wrong options: Apert complex syndactyly · constriction band · radial club hand · thumb polydactyly

**"Absent pec major in 3-year-old — associated?"** → **syndactyly**\
✗ Wrong options: polydactyly · macrodactyly · foot anomalies

**"Symbrachydactyly + forearm lacks rotation — which CT?"** → **unilateral absent pec major / chest wall hypoplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: pectus excavatum/carinatum

**"Symbrachydactyly X-ray — also found?"** → **absence of pectoralis major**\
✗ Wrong options: craniosynostosis · Lisch nodules · ash-leaf · leukocoria

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Polydactyly of Hand

*Hand · 67 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — extra digit; **most common congenital hand malformation**; diagnosis clinical

| Form | Digit | Ethnicity |
|---|---|---|
| **Preaxial** | Thumb (radial) | **Caucasian** > African American |
| **Postaxial** | Small finger (ulnar) | **10× African American** (1.2% vs 0.09%) |
| Central | Index/long/ring | Often with **syndactyly** |

### Genes & associations

**Preaxial** — usually **unilateral, sporadic**; ✓ M > F; **Type VII (triphalangeal)** → **Holt-Oram, Fanconi anaemia**, Blackfan-Diamond, imperforate anus, cleft palate, tibial defects

**Preaxial spectrum** — triphalangeal thumb, preaxial polysyndactyly, **mirror hand** = AP-axis limb-bud error

**Postaxial** — **autosomal dominant** (African American); sex-linked recessive modifier hypothesised

**Postaxial in Caucasian** — complex genetics → work up for **chondroectodermal dysplasia / Ellis-van Creveld**

**Pollex abductus** — abnormal **EPL–FPL connection**, ~20% hypoplastic/duplicated thumbs; abducted digit + **absent IP crease**

**Look-alikes** — constriction band = pseudosyndactyly (post-formation injury) · syndactyly → **Apert, Poland** · macrodactyly → **NF, Klippel-Trenaunay-Weber**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Wassel classification (preaxial)

| Type | Level | Image |
|---|---|---|
| I | Bifid distal phalanx | ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-wassel-deformity.jpg) |
| **II** | Duplicated distal phalanx (**2nd commonest, 15%**) | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-wasseltypeii.jpg) |
| III | Bifid proximal phalanx | ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-capture-type-3.jpg) |
| **IV** | **Duplicated proximal phalanx (commonest, 43%)** | ![Type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-type-4.jpg) |
| V | Bifid metacarpal | |
| VI | Duplicated metacarpal | ![Type VI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-type-6.jpg) |
| VII | **Triphalangia** | ![Type VII](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6079-type-7.jpg) |

### Postaxial classification

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Well-formed digit | Formal reconstruction (type 2 combination): **keep radial digit**, reconstruct collaterals from ulnar remnant |
| **B** | Rudimentary skin tag | **Tie off in nursery / amputate <1 y** |

## 5 Management

### Preaxial (thumb) duplication – Wassel classification & reconstruction

**Goals** — thumb **80% of contralateral size**; resect smaller (usually **radial**) thumb; preserve/reconstruct **UCL (ulnar collateral)** for pinch; one-stage

| Procedure | Wassel type | Technique |
|---|---|---|
| **Bilhaut-Cloquet** (type 1 combination) | **I, II, III** | Remove central bone + nail, join halves; **~20% late deformity** (stiffness, angulation, growth arrest, nail) |
| Type 2 combination | **III, IV** (V–VI need intrinsic/collateral transfer) | Keep one skeleton + nail, augment with soft tissue, **ablate radial** digit |
| Type 3 combination (on-top plasty) | V, VI, VII | Segmental distal transfer |
| Triphalangeal (VII) | VII | Excise vs fuse extra phalanx |

**Equal-size type IV** — excise **radial** thumb → keep ulnar thumb's **MCP UCL**

**Ulnar-side scar after resection** — **ulnar (valgus) MCP instability**, weak pinch\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal/volar/radial instability · EPL loss

### Central polydactyly

**Rx** — **early** osteotomy + ligament reconstruction → prevent angular growth deformity

![Postaxial polydactyly](../../MCQ/attachments/q219005-a-postaxial-polydactyl.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | Preaxial | Postaxial |
|---|---|---|
| Ethnicity | Caucasian | **African American** |
| Genetics | Sporadic (VII syndromic) | **AD** |
| Syndrome work-up | Triphalangeal → Holt-Oram, Fanconi | **Caucasian** → **Ellis-van Creveld** |
| Excise | **Radial** thumb | **Ulnar** digit |

## How it's asked

**"Bilhaut-Cloquet indicated for which Wassel type?"** → **Type II** (I–III)\
✗ Wrong options: IV–VII

**"Which image is preaxial?"** → **duplicated thumb**

**"Ulnar-side thumb resection scar, weak pinch — finding?"** → **ulnar instability of thumb MCP** (UCL)

**"Caucasian with this anomaly — work up for Ellis-van Creveld"** → **postaxial polydactyly**

**"More common in African Americans"** → **postaxial polydactyly**

**"Inheritance of postaxial polydactyly"** → **autosomal dominant**

**"Commonest Wassel type"** → **IV**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Preiser's Disease (Scaphoid AVN)

*Hand · 68 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **idiopathic AVN of scaphoid**; Preiser 1910; not fracture

**Epidemiology** — rare, **<1/100,000/y**; mean age **45 y**

**Ranking** — scaphoid = **2nd commonest carpal AVN** after lunate (Kienböck)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — chronic, progressive **dorsoradial wrist pain**, atraumatic

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **sclerosis + fragmentation of proximal pole**, **no fracture**

**MRI** — complete vs partial involvement; STIR marrow oedema, cysts, partial collapse; contrast MRI classifications (Kalainov, Schmitt)

![Preiser's: proximal pole sclerosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6051-scaphoid.jpg)

### Herbert & Lanzetta classification (1994)

| Stage | X-ray |
|---|---|
| 1 | Normal X-ray, **+ bone scan** |
| 2 | ↑ density proximal pole, generalised osteoporosis |
| 3 | **Fragmentation** proximal pole ± fracture |
| 4 | **Carpal collapse** ± OA |

## 5 Management

```
Preiser's disease
├─ Young, Herbert 1–2, minimal sx → NSAIDs + IMMOBILISATION (effective 20%)
├─ Fails nonop, stage 1 → ARTHROSCOPIC RESECTION / drilling
├─ Stage 2 → VASCULARISED BONE GRAFT / revascularisation
└─ Stage 3 (collapse) → PRC or scaphoid excision + 4-corner fusion (salvage)
```

**Other options** — microfracture drilling, allograft replacement

## X vs Y

| | **Preiser's** | **Kienböck's** | Trapezium AVN |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bone | **Scaphoid** | **Lunate** | Trapezium |
| Frequency | 2nd commonest | Commonest | Exceedingly rare |

## How it's asked

**"Atraumatic radial wrist pain, scaphoid sclerosis/collapse on X-ray & MRI?"** → **Preiser's disease**\
✗ Wrong options: Kienböck's · acute SL injury · basilar thumb OA · trapezium AVN

**"Herbert stage 1?"** → **normal X-ray, positive bone scan**

**"Salvage for collapse?"** → **PRC or 4-corner fusion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pronator Syndrome

*Hand · 69 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — compressive neuropathy of **median nerve proper at the elbow / proximal forearm** (proximal median nerve entrapment, PMNE)

**Incidence** — **rare, <1/100,000/y**

**Demographics** — ♀ > ♂; **5th decade**

**Risk** — **well-developed forearm muscles** (weightlifters, climbers, MMA / jiu-jitsu); repetitive pronosupination

**Association** — **medial epicondylitis**

### Compression sites

| Site | Note | Provocative test |
|---|---|---|
| **Supracondylar process** | Osseous remnant distal humerus, **1%** | Elbow X-ray |
| **Ligament of Struthers** | Supracondylar process tip → medial epicondyle (✗ Wrong options: arcade of Struthers = ulnar) | — |
| **Lacertus fibrosus** (bicipital aponeurosis) | Biceps tendon → deep volar forearm fascia | **Resisted elbow flexion + supination** |
| **Between humeral & ulnar heads of pronator teres** | **Most common site** | **Resisted pronation with elbow extended** |
| **FDS aponeurotic arch** | Rare; FDS = medial epicondyle → volar P2 digits 2–5 | **Resisted FDS of middle finger** |
| Gantzer muscle (accessory FPL head) | Listed by Adler & Wolf (OB) | — |

**Pronator teres attachments** — humeral head **medial epicondyle** + ulnar head **coronoid** → **mid-lateral radial shaft**

![Compression sites of median nerve at elbow](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6020-ps-anatomy.jpg)

### Median–ulnar anastomoses (Berrettini)

| Anastomosis (TO) | Site | Fibres / direction | Clue |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Martin–Gruber** | Forearm, distal to medial epicondyle | **Median/AIN → ulnar**, motor | Intrinsics work despite ulnar lesion |
| **Marinacci** | Forearm | **Ulnar → median** ("reverse MG"); **rarest** | Ulnar stimulation at elbow → **larger CMAP** than at wrist |
| **Riche–Cannieu** | **Palm** | **Recurrent median ↔ deep ulnar**; motor ± sensory | Normal thenar function despite advanced CTS; all-ulnar hand |
| **Berrettini** | **Hand** | **Common digital nerves** of ulnar + median (sensory) | **Most frequent** anomaly |

![Berrettini anastomosis between common digital nerves](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733273-5.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Paraesthesia** — thumb, index, middle, radial ½ ring (as CTS), **worse with repetitive pronosupination**

**Differentiators from CTS** — **aching proximal volar forearm pain** · numbness of **palm/thenar eminence (palmar cutaneous branch**, arises 4–5 cm proximal to carpal tunnel) · **no night symptoms**

**Tinel** — **+ proximal volar forearm / medial antecubital fossa**; **– at wrist**; no Phalen/Durkan

**Motor** — weakness of median-innervated forearm + hand muscles (e.g. **FPL, FDP index, FCR**; trouble using a key)

**Other** — pain on palpation of lacertus fibrosus; **scratch collapse +**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **elbow films mandatory** → **supracondylar process**

**EMG/NCV** — usually **inconclusive/negative**; help exclude other sites or **double crush**

**Differentials** — AIN syndrome, CTS, pronator teres strain

![Supracondylar process on elbow X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6020-sp.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Pronator syndrome
├─ Mild–moderate → rest + splint (avoid forearm rotation) + NSAIDs 3–6 mo
└─ Failed 3–6 mo nonop → decompress median nerve at elbow, ALL 5 sites
       (≈80% relief; lacertus release possible under local anaesthesia)
```

**After failed CTR + forearm signs** — **decompress median nerve at elbow**\
✗ Wrong options: revision CTR · AIN decompression: AIN purely motor · ulnar decompression · more night bracing

## X vs Y

| | **Pronator syndrome** | **CTS** | **AIN syndrome** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory | Radial 3½ **+ palm/thenar eminence** | Radial 3½, not palm | **None** |
| Night sx | **No** | **Yes** | No |
| Tinel | **Proximal forearm** | Wrist | — |
| Pain | **Proximal volar forearm ache** | — | — |
| Worse with | **Pronosupination** | Wrist flexion | — |
| NCS | Often negative | Diagnostic | — |

| Nerve (OB distractors) | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Median proper** | **Pronator syndrome** source |
| AIN | Radial FDP, FPL, PQ; **no sensory** |
| PIN | Forearm extensors; no sensory |
| Recurrent motor median | OP, APB, superficial FPB |
| Ulnar | FCU, FDP ring/small, adductor pollicis, deep FPB, interossei, hypothenar |

## How it's asked

**"Failed CTR, no night sx, forearm pain, lacertus tender — next step?"** → **decompress median nerve at elbow**

**"Jiu-jitsu, numb radial digits worse with pronosupination, Tinel – at wrist — nerve?"** → **median nerve proper**

**"Pain with resisted pronation, elbow extended — structure's attachments?"** → **medial epicondyle, coronoid, radial shaft (pronator teres)**\
✗ Wrong options: FDS · lacertus · brachioradialis · TCL

**"Pain with resisted elbow flexion + supination?"** → **lacertus fibrosus**

**"Pain with resisted middle-finger FDS?"** → **FDS arch**

**"Connection between common digital nerves of median and ulnar?"** → **Berrettini** (TO)

**"Reverse Martin–Gruber?"** → **Marinacci** (TO)

**"Mandatory imaging?"** → **elbow X-ray (supracondylar process)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pyogenic Flexor Tenosynovitis

*Hand · 70 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2.5–9.4%** of hand infections

**Severity (TO)** — **most serious hand infection** → sheath gliding destruction, tendon necrosis, osteomyelitis, amputation

**Commonest digits (TO)** — **ring, middle, index**

**Risk factors** — **diabetes**, **IVDU**, immunocompromise

### Anatomy – sheaths & spaces

| Digit | Sheath extent |
|---|---|
| Index, middle, ring | **DIP → just proximal to A1** (MC neck); **isolated** (✗ Wrong options: horseshoe) |
| **Thumb (FPL)** | IP → **radial bursa** |
| **Little** | DIP → **ulnar bursa** |

**Parona's space** — **between pronator quadratus fascia and FDP conjoined tendon sheaths** → radial–ulnar bursa communication → **horseshoe abscess** (thumb ↔ little finger)

![Flexor sheaths and bursae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6105-flexor-sheaths-of-the-hand.jpg)

### Microbiology – MRSA in IV drug users

| Organism | Setting |
|---|---|
| **Staph aureus** (40–75%) | **Most common** (gram + cocci; not gram + bacilli) |
| **MRSA** (29%) | **IV drug users** |
| Staph epidermidis, β-haemolytic strep, Pseudomonas | Skin flora |
| Mixed / gram-negative | Immunocompromised |
| **Eikenella** | **Human bite** / fight bite; IVDU licking needles |
| **Pasteurella multocida** | Animal bite |

**Distractors** — HSV → **herpetic whitlow** (vesicles, healthcare workers, **do not debride**, acyclovir) · Candida → **chronic paronychia** · E. coli → uncommon

## 2 Mechanism

**Route** — **penetrating trauma** (commonest) · direct spread from felon, septic joint, deep space · rarely haematogenous

## 3 Presentation

**Timing** — presents **24–48 h** after onset; palmar aspect of one digit

### Kanavel signs & diagnosis

| Kanavel sign | Note |
|---|---|
| **Flexed (partially flexed) resting posture** | Not hyperextended |
| **Tenderness along entire flexor sheath** | not extensor sheath (TO) · not localised to A1 (= trigger finger) |
| **Pain on passive extension** | **Most constant + first sign** (TO) |
| **Fusiform swelling** | 4th sign **added later, not by Kanavel** (TO) · not discrete swelling |

**NOT a Kanavel sign** — **pain on passive flexion** (TO ×2, OB) · tenderness over extensor sheath (TO ×2)

**✗ Wrong option** — inability to flex DIP → **FDP rupture**

![Kanavel signs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6105-pft1.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical (Kanavel)**; labs may show normal WBC with mildly ↑ ESR/CRP

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | Not required; exclude **foreign body** |
| MRI | **not needed for diagnosis**; cannot separate infective vs inflammatory; extent if equivocal |

**Differential** — felon, cellulitis, deep space infection, collar-button abscess, herpetic whitlow, septic arthritis, gout

## 5 Management

```
Suspected PFT
├─ Early (<24–48 h), no purulence → ADMIT, IV ANTIBIOTICS, SPLINT, ELEVATE
│      └─ no improvement in 24 h → I&D
└─ Late presentation / purulence / ischaemia → URGENT I&D + CULTURE-SPECIFIC IV ANTIBIOTICS
       ├─ Early disease → CLOSED CATHETER IRRIGATION (A1 → A5)
       └─ Advanced / necrosis → OPEN I&D (BRUNER / MIDLATERAL)
```

### Surgical drainage – indications, technique & stiffness risk

**Best outcome predictor** — **early IV antibiotics** correlate most closely with good outcome

**Empirical (OB)** — **vancomycin + piperacillin/tazobactam**

**Surgery indicated by** — **delayed presentation (>24–48 h)**, purulence, failed 24 h antibiotics\
✗ Wrong options: immunocompromise · age · malignancy · all four Kanavel signs alone

**Closed irrigation** — catheter **proximal to A1** → out at **A5 / DIP**; thumb exits just distal to carpal tunnel; little finger with ulnar bursa → 2nd catheter A1 → wrist

**Open vs closed** — **equally effective**; open → trend to more complications/reoperation; closed largely replaced open\
✗ Wrong options: two-incision superior

**Incision error** — **volar midline incision perpendicular to creases** → **flexion contracture** → Z-plasty revision (✓ Bruner zig-zag, ✓ midlateral)

**A1 release** — does not reduce contracture risk

**Poor-outcome risk factors** — **age >43 y**, **DM**, PVD, renal failure, **subcutaneous purulence**, **digital ischaemia**, **polymicrobial** infection

### Spread – thenar space & Parona's space

**Thumb PFT spreads first to** — **thenar space**\
✗ Wrong options: central · hypothenar · carpal tunnel (not a palmar space; posterior adductor = secondary)

**Palmar spaces** — thenar, central (midpalmar), hypothenar

**Thumb + little finger + distal forearm swollen** — **Parona's space** → horseshoe abscess

## 6 Complications

**List** — **stiffness** · tendon/pulley rupture · spread · soft-tissue loss · **osteomyelitis** · amputation

## X vs Y

| | PFT | Herpetic whitlow | Felon | Trigger finger |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Whole volar digit | Fingertip vesicles | Pulp abscess | A1 nodule |
| Posture | Flexed | Normal | Normal | Locking |
| Treatment | **I&D + IV abx** | **Acyclovir; do not debride** | Incise | Injection / A1 release |

## How it's asked

**"Kanavel signs seen in?"** → **tenosynovitis** (TO)

**"Not a Kanavel sign?"** → **pain on passive flexion** (TO)

**"All true except?"** → **tenderness over extensor sheath** (TO)

**"Most constant / earliest sign?"** → **pain on passive extension** (TO)

**"IVDU index finger PFT — true statement?"** → **early IV antibiotics correlate with good outcome**\
✗ Wrong options: index → horseshoe · MRI confirms

**"Purulence found at surgery — exam finding?"** → **pain on passive extension**

**"IVDU hand infection — commonest pathogen?"** → **MRSA**

**"Advanced PFT with ischaemia — next step?"** → **open I&D**

**"Contracture after I&D — technical error?"** → **volar midline incision perpendicular to creases**

**"Indication for urgent surgery?"** → **delayed presentation**

**"Risk of stiffness/amputation?"** → **age >43**

**"Thumb PFT spreads first to?"** → **thenar space**

**"Thumb PFT + swollen little finger + forearm?"** → **Parona's space** (PQ fascia–FDP sheaths)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Quadriga Effect

*Hand · 71 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Pathoanatomy

**Definition** — **active flexion lag of fingers adjacent** to a digit with injured/repaired **FDP**; injured finger reaches palm

**Common belly** — FDP **long, ring, little** share a common muscle belly + distal cross-connections (forearm, carpal tunnel sheaths) → excursion = shortest tendon (TO)

**Index FDP** — often **independent belly** → can be shortened without quadriga

**FDS** — **separate muscle bellies** → FDS repair/advancement **no quadriga**

**Name** — reins of 4 chariot horses: one tight rein → slack in the others

**Zone** — most quadriga injuries **zone I**

## 2 Mechanism

### FDP over-advancement or tethering – quadriga

**Causes** — **FDP over-advancement/over-tensioning** at repair · **adhesions** (Neu: mostly after crush) · FDP **retraction** · **"over-the-top" FDP repair / flexor–extensor tenodesis after DIP disarticulation** · too-short tendon graft

**Threshold** — **>1 cm** advancement (Orthobullets; Malerich: 1 cm tolerable, **1.5 cm → ulnar 3 digits lose 40° flexion**) · OB7866 option states **>1.5 cm** → risk of quadriga — read the options

**FDP avulsion reinserted (button) despite proximal migration** → tight, adhesed, improperly tensioned FDP → **quadriga** (TO)

![Quadriga: adjacent fingers cannot reach palm](../../MCQ/attachments/q211215-a-quad.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — can't close fingers tightly (e.g. over cricket ball, TO); **weak grip**

**Exam** — fist: **fingers adjacent to injured digit don't reach palm**; **full passive motion**; ↓ grip

**Radiographs** — usually not required

## 5 Management

```
Quadriga
├─ Mild, QoL unaffected → OBSERVATION
└─ Severe, function-limiting → RELEASE FDP of injured digit
       (or tendon lengthening / tenolysis / release of intertendinous connections)
```

**FDP release** — 85% resolution (Neu)

**Prevention** — don't advance FDP **>1 cm**

## X vs Y

| | **Quadriga** | **Lumbrical plus** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | FDP **too short/tethered** (over-advanced, tenodesed, adhesions) | FDP **lax/detached** (retracts after amputation at/proximal to DIP, long graft) |
| Finding | Injured finger reaches palm; **adjacent fingers lag** | **Paradoxical PIP/IP extension** on attempted fist |

| Distractor | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| Elson test (DIP hyperextends with PIP 90°) | Central slip injury |
| Can't actively extend MCP but holds it once placed | Sagittal band rupture |
| Intrinsic tightness | ↓ PIP flexion with MCP extended |
| AIN injury | Thumb IP + index DIP flexion lost |
| Ulnar nerve injury | Intrinsic minus / claw ulnar 2 digits |
| FDP repair rupture | Affected finger can't flex, not adjacent |

## How it's asked

**"FDP avulsion reinserted despite proximal migration, can't grip cricket ball at 6 mo?"** → **quadriga effect** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: lumbrical plus · swan neck · intrinsic tightness

**"Ring FDP repaired, long & small can't flex fully — intraoperative cause?"** → **overtensioning of FDP**

**"Shortened 12 mm, adjacent fingers can't reach palm, full passive motion — factor?"** → **common flexor muscle belly**

**"Injured finger flexes fully, others don't — which injury?"** → **FDP laceration with repair**\
✗ Wrong options: FDS repair · nail bed · P1 fracture · dorsal MCP dislocation

**"Shortening that causes it?"** → **2 cm**\
✗ Wrong options: 0.5 cm

**"FDP advanced to stump after DIP amputation; only ring tip reaches palm?"** → **quadriga**\
✗ Wrong options: lumbrical plus

**"Flexor–extensor tenodesis at revision amputation → 3-mo finding?"** → **can't make full fist, adjacent fingers ↓ ROM**

**"True about FDP avulsion?"** → **advancement >1.5 cm risks quadriga**\
✗ Wrong options: timing irrelevant to retraction · chronic → tenodese FDP to FDS · repair method irrelevant

**"Treatment of symptomatic quadriga?"** → **release FDP of injured digit**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radial Clubhand (radial deficiency)

*Hand · 72 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition (TO)** — longitudinal **failure of formation** along pre-axial (radial) border; hand **perpendicular** to forearm + **absent thumb**

**Incidence** — 1 / 100,000 (Orthobullets) · 1 / 50,000–100,000 · 1 / 30,000–100,000 (OB)

**Sex** — ♂ predilection (**3:2**)

**Bilateral** — **50–72%** (Orthobullets) · **38–58%** (OB)

**Syndromic** — **30–44%** associated with syndromes

### Aetiology – apical ectodermal ridge defect

**Embryology** — **defect of apical ectodermal ridge**\
✗ Wrong options: teratogen · AD · FGF excess (OB)

**Isolated RCH** — **spontaneous mutation** (most common cause; vs cleft hand, Madelung, syndactyly, symphalangism = AD)

**Teratogens (TO)** — **thalidomide, valproic acid, phenobarbital** (conflicts with OB "not linked to specific teratogens")

### Genes & lab markers

| Condition | Gene / inheritance |
|---|---|
| **Radial clubhand** | **Sonic hedgehog** (TO, Orthobullets) |
| **Holt-Oram** | **AD**, **TBX5** chr **12q24.1** (TO: "TXBS", chr 12); >70% mutated |
| **TAR** | **AR** |
| **Fanconi anaemia** | **AR**; impaired DNA-damage response; **↑ chromosomal breakage** |
| **Madelung (TO)** | **SHOX** (pseudo-autosomal region of sex chromosomes); **Léri-Weill dyschondrosteosis** |
| Congenital pseudarthrosis tibia (TO) | **NF1** |
| Blount's (TO) | No named gene |

### Associated syndromes & work-up (TAR, Fanconi, Holt-Oram, VACTERL)

**Mandatory screen** — **CBC + renal US + echocardiogram**

**NOT associated** — **osteogenesis imperfecta**

| Syndrome | Key features | Test |
|---|---|---|
| **TAR** | **AR**; **thumb PRESENT**; bilateral absent radii; platelets <50/nL, **transient** (improves in 1st year); lower limbs involved; NICU | **CBC**; platelet transfusion |
| **Fanconi** | **AR**; **aplastic anaemia** at **6–9 y** (1st decade); **most common inherited aplastic anaemia**; **lethal**; ↑ malignancy (**AML**, solid tumours); café-au-lait, microcephaly, triangular face, short stature, abnormal kidneys, ↓ fertility (TO); **↑ MCV, ↓ platelets** (TO) | **CBC, smear, chromosomal breakage** (diepoxybutane / mitomycin C) → **bone marrow transplant** |
| **Holt-Oram** | **AD**, **TBX5**; **ASD/VSD** (75%); heart block/conduction disease; absent/triphalangeal thumb → phocomelia; **radioulnar synostosis**; abnormal carpal bone in **all**; clavicle/scapula abnormality (TO) | **Echocardiogram** |
| **VACTERL** | Vertebral, Anal, Cardiac, TE fistula, oEsophageal atresia, Renal, Limb (TO: + radial) | **Spine X-rays**, **bronchoscopy** (TEF) |
| VATER | Vertebral, Anal, TE fistula, oEsophageal atresia, Radial, Renal (TO) | — |
| Heart-hand II (Tabatznik) | AD; short thumb DP, **arrhythmias** (✗ Wrong options: malformation), no gene | — |

**Life-threatening AR condition** — **Fanconi** → chromosomal breakage before pancytopenia

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — hand perpendicular to forearm, **absent thumb**, short forearm; **examine elbow** (flexion, biceps)

**Thumb predicts radius** — **thumb hypoplasia severity = greatest predictor of RLD severity** → Blauth V thumb → **Bayne-Klug IV (absent radius)**

**Subtle (TO)** — mild hypoplastic thumb, missing/fused carpals · **severe** — total radial absence, short humerus, hypoplastic glenoid

![Radial clubhand](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6067-case-a-clinical-photo-kosin-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — radius partly/entirely absent, thumb often absent; ulna may be curved (TO)

**Carpal ossification** — **capitate 1–2 mo, lunate 2–4 mo** → no carpal ossification = <12 mo old

**TAR X-ray** — absent radius **with thumb**

![TAR: absent radius, thumb present](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6067-tar-xray-colorado-moved.jpg)

### Bayne & Klug classification

| Type | Orthobullets | TO | Modified (OB / TO table) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | — | — | Thumb absent/hypoplastic, **normal radius** |
| **I** | Deficient distal radial epiphysis | Radius **shorter than ulna**, distal physis present | **>2 mm short**; ± synostosis / radial head dislocation |
| **II** | Deficient distal + proximal epiphyses | Small, thin; **no growth plate** | **Hypoplasia proximal + distal** |
| **III** | **Present proximally** (partial aplasia) | Small radial segment only | **Absent distal physis** + proximal hypoplasia |
| **IV** | **Completely absent** (total aplasia) | Completely absent, ulna curved | **Absence proximal + distal** |

**Type IV** — **most common** (and most severe); Heikel type 4 most common (TO)

![Bayne & Klug](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6067-classification-def.jpg)

### Blauth thumb hypoplasia (TO)

| Type | Findings (TO) | Treatment (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| I | Minor generalised hypoplasia | **No surgery** / 1st web lengthening |
| II | **Absent thenar intrinsics**, narrow 1st web, **UCL insufficiency** | **Opponensplasty + UCL reconstruction** |
| IIIA | II + extrinsic abnormalities, **stable CMC** | **Opponensplasty + UCL reconstruction** |
| **IIIB** | **Unstable CMC** (Manske) | **Pollicization** |
| IIIC | Remnant MC head only (Buck-Gramcko) | Pollicization |
| IV | **Pouce flottant** (floating thumb) | **Pollicization** |
| V | **Aplasia** | **Pollicization** |

**Pollicization prerequisites (TO)** — **flexible, functional index finger** (side-to-side grip) · wrist oriented on forearm · **after 2 y**

## 5 Management

```
Radial clubhand
├─ Stiff elbow / no elbow flexion / biceps absent → OBSERVATION (radial deviation = reach to mouth)
├─ Mild (I–II) → STRETCHING + SPLINTING
└─ Severe (III–IV), good elbow flexion → CENTRALIZATION ± tendon transfers
       └─ thumb absent / IIIB–V → POLLICIZATION (later)
```

**Infant (TO)** — stretch & splint from early infancy; **above-elbow splint / serial casting 0–6 mo** · stretching + night splint 6–18 mo · operate **2–3 y** (TO)

**Avoid (TO)** — aggressive early surgery → **distal ulnar growth arrest**; address **fibrous anlage**

### Treatment – centralisation indications, contraindications & thumb reconstruction

**Centralization** — carpus on ulna → ↑ reach, wrist tendon balance, ± ↑ limb length

**Timing** — **6–12 mo** (Orthobullets, TO) · not before **12 mo** for anaesthesia safety (OB) · 2–3 y (TO age guide)

**Technique** — resect carpus, **shorten ECU**, ± ulnar angular osteotomy (**spare distal ulnar physis**); ± 2-stage with **distraction ex-fix**; then **tendon transfers**

**Current approach (TO)** — **bilobed flap + anlage resection + ulnar osteotomy + temporary longitudinal wire** carpus → ulna

**Others (TO)** — **radialization** (distraction, carpus on ulna, radial tendons transferred ulnarly), Ilizarov, **vascularised epiphyseal transfer** (MTPJ — OB)

**Thumb** — reconstruction ~**18 mo** (Orthobullets) · pollicization **after 2 y** (TO)

**Adolescents (TO)** — wrist flexion needed → **no wrist fusion**; otherwise **fusion + partial correction**

### Operative criteria

| Centralization | Factor |
|---|---|
| ✓ Indication | **Good elbow flexion + biceps intact**, types III/IV |
| Contraindication | **Lack of elbow flexion** (<90°) → can't reach mouth |
| Contraindication | **Older child with good function** |
| Contraindication | Elbow extension contracture relying on radial deviation (Orthobullets) |
| Contraindication | Proximate terminal condition · severe soft-tissue contracture |
| Relative contraindication | **Age <12 mo** (OB) |
| Not contraindications | Cardiac defects, ↓ grip, **unstable carpus**, scoliosis, TEF, imperforate anus, hypoplastic thumb |

## X vs Y

| | **Radial clubhand** | **TAR** | **Madelung** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thumb | **Absent** | **Present** | Normal |
| Gene | Sonic hedgehog / spontaneous | AR | **SHOX** |
| Blood | Fanconi: pancytopenia | **Thrombocytopenia (transient)** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Radial clubhand — associated EXCEPT"** → **osteogenesis imperfecta**

**"Tests to order"** → **CBC + renal US + echo**

**"Rule out AR lethal condition"** → **CBC, smear, chromosomal breakage (Fanconi)**

**"Absent radius, thumb present"** → **TAR**

**"ASD/VSD, TBX5, AD family history"** → **Holt-Oram**

**"Bilateral RLD + radioulnar synostosis + syndactyly — add which test?"** → **echocardiogram**

**"Congenital UL anomaly most often from spontaneous mutation"** → **radial clubhand**

**"Aetiology of isolated type IV"** → **apical ectodermal ridge defect**

**"Pollicization treatment of choice"** → **Blauth IIIB + flexible index finger** (TO)

**"Relative contraindication to centralization in infant (no carpal ossification)"** → **age**\
✗ Wrong options: limited elbow extension, cardiac defect

**"7-mo, good elbow flexion, has thumb — surgery?"** → **centralization ± tendon transfers**\
✗ Wrong options: pollicization · free fibula

**"Contraindication to centralization"** → **lack of elbow flexion**

**"Blauth V thumb — radius?"** → **absent proximal + distal (type IV)**

**"SHOX mutation"** → **Madelung** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radial Tunnel Syndrome

*Hand · 73 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — compressive neuropathy of **PIN** in the **proximal forearm (radial tunnel)** → **pain only**, no motor or sensory deficit

**Incidence** — rare, **~3/100,000/y**; ♂ > ♀

**Lateral epicondylitis** — hard to distinguish; **coexists in 5%**

**Risk** — constant prono-supination with 1 kg force, elbow 0–45° flexion

### Anatomy – radial tunnel & PIN

**Radial tunnel** — **~5 cm**; radiocapitellar joint → past proximal edge of supinator

| Boundary | Structure |
|---|---|
| Lateral | **Brachioradialis, ECRL, ECRB** (mobile wad) |
| Medial | **Biceps tendon, brachialis** |
| Floor | Radiocapitellar capsule |

**Nerve origin** — radial nerve ← **posterior cord**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral/medial cord · anterior division

**PIN course** — between **2 heads of supinator** · contacts radial neck · over APL origin · along posterior interosseous membrane

**PIN motor** — **ECRB** (often radial n. proper), **EDC, EDM, ECU**, supinator, APL, EPB, EPL, EIP

**PIN sensory** — **dorsal wrist capsule** via terminal branch in **floor of 4th extensor compartment**; not cutaneous

**Wrist extension in PIN palsy (TO)** — **radial deviation** (ECRL intact, ECU lost)

![PIN between supinator heads](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6024-radial-tunnel-lateral.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Diagnosis & compression site (arcade of Frohse, posterior cord origin)

| Compression site (proximal → distal) | Note |
|---|---|
| Fibrous bands **anterior to radiocapitellar joint** (between brachialis & BR) | |
| **Leash of Henry** (radial recurrent vessels fanning over PIN at radial neck) | |
| **Medial (medio-proximal) edge of ECRB** | |
| **Arcade of Frohse** = proximal aponeurotic edge of **superficial** supinator | **Most common** |
| **Distal edge of supinator** | |

**Arcade of Frohse naming (TO)** — compression here sometimes designated **PIN / supinator syndrome**

**NOT PIN sites (TO)** — **ligament of Struthers** (supracondylar process → medial epicondyle, <1%, **median** n.) · **Gantzer muscle**

**Tendinous supinator** — next to bone → does not compress PIN

**Pressures** — radial tunnel normal **50 mmHg** → **250 mmHg** with supinator stretch (forced wrist flexion + pronation)

![Compression sites](../../MCQ/attachments/q407-illa-radtunnelsitescompression.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **deep aching dorsoradial proximal forearm**, lateral elbow → wrist; ↑ rotation, lifting; ± night pain

**Weakness** — **due to pain**, not denervation (subtle at most)

**Tenderness** — mobile wad over supinator arch, **3–5 cm distal to lateral epicondyle** (~4 cm; TO: 5 cm)

**Sensory** — **none**; 1st dorsal web changes → think more proximal

| Provocative test | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Resisted long (3rd) finger extension** | Pain at radial tunnel |
| **Resisted supination** (elbow & wrist extended) | Pain |
| **Passive pronation + wrist flexion** | Pain (↑ pressure to 250 mmHg) |
| **Radial tunnel injection** | **PIN palsy + pain relief = diagnostic** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (history + exam)

**EMG/NCV** — **inconclusive / normal**: pain carried by **unmyelinated group IV (C) + small myelinated IIA** fibres; large myelinated fibres normal

**Diagnostic LA injection** — into point of tenderness; avoid spread to lateral epicondyle

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — usually **negative**; may show **denervation oedema/atrophy** supinator/extensors · thick ECRB edge · prominent leash of Henry · PIN swelling

**MRI other causes** — tumour, ganglion, radiocapitellar synovitis, bicipital bursitis, radial head fracture/dislocation

![Denervation oedema](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6024-edema.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Radial tunnel syndrome
├─ First line ≥1 year → activity modification + PT + splint/night brace + NSAIDs
│      └─ 2nd line → steroid injection (diagnostic + therapeutic)
└─ Refractory to exhaustive nonop → radial tunnel release
       (arcade of Frohse + distal supinator + fibrous bands over radiocapitellar joint)
```

### First-line treatment – therapy & activity modification

**First line** — **PT + activity modification** (± night bracing) **≥1 year**\
✗ Wrong options: injection first · surgery

**Avoid** — prolonged elbow extension + forearm pronation + wrist flexion

**Steroid injection** — **2nd line**; **70% improve at 6 wk**, **60% pain-free at 2 y**; TO: if associated lateral epicondylitis

### Radial tunnel release

**Releases** — **arcade of Frohse**, **distal edge of supinator**, fibrous bands over radiocapitellar joint

| Approach | Interval |
|---|---|
| Dorsal (Henry / Kaplan) | **ECRB–EDC** |
| Dorsal | **BR–ECRL** |
| Dorsal | **Transmuscular BR-splitting** |
| **Anterior** | **BR–biceps** → used for **mass on MRI** |

**Posterior approach** — preferred; safe access to supinator + distal edge

**Outcomes — conflict** — **50–90% good/excellent, "disappointing"**, max recovery **9–18 mo** (Orthobullets) · **70–90%** success (Orthobullets techniques) · "excellent in most" (TO)

| Lower success group | Success |
|---|---|
| Multiple entrapment neuropathies | 60% |
| **Concomitant lateral epicondylitis** | 40% |
| **Workers' compensation** | **30%** |

## X vs Y

| | **Radial tunnel** | **PIN syndrome** | **Wartenberg** | Lateral epicondylitis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | **Yes** | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Motor | **No** | **✓ finger/thumb/wrist ext weak** | No | No |
| Sensory | **No** | No | **✓ dorsoradial hand** (superficial radial) | No |
| Tenderness | **3–5 cm distal** to epicondyle | — | Distal forearm | **Directly over** epicondyle |
| EMG | Normal | Abnormal | — | Normal |

**Other mimics** — **intersection syndrome** (1st/2nd compartment, crepitus with wrist extension; ECRL/ECRB tenosynovitis) · **pronator syndrome** (numb radial 3 digits) · **AIN** (thumb IP flexion weak) · **C6–7 radiculopathy** (EDS may show denervation) · radiocapitellar arthritis

## How it's asked

**"PIN compressed at all EXCEPT"** → **ligament of Struthers** (TO)

**"Does NOT cause PIN entrapment"** → **Gantzer muscle** (TO)

**"Most common site of PIN compression"** → **arcade of Frohse (free proximal aponeurotic supinator margin)** (TO)

**"RTS — all true EXCEPT"** → **weakness of MCP extension** (TO)

**"RTS — NOT true"** → **motor weakness** (TO)

**"Pain 4 cm distal to lateral epicondyle, pain on resisted long finger extension"** → **radial tunnel syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral epicondylitis, intersection

**"Arcade of Frohse compression — symptom?"** → **dorsoradial forearm pain worse with passive pronation**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsoradial numbness = Wartenberg · thumb IP weakness = AIN

**"Nerve's origin in brachial plexus"** → **posterior cord**

**"Untreated, normal EMG — first line"** → **PT + activity modification**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · release

**"Diagnostic injection result"** → **PIN palsy + pain relief**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Radiocarpal Fracture Dislocation

*Hand · 74 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **total loss of contact between carpus and radius**

**Incidence** — **0.2%** of wrist injuries; ♂ > ♀; high energy

**Direction** — **dorsal > volar**

### Anatomy – radiocarpal ligaments

| Ligament | Attachment (radius) | Restrains |
|---|---|---|
| **Short radiolunate** | **Volar lunate facet** (volar-ulnar "critical corner") | **Volar translation** of carpus (primary) |
| **Radioscaphocapitate (RSC)** | **Radial styloid** | **Ulnar translation** |
| Ulnolunate, ulnotriquetral | Volar TFCC (→ base ulnar styloid) | — |

**Distal radius** — biconcave triangular surface; apex = radial styloid; base = sigmoid notch

![Volar wrist ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422863-ligaments-of-the-wrist.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Force** — high-energy shear / **rotation on hyperextended, pronated wrist** (falls from height, MVC)

**Associations** — open #; **radial styloid avulsion (RSC)**; **volar lunate facet # (short RL)**; ulnar styloid #; scaphoid/lunate/trapezium #; **median > ulnar nerve injury**; **irreducible DRUJ (soft-tissue interposition)**; SL/LT dissociation

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — deformity (dorsal/volar), swelling, **pale cold hand** (radial/ulnar artery occlusion common), numbness; note crepitus

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Look for |
|---|---|
| **PA** | Radiolunate alignment (normally **⅔ of lunate on radius**; complete disruption → **ulnar translation**), intercarpal widening, SL/LT dissociation, **broken Gilula arcs** |
| **Lateral** | Dorsal/volar dislocation; loss of lunate–radius colinearity |
| **Teardrop view** | **10° proximal tilt** on lateral → **lunate facet fracture** |
| CT | Comminution, articular depression |
| MRI | SL and LT integrity |

**Differentials** — perilunate/lunate dislocation, distal radius fracture

![Radiocarpal dislocation, lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422863-radiocarpal-dislocation.jpg)

### Moneim classification

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| 1 | Radiocarpal fracture-dislocation **without** intercarpal dissociation |
| 2 | **With** intercarpal dissociation |

### Dumontier classification

| Group | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Purely ligamentous** or small cortical avulsion off radius | ![Group 1](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422863-dupontier-group-1-lateral.jpg) |
| 2 | **Large radial styloid fragment** (≥⅓ scaphoid fossa) | ![Group 2](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422863-dumontier-group-2.jpg) |

## 5 Management

```
Radiocarpal fracture-dislocation
├─ Stable after closed reduction / unfit → cast up to 6 wk
└─ Irreducible / unstable after reduction → ORIF + radiocarpal pinning + ligament repair
       └─ still unstable → ex-fix / radiolunate pin 4–6 wk or dorsal bridge plate
```

### Operative technique – three-column fixation

| Column | Fixation |
|---|---|
| Radial (styloid) | K-wire / compression screw / plate; anchor repair if small/ligamentous |
| **Intermediate (lunate facet)** | **Screw or tension-band wire**; **repair short RL + RSC** |
| Ulnar (controversial) | DRUJ injury / persistent instability → ulnar styloid fixation, DRUJ pinning in **mid-supination** |

**Also** — decompress NV structures; volar ± dorsal approach

### Ligament injury – short radiolunate

**Loss of reduction after casting** — unfixed **volar lunate facet fragment** → **short radiolunate ligament** → volar carpal subluxation\
✗ Wrong options: SL · RSC · LT · ulnolunate

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Acute CTS** | Risk: delay → **CTR** |
| Stiffness | Loss **30–40%** of F/E arc; MUA, hardware removal |
| Post-traumatic OA | Non-anatomic articular reduction |
| Chronic radiocarpal instability | — |
| Late intercarpal disruption | Occult SL/LT injury |

## How it's asked

**"Carpus subluxes volarly after casting — ligament on displaced fragment?"** → **short radiolunate (volar lunate facet)**

**"Ligament restraining ulnar translation?"** → **RSC (radial styloid)**

**"View for lunate facet fracture?"** → **teardrop (10° proximal lateral)**

**"Commoner nerve injury?"** → **median**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Raynaud's Syndrome

*Hand · 75 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions & epidemiology

**Raynaud's** — exaggerated **vasoconstriction of digital arteries** to stimuli

**Raynaud's phenomenon / syndrome (secondary)** — **known underlying cause**

**Raynaud's disease (primary)** — **idiopathic**; young **premenopausal ♀ <40 y**

**Prevalence** — vasospastic disease **3–5%** of US population; **40–50%** develop painful digital ulcers

### Associations of Raynaud's phenomenon

| Condition | Raynaud's incidence |
|---|---|
| **Scleroderma** | **80–90%** |
| Dermatomyositis | 30% |
| SLE | 18–26% |
| RA | 11% |
| **CREST** | Calcinosis, Raynaud's, oesophageal dysmotility, sclerodactyly, telangiectasia |
| Neurovascular compression | Thoracic outlet syndrome |
