## 2 Mechanism

**Triggers** — **cold** or **sympathetic stimuli** (pain, **emotional stress**)

**Triphasic colour change** — **white** (vasospasm) → **blue** (cyanosis, venous stasis) → **red** (rebound hyperaemia) → dysaesthesia

![White phase](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6098-white-discoloration.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

| | **Raynaud's disease** (primary) | **Raynaud's phenomenon** (secondary) |
|---|---|---|
| Age / sex | **<40, young ♀** | **>40**, occasional ♀ predominance |
| Distribution | **Bilateral** | **Asymmetric**, distal digits |
| Progression | **Slow** | **Rapid** |
| Pulses | Present | **Often absent** |
| Trophic changes / ulcer / gangrene | Rare | **Frequent** |
| Allen test | Normal | **Abnormal** |
| Labs | Normal | Often abnormal |
| Angiography / microangiology | Normal | Often abnormal |

![Digital gangrene](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6098-gangrene.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – Allen & Brown (Raynaud's disease)

| Criterion |
|---|
| Intermittent attacks of acral discoloration |
| **Bilateral** involvement |
| No clinical arterial occlusion |
| Gangrene / trophic changes **rare** |
| Symptoms **>2 years** |
| ♀ predominance |
| No other disease explaining findings |

**Differentiate by** — age · gender · time course of progression · severity of exam

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **normal, not indicated**

**Angiography** — secondary: often abnormal (e.g. incomplete ulnar artery obstruction)

**Doppler** — documents ↑ digital flow after BTX

## 5 Management

```
Raynaud's
├─ First line → stop smoking + avoid cold + biofeedback + medication
│      (CCB, topical nitrate, ASA, sildenafil …)
├─ Failed medical Rx, ischaemia/pain (± ulcer) → botulinum toxin A (off-label)
└─ Severe / impending gangrene / ischaemic ulcer → periarterial (digital) sympathectomy ± arterial reconstruction
       └─ segmental occlusion of one major artery → microvascular reconstruction
```

### Medical management

**Critical** — **smoking cessation + avoid cold**; thermal biofeedback; stress, protective garments

**Commonest drug** — **calcium channel blocker** (nifedipine; oral only)

**Other drugs** — ASA, **sildenafil** / PDE inhibitors, intra-arterial reserpine, dipyridamole, pentoxifylline, prostaglandins, ACE-I/ARB, TCAs, SSRIs, α-antagonists

### Topical vasodilator mechanism

**Topical nitrate** — **only topical drug** used; **↑ cGMP in vascular smooth muscle** → vasodilation

**Not topical** — NSAID (COX), steroid (PLA2), lidocaine (Na⁺), CCB (L-type Ca²⁺ — oral only)

### Botulinum toxin – mechanism, outcomes & pre-treatment testing

**Indication** — **failed medical management** + ischaemia/pain; **ulcers not a contraindication**; **off-label**

**Mechanism** — **cleaves pre-synaptic SNAREs → ↓ ACh release** → ↑ digital perfusion

**Other proposed** — blocks **α2 receptor recruitment** on vascular smooth muscle · ↓ **substance P & glutamate** (pain) · inhibits **RhoA kinase** → ↓ smooth-muscle constriction · may ↓ sympathetic tone

**Outcomes** — **all of**: ulcer healing, ↓ pain, ↓ cold intolerance, ↑ digital blood flow

**Absolute contraindications** — **pregnancy / breastfeeding** → **urine pregnancy test before injection** · anaphylactic allergy · active infection · aminoglycosides / anticholinergics

**Caution** — respiratory disease, myasthenia gravis

✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — proprioception (non-depolarising blockers), Ca²⁺ channel excitability (gabapentin/pregabalin), muscle strengthening

![Botox injection method](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6098-method-of-botox-injection.jpg)

### Periarterial sympathectomy indications

**Indication** — **Raynaud's failed medical Rx + ischaemic digital ulcer** / impending gangrene; esp. collagen vascular / occlusive disease

**Technique** — **periarterial (digital)** = commonest; strip **adventitia + sympathetic fibres**; may extend to forearm

**Not indicated** — **calcific arterial disease** · Buerger's in **active smoker** · CRPS (stellate block instead) · diabetic Charcot · TOS

**Thoracoscopic sympathectomy** — **out of favour** → **rebound hypersympathetic response**

**Microvascular reconstruction** — rare: **small-segment occlusion of one major artery**, other spared

## 6 Complications

**Digital ischaemia** — ulceration, **gangrene → amputation**

## How it's asked

**"BTX mechanism in Raynaud's"** → **cleaves pre-synaptic SNAREs → ↓ ACh → ↑ perfusion**

**"BTX outcomes in vasospastic disorders"** → **all of the above**

**"Before repeat Botox in scleroderma patient"** → **urine pregnancy test**\
✗ Wrong options: HbA1c, sympathectomy, CXR

**"Failed >1 y CCB + ARB + nitrate, no ulcer — next"** → **botulinum toxin A**\
✗ Wrong options: thoracoscopic sympathectomy, microvascular reconstruction

**"Topical drug for scleroderma fingertip ulcer — mechanism"** → **↑ cGMP** (nitrate)

**"Periarterial sympathectomy indicated"** → **Raynaud's, failed medical Rx, ischaemic ulcer**\
✗ Wrong options: smoking Buerger's, CRPS, Charcot, TOS

**"Why thoracoscopic sympathectomy abandoned"** → **rebound hypersympathetic response**

**"Incidence of Raynaud's in scleroderma"** → **80–90%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Replantation

*Hand · 76 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — 90% of UL amputations traumatic; **♂:♀ 4:1**; most at **digit** level

**Mechanisms** — **sharp** (best) · blunt · avulsion · crush

**Assess** — timing, level, number of digits, preservation of part, **associated injuries**, PMH; stump: zone of injury, viability, contamination

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Indications & contraindications by level (thumb, multiple digits, zone)

| Indications (primary / "absolute") | Relative indications |
|---|---|
| **Thumb at any level** (even proximal to FPL insertion) | Single digit **distal to FDS insertion (zone I)** — good function |
| **Multiple digits** | Ring avulsion (Urbaniak 2A) |
| **Through palm** | Through/above elbow |
| **Wrist or proximal** (distal forearm/wrist = excellent function) | — |
| **Almost any part in a child** | — |

| Primary contraindications | Relative contraindications |
|---|---|
| Severe vascular disease | **Single digit proximal to FDS insertion (zone II)** |
| **Mangled / crush** | Medically unstable |
| **Segmental / multilevel** | Disabling psychiatric illness |
| Ischaemia **>6 h with large muscle** | Contamination |
| — | Ischaemia **>12 h, no muscle** |

**Zone II single digit** — FDS insertion = mid-middle phalanx = distal border of zone II → **PIP stiffness**, ↓ sensation, FDP–FDS adhesions → patient **bypasses stiff index**, uses long finger

**Zone II exceptions** — **thumb, multiple digits, children**

**Distal limit** — need **~4 mm intact skin proximal to nail fold** for veins; distal to nail fold → vessels bi/trifurcate → do not replant (TO)

**Proximal phalanx level (TO)** — poor PIP motion → do not replant (single digit)

**Ring avulsion (Urbaniak)** — **2A** (vascular only) → repair · **2B** (bone/tendon/nerve) or **3** (degloving) → **amputation**

**Not reasons to refuse** — age, workers' comp, malingering

### Ischaemia time criteria

| Level | Warm | Cold |
|---|---|---|
| **Proximal to carpus** (muscle) | **<6 h** | **<12 h** |
| **Digit** (distal to carpus) | **<12 h** (Orthobullets/OB) · **≥8 h** (TO) | **<24 h** (Orthobullets/OB) · **up to 30 h** (TO) |

**Muscle necrosis (TO)** — irreversible after **6 h warm**; cooling to **4 °C** → 12 h

**Wrist with 8 h warm** — **relative contraindication**

**Forearm with 10 h warm** — do not replant (TO; good only if <6 h)

### Prognostic factors – mechanism & ischaemia time

**Most important factor** — **mechanism of injury** (sharp > crush/avulsion)

**Favourable survival** — **sharp**, **female**, **age <13**, **non-smoker**, warm <12 h / cold <24 h (digit)

**Unfavourable** — **crush**, avulsion, **ribbon sign** (corkscrew artery = intimal avulsion → needs segmental resection + vein graft)

**Level** — DIP–PIP replants good · **PIP–MCP poor** (flexion contracture)

## 5 Management

### Transport of amputated part

**Part** — rinse; wrap in **gauze moist with RL/saline** → **sealed plastic bag** → **ice water**; cool to **~4 °C** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: direct ice · dry ice

**Stump** — moist gauze, compression

### Surgical sequence & major limb replantation

**Shunt first** — proximal replants with large muscle (↓ warm ischaemia)

| Source | Sequence |
|---|---|
| **TO** | **Bone → extensor → flexor → artery → nerve → vein → skin** |
| OB | Bone → extensor → flexor → arteries → nerves → veins (veins may precede nerves) → skin |
| Orthobullets | Bone ± shortening → extensor → artery → veins → flexor → nerve → skin ± fasciotomy |

**Always** — **bone first**; **arteries before veins**; vascular repair = greatest priority (TO); artery right after bone if ischaemia >3–4 h

**Vein grafts** — use **generously**; bone shortening avoids grafts

**Finger order** — **thumb → long → ring → small → index**

**Multiple digits** — **structure-by-structure** (fastest) · **digit-by-digit = longest**

**Palm amputation, digits intact, clean** — **anatomic replantation of hand**\
✗ Wrong options: transpositional (for multidigit loss)

![Operative sequence](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6063-guidelines.jpg)

### Ray resection vs replantation – function & palmar width

**Ring finger amputated at proximal phalanx** — **early 4th ray resection + 5th ray transposition** → best cosmesis & grip\
✗ Wrong options: delayed → ↓ grip, pronation, cosmesis · revision amputation

**Transposition** — after **central ray** (long/ring) resection → prevents gap & **scissoring**

**Ray resection** — **↓ palmar width** → ↓ grip; good cosmesis; most **return to work**

| Ray resected | Greatest loss |
|---|---|
| **Long** | **Grip** |
| **Index** | **Pinch** |

**Contaminated avulsion-crush small finger + ribbon sign** — **ray resection**\
✗ Wrong options: replant even in child

### Postoperative monitoring – arterial vs venous insufficiency, leeches & antibiotics

**Environment** — warm room (**80 °F**); avoid caffeine, chocolate, nicotine; hydration; aspirin, dipyridamole, LMW dextran, heparin

**Monitoring** — **skin temperature most reliable**: drop **>2 °C in <1 h** or **<30 °C** · **pulse oximetry <94%**

| | **Arterial insufficiency** | **Venous congestion** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | **<12 h** post-op | **>12 h** |
| Cause | **Vasospasm → thrombosis** (commonest early failure) | Outflow failure |
| Treatment | **Release bandages**, **dependent position**, heparin, **stellate ganglion block**, early exploration | **Elevate**, **leeches**, heparin pledgets |
| Avoid | **Leeches** | — |

**Leeches** — *Hirudo medicinalis* → **hirudin** (100× heparin) → venous congestion

**Leech infection** — ***Aeromonas hydrophila*** (Gram −ve rod, leech gut) → **ciprofloxacin** (**DNA gyrase inhibitor**) first line · alt **TMP-SMX / 3rd-gen cephalosporin**

### Reperfusion injury – allopurinol

**Mechanism** — **hypoxanthine → xanthine** (xanthine oxidase)

**Agent** — **allopurinol** (xanthine oxidase inhibitor) improves outcome\
✗ Wrong options: tPA, heparin, COX-2, leeches

### WALANT anaesthesia

**WALANT (TO)** — wide-awake local anaesthesia **no tourniquet**; lidocaine/bupivacaine + **epinephrine**; no sedation; **Lalonde (2005)**

**Purpose (TO)** — proposed to **↓ waiting time** for surgery

**Advantages (TO)** — **↓ adhesions** (immediate mobilisation; collagen from day 3) · **intraoperative active testing** (tendon transfer tension) · comorbid patients / tourniquet to avoid

**Incorrect (TO)** — "not suitable for **tendon transfers**" → it **is** suitable for complex procedures

## 6 Complications

**Early failure** — **arterial thrombosis from vasospasm** (<12 h)

**Stiffness** — replanted digit ~**50% total motion**; **tenolysis = commonest secondary surgery** (**47.2%** of secondary procedures)

**Major limb** — **myonecrosis**, **myoglobinuria → renal failure** (fatal), acidosis, **hyperkalaemia** (TO)

**Others** — infection, **cold intolerance**

## How it's asked

**"Best indication — healthy 35-y"** → **wrist-level amputation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: thumb distal to nail bed · index at PP · forearm 10 h warm

**"Relative contraindication"** → **ring finger through proximal phalanx shaft**

**"Replantation indicated"** → **thumb through proximal phalanx**\
✗ Wrong options: multilevel, crush, Urbaniak 2B, long finger at PP

**"Indications EXCEPT"** → **long finger through proximal phalanx**

**"Absolute indication"** → **thumb proximal to FPL insertion**

**"Absolute indications EXCEPT"** → **single digit distal to FDS** (relative)

**"Index cut proximal to FDS, 15 h cold — why not replant?"** → **local anatomy (zone II stiffness)**\
✗ Wrong options: delay · age

**"Most important factor in outcome"** → **mechanism of injury**

**"Unfavourable for survival"** → **crushed digit**

**"Wrist amputation — true"** → **8 h warm ischaemia = relative contraindication**\
✗ Wrong options: veins before arteries · bone last

**"Repair sequence"** → **bone, extensor, flexor, artery, nerve, vein, skin** (TO)

**"Increases surgical time"** → **digit-by-digit**

**"Clean palm amputation, digits intact"** → **anatomic replantation of hand**

**"Temp drop 33 → 29 °C, SpO₂ 87% — EXCEPT"** → **leeches**

**"Leech prophylaxis MOA"** → **DNA gyrase inhibition** (ciprofloxacin)

**"Inhibits xanthine formation"** → **allopurinol**

**"Ring finger GSW at PP — best grip/cosmesis"** → **early 4th ray resection + 5th ray transposition**

**"Ray resection — true"** → **↓ palmar width**

**"Avulsion-crush small finger, ribbon sign, contaminated"** → **ray resection**

**"WALANT — incorrect"** → **unsuitable for tendon transfers** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sagittal Band Rupture

*Hand · 77 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Synonym** — **"boxer's knuckle"**; **zone 5** extensor injury

**Who** — **pugilists** (pros: index/middle; amateurs: ring/little); **RA** (chronic, attritional)

**Finger** — **middle most common (48%)** · little 31% · index 14% · ring 7% (Orthobullets) · "middle or ring" (OB203)

**Side** — **radial SB : ulnar SB = 9:1** → tendon subluxes **ulnarly**

**Association** — MCP collateral ligament injury; often dorsal capsule torn too

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**Sagittal band** — part of closed **girdle** around MC head with palmar plate; origin **volar plate + intermetacarpal ligament** at MC neck → inserts on extensor hood

**Primary stabiliser** of extensor tendon at MCP = **sagittal band**; **secondary = juncturae tendinum**

**Function** — resists **ulnar deviation** of tendon (esp. MCP flexion); prevents **bowstringing** in MCP hyperextension

| Sectioning | Result |
|---|---|
| Ulnar SB (partial/complete) | **No** dislocation |
| Radial SB distal part | No instability |
| **50% of proximal radial SB** | **Subluxation** |
| **Complete radial SB** | **Dislocation** |

**Instability greatest** — **wrist flexion** + MCP flexion; central digits > border; **middle least stable**, **little most stable** (junctura)

![Sagittal band girdle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6029-girdle.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Traumatic** — forceful resisted flexion or extension ("flicking"); **direct blow** to MCP (forced flexion); hood laceration

**Atraumatic** — **RA**; spontaneous

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — MCP pain, focal swelling, **snapping** with extension; palpable dorsal defect on fist, resolves with MCP extension

**Key finding** — **cannot actively extend MCP from flexion, but CAN HOLD extension once passively placed**\
✗ Wrong options: lag of 30°/60° · Bunnell intrinsic test · IP extension

**IP extension** — preserved (intrinsics)

**Pseudo-triggering** — snapping from subluxation/relocation → **avoid unnecessary trigger release**

**Dislocation into intermetacarpal gully** — most unstable MCP flexion + **wrist flexed**; least with wrist extended

**Provocative** — pain on resisted MCP extension with IPs extended

**RA** — ulnar deviation of digits at MCP

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray | PA, lateral, oblique; **Brewerton** (AP, dorsal fingers on cassette, MCP 45° flexed); **stress view → collateral avulsion** |
| **Dynamic US** | When **swelling obscures exam**; EDC subluxation on MCP flexion |
| **MRI** | Axial at long MCP, **MCP flexed**; acute = poor definition, discontinuity, thickening |
| MRI direction | **Ulnar SB defect → tendon radial**; **radial SB defect → tendon into ulnar gully** |

![MRI: radial sagittal band rupture, tendon subluxed ulnarly](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6029-mri-radial-sagittal-band-rupture-mf-and-subluxation-of-centr.jpg)

**Differentials** — collateral injury, EDC rupture, trigger finger, junctura disruption, congenital SB deficiency, MCP OA

### Rayan & Murray classification

| Type | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| I | SB injury, **no** tendon instability | ![Rayan & Murray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6029-rayan-and-murray.jpg) |
| II | SB injury + tendon **subluxation** | |
| III | SB injury + tendon **dislocation** | |

## 5 Management

```
Sagittal band rupture
├─ Acute (<6 wk Orthobullets / <3 wk OB) → extension / yoke / relative-motion splint 4–6 wk
├─ Professional athlete → open direct repair
└─ Chronic (>6 wk) or failed splint
       ├─ primary repair possible → direct repair (Kettlekamp)
       └─ not possible → extensor centralisation
```

### Treatment – sagittal band splinting vs open repair

**Splint** — **MCP in extension, IPs free** (extension or **yoke** splint)\
✗ Wrong options: volar finger splint · buddy tape · dorsal block · intrinsic-plus

**Relative-motion splint (OB)** — **MCP 30° hyperextension, PIP free**

**Timing conflict** — acute = **within 6 wk** (Orthobullets) · splint if seen **within 3 wk**, surgery if **>2–3 wk** or failed splint (OB203) — read the options

**Pro athlete (NFL)** — **open repair of sagittal band** → earlier rehab, quicker RTS\
✗ Wrong options: junctura repair

### Centralisation techniques

| Technique | Key detail |
|---|---|
| **Trapdoor** | **Ulnar-based partial-thickness capsular flap**, tendon deep to flap |
| **Kilgore** | Distally based **radial** EDC slip around **radial collateral** |
| **Carroll** | Distally based **ulnar** EDC slip, deep to tendon, around **radial collateral** |
| **McCoy** | **Proximally** based EDC slip around **lumbrical insertion** |
| **Watson** | Distally based EDC slip under **deep transverse MC ligament** |
| **Wheeldon** | **Ring junctura** divided, brought over tendon, sutured to torn radial SB (middle finger) |
| Other | Fascial strips / free tendon graft |

**Rehab** — 0–4 wk resting splint MCP + IP 0° · from 2 wk motion splint MCP 0°, IPs free · AROM 4–8 wk · strengthen >8 wk

## 6 Complications

**MCP flexion contracture** — after nonop / delayed presentation → secondary **intrinsic tightness**

## X vs Y

| Test / finding | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Can't extend MCP, holds when placed** | **Sagittal band rupture** |
| Can't hold MCP extension | EDC rupture/laceration, **PIN palsy** |
| Locking in flexion | Trigger finger (A1) |
| PIP laxity to valgus (PIP 30°, MCP 90°) | Collateral ligament injury |
| Rigid DIP with PIP flexed 90° over table | **Elson** — central slip |
| Can't flex DIP | Jersey finger (FDP) |

## How it's asked

**"Key diagnostic finding of SB rupture?"** → **holds MCP extension after passive extension**

**"Finding present in SB injury?"** → **can't actively extend MCP but maintains it after passive extension**

**"Amateur boxer 2–3 wk — initial treatment?"** → **extension / yoke splint (MCP extended, IPs free)**

**"Punched wall 3 days ago, dorsal defect on fist — next step?"** → **MCP extension splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: trigger injection · hood reconstruction

**"NFL player, tendon subluxing on MCP flexion?"** → **open repair of sagittal band**

**"Relative-motion splint position?"** → **MCP 30° hyperextension, PIP free**

**"Which band and which way?"** → **radial SB → tendon subluxes ulnarly**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scaphoid Fracture

*Hand · 78 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – most common carpal fracture

**Most common carpal fracture (TO)** — **scaphoid (60%) > triquetrum > trapezium**; 15% of acute wrist injuries

**Who** — ♂ 2:1, 3rd decade, contact sport

| Location | Adult | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Waist** | **65–70%** | Commonest |
| Proximal third | 25% (5–10% TO) | **AVN / nonunion** |
| Distal third | 10% (10–20% TO) | Heals well |
| Tubercle | 5% | — |

**Children** — historically **distal pole/tubercle** (ossifies distal → proximal; 52% tubercle TO) · **now waist (middle ⅓) most common** (OB) → read options

### Anatomy & blood supply

**Bone** — largest of proximal row; **>75% articular cartilage**; links proximal & distal rows

**Major supply** — **dorsal carpal branch of radial artery** → enters **dorsal non-articular ridge** → **retrograde** to **proximal 80%**

**Minor supply** — **superficial palmar arch (volar radial a.)** → **distal tubercle** → distal 20%

**AVN by level** — **proximal ⅕ → 100%** · **proximal ⅓ → 33%** (untreated)

![Retrograde scaphoid blood supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-blood-supply-ttc-moved.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Wrist loading biomechanics

**Mechanism** — **axial load on hyper-dorsiflexed, pronated, ulnar-deviated wrist** (FOOSH)

**Force transmission** — neutral: scaphoid fossa ≈ lunate fossa (slight scaphoid predominance) · **extension → ↑ force through scaphoid fossa**

**Stability** — transverse > vertical/oblique

**Humpback** — distal fragment **flexes**, proximal **extends** (pulled by SLIL) → DISI

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – snuffbox tenderness, scaphoid view & MRI

**Signs** — **snuffbox tenderness** · **scaphoid tubercle tenderness** · **scaphoid compression** (axial thumb load) · pain on **resisted pronation**

**All 3 positive in 24 h** — sensitivity 87–100%, specificity 74%

![Anatomic snuffbox](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-snuffbox.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Imaging | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| X-ray series | PA, lateral, **45° semi-pronated oblique**, **scaphoid view** |
| **Scaphoid view** | **PA in 20° ulnar deviation** → waist best |
| Initial X-ray negative | **27%** → **repeat in 14–21 d** (immobilise meanwhile) |
| **MRI** | **Most sensitive for occult # <24 h** (~100%); **proximal pole vascularity (T1)** |
| Bone scan | Occult # at **72 h** (sens 100%, spec 98%) |
| **CT (1 mm along scaphoid axis)** | **Displacement, humpback, union/nonunion after fixation**; not for occult # |

| Measurement / sign | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Displacement >1 mm** | Unstable → fix |
| **Intrascaphoid angle >35°** / humpback | Flexion malalignment → ORIF (volar) |
| **Radiolunate angle >15°** | **DISI** → ORIF |
| Cysts, fracture widening | **Delayed union** (Herbert C) |
| Sclerosis / ↓ T1 signal proximal pole | **AVN** (MRI T1 best) |

✗ Wrong options: **Wrong views** — 30° supinated oblique = **pisiform**; hyperextension axial = **carpal tunnel view**; clenched fist = **SL ligament**

**High suspicion + negative X-ray + wants to RTP** — **MRI**

![Scaphoid view (20° ulnar deviation)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-scaphoidview.jpg)

**Herbert & Fisher (stability)**

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| A | **Stable acute** (tubercle A1, incomplete waist A2) |
| B | **Unstable acute** — distal oblique, complete waist, **proximal pole**, trans-scaphoid perilunate |
| C | **Delayed union** (cysts, widening) |
| D | **Nonunion** |

**Mayo (location)** — I distal tubercle · II distal articular · III distal ⅓ · IV middle ⅓ · V proximal ⅓

**Russe (fracture line)** — I **horizontal oblique** · II **transverse** · III **vertical oblique (least stable)**

| Russe I | Russe II | Russe III |
|---|---|---|
| ![Russe I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-russe-type-i.jpg) | ![Russe II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-russe-type-ii.jpg) | ![Russe III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-russe-type-iii.jpg) |

## 5 Management

```
Scaphoid fracture
├─ Suspected, X-ray negative → immobilise + repeat X-ray 14–21 d  OR  MRI
├─ Nondisplaced waist/distal → cast (≥8–12 wk)  OR  percutaneous screw (faster union/RTW)
├─ PROXIMAL POLE (any) → ORIF via DORSAL approach, headless compression screw
└─ Displaced >1 mm / humpback / DISI / perilunate / comminuted → ORIF
       ├─ waist/distal + humpback → VOLAR approach
       └─ delayed (≥6 wk) with cysts → ORIF + bone graft (volar for waist)
```

**Nonop union** — **<1 mm displacement → 90%** (88–95% if started <3 wk); ↑ nonunion if immobilisation delayed **>4 wk**

**Cast** — **wrist alone sufficient** (thumb & elbow add nothing but stiffness — OB); long vs short arm: no consensus; ≥8 wk, up to 12–14 wk; RTP only when healed

**PEMF** — may help delayed union

### Operative vs nonoperative – percutaneous fixation of nondisplaced waist fractures

**Percutaneous vs cast** — **↓ time to union (6–7 vs 10–12 wk)**, **faster RTW/sport**; similar total cost; no higher union rate; no better 2-y grip/ROM

**Union after surgery** — 90–95%

**Strongest reason to fix (stem)** — **fracture location (proximal pole)**\
✗ Wrong options: age, job, dominance, chronicity at 1 wk

### ORIF indications & approach (displacement, proximal pole, delayed presentation)

**Operative criteria**

| Treatment | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **Cast** | Stable, **nondisplaced (<1 mm)** waist/distal; start **<3–4 wk** |
| **Percutaneous screw** | Nondisplaced waist (faster union/RTW) · displacement >1 mm **without** significant angulation |
| **ORIF** | **Displaced**, humpback, DISI, perilunate, comminuted, unstable vertical oblique, **proximal pole** |
| ORIF + graft | **Delayed presentation with cysts** |
| Vascularised graft | **Nonunion with cysts/AVN** only |

**ORIF indications** — **displacement >1 mm** · **intrascaphoid angle >35°** · **radiolunate angle >15° (DISI)** · humpback 15° · **perilunate** · comminuted · unstable vertical oblique · **proximal pole**

| | **Volar approach** | **Dorsal approach** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | **Waist & distal ⅓, humpback** | **Proximal pole** |
| Interval | **FCR – radial artery** | Limit to proximal half of scaphoid |
| Blood supply | **Spared** (TO ✓) | Risk to dorsal ridge vessels |
| At risk | — | **EPL**; proximal pole cartilage |
| Weakness | Reduction harder (TO); not for oblique/proximal pole (can't be perpendicular) | Poor distal ⅓ access |
| Must close | RSC ligament | — |

**TO: volar better because** — **(1) less vascular injury, (3) distal ⅓ easier**\
✗ Wrong options: easier reduction · oblique fractures

**Proximal pole** — **open reduction + headless differential-pitch screw via dorsal approach** (100% healing reported)\
✗ Wrong options: cast · K-wires · percutaneous · vascularised graft acutely

**K-wires** — only if proximal fragment too small for screw head

**Displaced waist with cysts at 6 wk** — **ORIF + bone graft, volar**

### Screw fixation technique – central axis position

**Most important** — **long screw in central axis** (into subchondral bone) → max rigidity

**Center-center** on AP & lateral; **volar approach → excise proximal trapezium** to get dorsal-ulnar start point (else too volar & radial → nonunion)

**Open vs percutaneous dorsal** — open: **↓ risk of screw prominence** above subchondral bone (direct view)

**Don't violate STT cartilage**

![Percutaneous screw fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6034-orif.jpg)

### Paediatric scaphoid fractures

**Most common now** — **middle ⅓ (waist)**; unicortical up to 21%

**Treatment** — nondisplaced → cast **4–6 wk**; displaced → ORIF

## 6 Complications

### Persistent pain after fixation – CT assessment

**Nonunion definition** — no union **6 mo** after cast/fixation

**Pain months after fixation, hardware OK on X-ray** — **fine-cut CT along scaphoid axis**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI (metal) · bone scan (nonspecific) · clenched-fist view

| Complication | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | 5–10% after cast; ↑ proximal pole, **vertical oblique**, displacement >1 mm, age, **smoking** |
| **AVN** | 13–50%; proximal ⅕ ≈ 100% |
| Malunion | **Humpback** (flexed distal fragment) |
| Screw prominence | Subchondral penetration → arthrosis |
| **SNAC** | End result of nonunion |

### Vascularised bone graft (medial femoral condyle)

**Indication** — nonunion with **cysts / AVN / collapse**

**MFC graft pedicle** — **longitudinal branch of descending geniculate artery** (superomedial genicular in 11–20%)

**MFT (medial femoral trochlea)** — **transverse branch** of DGA → **proximal pole** (osteochondral)

**1,2 ICSRA** — distal radius pedicled; smaller graft

**Not for acute fractures** — vascularised graft only for nonunion

## X vs Y

| Imaging question | Answer |
|---|---|
| Occult fracture <24 h | **MRI** |
| Occult at 72 h (no MRI) | Bone scan |
| Displacement / union after fixation | **CT** |
| Proximal pole AVN | **MRI T1** |

| Graft | Pedicle | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **MFC** | **Longitudinal br. DGA** | **Waist nonunion with collapse** |
| **MFT** | **Transverse br. DGA** | **Proximal pole** (needs cartilage) |
| 1,2 ICSRA | Radial artery branch | Smaller dorsal graft |

## How it's asked

**"Most commonly fractured carpal bone?"** → **scaphoid** (TO)

**"Snuffbox tender, X-ray normal, wants to compete tomorrow"** → **MRI**

**"How to shoot a scaphoid view?"** → **PA in 20° ulnar deviation**

**"Percutaneous vs cast for nondisplaced waist"** → **reduced time to union**

**"Nonop — immobilise what?"** → **wrist only** (OB)

**"Most compelling reason for ORIF"** → **location (proximal pole)**

**"Volar better than dorsal because"** → **less vascular injury + distal ⅓ easier** (TO)

**"Proximal pole fracture"** → **open reduction + headless screw via dorsal approach**

**"Displaced waist, cysts at 6 wk"** → **ORIF + graft, volar**

**"Most important for fixation strength"** → **central-axis screw into subchondral bone**

**"Nonunion after volar screw — what would have helped"** → **center-center position**

**"Open vs percutaneous dorsal"** → **↓ screw prominence**

**"Pain 7–9 mo after fixation"** → **CT along scaphoid axis**

**"Force through scaphoid fossa ↑ with"** → **wrist extension**

**"MFC vascularised graft pedicle"** → **longitudinal branch of descending geniculate**

**"Commonest paediatric pattern"** → **middle ⅓** (OB)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scaphoid Fracture Nonunion

*Hand · 79 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Incidence** — **5–10% after immobilisation** (Orthobullets) · "up to 10%" (OB) · **~25% after surgical fixation** (some studies)

**Demographics** — ♂:♀ **2:1**; 3rd decade

**Risk factors** — **proximal pole** fracture · **vertical oblique** pattern · **displacement >1 mm** · age · **nicotine** · **delayed diagnosis/treatment**

**Why prone** — retrograde supply, limited cancellous bone, few soft-tissue attachments, delayed Dx

**Missed fracture** — fracture line may need **1–2 wk** to appear on X-ray

### Anatomy – blood supply

**Shape** — boat / twisted peanut; oblique to long axis; **largest proximal-row bone**; **>75% articular cartilage**

**Articulations** — radius, lunate, trapezium, trapezoid, capitate

| Vessel | Entry | Supplies |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal carpal branch of radial a.** (major) | Dorsal non-articular ridge | **Proximal 80%, retrograde** |
| Superficial palmar arch (volar radial a.) | Distal tubercle | Distal 20% |

**AVN rate** — **15–50%** of scaphoid fractures; ↑ proximally; **proximal 1/5 → ~100%**

**AVN site** — **proximal** pole\
✗ Wrong options: distal

![Scaphoid blood supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422868-illustration-of-scaphoid-blood-supply.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Humpback deformity** — proximal pole **extends** with lunate (SL ligament) · distal pole **flexes** (scaphotrapezial lig.) · hinge = **volar radioscaphocapitate ligament**

**Natural history** — untreated → carpal derangement, pain, cartilage loss → **SNAC**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — remote fall / "sprain"; get prior op notes & imaging

**Symptoms** — wrist pain with motion, ↓ grip; some asymptomatic

**Exam** — swelling, **snuffbox / fracture-site tenderness**, ↓ ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | PA, lateral, 45° semi-pronated oblique, **scaphoid view**; cysts, sclerosis, resorption, hardware failure, **humpback on lateral**, SNAC |
| **CT** | **Best to evaluate nonunion & plan surgery**; **1 mm cuts in scaphoid plane**; gap, angulation, prior technical error, SNAC cysts |
| MRI | **Osteonecrosis**: ↓ T1 signal **proximal fragment** (different signal between fragments); inconsistent utility, gadolinium helps |

**Intra-op punctate bleeding** — **highly predictive** of vascular proximal pole; absent → vascularised graft

![Scaphoid view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422868-scaphoidview.jpg)

### Diagnosis – nonunion, humpback deformity & proximal pole osteonecrosis

**Diagnosis** — history + exam + X-ray + **CT**; MRI for AVN

| MRI picture | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Fracture line + **different signal between fragments** | **Scaphoid nonunion + osteonecrosis** |
| Most/all of scaphoid necrotic | **Preiser's** |
| Lunate | Kienböck |
| Fluid area with dense rim | Intraosseous ganglion |
| SL ligament injury, widened interval | SL dissociation |

**Sequela of nonop Rx** — **humpback deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: distal pole AVN · capitolunate OA = SLAC III · radiolunate OA

![Humpback deformity](../../MCQ/attachments/q211952-illa-humpback-deformity.jpg)

### Classification – stability

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| **Stable** | Length & alignment kept, **fibrous union** |
| **Unstable** | Loss of length/alignment, carpal instability or chondral change |

## 5 Management

```
Scaphoid nonunion
├─ No AVN, no SNAC → ORIF + non-vascularised graft + screw
│      ├─ no humpback → inlay (Russe)
│      └─ humpback / carpal collapse → interposition wedge (Fisk), VOLAR approach
├─ Proximal pole AVN / no punctate bleeding, no pancarpal OA → vascularised graft
│      ├─ pedicled distal radius (1,2-ICSRA dorsal · Mathoulin volar · Sotereanos)
│      └─ free MFC (volar, humpback) · MFT if cartilage lost · iliac crest
└─ Advanced SNAC / arthritis → salvage (PRC, 4-corner / scaphoid excision + intercarpal fusion, arthrodesis)
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| No union by **6 mo**, technical error (screw position, implant failure, distraction) | **ORIF + graft** |
| Inadequate prior immobilisation / refuses surgery | Cast (up to 6 mo) ± **PEMF** — 69% union by 3 mo (fixed, no error) |
| Technical error / displacement | **no cast** |

### Without osteonecrosis – ORIF with non-vascularised graft (volar approach for humpback)

**Standard** — **ORIF + autologous bone graft**; union **92%**; best when failure = simple technical error\
✗ Wrong options: cast · arthroscopy · 4CF / arthrodesis in young w/o SNAC

**Humpback, no AVN, no prior surgery** — **volar approach + standard (non-vascularised) graft + screw**\
✗ Wrong options: K-wire · VBG · dorsal

**Volar advantages** — corrects humpback (K-wire joysticks), grafts volar defect, **preserves dorsal blood supply**

**Dorsal approach** — most acute waist fractures; proximal pole visualisation; **↑ devascularisation risk**

**Screw direction** — volar: **distal → proximal** · dorsal: proximal → distal; plate = volar buttress; K-wire removable

**Casting nonunion** — 4–6 mo, high failure; thumb spica / above-elbow **does not improve union**

| Graft | Type | Indication | Union |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Russe (inlay)** | Non-vasc corticocancellous, volar (FCR–radial a.) | **No collapse/humpback** | **92%** |
| **Fisk (interposition wedge)** | Non-vasc, distal radius (Fernandez: iliac crest) | **Humpback / carpal collapse** | 72–95% |
| BMP / PRP | Adjunct | No SNAC; avoids free flap | High (series) |

### Proximal pole AVN – vascularised (MFC) graft

**Indications (VBG)** — waist nonunion + **proximal pole AVN** · **no punctate bleeding** · not with pancarpal OA / collapse · failed non-vasc graft

**Relative contraindication** — no intact cartilage shell · advanced collapse with OA (e.g. SNAC + scaphocapitate arthrosis → arthrodesis)

| Graft | Pedicle / approach | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **1,2-ICSRA (Zaidemberg)** | Branch of **radial a.**, **dorsal** (1st–2nd compartments) | **71% nonunion / 50% AVN**; ↑ failure with DISI/humpback |
| Mathoulin | **Volar carpal a.**, volar | Wedge for humpback |
| Sotereanos | Artery of **4th dorsal compartment**, dorsal capsular pedicle | Inlay |
| Local VBG overall | — | 82% good/excellent |
| **Free MFC** | **Descending genicular a.** (longitudinal br., from **SFA**) or superomedial genicular; **volar**, anastomosis to **radial a.** | **100% by 13 wk** (1 study) |
| **Free MFT** | Osteochondral; DGA periosteal branches | AVN + **cartilage loss** of proximal pole; >90% |
| Free iliac crest | **Deep circumflex iliac a.** | 76% |

**AVN + no bleeding + collapse** — **free vascularised MFC via volar approach**\
✗ Wrong options: non-vasc · 1,2-ICSRA dorsal = inferior union

**VBG vs non-vasc — conflict** — **88% vs 47%** union with proximal pole AVN (OB8865) · **no difference** in type/source on meta-analysis for waist nonunion (OB219567) · no clear superiority (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Graft source** — no single source superior (iliac crest = distal radius); young patients **85–100%** union

**MFC uses** — **proximal scaphoid AVN / nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: lunate OCD · acute humpback · SNAC with arthrosis · Kienböck stage I (4+5 ECA there)

![1,2-ICSRA graft](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422868-icrsa.jpg)

![MFC graft](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422868-vtgmm.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Osteonecrosis** — proximal fractures

**Graft failure → SNAC** — radioscaphoid (styloid) → capitolunate arthrosis

![SNAC wrist](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422868-snac-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Russe** | **Fisk** | **1,2-ICSRA** | **Free MFC** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vascularised | No | No | ✓ pedicled | ✓ free |
| Approach | Volar | Volar | **Dorsal** | **Volar** |
| Humpback correction | No | **Yes** | Poor | **Yes** |
| Proximal pole AVN | No | No | Yes (inferior) | **✓ best** |

## How it's asked

**"MRI 18 mo after fall — fracture line, different fragment signal"** → **scaphoid nonunion + osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: Preiser, Kienböck

**"Radiographic sequela of nonop Rx"** → **humpback deformity**

**"Waist nonunion 6 mo, young"** → **ORIF + autologous bone graft**

**"Humpback, no AVN — best surgery"** → **volar, standard graft, screw**

**"Army medic, nonunion >1 y, no SNAC"** → **ORIF + bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: cast, PRC, fusion

**"Delayed Dx humpback on CT"** → **ORIF single screw, volar approach**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal · cast · 4CF

**"MFC VBG + fixation best for"** → **proximal scaphoid AVN**

**"AVN, no bleeding at surgery, collapse"** → **free vascularised femoral graft, volar approach**

**"Waist nonunion with AVN — true statement"** → **vascularised = non-vascularised union rates**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal corrects humpback · iliac crest superior

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scaphoid Lunate Advanced Collapse (SLAC)

*Hand · 80 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### SL dissociation – pathomechanics

**Definition** — predictable degenerative arthritis from **chronic SL dissociation**; **most common form of wrist arthritis** (Watson & Ballet 1984)

**Causes** — SL ligament tear (most common cause of carpal instability); also **crystalline arthropathy**

**SLIL** — C-shaped: dorsal, proximal (fibrocartilage), volar; **dorsal = thickest (3 mm) & strongest** → **most critical for SL stability** (volar ~1 mm)

**Not key SL stabilisers** — RSC (sectioning with intact SLIL → no kinematic change), DRC, DIC, volar SLIL

**SL dissociation** — **scaphoid flexes + lunate extends** = **DISI**; SL angle **>70°**, lunate >10° extended

**Why radiolunate spared** — lunate stays **concentric** in spheroidal lunate fossa in all positions of rotation

## 2 Mechanism

### Stages & sequence of arthritis (radial styloid, radioscaphoid, capitolunate; radiolunate spared)

**Sequence** — **radial styloid–scaphoid → entire radioscaphoid → capitolunate** (capitate migrates proximally into SL gap, subluxes dorsally on lunate)

**First joint affected** — **radioscaphoid** (radial styloid tip)

**Spared** — **radiolunate** (and lunotriquetral)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain at SL interval, difficulty weight-bearing through wrist, stiffness, progressive weakness

**Signs** — dorsal SL tenderness, ↓ ROM, ↓ grip

**Watson scaphoid shift** — pressure on **scaphoid tuberosity**, wrist **ulnar → radial deviation** → scaphoid subluxes dorsally (pain) → clunk on release; **positive early only** (arthritis stabilises scaphoid later); compare sides

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — PA + lateral; stress views and MRI unnecessary for staging

**SL diastasis (PA)** — **>3 mm** (Orthobullets) · **>2 mm** + asymmetric to other side (OB) — read the options

**PA by stage** — I: **radial styloid beaking**, sclerosis · II: narrowing whole scaphoid fossa · III: capitolunate narrowing, capitate migrates proximally

**Lateral** — **DISI**, capitate subluxed dorsally on lunate

**MRI** — thinning of proximal scaphoid / scaphoid facet / capitolunate cartilage, synovitis

### Watson classification

| Stage | Arthritis | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Radial styloid – scaphoid** | ![SLAC I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6043-slac1.jpg) |
| **II** | **Scaphoid – entire scaphoid facet** of radius | ![SLAC II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6043-slac2a.jpg) |
| **III** | **Capitate – lunate** (+ scaphocapitate) | ![SLAC III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6043-slac3.jpg) |
| "IV" (controversial) | **Radiolunate / pancarpal** | — |

**Radiolunate spared** — all Watson stages; **pancarpal ("stage IV")** → think other aetiology (RA, AVN)

**Differential** — **SNAC** (stage II = scaphocapitate; proximal pole preserved as extension of lunate)

## 5 Management

```
SLAC wrist
├─ Mild → NSAIDs, splint, steroid injection
├─ Stage I → radial styloidectomy / AIN + PIN denervation
├─ Stage II → PRC or scaphoid excision + 4CF
│      (smoker / fewer complications → PRC)
├─ Stage III (capitolunate) → scaphoid excision + 4CF or wrist fusion
└─ Radiolunate / pancarpal → total wrist fusion
```

### Operative criteria by joint status

| Procedure | Needs intact | Contraindicated by |
|---|---|---|
| **PRC** | **Radiolunate facet + capitolunate (capitate head)** + **RSC** | **Capitolunate arthritis (stage III)**, incompetent RSC |
| **Scaphoid excision + 4CF** (capitate–lunate–triquetrum–hamate) | **Radiolunate facet** | **Radiolunate arthritis** |
| Total wrist fusion | — | — (best pain relief + grip, loses motion) |

**Not contraindications** — radioscaphoid arthritis, capitolunate arthritis (for 4CF), SL disruption, scaphoid osteonecrosis

### Stage I – styloidectomy & denervation

**Radial styloidectomy** — open or arthroscopic (1-2 portal); removes scaphoid–styloid impingement

**Denervation** — **PIN + AIN** (distal branches = proprioception/capsule only) ± dorsal ulnar sensory, radial sensory, LABC, palmar cutaneous; **↓ pain, no ↑ function/strength/ROM**; can add to bony procedures for II–III

### Salvage – four-corner fusion vs PRC (capitolunate arthritis contraindication, RSC ligament)

**PRC** — excise scaphoid + lunate + triquetrum; capitate sits in lunate fossa; **preserve RSC** = **primary stabiliser after PRC** (prevents ulnar translocation)\
✗ Wrong options: DRC, DIC, ulnocapitate, ulnotriquetral

**4CF + scaphoidectomy** — motion through radiolunate joint; **only option addressing all stage III sites**

**PRC vs 4CF (stage II)** — similar long-term results; **PRC = fewer complications**, technically easier, no prolonged immobilisation → **preferred in smokers** (Tomaino)\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion, hardware, dorsal impingement

**Not for SLAC** — STT fusion (SL instability, STT OA, Kienböck) · scaphocapitate fusion (Kienböck) · **distal pole excision (SNAC)** · RSL fusion · **TWA** (young/active, e.g. farmer; OK for older low-demand / RA)

**Total wrist fusion** — pancarpal / radiolunate arthritis, failed PRC; less suitable in a 65-year-old needing motion

### Radioscapholunate fusion – distal scaphoid excision

**RSL fusion** — radioscaphoid + radiolunate OA with **preserved midcarpal joint**

**Add distal scaphoid pole excision (~20%)** — ↑ ROM (dart-thrower's motion), ↓ strain → **↑ fusion rate**; ± triquetrum excision

**Without it** — nonunion, poor motion (esp. radial deviation), weak grip

## X vs Y

| | **SLAC** | **SNAC** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | SL ligament dissociation | Scaphoid nonunion |
| Stage II | Entire radioscaphoid | **Scaphocapitate** |
| Proximal scaphoid | Arthritic | **Preserved** (moves with lunate) |
| Stage I extra option | — | **Distal pole excision** |
| Radiolunate | Spared | Spared |

| | **PRC** | **Scaphoidectomy + 4CF** |
|---|---|---|
| Stage | **II** | **II–III** |
| Capitolunate OA | **contraindicated** | Yes |
| Radiolunate OA | No | No |
| Complications | **Fewer** | Nonunion, hardware, impingement |

## How it's asked

**"First joint affected in SLAC?"** → **radioscaphoid**\
✗ Wrong options: capitolunate · radiolunate

**"Stage III SLAC joint?"** → **capitolunate**

**"Order of degeneration?"** → **radial styloid → radioscaphoid → capitolunate**

**"Stage III — arthritis absent between?"** → **lunate and radius**

**"Stage III, preserve motion?"** → **scaphoid excision + 4CF**\
✗ Wrong options: PRC · STT · neurectomy · wrist fusion

**"Primary stabiliser after PRC?"** → **radioscaphocapitate**

**"Stage III SLAC treatment?"** → **scaphoid excision + capitate-lunate-triquetrum-hamate arthrodesis**

**"Stage II, smoker, lowest complication risk?"** → **PRC**\
✗ Wrong options: 4CF · styloidectomy · TWA

**"Stage III, farmer, failed injections?"** → **scaphoid excision + 4CF**\
✗ Wrong options: scaphocapitate · distal pole excision · TWA

**"Degeneration that contraindicates PRC?"** → **capitate–lunate**

**"Contraindication to 4CF?"** → **radiolunate arthritis**

**"RSL fusion nonunion + stiff — what step was missed?"** → **distal scaphoid pole excision**

**"Most critical structure for SL stability?"** → **dorsal SLIL**

**"Result of SL dissociation?"** → **scaphoid flexion + lunate extension (DISI)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scaphoid Nonunion Advanced Collapse (SNAC)

*Hand · 81 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Scaphoid anatomy

**Main blood supply** — **dorsal carpal branch of radial a.** → dorsal nonarticular ridge → **proximal 80%, retrograde**

**Minor supply** — **superficial palmar arch branch** (volar radial) → distal tubercle → distal 20%

**Motion** — flexes with wrist flexion + radial deviation; extends with extension + ulnar deviation

## 2 Mechanism

### Natural history – radiolunate preserved

**Sequence** — **radioscaphoid (radial styloid) first** → scaphocapitate/midcarpal → pancarpal

**Radiolunate** — **preserved** until very end stage → **least likely** in natural progression

**Poor union predictors** — nonunion **>5 y**, **proximal pole necrosis**, heavy work, prior radial styloidectomy, immobilisation duration

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — ↓ grip & pinch, stiffness in **extension + radial deviation**

**Exam** — tender **radioscaphoid** joint; ↓ extension & radial deviation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + lateral wrist**

**Findings** — scaphoid nonunion + radioscaphoid arthritis progressing per stage; **capitolunate & lunate fossa preserved** until late

**Differential** — SLAC wrist, scaphoid nonunion without arthrosis

### SNAC classification

| Stage | Orthobullets (radiographic) | | OB7857 explanation |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Radial styloid + radial side of scaphoid** | ![SNAC I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12119-snac-wrist-stage-i.jpg) | Radial styloid |
| **II** | Stage I + **scaphocapitate** | ![SNAC II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12119-snac-wrist-stage-ii.jpg) | Radioscaphoid |
| **III** | **Periscaphoid** (proximal lunate & capitate may be kept) | ![SNAC III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12119-snac-wrist-stage-iii.jpg) | Midcarpal |

**Source conflict** — stage II = scaphocapitate (Orthobullets) · radioscaphoid (OB7857) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
SNAC wrist
├─ Frail / low demand → OBSERVATION
├─ Stage I → RADIAL STYLOIDECTOMY + scaphoid repair/SL stabilisation, or DISTAL POLE EXCISION
└─ Stage II–III → PRC / 4-CORNER FUSION / wrist arthrodesis
       ├─ capitate head arthritis → no PRC → 4-CORNER FUSION
       └─ capitate head intact → PRC or 4CF
```

**Union attempt** — only if **no arthrosis or stage I** (with styloidectomy); no arthroscopic debridement with arthrosis

### Salvage – four-corner fusion, PRC & distal pole excision (contraindications, RSC ligament)

| Procedure | Indication | Key facts / contraindications |
|---|---|---|
| **Distal pole excision** | Stage I (SNAC I–III not contraindications) | **Pre-existing DISI = contraindication** (↑ SL angle, destabilises proximal row); not for STT OA |
| **PRC** | Stage II–III | Needs intact **capitate head + lunate fossa**; **capitolunate/capitate head arthritis = contraindication**; ↓ motion & grip; **preserve RSC** |
| **Scaphoid excision + 4-corner fusion** (or capitolunate arthrodesis) | Stage II–III (incl. capitate arthritis) | Retains **60% motion, 80% grip** |
| Wrist arthrodesis | Stage II–III | Final salvage |

**RSC ligament** — most **volar-radial** ligament; radial styloid → carpus obliquely, radial to long & short RL ligaments → excision at PRC → **ulnar translocation of carpus**

![RSC ligament at PRC](../../MCQ/attachments/q211226-illa-rsc.jpg)

**62 y, stage III SNAC, failed splint/NSAIDs** → **scaphoid excision + 4-corner fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: debridement · ORIF · bone graft ± vascularised

## 6 Complications

**PRC + RSC excised** → **ulnar translocation**\
✗ Wrong options: capitate AVN (that is a contraindication, not complication) · DRUJ instability

**Distal pole excision** → ↑ SL angle / DISI, worsening arthritis in half (Malerich)

### Prognosis

**Punctate bleeding at surgery** — union **92%** obvious · **71%** questionable · **0%** none

## X vs Y

| | **PRC** | **4-corner fusion** |
|---|---|---|
| Needs capitate head cartilage | **Yes** | No |
| Stability depends on | **RSC ligament** | Fusion |
| Motion / grip | ↓ | **60% / 80%** |

## How it's asked

**"Untreated scaphoid nonunion — least likely arthritis?"** → **radiolunate**

**"Stage III SNAC, failed nonop?"** → **scaphoid excision + 4-corner fusion**

**"Motion-sparing salvage on capitate head; most volar-radial ligament excised — outcome?"** → **ulnar translocation of carpus**

**"Contraindication to distal pole excision?"** → **DISI**\
✗ Wrong options: SNAC I–III arthrosis

**"Arthritis between which bones contraindicates PRC?"** → **capitate & lunate (capitate head)**

**"Main blood supply of scaphoid?"** → **dorsal carpal branch of radial artery (retrograde, proximal 80%)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scapholunate Ligament Injury & DISI

*Hand · 82 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Acute** — **10–30%** of intra-articular DRF / carpal fractures (Orthobullets) · **30%** of DRF (OB)

**Degenerative** — tears in **>50% of >80-y-olds**; incomplete > complete

**Commonest carpal instability (TO)** — **SL instability / DISI** = commonest form of **CID** (carpal instability dissociative)

### Anatomy – dorsal SLIL is strongest

**SLIL** — **C-shaped**; **dorsal, proximal (membranous), volar** components

| SLIL component | Thickness | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal** | **3 mm** | **Strongest; greatest constraint to SL translation**; must tear for dissociation |
| Volar | 1 mm | 2nd |
| Proximal (fibrocartilaginous membrane) | — | **Minimal strength** |

**Yield strength** — **dorsal > volar > proximal**

**Intercalated segment (TO)** — scaphoid, lunate, triquetrum: **no tendon insertions**

**Balance** — scaphoid wants to **flex** (oblique, distal row pull, RSC); triquetrum + lunate want to **extend**; SLIL = tether

**Secondary stabilisers (TO)** — DRCL, **DIC**, **scaphotrapezial (STT)**, **RSC**, scaphocapitate

**SLIL alone cut** — no static change; needs ≥1 secondary stabiliser (TO)

## 2 Mechanism

**Injury** — FOOSH with **wrist extension + ulnar deviation + carpal supination**; also repetitive (crutch walking) (TO)

**Pathoanatomy** — **scaphoid flexes + lunate extends** → **DISI** → untreated → **SLAC**

### Secondary stabilisers & progression to static DISI

| Stage (TO) | Ligaments | X-ray |
|---|---|---|
| **Dynamic instability** | SLIL torn/incompetent; secondary stabilisers intact | **Normal static**; abnormal on **stress / clenched fist** |
| **Static (SL dissociation)** | SLIL + secondary stabiliser | Abnormal static → DISI |
| **SLAC** | Attritional changes | Progressive arthritis |

**Primary secondary stabiliser (TO)** — **STT ligament complex** (+ scaphocapitate) → failure = **dynamic → static DISI**\
✗ Wrong options: DIC · space of Poirier · volar radiolunate

**Static DISI = repair window passed (TO)** — reconstruction / capsulodesis / salvage

### Iatrogenic DISI after distal scaphoid excision

**Distal pole scaphoid excision (for STT OA)** — shortens scaphoid → triquetral extension moment dominates → **DISI** (SL angle 80°)

**Do not cause DISI** — STT fusion, trapezoid resection, LRTI, 4-corner fusion

| STT procedure | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Distal scaphoid excision** | **DISI**; **flexor** tendon rupture (sharp remnant) |
| STT fusion | **Radial styloid impingement ~33%**; adjacent OA radiocarpal 42%, TMC 28% |

**STT OA** — 2nd commonest carpal OA; linked to thumb CMC OA; options: distal pole excision, STT fusion, pyrocarbon

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **dorsoradial wrist pain**, ↑ with loading (**push-ups**), clicking, ↓ grip, giving way

**Tenderness** — snuffbox / **dorsal SL interval just distal (~1 cm) to Lister's tubercle**

**Watson (scaphoid shift) test** — thumb on **volar scaphoid tubercle**, wrist **ulnar → radial deviation** → proximal pole subluxes dorsally → **painful clunk** on release

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – clenched-fist views, scapholunate angle & DISI

**Next step, normal PA after FOOSH with dorsal tenderness** — **(bilateral) PA clenched-fist view** (capitate driven proximally)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy first · CT · EMG

**Not radial deviation PA** — **closes** SL gap

![Clenched-fist view widening SL gap](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6041-reivew-clenched-fist.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

| View / sign | Threshold / meaning |
|---|---|
| **Terry Thomas** (PA / clenched fist) | **SL gap >3 mm** |
| **Cortical ring sign** | Flexed scaphoid seen end-on |
| Scaphoid | Shortening; **humpback** = unstable scaphoid fracture / malunion |
| **SL angle (lateral)** | Normal **30–60°** (TO) · **30–70°** (OB); **>70° = DISI / SL dissociation**; static DISI **>60° (often >80°)** (TO module) |
| **Radiolunate angle** (TO) | Normal **15° ext – 15° flex**; **>15° extension → DISI** |
| **Capitolunate angle** | **>20°** (Orthobullets) · **>15°** (TO) |
| Carpal height ratio | ↓ as collapse progresses |
| **VISI** | SL angle **<30°** |

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| Arthrography | Screens; shows tear but **not size**; compare contralateral |
| MRI | Overused; **low sensitivity** |
| **Arthroscopy** | **Gold standard** |

![DISI: SL angle >70°](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6041-disi-lateral-moved.jpg)

![Terry Thomas + cortical ring sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6041-disi-ap-terry-thomas-sign-and-cortical-ring-moved.jpg)

### SLAC stages (TO)

| Stage | Arthritis |
|---|---|
| I | **Radial styloid–scaphoid** |
| II | Whole radioscaphoid |
| III | **Capitolunate (midcarpal)** |
| IV | Pancarpal incl. radiolunate |

**Radiolunate joint (TO)** — **preserved** in SLAC; SL joint itself spared until late (OB)

### Perilunate spectrum (rocking lunate, Mayfield I)

| Mayfield stage (TO) | Injury |
|---|---|
| **I** | **SL dissociation / scaphoid fracture / scaphoid rotation** (scaphoid fracture **not always** present) |
| II | Lunocapitate → perilunate dislocation |
| III | **LT disruption** / triquetral dislocation |
| IV | **Lunate dislocation** (extruded) |

![Mayfield stages](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141691-2.png)

**Rocking lunate (TO)** — **SL + LT ligament tears**

**SL tear (TO)** → ↑ SL angle (lunate follows triquetrum into extension) · **LT tear** → ↓ SL angle

## 5 Management

```
SLIL injury
├─ Acute undisplaced / chronic asymptomatic → NSAIDs, splint, repeat imaging
├─ Acute, reducible, repairable → OPEN REPAIR + K-wires ± dorsal capsulodesis
├─ Reducible, not repairable or >18 mo → RECONSTRUCTION (FCR / Brunelli)
└─ Fixed unreducible DISI or SLAC → SALVAGE
       ├─ SLAC I-II → PRC or scaphoid excision + 4-corner fusion
       ├─ SLAC III → 4-corner fusion
       └─ SLAC IV → total wrist arthrodesis
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute**, no malalignment, repairable | **SL ligament repair** |
| Chronic **reducible**, **<18 mo** (Orthobullets) · **<6 wk** acute (TO) | Repair |
| Acute non-repairable or reducible **>18 mo** | **Reconstruction** |
| Pathoanatomy = scaphoid fracture | ORIF / CRPP ± arthroscopy |
| **Rigid unreducible DISI** / severe DJD | **STT or SLC fusion** (SL fusion alone → **highest nonunion**) |

### Acute repair

**Acute SL dissociation (5 days)** — **open repair + percutaneous pinning**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · arthroscopic pinning · capsulodesis (for chronic missed)

**Technique** — incision distal to radial styloid (protect **radial sensory n.**); 2 parallel K-wires in scaphoid → **joystick scaphoid into extension/supination/ulnar dev, lunate into flexion/radial dev** → drive into lunate; suture anchors

**Augment** — **Blatt / Berger dorsal capsulodesis** (DIC advancement); alone for chronic when repair not feasible

**Post-op** — K-wires out **8–10 wk**; no heavy labour 4–6 mo

**Casting alone** — insufficient for most; may suit incomplete tears

### Reconstruction

**Options** — **FCR transfer (modified Brunelli, direct reduction)**, ECRB tenodesis (indirect), **3LT**, SLAM, **RASL** (screw, sacrifices motion), bone-ligament-bone graft; **avoid weave** (late failure)

**Salvage (TO)** — **4-corner fusion** (keeps ~50% flex-ext; preserves radiolunate) · **PRC** (needs intact **capitate head + lunate fossa**)

## 6 Complications

**List** — **SLAC progression**, arthritis, post-op pain, stiffness, fatigue, ↓ grip

## X vs Y

| | **DISI** | **VISI** |
|---|---|---|
| Ligament | **SLIL (dorsal)** | **LTIL** + dorsal radiotriquetral ± volar radiolunate |
| Lunate | **Extended** | **Flexed** (triquetrum extends) |
| SL angle | **>70°** | **<30°** |
| Pain | Dorsoradial | **Ulnar**, ↑ pronation + ulnar deviation |
| Tests | **Watson** | **LT shuck / Reagan ballottement, Kleinman shear** |
| Normal variant? | **Always pathological** | Can occur with **ligamentous laxity** |
| Frequency | Commoner | Less common; LT arthrodesis nonunion 20–40% |

| Carpal translation | Ligament |
|---|---|
| **Ulnar translation** of carpus | **RSC** (radiocarpal dislocation, PRC) |
| **Volar translation** | **Short radiolunate** (volar-ulnar critical corner) |
| Lunotriquetral fusion | Commonest **carpal coalition** |

## How it's asked

**"True about SL dissociation EXCEPT"** → **SL angle 45° in advanced cases** (TO)

**"FOOSH, SL gap 3.7 mm, radiolunate 25° extension — Dx?"** → **SL instability with DISI** (TO)

**"SL dissociation, all true EXCEPT"** → **VISI** (TO) (✓ DISI, Terry Thomas, cortical ring)

**"Watson +, PA normal — best view?"** → **PA clenched fist**\
✗ Wrong options: radial deviation · carpal tunnel view

**"6 d after rollerblading, dorsal tenderness, normal X-rays — next?"** → **bilateral PA clenched-fist X-ray**

**"Radiographic finding of DISI?"** → **lunate extended relative to scaphoid**\
✗ Wrong options: SL angle <30°

**"Untreated SL injury — first carpal deformity?"** → **scaphoid flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: lunate flexion · humpback

**"SL angle 80° — which ligament?"** → **dorsal scapholunate**

**"72-y SL gap after MVA — ligament?"** → **dorsal SL interosseous**

**"Acute SL dissociation, footballer, 5 d?"** → **open repair + percutaneous pinning**

**"DISI 2 y after STT surgery — index operation?"** → **distal scaphoid resection**

**"Counsel before distal pole scaphoid excision?"** → **DISI**

**"Secondary stabiliser whose failure → static DISI?"** → **STT ligament complex** (TO)

**"Mayfield stage I true?"** → **scaphoid fracture not always present** (TO)

**"Rocking lunate results from?"** → **SL + LT ligament tear** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Seymour Fracture

*Hand · 83 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition** — **displaced distal phalanx physeal fracture + nailbed injury** in **children** (TO) = **open fracture**

**Not** — adult thumb hyperextension sports injury (TO)

**Physeal involvement** — 20–30% of paediatric phalangeal fractures

**Commonest digit** — **middle finger** (TO)

| Fracture types (TO) | Nailbed injury types |
|---|---|
| **SH I** | Nailbed laceration |
| **SH II** | **Nail plate subluxation** (superficial to eponychial fold) |
| **Juxta-epiphyseal metaphyseal**, 1–2 mm distal to physis | **Interposed germinal matrix** at fracture |

### Anatomy – why it flexes

**Extensor** — inserts **dorsal epiphysis** (with volar plate) → extension force on epiphysis

**FDP** — inserts **volar metaphysis** → flexion force on shaft

**Result** — **flexion moment at physis** → **apparent mallet deformity** (TO)

## 2 Mechanism

**Direct trauma / crush** — **finger caught in door** (TO), hammer, heavy object, ball, sport

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — apparent **mallet deformity**; swelling, ecchymosis, subungual haematoma; **nail plate superficial to eponychial fold**

**Delayed** — tender swollen DIP, infection

![Seymour fracture: flexion at physis, nail plate displaced](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6000-smimages.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — PA (**may look normal**) + **lateral** (flexion at fracture)

**Signs** — **widened physis** (→ **interposed tissue**) or epiphysis–metaphysis displacement

**vs mallet fracture** — paediatric mallet = **SH III/IV** avulsion, line **enters DIPJ**; Seymour line **traverses physis**, not DIPJ

## 5 Management

```
Seymour fracture
├─ Closed, minimally displaced, no interposition → CLOSED REDUCTION (hyperflexion) + SPLINT
├─ Closed, displaced/unstable → CLOSED REDUCTION + PIN ACROSS DIPJ
│      └─ reduction difficult → assume interposition → OPEN
└─ Open (nail plate above eponychial fold) → I&D + REMOVE INTERPOSED TISSUE + REDUCE + NAILBED REPAIR ± K-wire
```

### Anatomy & management – nail bed repair, remove interposed tissue, reduce

**Open Seymour** — **nail plate removal + nailbed repair + removal of interposed soft tissue (germinal matrix) + open reduction**

**Must include** — **removal of interposed soft tissue** (pinning optional adjunct)

**Pinning (conflict)** — open reduction + **retrograde K-wire across fracture + DIPJ** (Orthobullets, Abzug) · pinning **not required**; nail plate replaced under fold = sufficient stability (OB)

**Avoid DIPJ-sparing pin** — poor purchase in epiphysis

**Avoid closed reduction + pin** in interposition → locks germinal matrix in fracture

**Avoid mallet splint in situ / buddy taping** — growth & nail disturbance

**Nailbed** — repair every nailbed injury; absorbable (6-0) suture or Dermabond

**Post-op** — extension splint **4–6 wk**; IV Abx → short oral course

**Open vs closed** — **open management fewer complications**; Abzug: surgery for open **and** closed

**ED management** — 89% managed in ED; OR mainly for failed closed reduction (Lin)

**Antibiotics** — **within 24 h → infection 6.9%** vs **>24 h → 76.5%** (Rask; overall 27.3%); **clindamycin** for delayed (bone penetration)

**Delayed treatment** — infection 0% (acute appropriate) · 15% (partial) · **45% (delayed)** (Reyes & Ho)

![Seymour SH II lateral](../../MCQ/attachments/q5656-a-fig-a.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Complication – osteomyelitis

**Highest risk, delayed presentation** — **osteomyelitis**\
✗ Wrong options: growth arrest · septic arthritis · extension deformity

**List** — **nail dystrophy**, **growth disturbance / physeal arrest**, secondary displacement, **chronic osteomyelitis** (not treated as open), **flexion deformity**

**Nail growth disturbance** — 11/24 despite full motion in 23/24 (Krusche-Mandl)

## X vs Y

| | **Seymour fracture** | Paediatric mallet fracture |
|---|---|---|
| Fracture | **SH I/II or juxta-epiphyseal** | **SH III/IV** |
| DIPJ involvement | No | Yes |
| Nailbed | **Injured (open)** | Usually intact |
| Key treatment | **Debride, remove matrix, repair nailbed** | Splint |

## How it's asked

**"True of Seymour fracture EXCEPT"** → **adult thumb hyperextension in sports** (TO)

**"12-y crushed middle finger, mallet posture, subungual haematoma — next?"** → **nail plate removal, nailbed repair, remove interposed tissue, open reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: closed pin · mallet splint

**"Surgery must include?"** → **removal of interposed soft tissue**\
✗ Wrong options: pinning

**"Anatomic cause of deformity?"** → **extensor on dorsal epiphysis, flexor on volar metaphysis**

**"Delayed Seymour, highest-risk complication?"** → **osteomyelitis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Skin Grafting

*Hand · 84 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **avascular** dermal tissue covering a **well-perfused** bed; usually autologous

**Nutrition** — diffusion from wound bed (STSG & FTSG)

**Goals** — cover deep structures · bacterial barrier · restore function · prevent contracture

**Indication** — well-perfused bed over **muscle or subcutaneous tissue**

**Contraindication** — exposed **bone, tendon, nerve, vessel** → flap

**Palmar vs dorsal skin** — palm: **no hair or sebaceous glands**, **Meissner + Vater-Pacini** corpuscles; dorsum = like rest of body → STSG adequate

## 5 Management

### STSG vs FTSG

| | **STSG** | **FTSG** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | **Dorsal hand**; beds where contraction won't limit motion | **Volar hand & fingertips** |
| Donor | **Anterolateral thigh** | **Proximal forearm, medial upper arm, hypothenar eminence** |
| Content | Keratinocytes + variable dermis | **Full epidermis + dermis**, hair follicles, sweat glands; **no subcutaneous fat** |
| Contracture | More (thicker = less) | **Less** |
| Sensation / durability | Less | **Better reinnervation, more shear-resistant** |

**STSG thickness** — thin **0.005–0.012 in** · intermediate **0.012–0.018** · thick **0.018–0.030**

**Meshed STSG** — ↑ surface area, ↓ haematoma & infection → **better take**

**Defat FTSG** — fat = barrier to vascular ingrowth → graft failure

**FTSG technique** — gentle tension, multiple **tie-over sutures** (↓ shear), medicated gauze + moist cotton, dressing **5–7 d**

**FTSG cons** — haematoma/seroma → failure; revascularisation **2–3 d**

![FTSG](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6064-full-thickness-skin-graft.jpg)

### Palmar defect coverage

**Palm defect (post-resection)** — **flap + full-thickness skin coverage** (e.g. posterior interosseous artery island flap)

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — VAC alone) · wet-to-dry alone · STSG alone · flap + meshed STSG

![Palmar defect after mass resection](../../MCQ/attachments/q109-a-palm-cancer-met.jpg)

### Other grafts

**Allograft** — **temporary** bed preparation for autograft

**Xenograft** — **biologic dressing**

## How it's asked

**"Palmar soft-tissue defect after mass excision — coverage?"** → **flap with full-thickness skin**\
✗ Wrong options: meshed STSG

**"Graft for dorsal hand"** → **STSG**

**"Why remove fat from FTSG?"** → **barrier to vascularisation**

**"Meshing advantage"** → **↓ haematoma/infection, better take**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Snapping Extensor Carpi Ulnaris (ECU)

*Hand · 85 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Sports** — **tennis** (backhand), **golf** (club strikes ground), rugby; overuse (e.g. suturing)

### Anatomy – 6th compartment & subsheath

| Compartment | Contents | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | APL, EPB | De Quervain |
| **2** | ECRL, ECRB | **Intersection syndrome** (1st/2nd crossing) |
| 3 | EPL | — |
| 4 | EIP, EDC | — |
| **5** | EDM | **Vaughan-Jackson** |
| **6** | **ECU** | **Snapping ECU** |

**ECU** — inserts **base of 5th MC**; innervated by **PIN**

**ECU subsheath** — fibro-osseous tunnel, **part of TFCC**, most critical to ECU stability

**Kinematics** — pronation: straight course · **supination: exits 6th compartment at ~30°** → max tension on subsheath

**Subluxation position** — **supination + wrist flexion + ulnar deviation**

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathology** — **attenuation or rupture of ECU subsheath** → subluxation → snapping → **ECU tendonitis**

| Lesion | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| **Attenuation** | Sheath intact but stripped ulnar/palmar → **false pouch** |
| **Ulnar-sided tear** | Subluxes in supination, **reduces in pronation** |
| **Radial-sided tear** | Subluxes in supination, lies **on top of torn subsheath** in pronation |

![Ulnar vs radial subsheath tears](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6030-ulnar-sided-tears-top-result-in-dislocation-of-the-tendon-in.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — dorsoulnar wrist **pain + snapping/popping**

**Exam** — painful snap with **flexion + supination**; **reduces with pronation**

**Negatives** — no foveal pain with ulnar compression (TFCC) · no DRUJ instability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – snapping with supination/flexion & examination tests

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (click with pathognomonic motion)

| Test | Positive in |
|---|---|
| **Flexion + supination → painful snap** | **Snapping ECU** |
| **ECU synergy test** (elbow 90°, full supination, wrist neutral, fingers extended → resisted **thumb + middle finger abduction**) | **ECU tendonitis** |
| Ulnar → radial deviation + scaphoid pressure | Watson (SL) |
| Ulnar soft spot tenderness (styloid–FCU) | TFCC |
| Axial load + max ulnar deviation | Ulnar styloid impingement / ulnocarpal abutment |
| Piano key | DRUJ instability |
| Finkelstein / Eichhoff | De Quervain |
| Resisted wrist extension, elbow extended, pronated | Lateral epicondylitis |

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | **Unremarkable** |
| **Ultrasound** | **Dynamic** assessment of ECU stability |
| MRI | Tendonitis (**thickening + high T2 intrasubstance signal**), degenerative tears, TFCC pathology |

![ECU position in pronation vs supination](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6030-ecu-during-pronation-left-straight-coures-and-supination-rig.jpg)

**Differential** — TFCC tear · hook of hamate fracture · ulnar styloid fracture · ulnar styloid impaction · ulnar tunnel · pisotriquetral arthritis

## 5 Management

```
Snapping ECU
├─ First line → SPLINT / LONG ARM CAST: PRONATION + EXTENSION + RADIAL DEVIATION
└─ Fails nonop → SUBSHEATH SURGERY ± WRIST ARTHROSCOPY
       ├─ Acute → DIRECT SUBSHEATH REPAIR
       └─ Chronic → EXTENSOR RETINACULUM FLAP RECONSTRUCTION
```

### Nonoperative immobilisation position

**Position** — **pronation, slight extension, radial deviation** → minimal ECU angulation vs ulna

**Avoid supination / ulnar deviation** — lower threshold for subluxation

**Arthroscopy** — **concurrent TFCC tears in 50%**

## X vs Y

| | Snapping ECU | TFCC tear | Ulnar styloid impaction | LT tear |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Key feature | **Snap on supination**, reduces in pronation | **Deep foveal pain**, worse with ulnar compression | Pain with pronation, + UV | VISI, LT shuck pain |
| Snap/pop | **Yes** | No | No | No |

## How it's asked

**"Golfer, ulnar pain + snap pronation → supination, normal X-ray, no foveal pain?"** → **ECU subluxation**\
✗ Wrong options: TFCC · ulnar abutment

**"Tennis backhand pop, dorsoulnar tenderness?"** → **ECU subluxation**\
✗ Wrong options: intersection (radial side)

**"Exam finding in subluxing ECU?"** → **pain with flexion + supination**

**"MRI ECU thickening + T2 signal — positive test?"** → **ECU synergy test**

**"Immobilisation position?"** → **pronation, extension, radial deviation**

**"Chronic reconstruction?"** → **extensor retinaculum flap**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Swan Neck Deformity

*Hand · 86 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **PIP hyperextension + DIP flexion** ("double curve") from PIP force imbalance

**Incidence** — up to **50% of RA** patients

### Pathomechanics – triangular ligament & lateral band tenotomy

**Primary lesion** — **lax volar plate** → PIP hyperextension; causes: trauma, generalised laxity, RA (chronic PIP synovitis)

**Secondary lesion** — **PIP extension force > flexion force**

| Cause | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Mallet finger** | DIP extension force transferred to PIP |
| **FDS rupture / laceration** | Lost dynamic check-rein → unopposed PIP extension + volar plate failure |
| **Intrinsic contracture** | Lateral bands tethered (transverse retinacular lig.) → extension force goes to PIP, not terminal tendon |
| **MCP volar subluxation** | RA |
| **Cerebral palsy** | Extrinsic extensor overpull ± intrinsic spasticity |

**Cascade (TO)** — PIP hyperextends → **lateral bands sublux dorsally** (above axis) → ↑ extensor lever arm → self-perpetuating; extensor "used up" at PIP → slack at DIP + FDP pull → **DIP flexion**

**Triangular ligament** — dorsal, base of middle phalanx just distal to central slip; **keeps lateral bands dorsal** → **contracture/tightness → swan neck**\
✗ Wrong options: boutonniere: lateral bands volar

**Not swan neck causes** — volar plate **contracture**, sagittal band insufficiency (extensor subluxation at MCP)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **snapping and locking** of fingers

**Exam** — resting PIP hyperextension + DIP flexion

**Bunnell-Littler (intrinsic tightness) test (TO)** — PIP flexion **more restricted with MCP extended** than flexed → **intrinsic tightness**\
✗ Wrong options: capsule contracture · volar plate laxity · extrinsic adhesions

![Swan neck deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6013-clinical-photo-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP + lateral hand; PIP joint destruction defines Nalebuff IV

### Nalebuff classification (TO)

| Type | Finding | Treatment principle |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | PIP flexible in all positions | Restore check-rein: **FDS tenodesis** (most widely used), dermodesis, **SORL / ORL reconstruction** (Brooks-Graner), volar plate repair, lateral band translocation |
| **II** | PIP flexion limited with **MCP extended** (+ Bunnell-Littler) | **Intrinsic release + type I procedure** (e.g. **FDS tenodesis + intrinsic release**) |
| **III** | PIP flexion limited in all MCP positions; **joint space preserved** | **MUA** (aim 80–90° flexion) → dorsal skin release / Z-plasty → **lateral band mobilisation**; check flexor gliding; then type I procedure ± intrinsic release |
| **IV** | Stiff PIP + **radiographic destruction** | **Arthrodesis** or **arthroplasty** (+ soft-tissue balancing) |

## 5 Management

```
Swan neck
├─ Flexible, early → double ring splint (block PIP hyperextension)
└─ Progressive → volar plate advancement + PIP balancing
       ├─ FDS rupture → FDS tenodesis
       ├─ extrinsic overpull (CP) → central slip tenotomy (Fowler)
       └─ intrinsic tightness → intrinsic release
```

### Operative procedures

**Nonop** — **double ring splint** prevents PIP hyperextension

**Volar plate advancement** — addresses laxity

**FDS tenodesis / sling (TO)** — volar zig-zag; window either side of **A3**; one FDS slip cut **~1.5 cm** from insertion, distal attachment intact; PIP flexed **20–30°**; fixed to proximal phalanx (anchor) or **distal edge of A2** → static tether

**ORL reconstruction (TO)** — ulnar lateral band or tendon graft; **SORL (Brooks-Graner)** → dynamic PIP + DIP stability

**Volar plate repair (TO)** — when volar plate disruption was initial injury; anchors/drill holes base of P2

**Intrinsic release (Littler) (TO)** — dorsoulnar incision; excise **triangular aponeurosis / oblique fibres** + lateral band at distal half of PIP; preserve **central slip, lateral slips, transverse fibres**

**Central slip tenotomy (Fowler)** — CP swan neck from **extrinsic extensor overpull** (structure C)\
✗ Wrong options: terminal tendon A · lateral band B · lateral slip D · lumbrical E

**CP alternatives** — intrinsic lengthening for intrinsic spasticity; lateral band rerouted volar to PIP axis (Van Heest & House)

**Type IV (TO)** — **arthrodesis** most predictable: **40–50° ulnar digits**, less for index; **silastic arthroplasty** preferred ring & small (motion > pinch)

![Extensor anatomy: C = central slip](../../MCQ/attachments/q211908-illa-extrinsic-tendon-anatomy.jpg)

### Thumb swan neck (Nalebuff type III thumb) (TO)

**Sequence (TO)** — **CMC subluxation/adduction** → compensatory **MCP hyperextension** for pinch

**Treatment (TO)** — fix foundation first: **CMC arthroplasty (LRTI)** → then MCP capsulodesis or fusion

## X vs Y

| | **Swan neck** | **Boutonniere** |
|---|---|---|
| PIP | Hyperextension | Flexion |
| DIP | Flexion | Hyperextension |
| Lateral bands | **Dorsal** | **Volar** |
| Key structure | Lax volar plate; tight **triangular ligament** | Central slip rupture; attenuated triangular ligament |
| Trauma origin | Mallet, FDS rupture | Central slip injury |

## How it's asked

**"Best procedure, type II swan neck?"** → **FDS tenodesis + intrinsic release** (TO)

**"PIP flexion worse with MCP extended?"** → **intrinsic tightness** (TO)

**"Triangular ligament tightness causes?"** → **swan neck**\
✗ Wrong options: boutonniere, sagittal band, volar plate contracture

**"CP swan neck, failed splint — tenotomy of?"** → **central slip (C)**

**"Type IV (destroyed PIP)?"** → **arthrodesis / arthroplasty** (TO)

**"Thumb swan neck — address first?"** → **CMC (LRTI)** (TO)

**"Initial treatment?"** → **double ring splint**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Syndactyly

*Hand · 87 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – most common web

**Definition** — **most common congenital limb malformation**; adjacent digits webbed (Greek *syn* = together, *dactylos* = digit) (TO)

**Incidence** — **1 in 2,000–2,500**; ♂ > ♀; Caucasians > African Americans

| Web | % |
|---|---|
| **Long–ring** | **50** (most common (TO)) |
| Ring–small | 30 |
| Index–long | 15 |
| Thumb–index | 5 |

### Genes & associations

**Pathophysiology** — **failure of apoptosis** to separate digits

**Genetics** — pure syndactyly **AD**, reduced penetrance, variable expression; **family history 10–40%**

**Associations** — **acrosyndactyly** (constriction ring / Streeter dysplasia) · **Poland** · **Apert** · **Carpenter** (acrocephalopolysyndactyly)

**Acrosyndactyly** — digits fused **distally**, proximal **fenestrations**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

### Classification (simple / complex / complicated)

| Type | Definition | Image |
|---|---|---|
