| **Simple** | **Soft tissue only**, no bony connection | ![Simple](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-illustration-of-types.jpg) |
| **Complex** | **Side-to-side fusion of adjacent phalanges** | ![Complex X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-xr-complex.jpg) |
| **Complicated** | **Accessory phalanges / abnormal bones** in fusion | ![Complicated](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-complicated-syndactyly.jpg) |
| Complete | Skin to **fingertips** | ![Complete](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-complete-syndactyly.jpg) |
| Incomplete | Skin short of fingertips | — |

### Temtamy & McKusick (TO)

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Most common**: long–ring (hand) / 2nd–3rd toes; AD, incomplete penetrance |
| II | **Synpolydactyly**: long–ring and/or 4th–5th toes + accessory digit in web |
| III | Ring–small |
| IV | Complete syndactyly **all fingers** |
| V | **Metacarpal/metatarsal fusion** ring–small / 3rd–4th toes |

## 5 Management

```
Syndactyly
├─ Acrosyndactyly → release in NEONATAL period
├─ Border digits (ring–small, thumb–index) / multiple fusions → release ~6 mo
│      (unequal lengths → tethering, angulation, rotation, shortening)
├─ Central (long–ring) → ~1 y / 12–18 mo (Orthobullets: up to 2 y)
└─ Simple toe syndactyly, no functional issue → nonop
```

### Timing & technique

**Timing — conflict** — **~1 y** (Orthobullets) · **12–18 mo**; delay **>18 mo ↓ scar contracture & web creep** (TO)

**Multiple digits** — **two stages, one side of a digit at a time** → protect digital artery

**Sequence** — release **digits of unequal length first** (border); middle–ring **6 mo later**; all **before school age**

**Bilateral** — simultaneous if **<18 mo**; staged if >18 mo

**Goals (TO)** — new **paronychial nail folds** · interdigital commissure skin · **new web space**

**Incisions** — **interdigitating zigzag flaps** (Cronin, dorsal + volar) → avoid longitudinal scar · **dorsal fasciocutaneous flap** for web · modified double reverse Z-plasty (TO)

**Sutures** — **absorbable (5-0 chromic catgut)**

**Grafts** — **FTSG preferred, less contracture** (TO) · FTSG ↑ web creep, hyperpigmentation, hair; STSG ↑ flexion/extension lag & graft breakdown (Deunk, no preference)

![Zigzag flaps](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-zigzag-technique.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Complication – web creep

**Web creep** — **most common** (8–60%) = **distal migration of the web commissure**; warn parents pre-op; regular follow-up

**Early creep** — **necrosis of tip of dorsal quadrilateral flap** + loss of FTSG in web

**Late creep** — adolescence; **discrepant growth** of scar/graft vs surrounding tissue

**Treatment** — reconstruct web with **local skin flaps**

| Other complication (TO) | Fact |
|---|---|
| Infection | Rare, **1.6%** |
| Scar contracture | STSG, bone growth |
| Devascularisation / digital nerve injury | Operate **one side at a time** |
| **Nail deformity** | — |

![Web creep](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6076-web-creep.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"18-mo, middle–ring release — likely complication"** → **web creep** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: nail deformity, infection, contracture

**"Most common complication involves"** → **web commissure**

**"Most common finger involvement"** → **long–ring** (TO)

**"Release border digits when?"** → **~6 mo**

**"Acrosyndactyly release when?"** → **neonatal period**

**"Bony side-to-side fusion"** → **complex**

**"Early web creep cause"** → **dorsal flap tip necrosis / FTSG loss**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tendon Transfer Principles

*Hand · 88 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Principles of transfer

**Strength** — force ∝ **cross-sectional area** (max at resting length); amplitude ∝ **muscle length**; work = force × amplitude

**Grade loss** — **lose 1 MRC grade** after transfer → transfer **grade 5** donor (foot drop: 4/5 acceptable → ≥3/5 antigravity)

**Donor** — **expendable**, similar excursion + power

**One tendon → one function**

**Synergistic** — rehab easier (finger flexion + wrist extension; wrist flexion + finger extension)

**Line of pull** — **straight** (avoids tethering, late failure); pulley or tenodesis adjusts excursion

**In-phase vs out-of-phase** — in-phase best, but **out-of-phase acceptable** (often the only donor) — not a contraindication

| Smith 3-5-7 rule | Excursion |
|---|---|
| Wrist flexors / extensors | **3 cm** |
| **EDC, FPL, EPL** | **5 cm** |
| **FDS, FDP** | **7 cm** |

**Selection** — missing function → available units → options

**Brachial plexus priorities** — elbow flexion (musculocutaneous) → shoulder stabilisation (suprascapular) → brachiothoracic pinch (pectoral) → sensation C6–7 (lateral cord) → wrist extension + finger flexion

### Prerequisites – supple joints

**Precludes transfer** — **target joint cannot be passively corrected to neutral** (static contracture / bony deformity) → release first

**Not contraindications** — full PROM · motion only in direction of correction · out-of-phase donor · no pulley

**Nonop (therapy, splints, antispasticity)** — ↓ PROM, spasticity

## 3 Presentation

### Nerve palsy patterns

| Palsy | Low | High |
|---|---|---|
| **Radial** (vs PIN origin) | PIN syndrome (wrist extension spared via ECRL) | + triceps, BR, **ECRL** lost |
| **Median** (vs AIN origin) | **Loss of opposition (APB)** | + **FPL, FDP index/middle, FDS, PL** lost |
| **Ulnar** | Power pinch ↓, **Wartenberg**, **clawing marked** | + **FDP ring/small lost** (key deficit) → **less clawing** |

**Brachial plexus avulsion signs** — **Horner's (C8–T1)**, appears 2–3 d · severe pain in anaesthetic limb · **loss of rhomboids**

**Studies** — sensory/motor evoked potentials > standard EMG/NCS

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Bouvier test (claw hand)

**Technique** — wrist neutral, **block MCP hyperextension** → can PIP extend?

| Result | Meaning | Transfer insertion |
|---|---|---|
| **Positive** (PIP extends) | **Simple claw**, central slip intact | MCP flexion only → **A1/A2 pulley or proximal phalanx** |
| **Negative** (PIP doesn't extend) | **Complex claw** | MCP flexion + PIP extension → **lateral bands** |

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — PIP 90° + rigid DIP = **Elson** (central slip)) · PIP flexes with MCP extended not flexed = **extrinsic tightness** · wrist tenodesis = tendon injury · thumb IP flexion on paper = **Froment**

## 5 Management

### Timing

| Timing | Indications |
|---|---|
| **Early (3 wk–3 mo)** | Total/near-total plexus injury, high energy |
| **Late (3–6 mo)** | Partial upper plexus, low energy |

**Post-op** — protect **3–4 wk** → ROM; protective splint 3–6 wk

### Specific transfers

| Goal | Donor | Recipient |
|---|---|---|
| **Musculocutaneous** — elbow flexion | Pec major, latissimus | Biceps |
| Elbow flexion | Common flexor mass | Proximal humerus (**Steindler flexorplasty**) |
| **Radial** — elbow extension | Deltoid, latissimus, biceps | Triceps |
| **Wrist extension** | **Pronator teres** | **ECRB** |
| Finger extension | **FDS, FCR or FCU** | EDC |
| Thumb extension | **PL or FDS** | EPL |
| **Low median** — opposition | **FDS ring** (FCU pulley, **Bunnell**) | Base P1 / APB |
| | **EIP** (around ulnar wrist) | APB |
| **High median** — thumb IP flexion | **BR** | FPL |
| Index/middle flexion | FDP ring/small (ulnar) | FDP index/middle (side-to-side) |
| **Ulnar** — thumb adduction | FDS or ECRB | Adductor pollicis |
| Index abduction | APL, ECRL, EIP | 1st DI |
| Anti-claw | FDS, ECRL (**volar to transverse MC ligament**) | Lateral bands ulnar digits |

### Radial nerve/PIN palsy transfers

**Most consistent radial palsy transfer** — **PT → ECRB**\
✗ Wrong options: **BR → ECRB**: BR is radial-innervated

**PL** — not always present; FDS → APL/EPB unnecessary if EPL rerouted

**PIN transected, no recovery at 1 y** — **FCR → EDC + PL → EPL**\
✗ Wrong options: sural graft at 1 y · wrist fusion + FCU · PT → wrist extensors (wrist extension preserved by ECRL)

### Median/ulnar palsy transfers – opponensplasty & claw correction

| Opposition transfer | Note |
|---|---|
| **Ring/long FDS** (Bunnell, FCU pulley) | Low median; not high median (FDS denervated) |
| **EIP → APB** | **High median** (radial-innervated) ✓; also after median laceration |
| Camitz **PL** | not high median (denervated); **no pronation/MC flexion** → not true opposition |
| Huber **ADM** | Ulnar-innervated |

![Bunnell opponensplasty](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6065-bunnell-opponensplasty-illustration.jpg)

![EIP opponensplasty](../../MCQ/attachments/q4738-illa-illus-a-eip.jpg)

**High median from supracondylar spur + ligament of Struthers** — decompress + FPL/FDP-I + **EIP → APB**\
✗ Wrong options: FDS · Camitz · neurotisation = pre-ganglionic plexus · CMC fusion

**Low median laceration (4 cm above wrist) after repair** — sensation usually returns; thenar motor unpredictable → **EIP opponensplasty at 6 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: FDP tenolysis · CTR · EDM → 1st DI (ulnar intact; neurotisation = distal AIN → median motor) · ulnar

**Burkhalter** — MCP flexor replacement for intrinsic loss → ↑ grip, ↓ clawing

### Tensioning EIP-to-EPL transfer

**EPL rupture after DRF** — EIP → EPL (synergistic)

**Best tensioning** — **wide-awake local anaesthesia, functional testing** (max extension without losing flexion)\
✗ Wrong options: maximum tension · tenodesis estimate

## 6 Complications

**Adhesions** — aggressive therapy ± **tenolysis**

**Prognosis** — **age = leading factor** (worse >30 y); **distal > proximal** lesions

## X vs Y

| | **Low ulnar palsy** | **High ulnar palsy** |
|---|---|---|
| FDP ring/small | Intact | **Lost** |
| Clawing | **More** | **Less** (ulnar paradox) |

| | **Low median** | **High median** |
|---|---|---|
| Deficit | Opposition | Opposition + thumb IP + index/middle flexion |
| Opposition donor | **FDS ring** or EIP | **EIP** (FDS, PL denervated) |

## How it's asked

**"Precludes tendon transfer?"** → **joint not passively correctable to neutral**\
✗ Wrong options: out-of-phase donor · no pulley

**"PIN transected, no function at 1 y?"** → **FCR → EDC + PL → EPL**

**"Most consistent radial palsy transfer?"** → **PT → ECRB**\
✗ Wrong options: BR → ECRB

**"High median palsy (Struthers) — restore opposition?"** → **EIP → APB**\
✗ Wrong options: ring FDS · Camitz

**"Median laceration 4 cm proximal to wrist — secondary op at 6 mo?"** → **EIP opponensplasty**

**"Ulnar claw — test to choose insertion site?"** → **Bouvier: block MCP extension, PIP fails to extend**\
✗ Wrong options: Elson · Froment

**"Tension EIP → EPL?"** → **awake under local, functional testing**

**"Excursion of FDS/FDP?"** → **7 cm**

**"Strength after transfer?"** → **loses one grade**

**"Leading prognostic factor?"** → **age (worse >30)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Thromboangiitis Obliterans (Buerger's disease)

*Hand · 89 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition** — **non-atherosclerotic, segmental, inflammatory** disease of **small + medium vessels** of hands and feet

**Demographics** — 12.6/100,000 (US); **♂:♀ 3:1**; **<45 y**

**Risk factors** — **smoking**, chewing tobacco

**Pattern** — **distal vessels first**, proximal last

## 2 Mechanism

| Phase | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Acute** | Thrombus with **neutrophils + giant cells** occludes lumen, **wall spared** |
| Subacute | Progressive thrombus organisation |
| Chronic | Inflammation gone; organised thrombus + vascular fibrosis |

## 3 Presentation

**Early** — **intermittent claudication** (feet, legs, hands, arms), numbness/tingling

**Late** — critical ischaemia → **rest pain**; frequently **painful**\
✗ Wrong options: painless

**Exam** — ulcers, **necrotic distal digits**, large erythematous superficial vessels, cold hands/feet, ↓ pulses, sensory findings up to 70%

**Allen test** — **positive in young smoker with digital ischaemia** → suggestive

![Necrotic digits](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6099-necrotic-fingers.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Arteriography** — **study of choice** if diagnosis uncertain; excludes mimics

**Signs** — **"corkscrew" vessels** (diagnostic in right setting) · collaterals → **"spider leg"**

![Corkscrew arteriogram](../../MCQ/attachments/q4486-illa-arteriogram.jpg)

**Echocardiogram** — exclude **proximal embolic source**

**Labs** — exclude alternative diagnoses (autoimmune/vascular mimics)

## 5 Management

```
Buerger's disease
├─ All tobacco users → smoking cessation + symptomatic care
├─ Refractory pain / digital ischaemia → sympathectomy (controversial)
└─ Gangrene / non-healing ulcer / refractory pain → amputation
```

**Smoking cessation** — **only treatment that ↓ amputation risk** (education, pharmacotherapy, groups)

**Symptomatic** — avoid cold, gentle exercise, daily **aspirin**, vasodilators\
✗ Wrong options: shown to ↓ amputation or halt progression

**Sympathectomy** — cut nerves to affected area; controversial

**No prophylactic amputation** of unaffected digits — not described

**Prognosis** — **quit → 94% avoid amputation**; continue → **43% amputation within 8 y**

## How it's asked

**"45-y male smoker, digital ulcers — true statement?"** → **arteriography useful for diagnosis**\
✗ Wrong options: aspirin ↓ amputation · prophylactic amputation · painless · proximal first

**"Only treatment that reduces amputation?"** → **smoking cessation**

**"Arteriogram sign?"** → **corkscrew vessels**

**"Acute-phase histology?"** → **occlusive thrombus, vessel wall spared**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Thumb CMC Dislocation

*Hand · 90 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **<1% of hand injuries**; **dorsal (dorsoradial) ≫ volar**

### Stabilisers – dorsoradial ligament & anterior oblique ligament

**Ligaments** — 16 total; **7 TMCJ stabilisers** (sAOL, dAOL, DRL, POL, UCL, IML, DIML) + 9 trapezial

**4 key restraints** — **AOL**, POL, IML, **DRL**

| Ligament | Fact asked |
|---|---|
| **Dorsoradial (DRL)** | **Primary stabiliser vs dorsal/dorsoradial subluxation** in all positions; strongest & thickest; "check rein"; taut first; joint stays reduced if all others cut; **torn in dorsal dislocation**; lax in stage IV OA |
| Superficial AOL | does not stabilise in flexion or prevent dorsal subluxation; laxity allows pronation |
| **Deep AOL (beak)** | Volar trapezial tubercle → volar beak of MC; **pivot for pronation**; taut only in **hitchhiker** position; **stripped/peeled off MC base but continuous** in dislocation; stays on volar fragment in Bennett/Rolando |
| Posterior oblique (POL) | Purely dorsal, ulnar to DRL; not primary |
| Intermetacarpal / dorsal IML | **Restrains radial translation** of 1st MC |
| Trapeziotrapezoid | Stabilises TT joint, not CMC |

**Historical vs current** — AOL once thought primary; **DRL now accepted primary** (biomechanical studies)

![Thumb CMC ligaments labelled](../../MCQ/attachments/q217861-illa-thum-label.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest** — **axial force on flexed thumb**

**Less common** — dorsal force into 1st web (motorcycle handlebar)

**Pathoanatomy** — **DRL torn**; **AOL stripped from MC base, remains continuous**

## 3 Presentation

**Findings** — thenar pain, swelling, bruising; cannot make a fist

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray PA + lateral | Joint-space widening, **dorsoradial shift of MC** |
| **Roberts view** | Base-of-thumb fractures |
| MRI | Persistent/recurrent instability; plan reconstruction |

![Dorsoradial CMC dislocation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob10119-fig-a1.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Thumb CMC dislocation
├─ Stable after reduction (AOL intact) → CLOSED REDUCTION + SPICA IN EXTENSION + PRONATION
├─ Incongruent / unstable → CRPP ± open capsulorrhaphy
└─ Grossly unstable (AOL possibly torn) → LIGAMENT RECONSTRUCTION (TENDON AUTOGRAFT) + TEMPORARY PINS
```

### Ligament reconstruction with FCR

**Recommended for gross instability** — reconstruction + pinning → **better abduction & pinch** than CRPP

**Structure reconstructed** — **Orthobullets**: dorsal capsuloligamentous complex · **OB (Eaton-Littler, FCR graft)**: **volar oblique ligament** — read the options

**Eaton-Littler** — FCR graft; better results in **non-arthritic thumbs**; APL is used but is not the structure reconstructed

## 6 Complications

**List** — anterior osteophyte often visible · **low recurrence**

## X vs Y

| | CMC dislocation | Bennett / Rolando fracture |
|---|---|---|
| AOL | **Peeled off MC base, continuous** | **Attached to volar fragment** |
| DRL | **Torn** | — |
| MC translation | Ligamentous | Through fracture |

## How it's asked

**"Dorsal or volar? Which ligament torn?"** → **dorsal; DRL torn (AOL intact, peeled off)**

**"Stabiliser in opposition besides AOL?"** → **dorsoradial ligament**

**"Incongruent after reduction — primary restraint to dorsal subluxation?"** → **dorsoradial ligament**

**"Primary stabiliser vs dorsoradial subluxation?"** → **DRL**\
✗ Wrong options: volar oblique (old teaching) · dorsal IML (radial translation)

**"Eaton-Littler FCR reconstruction rebuilds?"** → **volar oblique ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: APL

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Thumb Collateral Ligament Injury

*Hand · 91 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**UCL vs RCL** — **UCL 10× more common**; UCL = **86%** of athletic thumb injuries; most common injury at thumb MCP (TO)

**Skier's thumb** — **acute** (ski pole); most common UE skiing injury (7–32%)

**Gamekeeper's thumb** — **chronic** attenuation from repetitive valgus (Scottish gamekeepers breaking rabbits' necks) (TO)

### UCL anatomy – proper vs accessory ligament & testing positions

| Ligament | Course | Taut / restrains radial stress in |
|---|---|---|
| **Proper UCL (pUCL)** | **Dorsal third of MC head** → **volar P1** | **Flexion** (+ dorsal capsule) |
| **Accessory UCL (aUCL)** | Palmar to pUCL → **volar plate** | **Extension** (+ volar plate) |

![Proper vs accessory UCL](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141908-1.png)

**Static stabilisers** — bony shape (flat MC condyles), collaterals, volar plate, dorsal capsule

**Dynamic stabilisers** — **adductor pollicis** (inserts P1, **superficial to UCL**, ulnar n.); EPL, EPB, FPL, APB, FPB

**RCL side** — covered by **abductor aponeurosis** → Stener-like RCL lesion rare; RCL tear → **subluxation** rather than overt instability

## 2 Mechanism

**UCL** — **radially directed force → hyperabduction / extension** = **valgus** (ski pole, FOOSH)

**RCL** — **axial load** (adduction)

**Site of UCL rupture** — **distal (P1 base)** in most; bony avulsion **20–30%** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: **avulsion from MC head** (TO)

**RCL rupture site** — evenly proximal / midsubstance / distal

### Stener lesion – adductor aponeurosis

**Stener lesion** — completely torn UCL (pUCL + aUCL) retracts **proximal + superficial to adductor aponeurosis** → interposed aponeurosis **blocks reduction & healing** → **surgery**

**Blocking structure** — **adductor pollicis aponeurosis** (**ulnar n.**)\
✗ Wrong options: opponens, FPB, APB (median thenars) · volar plate · FPL · lumbricals · anterior oblique ligament

**Incidence** — **65–85%** of complete ruptures (OB) · **64–87%** of grade III (TO)

**Second bony fragment** — may mark distal UCL end → Stener lesion

![Stener lesion](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141908-2.png)

| ✗ Distractor | Where it blocks |
|---|---|
| Volar plate, sesamoids, FPL | Thumb MCP **dorsal dislocation** |
| Lumbricals | Finger MCP (2–5) dorsal dislocation |
| Anterior oblique (beak) ligament | Holds **Bennett** fragment; CMC arthritis/instability |

## 3 Presentation

**UCL** — **ulnar MCP pain** worse with **pinch/grasp**; tender **distally**; **palpable ulnar mass = Stener**; haematoma

**RCL** — **radial MCP pain**; tender **proximally**; presents late (**>10 wk**) vs UCL (2–10 wk)

### Stress examination

**Technique** — X-ray **first** (don't displace avulsion); stress in **extension and 30° flexion**; prevent P1 rotation; ± local anaesthetic

| Laxity | Injured |
|---|---|
| **30° flexion only** | **Isolated proper UCL** (torn off **P1**) — partial |
| **Extension (and flexion)** | **Proper + accessory UCL ± volar plate** — complete |

**RCL** — anterior/posterior drawer → volar subluxation, no endpoint

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — PA, lateral, oblique; **avulsion at ulnar base of P1** / condylar fx; **sag sign** (P1 **supination**, UCL) · P1 **pronation** (RCL) · **volar P1 subluxation** on lateral (dorsal capsule/extensor injury)

**Stress views** — controversial; only after bony avulsion excluded

**MRI** — equivocal exam; **gold standard**, **100% sensitivity + specificity** for tear/Stener (TO)

**US** — operator-dependent; **76–88% sens, 81–83% spec**, 81% accuracy

![Stener lesion MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6040-626c6c7fa32a771f1ff764297c8745-big-gallery.jpg)

### UCL/RCL instability grading

| Grade | Findings | Tear |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sprain, no instability | Incomplete |
| 2 | Asymmetric laxity, **endpoint present** | Incomplete |
| **3** | **No endpoint**; **30–35° opening** or **10–15° > contralateral** | **Complete** |

**Complete tear thresholds** — **>35° opening and/or >15° vs other side** (TO) · 30–35° (Orthobullets) · criteria range 15–45°; many use **>35°** (Newland, OB) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
Thumb UCL injury
├─ Grade 1–2 / <15° side difference → immobilise 4–6 wk (spica)
├─ Grade 3 / Stener / displaced fragment → acute repair
│      └─ ± adductor advancement
└─ Chronic (>3–8 wk) / poor tissue → tendon graft reconstruction
       └─ failed / arthritic → MCP fusion 15° flexion
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative (4–6 wk; TO partial: spica 4 wk) | Operative repair |
|---|---|
| **Partial tear** (unstable in flexion only) | **Complete tear** (unstable flexion + extension) |
| **<15° side-to-side** difference | **>15° side-to-side** |
| Bony skier's thumb, **stable + undisplaced** fragment | **>30–35° opening** |
| | **Stener lesion** |
| | Fragment **displaced >1 mm**, **malrotated**, or **>⅓ joint surface** (TO) |

**Not a surgical indication (TO)** — **ipsilateral metacarpal fracture**

**Reconstruction** — chronic **>3 wk** (TO) / **>3–8 wk** (Orthobullets); **PL graft** through bone tunnels (screw, button, anchor); 92% satisfaction

**Adductor advancement** — with acute repair (100% RTS) or chronic salvage

**MCP fusion** — salvage; **15° flexion**

### Repair technique

**UCL** — S/chevron incision; transosseous sutures / anchors; immobilise **IP free**; strength 4–6 wk; >90% excellent; RTP ~100%

**RCL** — straight radial incision; may resect **abductor aponeurosis**; spare **dorsal radial sensory branches**; anchors/pull-out; ± K-wire; **96% good–excellent**

### RCL vs UCL – delayed repair

**RCL** — **more amenable to primary repair even with delayed presentation**

| | **UCL** | **RCL** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **10×** | 1× |
| Mechanism | **Abduction moment** | Axial load / adduction |
| Rupture site | **Distal** | Even proximal/mid/distal |
| Overlying aponeurosis | **Adductor** → Stener; may need removal | Abductor → Stener rare |
| Presentation | 2–10 wk | **>10 wk** |
| Delayed repair | Reconstruct if chronic | **Still repairable** |
| X-ray | P1 supination (sag) | P1 pronation |

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Stiffness** (MCP, IP) | **Most common** after repair |
| Persistent instability | **15%** of grade 3 treated nonop → reconstruct |
| **Skin necrosis** | Pull-out suture technique |
| Superficial radial neurapraxia | Observe |
| ↓ pinch strength | |

## How it's asked

**"Role of proper vs accessory UCL?"** → **proper: radial stress in flexion; accessory: radial stress in extension**

**"Skier, laxity in 30° flexion but not extension — MRI?"** → **proper UCL torn off proximal phalanx**

**"Skier's thumb — all EXCEPT"** → **avulsion from 1st MC head** (TO; it's P1 base)

**"Not an indication for surgery in gamekeeper's thumb?"** → **ipsilateral metacarpal fracture** (TO)

**"Structure blocking UCL reduction — muscle & nerve?"** → **adductor pollicis, ulnar nerve**

**"Lettered figure — what blocks reduction?"** → **adductor aponeurosis (E)**

**"Palpable ulnar nodule, UCL retracted on MRI — blocking structure?"** → **adductor aponeurosis**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior oblique ligament · volar plate · FPL · lumbricals

**"RCL vs UCL — true of RCL?"** → **more amenable to delayed repair**\
✗ Wrong options: adductor removal · abduction moment · distal rupture · presents <2 wk

**"Most common complication after repair?"** → **stiffness**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Thumb Hypoplasia

*Hand · 92 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **1/100,000** live births; ♂ = ♀

**Laterality** — **bilateral ~60%**; right > left

**Association** — often partial/complete **absence of radius**

**Embryology** — AER, progress zone, **FGF** signalling abnormalities

### Associated syndromes & lab markers

**Associated anomalies** — **>80%**: **VACTERL**, **Holt-Oram**, **TAR**, **Fanconi anaemia**, Cornelia de Lange, Okihiro; radial aplasia, thrombocytopenia, renal/cardiac/CNS

**Work-up** — cardiac (auscultation, **echo**), renal + abdominal **US**

**Labs** — **FBC + peripheral smear** → Fanconi

**Chromosomal challenge (breakage) test** — **detects Fanconi anaemia before marrow failure**

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — thenar hypoplasia, absent skin creases (muscle/tendon anomaly), excessive MCP abduction, **UCL laxity**, **web-space tightness**

**Pollex abductus** — **FPL** attaches to normal insertion **and extensor tendon**; frequent in **type IIIA**

**Also** — variable EPL insertion

![Thenar hypoplasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-example-of-thenar-hypoplasia.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **bilateral hand, wrist and forearm** films → musculotendinous vs osseous deficiency

### Blauth classification

| Type | Description | Treatment | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | **Minor**: all components present, small | None (opponensplasty if ↓ abduction) | ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-type-1.jpg) |
| II | All bones present; **MCP UCL instability**, thenar hypoplasia, **adduction contracture** | MCP stabilisation + 1st web release + opponensplasty | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-thumb-hypoplasia-2.jpg) |
| **IIIA** | Musculotendinous + osseous deficiency; proximal ⅓ MC aplasia, **CMC intact**; no active MCP/IP motion | **Reconstruct** (as II) | ![Type IIIA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-thumb-hypoplasia-3.jpg) |
| **IIIB** | Basal (proximal ⅔) MC aplasia, **deficient CMC**; no active MCP/IP motion | **Amputate + pollicise** | |
| IV | **Floating thumb** (skin + NV pedicle only) | Amputate + pollicise | ![Type IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-thumb-hypoplasia-4.jpg) |
| V | **Absent thumb** | Pollicise | ![Type V](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-thumb-hypoplasia-5.jpg) |

**IIIA vs IIIB** — **CMC joint stability**\
✗ Wrong options: osseous completeness · musculotendinous integrity · MCP stability · EPL (all deficient in both)

## 5 Management

```
Hypoplastic thumb — CMC joint stable?
├─ Yes (I, II, IIIA) → reconstruct
│      ├─ I, adequate abduction → observe
│      ├─ I, ↓ abduction → opponensplasty
│      └─ II / IIIA → web release + opponensplasty + MCP stabilisation
└─ No (IIIB, IV, V) → thumb amputation + index pollicisation
```

### Reconstruction vs pollicisation – CMC joint stability

**Deciding factor** — **CMC stability** (resists grasp/pinch; full circumduction arc)\
✗ Wrong options: contralateral thumb · thenar function · EPL · MCP stability · web contracture · absent IP joint

**Antiposition** — abducted, slightly extended, pronated

| Procedure | Technique |
|---|---|
| **Opponensplasty** | **FDS** or **ADM** |
| 1st web deepening | **Z-plasty** |
| MCP stabilisation | **Fusion** · UCL reconstruction with **FDS** · UCL with free graft |
| **Pollicisation** | Index on NV bundles; detach 1st dorsal + palmar interossei; shorten by removing **index MC + physis**; stabilise index MCP; rebalance tendons; plan skin to avoid grafts |

**9-month-old, basal MC aplasia, deficient CMC, no MCP/IP motion** → **pollicisation**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · opposition transfer alone · web release + transfer + MCP fusion · UCL reconstruction

![Post-operative pollicisation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6083-post-operative-pollicization.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Most important variable, reconstruct vs ablate?"** → **CMC joint stability**

**"Key difference Blauth IIIA vs IIIB?"** → **CMC joint stability**

**"Adopted 3-year-old with hypoplastic thumb — deciding factor?"** → **CMC stability**

**"Deficient CMC, absent MCP/IP motion — definitive treatment?"** → **pollicisation**

**"Detect Fanconi before marrow failure?"** → **chromosomal breakage (challenge) test**

**"Pollex abductus?"** → **FPL inserts normally + into extensor; type IIIA**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Triangular Fibrocartilage Complex (TFCC) Injury

*Hand · 93 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy – components, foveal insertion & radioulnar ligaments

**Function (TO)** — transmits **~20% of wrist load**; **main stabiliser of DRUJ**; cushion between ulnar head and ulnar carpus

| TFCC components | Not components |
|---|---|
| Dorsal + volar **radioulnar ligaments** (superficial + deep limbs) | **Extensor digiti minimi (EDM) sheath** (TO) |
| Central **articular disc** | LT interosseous ligament |
| **Meniscus homologue** | RSC ligament |
| **Ulnar collateral ligament** | FCU sheath |
| **ECU subsheath** (6th compartment) | |
| Origin of **ulnolunate + ulnotriquetral** ligaments | |

![TFCC components](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6047-shows-elements-of-tfcc.jpg)

**Radioulnar ligaments** — **primary restraint to dorsal/palmar translation of radius on (fixed) ulna**; span sigmoid notch margins ↔ ulna

**Deep fibres** — **ligamentum subcruentum → ulnar fovea** (key DRUJ stabiliser; repair these) · **superficial → ulnar styloid**

**Articular disc** — load bearing, almost no stabilising role

**UL / UT / ECU subsheath** — stabilise carpus to ulna; minimal DRUJ stability

**Volar RUL injury** → **dorsal subluxation of ulna** · dorsal RUL injury → volar subluxation of ulna

**Secondary DRUJ stabiliser** — dorsal oblique band of IOM

**Blood supply** — periphery vascular (10–40%); **central disc avascular**

### Concepts of TFCC structure

| Concept | Author |
|---|---|
| 2-D structure | **Palmer & Werner** (1981) |
| **"Hammock" / suspension** (3 zones: foveal, distal hammock, UCL) | **Nakamura** |
| **"Iceberg"** (proximal foveal pc-TFCC = submerged, functional; distal dc-TFCC = visible tip, shock absorber) | **Atzei & Luchetti** (TO) |

**Iceberg (TO)** — pc-TFCC not seen through radiocarpal portals → indirect **hook sign** / DRUJ portals; pc-TFCC tear → DRUJ instability, ↓ rotation, ↓ grip, high reoperation

## 2 Mechanism

**Most common mechanism** — **fall on extended wrist + forearm pronation**\
✗ Wrong options: supination · flexion · axial load ulnar deviation

**Type 1** — traumatic; traction on ulnar wrist

**Type 2** — degenerative; **positive ulnar variance**, ulnocarpal impaction; central tears with repetitive activity

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – mechanism & examination

**Symptoms** — ulnar-sided wrist pain, clicking, painful **turning a door key**

**Fovea sign** — tender **soft spot between ulnar styloid and FCU**, volar ulnar head → pisiform; **95% sensitive, 87% specific** for foveal / UT injury

**Provocation** — pain with **ulnar deviation** (compression) or radial deviation (tension), resisted ulnar deviation

**Piano key sign (TO)** — ballotable distal ulna = **DRUJ instability**: **TFCC tear, Galeazzi (nonunion), Madelung**; **not isolated ulnar styloid fracture**

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — ECU subluxation (snap in supination + flexion + ulnar deviation; ECU tenderness)) · hook of hamate (CT / carpal tunnel view) · ulnar styloid fracture (X-ray) · Guyon's (interossei weakness, ulnar numbness)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — usually **normal**; **zero-rotation PA → ulnar variance**; pronated grip PA (dynamic); widened DRUJ / dorsal ulna on true lateral if unstable

**Arthrography** — extravasation (largely replaced by MRI)

**MRI / MR arthrogram** — **sensitivity 74–100%**; lunate ulnar-side tear = ulnocarpal impaction

**Arthroscopy** — **gold standard / most accurate**; after several months failed splinting

![MRI: TFCC tear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6047-tfcc-mri-2.jpg)

### Palmer classification

| Class 1 – Traumatic | Lesion |
|---|---|
| **1A** | **Central perforation** |
| **1B** | **Ulnar avulsion** (± styloid fx; Orthobullets: without) |
| 1C | Distal avulsion (UL/UT origin) |
| 1D | Radial avulsion |

| Class 2 – Degenerative | Lesion |
|---|---|
| 2A | Wear / thinning |
| 2B | 2A + lunate and/or ulnar chondromalacia |
| 2C | 2B + perforation |
| 2D | 2C + ligament (LT) disruption |
| 2E | 2D + ulnocarpal + DRUJ arthritis |

![Palmer classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q219370-illa-palmer-classification-tfcc.jpg)

**Defect adjacent to ulna, stable DRUJ (TO)** — **Class 1B**

**Need for surgery ↑ (TO)** — associated fractures, instability, DRUJ injury; most isolated tears no early surgery

**Differentials** — ulnocarpal abutment, ulnar styloid impaction, ECU tendonitis, hook of hamate fracture, ulnar tunnel, pisotriquetral OA

## 5 Management

```
TFCC injury
├─ All acute Type 1 → immobilise + NSAIDs ± steroid (first line)
│      ├─ fails, 1A central → arthroscopic debridement
│      └─ fails, 1B–1D peripheral → arthroscopic repair
└─ Type 2 → nonop first
       ├─ ulnar + variance >2 mm / LT instability → ulnar shortening osteotomy
       ├─ ulnar + variance <2 mm, 2A–C → wafer
       └─ 2D → limited ulnar head resection
```

### Treatment – immobilisation, debridement & wafer procedure with ulnar-positive variance

**Acute 1B, DRUJ stable, 5 days** — **immobilisation + NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy yet · repair · shortening · wafer

**Debridement** — 1A; 3-4 + 6R portals; **keep 2 mm peripheral rim**; 80% pain relief; **not effective with ulnar-positive variance**

**Repair** — 1B–1D (peripheral, vascular); acute athletic best; outside-in / inside-out / transosseous; immobilise **6 wk**; acute (<3 mo) → **80% motion + grip**; risk ECU tendonitis (knot), dorsal sensory nerve

**DRUJ (Mulford)** — mild acute → immobilise 4–6 wk in stable position; complex reducible + styloid fx → ORIF; without fx → TFCC repair + pin DRUJ

**Ulnar diaphyseal shortening** — variance **>2 mm**, **LT instability** (tightens ulnocarpal ligaments); plate; nonunion, hardware removal

**Wafer** — removes **2–4 mm** distal ulnar head (open or arthroscopic through central defect); ulnar cortex intact, not into DRUJ; no plate

**Wafer threshold** — variance **<2 mm** (Orthobullets) · **2 mm positive variance + ulnocarpal impingement** = indication with debridement (OB) — read the options

**Darrach** — **contraindicated** (ulnar stump instability, ECU subluxation); salvage only

## 6 Complications

**Repair** — ECU tendonitis from knot, dorsal ulnar sensory nerve injury

**Ulnar shortening** — nonunion, hardware irritation

**Positive variance** — worse debridement/repair outcomes

## X vs Y

| | **Ulnar shortening osteotomy** | **Wafer** |
|---|---|---|
| Variance | **>2 mm** | **<2 mm** (2A–C) |
| Fixation | Plate | None |
| Extra benefit | Tightens UL/UT → **LT instability** | Arthroscopic via central tear |
| Complications | Nonunion, hardware | Technically difficult |

## How it's asked

**"Components of TFCC EXCEPT?"** → **EDM sheath** (TO)

**"Anatomical component of TFCC?"** → **ECU tendon sheath**\
✗ Wrong options: LTIL · RSC · FCU sheath

**"Main restraint to dorsal/palmar radius translation?"** → **dorsal + palmar radioulnar ligaments**\
✗ Wrong options: disc

**"Deep radioulnar fibres insert on?"** → **ulnar fovea**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar styloid = superficial

**"Deep-fibre injury → dorsal ulnar subluxation — which structure?"** → **volar radioulnar ligament**

**"Fall on pronated extended wrist, tender distal to ulnar styloid, no ECU tenderness?"** → **TFCC tear**

**"Normal X-ray + CT, foveal tenderness, no snapping?"** → **TFCC tear**\
✗ Wrong options: ECU subluxation · hamate

**"Most common mechanism?"** → **wrist extension + forearm pronation**

**"Indication to add wafer to debridement?"** → **2 mm positive ulnar variance + ulnocarpal impingement**

**"Acute 1B, stable DRUJ, 5 days?"** → **immobilization with NSAIDs**

**"Defect adjacent to ulna, stable DRUJ — Palmer?"** → **Class 1B** (TO)

**"Iceberg concept given by?"** → **Atzei & Luchetti** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Palmer & Werner · Nakamura

**"Piano key sign NOT seen in?"** → **ulnar styloid fracture** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: TFCC tear · Galeazzi · Madelung

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Trigger Finger

*Hand · 94 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2–3%** general population · **10% of diabetics**

**Who** — **♀ >50 y**, **diabetics** (bilateral, multiple digits)

**Adult digits** — **ring & long** most common

**Associations** — RA, calcific tendinitis, septic tenosynovitis, **CTS (>60%** of trigger digits have clinical/EDS CTS), congenital trigger thumb · DM, amyloidosis, hypothyroidism, sarcoidosis, gout, pseudogout

### Pathology – A1 pulley

**Site** — **stenosing tenosynovitis at A1 pulley** (overlies MCP) → tendon–pulley size mismatch\
✗ Wrong options: Grayson's, ORL, TCL, extensor

**Histology** — **fibrocartilaginous metaplasia** of tendon/pulley; chondrocyte proliferation; **↑ type III collagen**; hyperglycaemia → collagen cross-links

**Nodule** — on **FDP** (FDS often unaffected)

**Trigger thumb** — **Av (variable annular) pulley** causes stenosis in up to **75%**

![A1 pulley](../../MCQ/attachments/q1050-illa-triggerfinger300.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — progressive pain at A1 (distal palm), clicking, catching, **locking in flexion at PIP**; referred dorsal MCP/PIP pain

**Exam** — tender A1, **palpable nodule**, triggering on flexion/extension

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; X-ray not needed

### Green classification

| Grade | Feature |
|---|---|
| I | Palm pain, tenderness at A1 |
| II | **Catching** |
| III | **Locking, passively correctable** |
| IV | **Fixed locked digit** |

**Differentials** — **lumbrical plus** (paradoxical extension on flexion) · joint contracture (can't passively extend) · **pyogenic flexor tenosynovitis** (Kanavel, ↑ markers) · **MCP locking** (collateral/volar plate caught on MC head osteophyte)

## 5 Management

```
Adult trigger finger
├─ First line → STEROID INJECTION (1–3; series of 2 before surgery) ± splint, NSAIDs
└─ Failed nonop → A1 PULLEY RELEASE (open standard / percutaneous)
       └─ persistent triggering → RELEASE ULNAR FDS SLIP ± A0 / A3
```

### Nonoperative

**Splint** — immobilise **MCP or DIP only**; PIP free; relief 40–97%

**Injection** — **best initial treatment**; relief **60–90%** (up to 90% non-diabetics); in/superficial to sheath

**Diabetics** — less relief (Orthobullets classic) · recent literature: success not influenced by DM — read options; surgery cost-effective in DM

**Poorer response** — longer symptom duration

**Injection complications** — fat atrophy, **tendon rupture**, **transient hyperglycaemia** (diabetics)

### Infection risk – timing after steroid injection

**Rule** — injection **within 90 days of surgery → ↑ postoperative infection**

**Last injection 2 mo ago** → schedule surgery in **1.5 mo** (>90 days)

### Operative

| | Open release | Percutaneous release |
|---|---|---|
| Status | **Standard**; easier to confirm release | 18–19 G needle, bevel parallel to tendon |
| Success | >90% | >90% (↑ with **US**) |
| RTW | — | Earlier |
| Avoid in | — | **Thumb & little finger** (nerve proximity) |

**Recurrence** — ↑ in **diabetics**

**WALANT / local** — allows intraoperative check of triggering; post-op early active ROM 4×/day

**Extra releases** — **A0** (aponeurosis proximal to A1) · one/both FDS limbs · A3

**RA** — **FDS slip excision without A1 release** → spares A1 → does not worsen **ulnar drift**

**Ulnar FDS slip** — resected to help prevent MCP ulnar drift

### Percutaneous thumb release – radial digital nerve risk

**Most at risk** — **radial digital nerve of thumb** (superficial, **oblique** course across A1)

**Distance** — mean **2.9 mm** from release point; within 2 mm in 5/10 hands (Bain) · 2.7 mm (Orthobullets pulley data)

**Little finger** — variable nerve crossing → also avoid percutaneous

### Paediatric trigger finger/thumb – additional releases & digital nerve risk

**Trigger thumb** — **10× more common** than trigger finger in children; A1 constriction; **IP flexion contracture**; **>60% resolve spontaneously** (Baek); surgery → **radial digital nerve** crosses field

**Paediatric trigger finger** — **Notta's node proximal to A1**, flexion contracture, triggering; causes: proximal **FDS decussation**, FDS/FDP nodules, thick A2, **tight A3**

**A1 release alone** — often fails (6/18, Cardon) → **release one/both FDS limbs** ± A3 (Bae: all → A1 + single FDS slip resection)

**Timing** — operate **2–4 y** (prevent IP contracture)

**Revision (persistent triggering)** — **extensile incision**; order per OB answer: **A3 release → then 1 FDS slip** · per OB explanation: ulnar FDS slip → then A3 / remaining slip

**Avoid in children** — complete **A2 release** (bowstringing) · **FDP debulking** (adhesions) · horizontal incision (no prox/distal exposure)

![Paediatric trigger thumb](../../MCQ/attachments/q3479-a-peddie-trigger.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Radial digital nerve injury** — **thumb release** (superficial, oblique)

**Bowstringing** — **A2/A4 damage** → pulley reconstruction

**Others** — stiffness, scar tenderness, dehiscence, haematoma, **tendon scoring** (percutaneous), infection

## How it's asked

**"Diabetic, ring finger locking, distal palm tender — structure?"** → **A1 pulley**

**"Child — release in addition to A1"** → **one or both limbs of sublimis (FDS)**\
✗ Wrong options: A4

**"Paediatric trigger thumb surgery — at risk"** → **radial digital nerve**

**"Persistent paediatric triggering after A1 release"** → **extensile incision, A3 release, then 1 FDS slip**\
✗ Wrong options: A2 release · FDP debulking

**"Percutaneous thumb release — consent for"** → **radial digital nerve of thumb**

**"Injected 2 mo ago — when to operate?"** → **in 1.5 mo (>90 days)**

**"RA trigger finger"** → **FDS slip excision, preserve A1**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ulnar Styloid Impaction Syndrome

*Hand · 95 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathoanatomy

**Definition** — **impaction of ulnar styloid tip on triquetrum** → ulnar-sided wrist pain; **long ulnar styloid** or **ulna-positive** wrist

**Demographics** — common; **Asians > Whites** (more positive variance)

**Causes of ulnar positivity** — normal variant · trauma / **radial malunion** · congenitally short radius · **premature radial physeal closure** · **Madelung deformity** (volar-ulnar distal radial physis dyschondrosis)

**Ulnar impaction (OB)** — degenerative; excess ulnocarpal load → TFCC degeneration → chondromalacia lunate/triquetrum/ulnar head → **LT instability** → ulnocarpal & DRUJ OA; mostly ulna +ve, may occur neutral/negative

### Anatomy – ulnocarpal load

**Ulnocarpal load** — **~20%** of wrist load

**Pronation & grip** — **↑ ulnar variance** & load

### Ulnar variance

| Variance | Ulna − radius | Radius load | Ulna load | Image |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Neutral | 0 (<1 mm) | **80%** | **20%** | ![Neutral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6049-neutral-moved.jpg) |
| **Positive** | **+2.5 mm** | 60% | **40%** | ![Positive](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6049-positive-moved.jpg) |
| Negative | −2.5 mm | 95% | 5% | ![Negative](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6049-negative-moved.jpg) |

**+2.5 mm ulnar load — conflict** — **40%** (Orthobullets table) · **up to 50%** (Orthobullets anatomy text)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — ulnar wrist pain, **worse with pronation / grip**

**Exam** — swelling, tenderness **ulnar styloid / TFCC**, painful ↓ ROM

**Ulnar stress test** — **max ulnar deviation + axial load + supination → pronation** → reproduces pain

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View / test | Fact |
|---|---|
| **PA wrist** | **Positive ulnar variance** + **subchondral sclerosis / cysts** ulnar styloid ± triquetrum |
| **Styloid process index** | (ulnar styloid length − ulnar variance) ÷ ulnar head width; **<0.22 normal** |
| **Pronated grip PA** | **Dynamic** ulnar variance |
| Contralateral views | Comparison |
| MRI | **TFCC** and **LTIL** |

### Diagnosis & failed treatment – positive ulnar variance, styloid impaction

**Failed OT + steroid + isolated TFCC debridement** — cause = **unaddressed positive ulnar variance**\
✗ Wrong options: inadequate debridement · SL tear · scaphoid fracture

**TFCC debridement alone** — **90%** improve & return to work (when variance not the problem)

**Differentials** — TFCC injury · **ulnocarpal abutment** · DRUJ injury · pisotriquetral OA · ECU tendinitis · LT tear

## 5 Management

```
Ulnar styloid / ulnar impaction
├─ First line → rest, NSAIDs, splint, steroid injection ≥6–12 wk
│      (may be definitive in non-athletes; athletes often recur → finish season)
└─ Refractory → ulnar shortening osteotomy (gold standard)
       ├─ positivity >2 mm → diaphyseal USO
       └─ positivity <2 mm → wafer resection (open / arthroscopic), aim 2–3 mm ulnar minus
```

### Ulnar shortening osteotomy – nonunion risk

**USO** — **gold standard**; preferred in athletes after failed cast + PT

**Wafer (partial ulnar head/styloid resection)** — <2 mm positivity; encouraging early results, **not proven superior**; post-op immobilisation ~6 wk

**USO nonunion** — **4–8%**; **~6% in smokers**

| Factor | Nonunion effect |
|---|---|
| **Smoking** | **↑ (≈6%)** |
| **Low BMD** | **↑** |
| Transverse vs oblique | **No difference** (4.16% vs 3.86%) |
| Volar vs ulnar plate | **No difference** |
| BMI >30 | **No association** |

## 6 Complications

**USO complications** — **nonunion** · tendon rupture · **persistent pain / hardware irritation** · infection

## How it's asked

**"Failed OT, injection, TFCC debridement — why still pain?"** → **positive ulnar variance** (needs USO)

**"Martial artist, failed cast + PT, USO recommended — pathology"** → **ulnar impaction** (MRI: ulnar lunate/triquetral changes)

**"USO — true statement"** → **nonunion ~6% in smokers**\
✗ Wrong options: oblique worse · BMI · plate position · BMD irrelevant

**"Gold-standard operation"** → **ulnar shortening osteotomy**

**"Load through ulna in neutral variance"** → **20%**

**"View for dynamic variance"** → **pronated grip PA**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ulnar Tunnel Syndrome

*Hand · 96 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **less common than cubital tunnel**

**Risk** — **cyclists → "handlebar palsy"**; manual workers

### Anatomy – Guyon's canal

**Length** — **~4 cm**; proximal TCL edge → **aponeurotic arch of hypothenar muscles**

**Contents** — ulnar nerve + artery; nerve **bifurcates** into superficial sensory + deep motor

| Boundary | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Floor** | **Transverse carpal ligament**, hypothenar muscles |
| **Roof** | **Volar carpal ligament** |
| Ulnar (medial) | **Pisiform**, pisohamate ligament, abductor digiti minimi |
| Radial (lateral) | **Hook of hamate** |

**Deep motor branch** — **all interossei**, **lumbricals 3 & 4** (Orthobullets) · "4th & 5th lumbricals" (TO, as printed) · hypothenars · **adductor pollicis** · **deep head FPB**

**Dorsal ulnar cutaneous branch** — arises **~5 cm proximal** to ulnar styloid / Guyon's canal → **dorsal ulnar hand spared** in ulnar tunnel

**Innervated proximal to Guyon (spared)** — **FCU**, **FDP ring/little**; ECU = PIN; FPL = AIN

![Guyon's canal zones](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6022-zones.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Causes – ganglion & imaging

**Commonest** — **ganglion cyst = 80% of non-traumatic cases** (often from **pisohamate joint**)

**Others** — lipoma · repetitive trauma · **ulnar artery thrombosis/aneurysm (hypothenar hammer)** · **hook of hamate fracture/nonunion** · pisiform dislocation · inflammatory arthritis/RA · anomalous muscle (ADM), fibrous bands · palmaris brevis hypertrophy · idiopathic

**Ulnar artery occlusion (TO)** — may cause **Raynaud in ulnar 3 digits** (sympathetic fibres travel with artery)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain/paraesthesia **volar ulnar 1½ digits**; weak intrinsics, grip, pinch

**Spared** — **dorsal ulnar hand sensation** · **FDP ring/little** strength · median territory

| Sign | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Claw ring + little** | Loss of intrinsics (MCP flexion, IP extension) |
| **Froment** (book test) | Weak **adductor pollicis** → compensatory **thumb IP flexion by FPL** (AIN); thumb + index IPJ flexion when pinching paper |
| **Jeanne** | Thumb **MCP hyperextension** (EPL) with IP flexion |
| **Wartenberg sign** | **Abducted little finger** — weak **3rd palmar interosseous** (ulnar) + unopposed **EDQ/EDM** (PIN) |
| Weak pinch | Loss of thumb adduction → **up to 70% pinch lost** |
| Weak grasp | Loss of MCP flexion |
| Allen test | **Ulnar artery thrombosis** |
| Difficulty crossing index/middle | Interossei weakness |

**Wartenberg distractors** — EDQ (unopposed, not weak) · ADM weakness → adducted · dorsal interossei → adducted · **Wartenberg syndrome** = superficial radial nerve (cheiralgia paraesthetica)

![Froment sign](../../MCQ/attachments/q3225-a-froments-pic.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Guyon's canal zones & motor vs sensory findings

| Zone | Location | Typical cause | Deficit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Proximal to bifurcation** | **Ganglion, hook of hamate fracture** | **Mixed motor + sensory** |
| **2** | **Deep motor branch** (radial) | **Ganglion, hook of hamate fracture** | **Motor only**; **most commonly affected (TO)** |
| **3** | **Superficial sensory branch** (ulnar) | **Ulnar artery thrombosis / aneurysm** | **Sensory only** |

**No zone 4** — 3 zones only

**Lesion in Guyon's canal (TO)** — can give **both** motor + sensory loss (motor, sensory or mixed by zone)

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray / carpal tunnel view / CT | **Hook of hamate fracture** |
| **MRI wrist** | **Ganglion** (T2 hyperintense) → **study that identifies causative lesion**; gradient echo shows aneurysm |
| **Doppler US / arteriogram / MR angiography** | **Ulnar artery thrombosis / aneurysm** |
| US | Ganglion (well-circumscribed, homogeneous → no biopsy) |

![MRI: ganglion in Guyon's canal](../../MCQ/attachments/q213015-illb-coronal-t2.jpg)

### Electrodiagnostics

**NCS/EMG** — diagnosis + prognosis; **CV <50 m/s across elbow**; ↓ SNAP & CMAP amplitudes (Orthobullets, TO)

**EMG localisation** — fibrillations in **adductor pollicis**\
✗ Wrong options: FCU · FDP little · ECU · FPL

**Pure motor lesion** — normal sensory conduction, **delayed motor to 1st dorsal interosseous**

**EMG/NCV** — confirms neuropathy but **does not identify cause** (→ MRI)

## 5 Management

```
Ulnar tunnel syndrome
├─ Mild, no lesion → ACTIVITY MODIFICATION, NSAIDs, SPLINT
├─ Compressive lesion (ganglion) with neuro deficit → EXCISION + DECOMPRESSION
│      └─ excise stalk (pisohamate) → ↓ recurrence
└─ Failed nonop / severe → RELEASE ALL 3 ZONES
       └─ Ulnar artery thrombosis → SEGMENTAL RESECTION + VEIN GRAFT
```

**Local decompression** — release hypothenar origin · excise ganglion · **resect hook of hamate** · vascular repair · **explore all three zones**

**Ulnar artery thrombosis (TO)** — **segmental resection + vein graft** preferred when feasible

**With CTS** — add **carpal tunnel release**

### Tendon transfers

| Goal | Transfer |
|---|---|
| **Correct claw** | ECRL / ECRB / PL graft, **volar to transverse MC ligament**, 2- or 4-tailed to **A2 pulley** ring + little |
| **Power pinch** | **Smith transfer**: ECRB or **FDS ring** |
| **Little finger adduction (Wartenberg)** | **Ulnar insertion of EDM** → A1 pulley / RCL little finger |

## 6 Complications

**Recurrence** — main complication (ganglion stalk left)

## X vs Y

| | Ulnar tunnel (Guyon) | Cubital tunnel |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal ulnar hand sensation** | **Spared** | **Lost** |
| Clawing | **More** | Less |
| FCU / FDP ring-little | Normal | Weak |
| Tinel | Wrist | Elbow; + elbow flexion test |
| Compression site | **Ganglion** in canal | Osborne's ligament / FCU heads / anconeus epitrochlearis |

| Look-alike | Differentiator |
|---|---|
| **Limburg-Comstock** | FPL–FDP index anomalous connection (~20%); cannot flex thumb IP without index DIP |
| Parsonage-Turner | Viral brachial neuritis; shoulder pain; cannot flex thumb IP + index |
| C8–T1 radiculopathy | Neck; dermatomal |
| AIN (Gantzer muscle) | Loss FPL, FDP index, PQ; no sensory loss |

## How it's asked

**"Lesion in Guyon's canal leads to?"** → **both motor + sensory loss** (TO)

**"Ring/little numbness, no weakness, ulnar artery aneurysm?"** → **zone 3 compression** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: normal EDS · Jeanne · cervical

**"Intrinsic atrophy, normal sensation, lesion at Guyon — zone?"** → **zone 2**

**"Ganglion in Guyon + weak 1st DI — additional finding?"** → **Froment sign**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal ulnar numbness

**"Dorsal sensation normal, Froment + — EMG fibrillations in?"** → **adductor pollicis**

**"Abducted little finger — weak muscle?"** → **3rd palmar interosseous**

**"Ganglion between TCL and volar carpal ligament — sensory area?"** → **volar ulnar 1½ digits only**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal ulnar

**"Weak key pinch, volar ulnar numbness, dorsal normal — cause?"** → **ganglion in Guyon's canal**

**"Pure motor, ganglion on MRI/US — next step?"** → **cyst excision**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · hamate excision

**"Which study identifies the causative lesion?"** → **MRI wrist**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG/NCV

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ulnar Variance

*Hand · 97 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & demographics

**Ulnar variance (UV)** — length of ulna vs radius at the wrist; **neutral (ulnar zero) = difference <1 mm**

**Demographics** — UV **lower in males**; **UV ↑ with age**

**Child gymnasts** — **positive UV** (distal radial physeal arrest)

### Load distribution & radiographic measurement

| Ulnar variance | Ulna − radius | Load through radius | Load through ulna | Image |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Positive | **+2 mm** | 60% | **40%** | |
| Positive | +1 mm | 70% | 30% | ![Positive UV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6046-positive.jpg) |
| **Neutral** | 0 (<1 mm) | **80%** | **20%** | ![Neutral UV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6046-neutral.jpg) |
| Negative | −1 mm | 90% | 10% | |
| Negative | **−2 mm** | **95%** | **5%** | ![Negative UV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6046-negative.jpg) |

**Palmer & Werner** — +2.5 mm → ulnocarpal load **18% → 42%**; −2.5 mm → **4.3%**

**Dynamic** — UV **more positive in pronation** and **with grip**; **↓ in supination**

## 2 Mechanism

### Causes

| Cause | Positive UV | Negative UV |
|---|---|---|
| Congenital | **Madelung deformity** | Reverse Madelung |
| Trauma | DRF with radial shortening, **growth arrest** (SH injury), **Galeazzi, Essex-Lopresti** | Ulnar fracture with shortening |
| Iatrogenic | **Radial head resection** | Joint-levelling (ulnar shortening / radial lengthening) |

### Carpal instability adaptive (CIA) after radius malunion

**CIA (TO)** — carpal malalignment **secondary to extrinsic deformity**; carpal bones + intrinsic/extrinsic ligaments **normal**

**Commonest cause (TO)** — **DRF malunion with dorsal tilt** (normal volar tilt ≈ 11°) → **lunate extends (tilts dorsally)** to realign with radial surface → **adaptive DISI**\
✗ Wrong options: lunate flexes · ulnar translocation

**Key difference from CID (TO)** — in CIA **intrinsic interosseous ligaments intact**; CID = primary intrinsic ligament failure

**Other causes (TO)** — **scaphoid humpback malunion** · metacarpal malunion · **Madelung (↑ volar tilt → adaptive VISI)**

**Clinical (TO)** — pain, stiffness, ↓ grip, **↓ extension**, midcarpal prominence; **Watson + ballottement negative**

**X-ray (TO)** — DR malunion (↓ volar tilt, ↓ inclination, shortening, DRUJ incongruity); **↑ SL angle but SL gap normal**

**Treatment (TO)** — **correct skeletal deformity**: **opening wedge DR osteotomy + structural graft + volar locking plate** → carpus resets; scaphoid malunion → volar wedge graft (iliac crest / vascularised MFC) + headless screw; **no ligament reconstruction or intercarpal fusion**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**View** — **PA wrist, shoulder abducted 90°, elbow flexed 90°, neutral rotation**

**PA vs AP** — UV **more positive on PA** (pronation); more negative on AP (supination)\
✗ Wrong options: radial inclination differs · AP better for scaphoid · DRUJ/lunate facet better on one view

**Measurement** — line tangential to **ulnar articular surface** ⟂ shaft vs line tangential to **lunate fossa of radius** ⟂ shaft; ulnar line distal = positive, proximal = negative; normal 0 mm

**MRI** — estimates but **cannot quantify** UV (wrist position not reproducible)

![UV changes with forearm rotation](../../MCQ/attachments/q218905-illb-supination-pronation-ulnar-variance.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Principle** — treat the condition: ulnar abutment, TFCC tear, Kienböck

## 6 Complications

### Associated conditions

| Positive UV | Negative UV |
|---|---|
| **Ulnar abutment (impaction) syndrome** | **Kienböck disease** |
| **TFCC tears** | **Ulnar impingement** (ulna hits radius proximal to sigmoid notch) |
| LT ligament tears, SLD | |
| Arthrosis ulnar head / lunate / triquetrum | |

![Positive UV with ulnocarpal abutment](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6046-ulnar-positive-variance-ot04-266-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | CIA | CID |
|---|---|---|
| Intrinsic ligaments | **Intact** | Torn (e.g. SL) |
| SL gap | Normal | Widened |
| Watson test | Negative | Positive |
| Treatment | **Correct osteotomy** → carpus resets | Ligament repair / reconstruction / fusion |

## How it's asked

**"−2.5 mm UV — load distribution?"** → **95% radius / 5% ulna**

**"Neutral UV load?"** → **80% radius / 20% ulna**

**"PA vs AP view?"** → **UV more positive on PA**

**"UV more positive in?"** → **pronation and grip**

**"Negative UV associated with?"** → **Kienböck**

**"CIA after dorsally tilted DR malunion — lunate?"** → **extends (dorsal tilt)** (TO)

**"CIA vs CID fundamental difference?"** → **intrinsic ligaments intact in CIA** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ulnocarpal Abutment Syndrome

*Hand · 98 / 103*

## 1 Basics

### Load transmission

| Ulnar variance | Ulna load | Radius load |
|---|---|---|
| **Neutral** | **20%** | **80%** |
| **+2 mm** | **40%** | 60% |

**Definition** — excessive impact between **ulnar head and carpus (primarily lunate)** → common ulnar-sided wrist pain

**Positive variance causes/associations** — SL dissociation, **TFCC tears**, LT tears, **radial shortening after Colles**, **distal radius physeal fracture** in adolescence (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — ulnar/dorsal DRUJ pain; ↑ with **ulnar deviation**, **axial load**, rotation (jar opening, wringing towel, door opening, punching, weight bearing on extended wrist); relief with rest

| Test | Technique / meaning |
|---|---|
| **Ballottement** | Dorsal–palmar ulna displacement in ulnar deviation → pain |
| **Nakamura ulnar stress** | Pronated wrist ulnar deviated + axial load, flex/extend → pain |
| **Fovea test** | Palpate between styloid + FCU → **TFCC / ulnotriquetral ligament tear** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Required view** — **PA/AP in neutral rotation** (zero rotation) to measure variance

**Pronated grip view** — **↑ impaction** (dynamic positive variance)

**X-ray signs** — **positive ulnar variance** · **sclerosis/cysts of lunate + ulnar head**

**MRI** — TFCC tear (changes treatment); T1/T2 lunate signal change

**Arthrography** — TFCC + LT tears

![Ulnar impaction](../../MCQ/attachments/q1371-illa-ulnarimpaction.jpg)

### Differential – benign variant

**Differentials** — ulnar styloid impaction, DRUJ instability/arthritis, TFCC tear, LT tear, pisotriquetral arthritis, ECU tendinitis/instability, **LT coalition**

**Lunotriquetral coalition** — **uncommon normal variant, no significant sequelae**; incidental; commoner in African descent; persistent pain → arthrodesis\
✗ Wrong options: takedown for ROM · scaphoid fracture risk · abnormal TFCC · inflammatory arthritis

**Coalition syndromes** — arthrogryposis, diastrophic dwarfism, Turner, Ellis-van Creveld

## 5 Management

```
Ulnocarpal abutment
├─ Mild → rest, splint, supportive
├─ Positive variance ± DRUJ incongruity (young, active) → ulnar shortening osteotomy
│      └─ variance <4 mm → arthroscopic wafer
└─ DRUJ arthritis → salvage
       ├─ laborer → hemiresection + TFCC / Sauvé-Kapandji
       └─ low demand → Darrach
```

### Ulnar shortening osteotomy vs wafer – indications & outcomes

**Ulnar shortening osteotomy** — **treatment of choice** for most positive variance + **DRUJ incongruity** in young adults (TO: MMA student after adolescent DRF → **distal ulnar osteotomy**; ✗ Wrong options: TFCC repair alone · interposition · Darrach)

**USO advantages** — preserves ulnar dome cartilage + DRUJ; **tightens TFCC + ulnocarpal ligaments**; may improve DRUJ congruity; **carpal cysts can resolve**

**USO approach** — **subcutaneous border of ulna**; ± arthroscopic TFCC repair/débridement

**USO pitfalls** — protect **dorsal sensory branch of ulnar n.**; **dorsal plate → ECU tendinitis**

**USO contraindications** — **DRUJ arthrosis** · **reverse oblique DRUJ inclination** (↑ radioulnar contact)\
✗ Wrong options: "results encouraging with DRUJ degeneration"

**Wafer** — arthroscopic removal of **2–4 mm** under TFCC; variance **<4 mm**

| At 1 y | USO vs wafer |
|---|---|
| Outcome scores, grip, ROM, pain | **Equal** |
| **Reoperation / complications** | **↑ USO** (symptomatic hardware) |
| Return to work | **Wafer faster** |

### DRUJ arthritis – hemiresection arthroplasty

| Procedure | Indication / key fact |
|---|---|
| **Hemiresection arthroplasty** | Post-traumatic DRUJ arthritis; **needs intact or reconstructed TFCC** (prevents ulnar instability); **heavy laborer** |
| **Sauvé-Kapandji** | DRUJ fusion + proximal ulnar **pseudarthrosis**; **manual laborers**; preserves ulnocarpal support |
| **Darrach** (ulnar head resection) | **Low-demand / elderly**; risk **painful proximal stump instability** |
| Ulnar head replacement | Severe arthrosis; **salvage failed Darrach** |

**Laborer with DRUJ arthritis + TFCC tear** — **hemiresection + TFCC repair/reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: Darrach · complete head resection · isolated TFCC reconstruction · styloid repair

![Sauvé-Kapandji](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6048-sauve.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"MMA puncher, positive variance after adolescent DRF — best treatment?"** → **distal ulnar (shortening) osteotomy** (TO)

**"USO most indicated for?"** → **young, ulnar positive + DRUJ incongruity**\
✗ Wrong options: neutral/negative · elderly with DRUJ arthritis

**"USO — all true EXCEPT"** → **good results despite DRUJ arthritis**

**"USO vs wafer at 1 y?"** → **↑ reoperation with USO**\
✗ Wrong options: better ROM/grip · earlier RTW

**"Construction worker, DRUJ arthritis + TFCC tear?"** → **hemiresection arthroplasty + TFCC repair**

**"Fused lunate–triquetrum on X-ray?"** → **benign variant, no sequelae**

**"Load through ulna with +2 mm variance?"** → **40%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Wartenberg's Syndrome

*Hand · 99 / 103*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **"cheiralgia paresthetica"** = compressive neuropathy of the **superficial sensory radial nerve (SRN)** (TO); **no motor deficit**

**Incidence** — rare, **<1/100,000/yr**

**Demographics** — **♂:♀ 1:4**; 20–70 y

**Association** — **De Quervain's in 20–50%**

### Anatomy – superficial radial nerve

**Origin** — radial nerve divides anterior to lateral epicondyle → **PIN (motor)** + **SRN (sensory)** (TO)

**Course** — deep to **brachioradialis** in forearm

**Emerges subcutaneous** — between **brachioradialis & ECRL**, **9 cm** proximal to radial styloid (Orthobullets, TO) · **~8 cm** (OB explanation) — read the options

**Dorsal branch** — 1–3 cm radial to **Lister's tubercle** → 1st & 2nd web spaces

**Palmar branch** — within 2 cm of 1st dorsal compartment, over EPL → dorsolateral thumb

**Overlap** — with **LACN** → hypoaesthesia may be absent

![SRN emerging between brachioradialis and ECRL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6025-hoppenfield-5-moved.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Compression site & sensory-only presentation

**Site** — **scissoring of brachioradialis + ECRL tendons in forearm PRONATION** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: supination · ECRL/ECRB · APL/EPB

**Other** — **fascial bands** at subcutaneous exit

**Causes** — forearm fracture, **handcuffs**, tight **wristwatch**/bracelet/cast; repetitive circumduction (knitting)

![Scissoring of brachioradialis and ECRL in pronation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6025-scissor.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — burning, ill-defined pain + paresthesia/numbness **dorsoradial hand**, dorsal 1st web & thumb; dislikes watch

**Aggravated by (TO)** — **wrist flexion + ulnar deviation + pronation**

**Motor** — **none** (TO)

| Exam | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Tinel over SRN** | **Most common finding** |
| Wrist flexion + UD + pronation 1 min | Provocative |
| **Finkelstein** | ↑ symptoms in **96%** (nerve traction), even without 1st compartment tenosynovitis (TO) |
| Diagnostic wrist block | Temporary relief |

![Provocative test: flexion, ulnar deviation, pronation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob6025-flexion.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — limited value; old forearm fracture

**EMG/NCS** — limited value; often **normal**

## 5 Management

```
Wartenberg's syndrome
├─ First line → rest, activity modification, remove watch band, NSAIDs, splint
│      └─ non-responders → (US-guided) steroid injection
└─ Persists >6 mo → SRN decompression between BR & ECRL
```

### Radial sensory nerve decompression

**Nonop** — most improve (TO); **spontaneous resolution common**; steroid injection evidence limited

**Surgery** — **after 6 mo** of failed nonop (Orthobullets); OB MCQ answered decompression after 6 wk failed nonop

**Procedure** — **SRN decompression in BR–ECRL interval**: neurolysis + release of fascia\
✗ Wrong options: LACN · ECRL–ECRB · BR–ECRB · APL–EPB

**Incision** — longitudinal, **volar to Tinel** → protects **LACN**, avoids scar tethering over SRN

**Outcome** — **74%** success

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — failed decompression, persistent pain/numbness, wound dehiscence, infection

## X vs Y

| | **Wartenberg's syndrome** | **De Quervain's** | **Intersection syndrome** | LACN neuritis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | SRN | 1st compartment (APL, EPB) | 1st crossing 2nd compartment | LACN |
| Site | BR–ECRL, ~8–9 cm above styloid | Radial styloid | **4–6 cm proximal to Lister's** (TO) | Lateral forearm |
| Pronation ↑ pain | **Yes** | **No** | No | — |
| Sign | Tinel over SRN | Finkelstein | **"Wet leather" crepitus**, swelling | Tinel over LACN (mimics) |

**Wartenberg's sign (TO)** — **abducted little finger** in **ulnar nerve palsy**: unopposed radial-innervated EDM/EDC; more with fingers extended\
✗ Wrong options: Wartenberg's syndrome

## How it's asked

**"Wartenberg syndrome NOT characterised by?"** → **compression of motor branch of radial nerve** (TO)

**"Compression takes place between?"** → **brachioradialis & ECRL in pronation** (TO)

**"Failed nonop — decompress?"** → **radial sensory nerve between BR & ECRL**

**"Most common exam finding?"** → **Tinel over SRN**

**"Finkelstein positive in?"** → **96%**

**"Incision placed where?"** → **volar to Tinel** (protect LACN)

**"Abducted little finger in ulnar palsy?"** → **Wartenberg's sign** (TO)
